## Dedication / 献词
===Original===
To 

 Jeffrey Bernerd

===Chinese===
致

杰弗里·伯纳德（Jeffrey Bernerd）

## The Man Who Lived at Clapham / 住在克拉珀姆的人
===Original===
“The jury cannot accept the unsupported suggestion﻿—unsupported even by the prisoner’s testimony since he has not gone into the box﻿—that Mr. Noah Stedland is a blackmailer and that he obtained a large sum of money from the prisoner by this practice. That is a defence which is rather suggested by the cross-examination than by the production of evidence. The defence does not even tell us the nature of the threat which Stedland employed﻿ ﻿…”

The remainder of the summing up was creditable to the best traditions of the Bar, and the jury, without retiring, returned a verdict of “Guilty.”

There was a rustle of movement in the court and a thin babble of whispered talk as the Judge fixed his pince-nez and began to write.

The man in the big oaken pen looked down at the pale drawn face of a girl turned to him from the well of the court and smiled encouragingly. For his part, he did not blanch and his grave eyes went back to the figure on the Bench﻿—the puce-gowned, white-headed figure that was writing so industriously. What did a Judge write on these occasions, he wondered? Surely not a précis of the crime. He was impatient now to have done with it all; this airy court, these blurred rows of pink faces in the gloom of the public gallery, the indifferent counsel and particularly with the two men who had sat near the lawyer’s pews watching him intently.

===Chinese===
「陪审团不能接受这一无凭无据的臆说——被告既未曾上证人席作证，这臆说甚至连他的证词都无从依托——即诺亚·斯特德兰（Noah Stedland）先生是个讹诈犯、且曾以此道自被告手中攫得巨款的说法。这与其说是一项有证据支持的辩护，不如说是盘诘里暗示出来的东西。辩方甚至没有告诉我们，斯特德兰所施加的威胁究竟属于何种性质……」

这番结案陈词的其余部分，颇不负律师界最优良的传统；陪审团未经退席合议，便裁定了「有罪」。

法庭里响起一阵窸窣的骚动和低低的嗡嗡私语，法官戴上夹鼻眼镜，提笔写了起来。

站在高大橡木被告栏里的那个人，低头望着法庭中央一张转向他来的、苍白憔悴的姑娘面孔，报以鼓励的一笑。他自己倒未曾失色，一双沉静的眼睛转回法官席上那个身影——那身披紫褐法袍、一头白发、正伏案疾书的身影。这种场合法官写的究竟是什么呢，他暗自纳闷？总不会是罪案的摘要吧。他此刻只盼这一切早些了结；这间高旷的法庭，旁听席幽暗里一排排模糊的粉红面孔，心不在焉的律师们，尤其是坐在律师席近旁、目不转睛盯着他的那两个人。

===Original===
He wondered who they were, what interest they had in the proceedings. Perhaps they were foreign authors, securing firsthand impressions. They had the appearance of foreigners. One was very tall (he had seen him rise to his feet once), the other was slight and gave an impression of boyishness, though his hair was grey. They were both clean-shaven and both were dressed in black and balanced on their knees broad-brimmed hats of soft black felt.

A cough from the Judge brought his attention back to the Bench.

“Jeffrey Storr,” said his lordship, “I entirely agree with the verdict of the jury. Your defence that Stedland robbed you of your savings and that you broke into his house for the purpose of taking the law into your own hands and securing the money and a document, the character of which you do not specify but which you allege proved his guilt, could not be considered seriously by any Court of Justice. Your story sounds as though you had read of that famous, or infamous, association called the Four Just Men, which existed some years ago, but which is now happily dispersed. Those men set themselves to punish where the law failed. It is a monstrous assumption that the law ever fails! You have committed a very serious offence, and the fact that you were at the moment of your arrest and capture in possession of a loaded revolver, serves very gravely to aggravate your crime. You will be kept in penal servitude for seven years.”

Jeffrey Storr bowed and without so much as a glance at the girl in the court, turned and descended the steps leading to the cells.

===Chinese===
他纳闷这两人是谁，对这场审判又抱着什么兴味。也许是搜集第一手印象的外国作家吧。他们生得一副外国人的模样。一个身材很高（他见过他起身一回），另一个瘦削，透着一股少年气，虽然头发已经花白。两人都把脸刮得干干净净，都一身黑衣裳，膝头各端着一顶黑色软毡宽边帽。

法官咳了一声，将他的注意力拉回法官席。

「杰弗里·斯托尔（Jeffrey Storr），」法官大人说，「本庭完全赞同陪审团的裁定。你辩称斯特德兰侵夺了你的积蓄，你闯入他的宅子是为了将法律操之己手，索回那笔钱和一份文件——文件的性质你语焉不详，却一口咬定它能证明他的罪——这样的辩护，任何一家法院都不可能认真看待。你这套说辞听着倒像是从那个名声赫赫——或者说恶名昭彰——的团体，即四义士（The Four Just Men）的传闻里读来的；那团体数年前确曾存在，如今幸而早已星散。那些人自任其职，专在法律失效之处施以惩罚。而说法律竟会失效，这是何等荒谬的臆断！你所犯罪行十分严重，况且你被捕之时身上还带着一把上了膛的左轮枪，这更重重加重了你的罪。你将被处以七年苦役。」

杰弗里·斯托尔欠了欠身，看也不看法庭里那位姑娘一眼，转身走下通往囚室的台阶。

===Original===
The two foreign-looking men who had excited the prisoner’s interest and resentment were the first to leave the court.

Once in the street the taller of the two stopped.

“I think we will wait for the girl,” he said.

“Is she the wife?” asked the slight man.

“Married the week he made his unfortunate investment,” replied the tall man, then, “It was a curious coincidence, that reference of the Judge’s to the Four Just Men.”

===Chinese===
那两个惹得被告又好奇又愠怒的外貌像外国人的男子，最先离开了法庭。

一到街上，两人中身材高的那个便停了步。

「我想我们等等那位姑娘。」他说。

「她是他的妻子？」瘦削的一个问。

「就在他做下那桩倒霉『投资』的同一礼拜里成的婚。」高个子答道，接着又说，「法官提起四义士，倒是个奇妙的巧合。」

===Original===
The other smiled.

“It was in that very court that you were sentenced to death, Manfred,” he said, and the man called Manfred nodded.

“I wondered whether the old usher would remember me,” he answered, “he has a reputation for never forgetting a face. Apparently the loss of my beard has worked a miracle, for I actually spoke to him. Here she is.”

Fortunately the girl was alone. A beautiful face, thought Gonsalez, the younger of the two men. She held her chin high and there was no sign of tears. As she walked quickly toward Newgate Street they followed her. She crossed the road into Hatton Garden and then it was that Manfred spoke.

===Chinese===
另一个微微一笑。

「当年判你死刑的，正是这间法庭呢，曼弗雷德（Manfred）。」他说。被唤作曼弗雷德的人点了点头。

「我还嘀咕那位老庭丁认不认得我，」他答道，「他有个出了名的本事，一张面孔只要见过便永不忘记。看来我剃掉胡子真创造了奇迹——我方才居然同他说上了话。喏，她来了。」

幸好姑娘是独自一人。一张美丽的面孔，两人中较年轻的冈萨雷斯（Gonsalez）心想。她高高扬着下巴，没有一点泪意。她快步朝新门街（Newgate Street）走去，两人跟在后面。她穿过马路走进哈顿花园（Hatton Garden），这时曼弗雷德开口了。

===Original===
“Pardon me, Mrs. Storr,” he said, and she turned and stared at the foreign-looking man suspiciously.

“If you are a reporter﻿—” she began.

“I’m not,” smiled Manfred, “nor am I a friend of your husband’s, though I thought of lying to you in that respect in order to find an excuse for talking to you.”

His frankness procured her interest.

“I do not wish to talk about poor Jeffrey’s terrible trouble,” she said. “I just want to be alone.”

===Chinese===
「对不起，斯托尔太太。」他说。她转过身，狐疑地打量这个外国人模样的人。

「如果你是记者——」她开口道。

「我不是。」曼弗雷德微笑道，「也不是你丈夫的朋友——虽然我起过念头，想在这方面对你说个谎，好找个由头同你搭话。」

他这份坦白倒赢得了她的兴趣。

「我不愿意谈论可怜的杰弗里这桩可怕的灾祸，」她说，「我只想一个人待着。」

===Original===
Manfred nodded.

“I understand that,” he said sympathetically, “but I wish to be a friend of your husband’s and perhaps I can help him. The story he told in the box was true﻿—you thought that too, Leon?”

Gonsalez nodded.

“Obviously true,” he said, “I particularly noticed his eyelids. When a man lies he blinks at every repetition of the lie. Have you observed, my dear George, that men cannot tell lies when their hands are clenched and that when women lie they clasp their hands together?”

She looked at Gonsalez in bewilderment. She was in no mood for a lecture on the physiology of expression and even had she known that Leon Gonsalez was the author of three large books which ranked with the best that Lombroso or Mantegazza had given to the world, she would have been no more willing to listen.

===Chinese===
曼弗雷德点了点头。

「这我明白。」他体恤地说，「不过我很想成为你丈夫的朋友，兴许还能帮到他。他在证人席上讲的那番话是真的——你也这么看吧，莱昂？」

冈萨雷斯点头。

「显然是真的。」他说，「我特别留意了他的眼皮。男人说谎时，谎话每重复一遍必要眨一回眼。你可曾留意过，我亲爱的乔治，男人双手攥紧时便说不出谎，而女人说谎时却要把两只手绞在一起？」

她茫然地望着冈萨雷斯。她全无心思听什么表情生理学的讲课；就算她知道莱昂·冈萨雷斯写过三大部著作、与龙勃罗梭（Lombroso）和曼特加扎（Mantegazza）贡献给世间的精品齐名比肩，她也不会更乐意听下去。

===Original===
“The truth is, Mrs. Storr,” said Manfred, interpreting her new distress, “we think that we can free your husband and prove his innocence. But we want as many facts about the case as we can get.”

She hesitated only a moment.

“I have some furnished lodgings in Gray’s Inn Road,” she said, “perhaps you will be good enough to come with me.”

“My lawyer does not think there is any use in appealing against the sentence,” she went on as they fell in one on either side of her.

Manfred shook his head.

===Chinese===
「实情是这样的，斯托尔太太，」曼弗雷德说，读懂了她新添的愁苦，「我们认为我们能够还你丈夫自由，证明他的清白。但我们想尽可能多掌握这桩案子的实情。」

她只迟疑了片刻。

「我在格雷律师学院路（Gray’s Inn Road）有几间带家具的出租屋，」她说，「二位若肯赏光，就请随我来吧。」

「我的律师认为，对这判决上诉也无用处。」她继续说。两人一左一右走在她身旁。

曼弗雷德摇了摇头。

===Original===
“The Appeal Court would uphold the sentence,” he said quietly, “with the evidence you have there is no possibility of your husband being released.”

She looked round at him in dismay and now he saw that she was very near to tears.

“I thought﻿ ﻿… you said﻿ ﻿… ?” she began a little shakily.

Manfred nodded.

“We know Stedland,” he said, “and﻿—”

===Chinese===
「上诉法院会维持原判。」他平静地说，「凭你们手头的证据，你丈夫绝无获释的可能。」

她惶然回头望他，这时他才看出，她已濒于落泪。

「我方才以为……你是说……？」她微微发着颤开口。

曼弗雷德点头。

「我们认识斯特德兰。」他说，「而且——」

===Original===
“The curious thing about blackmailers, is that the occiput is hardly observable,” interrupted Gonsalez thoughtfully. “I examined sixty-two heads in the Spanish prisons and in every case the occipital protuberance was little more than a bony ridge. Now in homicidal heads the occiput sticks out like a pigeon’s egg.”

“My friend is rather an authority upon the structure of the head,” smiled Manfred. “Yes, we know Stedland. His operations have been reported to us from time to time. You remember the Wellingford case, Leon?”

Gonsalez nodded.

“Then you are detectives?” asked the girl.

===Chinese===
「说起讹诈犯，有桩奇事：他们的枕部几乎观察不到。」冈萨雷斯若有所思地插话道，「我在西班牙各监狱查验过六十二颗头颅，每一例的枕外隆突都不过是一道骨嵴。而在凶杀者的头颅上，枕部凸出得活像一枚鸽蛋。」

「我这位朋友，算得上头部构造方面的一位权威。」曼弗雷德含笑道，「不错，我们了解斯特德兰。他的种种行当，不时有人报到我们这里。韦灵福德（Wellingford）那桩案子，你还记得吧，莱昂？」

冈萨雷斯点头。

「那么二位是侦探？」姑娘问。

===Original===
Manfred laughed softly.

“No, we are not detectives﻿—we are interested in crime. I think we have the best and most thorough record of the unconvicted criminal class of any in the world.”

They walked on in silence for some time.

“Stedland is a bad man,” nodded Gonsalez as though the conviction had suddenly dawned upon him. “Did you observe his ears? They are unusually long and the outer margins are pointed﻿—the Darwinian tubercle, Manfred. And did you remark, my dear friend, that the root of the helix divides the concha into two distinct cavities and that the lobule was adherent? A truly criminal ear. The man has committed murder. It is impossible to possess such an ear and not to murder.”

The flat to which she admitted them was small and wretchedly furnished. Glancing round the tiny dining-room, Manfred noted the essential appointments which accompany a “furnished” flat.

===Chinese===
曼弗雷德轻轻笑了。

「不，我们不是侦探——我们只是对罪案有兴趣。论未定罪罪犯阶层的档案，我想我们手里这一份，是全世界最好、最完备的。」

一行三人默默走了一程。

「斯特德兰是个恶人。」冈萨雷斯点着头说，仿佛这层认识此刻才蓦地照亮了他，「你观察他的耳朵了没有？长得出奇，外缘带尖——达尔文结节，曼弗雷德。还有，我亲爱的朋友，你留意到没有：耳轮的根部把耳甲分成了两个各自独立的腔，耳垂又是贴连的？一只地地道道的罪犯之耳。这人犯过命案。长着这样一只耳朵而不杀人的，天下没有。」

她引两人进屋的那套公寓狭小，家什寒陋。曼弗雷德扫了一眼小小的饭厅，便看全了「带家具」公寓应有的那几件基本陈设。

===Original===
The girl, who had disappeared into her room to take off her coat, now returned, and sat by the table at which, at her invitation, they had seated themselves.

“I realise that I am being indiscreet,” she said with the faintest of smiles; “but I feel that you really want to help me, and I have the curious sense that you can! The police have not been unkind or unfair to me and poor Jeff. On the contrary, they have been most helpful. I fancy that they suspected Mr. Stedland of being a blackmailer, and they were hoping that we could supply some evidence. When that evidence failed, there was nothing for them to do but to press forward the charge. Now, what can I tell you?”

“The story which was not told in court,” replied Manfred.

She was silent for a time.

“I will tell you,” she said at last. “Only my husband’s lawyer knows, and I have an idea that he was sceptical as to the truth of what I am now telling you. And if he is sceptical,” she said in despair, “how can I expect to convince you?”

===Chinese===
姑娘进里屋脱大衣去了，这时回来，在桌旁坐下；两人承她相让，已在桌边落座。

「我知道自己这样做有失谨慎。」她带着一丝极淡的笑说，「可我觉得二位是真想帮我，而且我莫名地确信你们帮得上！警察对我和可怜的杰夫并不刻薄，也没有不公。恰恰相反，他们帮了很多忙。我猜想，他们本来就疑心斯特德兰先生是讹诈犯，一直指望我们能拿出些证据来。证据拿不出来，他们便只有把指控进行到底这一条路可走了。那么，我能告诉二位些什么呢？」

「把法庭上没讲的那一段讲给我们听。」曼弗雷德答道。

她沉默了一会儿。

「我讲给你们听吧。」她终于说，「这事只有我丈夫的律师知道，而且我总觉得，他对我现在要讲的这番话究竟是不是实情，本就半信半疑。倘若连他都存疑，」她绝望地说，「我又怎能指望说服二位呢？」

===Original===
The eager eyes of Gonsalez were fixed on hers, and it was he who answered.

“We are already convinced, Mrs. Storr,” and Manfred nodded.

Again there was a pause. She was evidently reluctant to begin a narrative which, Manfred guessed, might not be creditable to her; and this proved to be the case.

“When I was a girl,” she began simply, “I was at school in Sussex﻿—a big girls’ school; I think there were over two hundred pupils. I am not going to excuse anything I did,” she went on quickly. “I fell in love with a boy﻿—well, he was a butcher’s boy! That sounds dreadful, doesn’t it? But you understand I was a child, a very impressionable child﻿—oh, it sounds horrible, I know; but I used to meet him in the garden leading out from the prep. room after prayers; he climbed over the wall to those meetings, and we talked and talked, sometimes for an hour. There was no more in it than a boy and girl love affair, and I can’t explain just why I committed such a folly.”

===Chinese===
冈萨雷斯一双热切的眼睛紧盯着她的眼睛，答话的正是他。

「我们已经信了，斯托尔太太。」曼弗雷德也点了点头。

又是一阵沉默。她显然不愿起头讲这段经历——曼弗雷德揣度，这经历未必给她添光彩。事实也果然如此。

「我还是个小姑娘的时候，」她平平淡淡地开了头，「在萨塞克斯（Sussex）一家女子学校念书——很大的一家，我想有两百多名学生。我不打算给自己做的事找半句托词。」她赶忙接着说，「我爱上了一个男孩子——唉，他是个肉铺里送肉的小伙计！这话说出来难听，是不是？可你们要明白，那时我是个孩子，一个极容易动心的孩子——哦，我知道这听着有多糟；我常在晚祷之后，到自习室通出去的那片园子里同他会面；赴这些约会，他都是翻墙进来的。我们聊啊聊啊，有时一聊就是一个钟头。这无非是少男少女的一场懵懂情事，我说不清自己究竟为什么会做出那样的傻事。」

===Original===
“Mantegazza explains the matter very comfortably in his Study of Attraction,” murmured Leon Gonsalez. “But forgive me, I interrupted you.”

“As I say, it was a boy and girl friendship, a kind of hero worship on my part, for I thought he was wonderful. He must have been the nicest of butcher boys,” she smiled again, “because he never offended me by so much as a word. The friendship burnt itself out in a month or two, and there the matter might have ended, but for the fact that I had been foolish enough to write letters. They were very ordinary, stupid love-letters, and perfectly innocent﻿—or at least they seemed so to me at the time. Today, when I read them in the light of a greater knowledge they take my breath away.”

“You have them, then?” said Manfred.

She shook her head.

===Chinese===
「曼特加扎在《论吸引》里，把这类事解释得十分妥帖。」莱昂·冈萨雷斯低声说，「不过恕我打断了你。」

「正如我所说，那不过是一场少年情谊，在我这一边近乎英雄崇拜，因为我觉得他了不起。他准是送肉小伙计里最规矩的一个。」她又笑了，「因为他连一句话也不曾冒犯过我。这场情谊一两个月便自生自灭，事情本可以就此了结，无奈我糊涂，写了几封信。都是些平平常常、傻里傻气的情书，清白得很——至少当时在我看来是这样。今天再读，凭着我多懂得的那些世事，它们直叫我屏息心惊。」

「那么信还在你手里？」曼弗雷德说。

她摇了摇头。

===Original===
“When I said ‘them’ I meant one, and I only have a copy of that, supplied me by Mr. Stedland. The one letter that was not destroyed fell into the hands of the boy’s mother, who took it to the headmistress, and there was an awful row. She threatened to write to my parents who were in India, but on my solemn promise that the acquaintance should be dropped, the affair was allowed to blow over. How the letter came into Stedland’s hands I do not know; in fact, I had never heard of the man until a week before my marriage with Jeff. Jeff had saved about two thousand pounds, and we were looking forward to our marriage day when this blow fell. A letter from a perfectly unknown man, asking me to see him at his office, gave me my first introduction to this villain. I had to take the letter with me, and I went in some curiosity, wondering why I had been sent for. I was not to wonder very long. He had a little office off Regent Street, and after he had very carefully taken away the letter he had sent me, he explained, fully and frankly, just what his summons had meant.”

Manfred nodded.

“He wanted to sell you the letter,” he said, “for how much?”

“For two thousand pounds. That was the diabolical wickedness of it,” said the girl vehemently. “He knew almost to a penny how much Jeff had saved.”

===Chinese===
「我说『它们』，其实只剩一封，而且我手里那份还是斯特德兰先生提供的抄件。没被销毁的那一封信，落到了那男孩的母亲手里，她把信交给了女校长，于是掀起了一场天翻地覆的风波。她威胁要写信给我远在印度的父母，但我郑重许诺与此人断绝往来之后，这事便不了了之。那封信怎么会到斯特德兰手里，我不知道；说实在的，直到我和杰夫结婚前一个礼拜，我才头一回听说世上有这么一个人。杰夫攒下了大约两千镑，我们正盼着婚期，这场横祸便落了下来。一个素不相识的人来信，约我去他办公室见面——我就是这样初次结识了这个恶棍。我只好揣着那封信去了，心里还怀着几分好奇，不明白他找我做什么。我这好奇没能持续多久。他在摄政街（Regent Street）旁岔出的路上有个小办公室，等他小心翼翼地收回他寄给我的那封信，便充分而坦率地说明了他那封召见的信究竟是什么意思。」

曼弗雷德点头。

「他是要把那封信卖给你们。」他说，「要价多少？」

「两千镑。恶就恶在这里。」姑娘愤然道，「杰夫攒了多少钱，他几乎一便士不差地知道。」

===Original===
“Did he show you the letter?”

She shook her head.

“No, he showed me a photographic reproduction and as I read it and recalled what construction might be put upon this perfectly innocent note, my blood went cold. There was nothing to do but to tell Jeff, because the man had threatened to send facsimiles to all our friends and to Jeffrey’s uncle, who had made Jeffrey his sole heir. I had already told Jeffrey about what happened at school, thank heaven, and so I had no need to fear his suspicion. Jeffrey called on Mr. Stedland, and I believe there was a stormy scene; but Stedland is a big, powerful man in spite of his age, and in the struggle which ensued poor Jeffrey got a little the worst of it. The upshot of the matter was, Jeffrey agreed to buy the letter for two thousand pounds, on condition that Stedland signed a receipt, written on a blank page of the letter itself. It meant the losing of his life savings; it meant the possible postponement of our wedding; but Jeffrey would not take any other course. Mr. Stedland lives in a big house near Clapham Common﻿—”

“184 Park View West,” interrupted Manfred.

“You know?” she said in surprise. “Well, it was at this house Jeffrey had to call to complete the bargain. Mr. Stedland lives alone except for a manservant, and opening the door himself, he conducted Jeffrey up to the first floor, where he had his study. My husband, realising the futility of argument, paid over the money, as he had been directed by Stedland, in American bills﻿—”

===Chinese===
「他给你看过那封信吗？」

她摇了摇头。

「没有，他给我看的是一份照相复制件。我读着它，想到这封全然清白的短信可能被人解读成什么样子，浑身的血都凉了。除了告诉杰夫，再无别的路可走，因为那人威胁说，要把复制件寄给我们所有的亲友，还有杰弗里的伯父——伯父立了杰弗里做唯一继承人。感谢上天，学校里那桩事我早就告诉过杰弗里，所以我不怕他起疑心。杰弗里去拜访了斯特德兰先生，我相信那场面闹得很凶；可斯特德兰虽上了年纪，仍是个高大有力气的男人，随后扭打起来，吃了点亏的是可怜的杰弗里。事情的结局是：杰弗里同意出两千镑买下那封信，条件是斯特德兰在信本身的空白页上签一张收据。这意味着他一生的积蓄化为乌有，意味着我们的婚期可能推迟；但杰弗里不肯走别的路。斯特德兰先生住在克拉珀姆公地（Clapham Common）附近的一所大宅里——」

「帕克维尤西街（Park View West），门牌 184。」曼弗雷德插口道。

「你知道？」她讶然道，「好吧，杰弗里就是到这所宅子里去交割那笔交易的。斯特德兰先生除一个男仆外独自居住，他亲自开门，把杰弗里引上二楼他的书房。我丈夫明白争辩无益，便照斯特德兰的吩咐，把钱用美钞付了——」

===Original===
“Which are more difficult to trace, of course,” said Manfred.

“When he had paid him, Stedland produced the letter, wrote the receipt on the blank page, blotted it and placed it in an envelope, which he gave to my husband. When Jeffrey returned home and opened the envelope, he found it contained nothing more than a blank sheet of paper.”

“He had rung the changes,” said Manfred.

“That was the expression that Jeffrey used,” said the girl. “Then it was that Jeffrey decided to commit this mad act. You have heard of the Four Just Men?”

“I have heard of them,” replied Manfred gravely.

===Chinese===
「美钞当然更难追查。」曼弗雷德说。

「等他付了钱，斯特德兰取出那封信，在空白页上写了收据，吸了墨，装进一只信封，交到我丈夫手里。杰弗里回到家拆开信封，发现里面不过是一张白纸。」

「他玩了偷梁换柱的把戏。」曼弗雷德说。

「杰弗里用的正是这个字眼。」姑娘说，「正是在那之后，杰弗里决意干下这桩疯狂的勾当。二位听说过四义士吧？」

「听说过。」曼弗雷德郑重地答道。

===Original===
“My husband is a great believer in their methods, and a great admirer of them too,” she said. “I think he read everything that has ever been written about them. One night, two days after we were married﻿—I had insisted upon marrying him at once when I discovered the situation﻿—he came to me.

“ ‘Grace,’ he said, ‘I am going to apply the methods of the Four to this devil Stedland.’

“He outlined his plans. He had apparently been watching the house, and knew that except for the servant the man slept in the house alone, and he had formed a plan for getting in. Poor dear, he was an indifferent burglar; but you heard today how he succeeded in reaching Stedland’s room. I think he hoped to frighten the man with his revolver.”

===Chinese===
「我丈夫对他们那一套手法深信不疑，对他们本人也倾慕得很。」她说，「我想，凡写到过他们的文字，他统统读遍了。一天夜里，我们婚后第二天——当初我一发现这处境，便执意立刻嫁给了他——他来找我。

「『格蕾丝，』他说，『我要把四义士的那套办法，用到斯特德兰这个魔鬼身上。』

「他把计划从头到尾说了个大概。他显然一直在踩看那所宅子，知道那男人除仆人外独自睡在屋里，还定下了进去的法子。可怜的人，他实在是个蹩脚的窃贼；不过今天二位也听到了，他终究摸进了斯特德兰的房间。我想，他原是指望用手枪吓住那人。」

===Original===
Manfred shook his head.

“Stedland graduated as a gun-fighter in South Africa,” he said quietly. “He is the quickest man on the draw I know, and a deadly shot. Of course, he had your husband covered before he could as much as reach his pocket.”

She nodded.

“That is the story,” she said quietly. “If you can help Jeff, I shall pray for you all my life.”

===Chinese===
曼弗雷德摇了摇头。

「斯特德兰那一手拔枪的本事，是在南非练出来的。」他平静地说，「他是我所知拔枪最快的人，且弹无虚发。不消说，你丈夫还没来得及摸到口袋，枪口早把他罩住了。」

她点头。

「事情就是这样。」她平静地说，「二位若能救出杰夫，我会为你们祈祷一辈子。」

===Original===
Manfred rose slowly.

“It was a mad attempt,” he said. “In the first place Stedland would not keep a compromising document like that in his house, which he leaves for six hours a day. It might even have been destroyed, though that is unlikely. He would keep the letter for future use. Blackmailers are keen students of humanity, and he knows that money may still be made, from that letter of yours. But if it is in existence﻿—”

“If it is in existence,” she repeated﻿—and now the reaction had come and her lips were trembling﻿—

“I will place it in your hands within a week,” said Manfred, and with this promise left her.

===Chinese===
曼弗雷德缓缓起身。

「那是一次疯狂的尝试。」他说，「首先，斯特德兰不会把那样一件惹祸的文件，放在一座一天要离开六个钟头的宅子里。它甚至可能已经被销毁了——不过这不太可能。他会把信留着，日后派用场。讹诈犯都是研究人性的行家，他知道，从你那封信上还能再榨出钱来。可它万一还在这世上——」

「万一还在这世上。」她重复道——这时情绪的反噬涌了上来，她的嘴唇发起抖来——

「一礼拜之内，我把它交到你们手上。」曼弗雷德说完，带着这个承诺离她而去。

===Original===
Mr. Noah Stedland had left the Courts of Justice that afternoon with no particular sense of satisfaction save that he was leaving it by the public entrance. He was not a man who was easily scared, but he was sensitive to impressions; and it seemed to him that the Judge’s carefully chosen words had implied, less in their substance than in their tone, a veiled rebuke to himself. Beyond registering this fact, his sensitiveness did not go. He was a man of comfortable fortune, and that fortune had been got together in scraps﻿—sometimes the scraps were unusually large﻿—by the exercise of qualities which were not handicapped by such imponderable factors as conscience or remorse. Life to this tall, broad-shouldered, grey-faced man was a game, and Jeffrey Storr, against whom he harboured no resentment, was a loser.

He could think dispassionately of Storr in his convict clothes, wearing out the years of agony in a convict prison, and at the mental picture could experience no other emotion than that of the successful gambler who can watch his rival’s ruin with equanimity.

He let himself into his narrow-fronted house, closed and double-locked the door behind him, and went up the shabbily carpeted stairs to his study. The ghosts of the lives he had wrecked should have crowded the room; but Mr. Stedland did not believe in ghosts. He rubbed his finger along a mahogany table and noted that it was dusty, and the ghost of a well-paid charlady took shape from that moment.

===Chinese===
那天下午，诺亚·斯特德兰先生离开法院时，并没有多少称心满意之感，唯一可算得意的，是他走的是公众出入口离场。他不是轻易被吓着的人，但他对种种印象敏感；在他听来，法官字斟句酌的那番话，与其说在字面上，不如说在语调里，藏着对他本人的一番含而不露的训斥。除了记下这一条，他的敏感也就到此为止。他是个家道殷实的人，而这份家财是零敲碎打攒起来的——有时那碎块也大得出奇——凭的是一些不为良心或悔恨这类无斤两的东西所拖累的本事。对这高个子、宽肩膀、灰面孔的男人来说，人生是一场赌局；杰弗里·斯托尔是个输家，他对他并无怨怼。

他能不动声色地想着斯托尔穿上囚衣、在某座苦役监狱里煎熬那漫长的刑期；面对脑中这幅图景，他心里涌起的，无非是赌桌上的赢家眼见对家倾家荡产时的那一份泰然。

他开了门锁走进自己那所门面窄小的宅子，回身关门，上了双重锁，踏着破旧的地毯上楼进了书房。被他毁掉的那些人生，冤魂本该挤满这间屋子；可斯特德兰先生不信鬼。他用手指抹过一张红木桌面，见上面蒙着灰，一位报酬优渥的打杂女佣的「鬼影」，便从这一刻起有了形。

===Original===
As he sprawled back in his chair, a big cigar between his gold-spotted teeth, he tried to analyse the queer sensation he had experienced in court. It was not the Judge, it was not the attitude of the defending counsel, it was not even the possibility that the world might censure him, which was responsible for his mental perturbation. It was certainly not the prisoner and his possible fate, or the white-faced wife. And yet there had been a something or a somebody which had set him glancing uneasily over his shoulder.

He sat smoking for half an hour, and then a bell clanged and he went down the stairs and opened the front door. The man who was waiting with an apologetic smile on his face, a jackal of his, was butler and tout and general errand-boy to the hard-faced man.

“Come in, Jope,” he said, closing the door behind the visitor. “Go down to the cellar and get me a bottle of whisky?”

“How was my evidence, guv’nor?” asked the sycophant, smirking expectantly.

===Chinese===
他仰身摊坐在椅子里，金斑牙齿间叼着一支大雪茄，极力想剖析自己在法庭上体会到的那种古怪滋味。那不是法官，不是辩护律师的态度，甚至也不是世人可能加诸他的非难，搅得他心神不宁。更不是那被告和他的下场，也不是那张苍白的妻子面孔。然而当时确曾有什么东西、或是甚么人物，害得他一再不安地回头张望。

他坐着抽了半个钟头的烟，随后门铃铿然一响，他下楼开了前门。门外那人堆着一脸赔笑候着，是他豢养的一条走狗，身兼管家、眼线兼跑腿杂役，伺候这位面相冷酷的主人。

「进来吧，乔普（Jope）。」他说着，随手在来客身后把门关上，「下地窖给我拿瓶威士忌来。」

「我今天的证词怎么样，老板？」这条谄媚的走狗满脸堆笑，巴巴地等着夸。

===Original===
“Rotten,” growled Stedland. “What did you mean by saying you heard me call for help?”

“Well, guv’nor, I thought I’d make it a little worse for him,” said Jope humbly.

“Help!” sneered Mr. Stedland. “Do you think I’d call on a guy like you for help? A damned lot of use you would be in a rough house! Get that whisky!”

When the man came up with a bottle and a syphon, Mr. Stedland was gazing moodily out of the window which looked upon a short, untidy garden terminating in a high wall. Behind that was a space on which a building had been in course of erection when the armistice put an end to Government work. It was designed as a small factory for the making of fuses, and was an eyesore to Mr. Stedland, since he owned the ground on which it was built.

===Chinese===
「糟透了。」斯特德兰咆哮道，「你说听见我喊救命，是什么意思？」

「呃，老板，我想把他的罪名再加重点儿。」乔普低声下气地说。

「救命！」斯特德兰先生冷笑一声，「你以为我会叫你这种货色救命？真动起手来，你顶个屁用！拿威士忌去！」

等那人捧着一瓶酒和一只苏打水瓶上来时，斯特德兰先生正闷闷地望着窗外。窗子对着一片不长而杂乱的园子，尽头横着一堵高墙。墙后是一块空地，上面原有一座建筑正盖到半途，一纸停战协定叫政府工程尽数下马，它便停在了那里。那建筑原是要做一间制造引信的小工厂的，如今成了斯特德兰先生的眼中钉——因为厂房占的那块地皮，本是他名下的产业。

===Original===
“Jope,” he said, turning suddenly, “was there anybody in court we know?”

“No, Mr. Stedland,” said the man, pausing in surprise. “Not that I know, except Inspector﻿—”

“Never mind about the Inspector,” answered Mr. Stedland impatiently. “I know all the splits who were there. Was there anybody else﻿—anybody who has a grudge against us?”

“No, Mr. Stedland. What does it matter if there was?” asked the valorous Jope. “I think we’re a match for any of ’em.”

===Chinese===
「乔普，」他蓦地转过身来，「法庭上有咱们认得的人吗？」

「没有，斯特德兰先生。」那人一怔，「就我所知没有，除了那位督察——」

「督察不消你说。」斯特德兰先生不耐烦地答道，「在场的那几个探子我全认得。除了他们还有谁——有没有跟咱们结着仇的？」

「没有，斯特德兰先生。就算有，又有什么要紧？」胆气过人的乔普问道，「来一个，咱们收拾一个。」

===Original===
“How long have we been in partnership?” asked Stedland unpleasantly, as he poured himself out a tot of whisky.

The man’s face twisted in an ingratiating smile.

“Well, we’ve been together some time now, Mr. Stedland,” he said.

Stedland smacked his lips and looked out of the window again.

“Yes,” he said after a while, “we’ve been together a long time now. In fact, you would almost have finished your sentence, if I had told the police what I knew about you seven years ago﻿—”

===Chinese===
「咱们搭伙多少年了？」斯特德兰一边给自己斟上一小杯威士忌，一边不客气地问。

那人的脸扭出一个讨好的笑。

「呃，咱们凑在一块儿也有些年头了，斯特德兰先生。」他说。

斯特德兰咂了咂嘴，又望向窗外。

「是啊，」过了片刻他说，「咱们在一块儿确实很久了。说起来，七年前我要是把知道的事捅给警察，你那徒刑如今都快服完了——」

===Original===
The man winced, and changed the subject. He might have realised, had he thought, that the sentence of seven years had been commuted by Stedland to a sentence of life servitude, but Mr. Jope was no thinker.

“Anything for the Bank today, sir?” he asked.

“Don’t be a fool,” said Stedland. “The Bank closed at three. Now, Jope,” he turned on the other, “in future you sleep in the kitchen.”

“In the kitchen, sir?” said the astonished servant, and Stedland nodded.

“I’m taking no more risks of a night visitor,” he said. “That fellow was on me before I knew where I was, and if I hadn’t had a gun handy he would have beaten me. The kitchen is the only way you can break into this house from the outside, and I’ve got a feeling at the back of my mind that something might happen.”

===Chinese===
那人缩了一下，赶忙岔开话头。他若肯细想，本可明白那七年徒刑，早被斯特德兰改判成了终身苦役；可乔普先生不是个肯动脑子的人。

「今天银行那边有活儿要办吗，先生？」他问。

「别犯傻。」斯特德兰说，「银行三点就关门了。听好，乔普，」他倏地转向那人，「往后你睡厨房去。」

「睡厨房，先生？」仆人愕然。斯特德兰点了点头。

「夜里有客来访的险，我不能再冒了。」他说，「那家伙扑到我身上时，我还没回过神来；要不是枪就在手边，我早吃了亏。要从外头撬进这宅子，只有厨房一条道。我心底总有一种感觉，觉得要出事。」

===Original===
“But he’s gone to gaol.”

“I’m not talking about him,” snarled Stedland. “Do you understand, take your bed to the kitchen.”

“It’s a bit draughty﻿—” began Jope.

“Take your bed to the kitchen,” roared Stedland, glaring at the man.

“Certainly, sir,” said Jope with alacrity.

===Chinese===
「可他不是已经蹲了大牢么。」

「我说的不是他。」斯特德兰吼道，「听见没有，把你的铺盖搬到厨房去。」

「那儿有点漏风——」乔普刚开口。

「把你的铺盖搬到厨房去！」斯特德兰瞪着那人咆哮。

「照办，先生。」乔普麻利地应道。

===Original===
When his servant had gone, Stedland took off his coat and put on one of stained alpaca, unlocked the safe, and took out a book. It was a passbook from his bank, and its study was very gratifying. Mr. Stedland dreamed dreams of a South American ranch and a life of ease and quiet. Twelve years’ strenuous work in London had made him a comparatively rich man. He had worked cautiously and patiently and had pursued the business of blackmail in a businesslike manner. His cash balance was with one of the best known of the private bankers. Sir William Molbury & Co., Ltd. Molbury’s Bank had a reputation in the City for the privacy and even mystery which enveloped the business of its clients﻿—a circumstance which suited Mr. Stedland admirably. It was, too, one of those old-fashioned banks which maintain a huge reserve of money in its vaults; and this was also a recommendation to Mr. Stedland, who might wish to gather in his fluid assets in the shortest possible space of time.

The evening and the night passed without any untoward incident, except as was revealed when Mr. Jope brought his master’s tea in the morning, and told, somewhat hoarsely, of a cold and unpleasant night.

“Get more bedclothes,” said Stedland curtly.

===Chinese===
仆人下去后，斯特德兰脱下外衣，换上一件沾了渍的羊驼呢上衣，开了保险柜，取出一册本子。那是他银行的存折，细细研读之下，甚是可喜。斯特德兰先生做着南美牧场、清闲度日的美梦。在伦敦苦心经营十二年，他已算得上一介富室。他行事谨慎而有耐心，把讹诈这门生意做得一板一眼。他的现金存在一家最有名的私人银行——威廉·莫尔伯里爵士有限公司（Sir William Molbury & Co., Ltd.）。莫尔伯里银行在伦敦金融城里素以客户事务的隐秘、乃至神秘著称——这一层最合斯特德兰先生的心意。而且它还是那种老派银行，库房里备着巨额的现金储备；这一点对斯特德兰先生同样是个长处，因为他也许巴望着在最短的时间里，把手头的浮财聚拢一空。

一夜过去，平安无事，唯一的端倪，是第二天早上乔普先生给主人送茶时嗓音有些沙哑，诉说这一夜又冷又难受。

「多领床被子。」斯特德兰简短地说。

===Original===
He went off to his city office after breakfast, and left Mr. Jope to superintend the operations of the charwoman and to impress upon her a number of facts, including the high rate at which she was paid, the glut of good charwomen on the market and the consequences which would overtake her if she left Mr. Stedland’s study undusted.

At eleven o’clock that morning came a respectable and somewhat elderly looking gentleman in a silk hat, and him Mr. Jope interviewed on the doormat.

“I’ve come from the Safe Deposit,” said the visitor.

“What Safe Deposit?” asked the suspicious Mr. Jope.

“The Fetter Lane Deposit,” replied the other. “We want to know if you left your keys behind the last time you came?”

===Chinese===
早饭后他进城上办公室去了，留下乔普先生督办打杂女佣的活计，并向她晓以若干事实，包括：她的工钱何等优厚，市面上贤惠的打杂女佣如何供过于求，以及斯特德兰先生的书房若留下一层灰，等待她的将是何等下场。

那天上午十一点钟，来了一位体面的、看去上了些年纪的绅士，头戴大礼帽。乔普先生在门垫上接待了他。

「我是保险寄存行来的。」来客说。

「哪家保险寄存行？」多疑的乔普先生问。

「费特巷（Fetter Lane）那家。」对方答道，「我们想知道，您上回来的时候，钥匙是不是落在我们那儿了？」

===Original===
Jope shook his head.

“We haven’t any Safe Deposit,” he said with assurance, “and the governor’s hardly likely to leave his keys behind.”

“Then evidently I’ve come to the wrong house,” smiled the gentleman. “This is Mr. Smithson’s?”

“No, it ain’t,” said the ungracious Jope, and shut the door in the caller’s face.

The visitor walked down the steps into the street and joined another man who was standing at a corner.

===Chinese===
乔普摇了摇头。

「我们没有什么保险寄存行。」他言之凿凿地说，「我们老爷也不至于把钥匙落在人家那儿。」

「那看来是我走错门了。」绅士微笑道，「这儿是史密森（Smithson）先生府上？」

「不是。」乔普没好气地说，冲着来客的面孔把门关上了。

来客走下台阶到了街上，与站在街角的另一个男人会合。

===Original===
“They know nothing of Safe Deposits, Manfred,” he said.

“I hardly thought it would be at a Safe Deposit,” said the taller of the two. “In fact, I was pretty certain that he would keep all his papers at the bank. You saw the man Jope, I suppose?”

“Yes,” said Gonsalez dreamily. “An interesting face. The chin weak, but the ears quite normal. The frontal bones slope irregularly backward, and the head, so far as I can see, is distinctly oxycephalic.”

“Poor Jope!” said Manfred without a smile. “And now, Leon, you and I will devote our attention to the weather. There is an anticyclone coming up from the Bay of Biscay, and its beneficent effects are already felt in Eastbourne. If it extends northwards to London in the next three days we shall have good news for Mrs. Storr.”

===Chinese===
「他们对保险寄存行一无所知，曼弗雷德。」他说。

「我本就不大信他会放在保险寄存行。」两人中身材高的那个说，「说实话，我相当有把握，他所有文件都存放在银行。那个叫乔普的，你见到了吧？」

「见到了。」冈萨雷斯悠悠地说，「一张有趣的脸。下巴绵软，耳朵倒十分端正。额骨向后歪斜得没有章法，而且那颗脑袋，就我目力所及，分明是副尖头型。」

「可怜的乔普！」曼弗雷德一丝笑意也无地说，「现在，莱昂，你我该把心思放到天气上头了。有一场反气旋正从比斯开湾（Bay of Biscay）北上，它的种种善果，伊斯特本（Eastbourne）已经先享用上了。三天之内它若能北进到伦敦，我们就有好消息带给斯托尔太太了。」

===Original===
“I suppose,” said Gonsalez, as they were travelling back to their rooms in Jermyn Street, “I suppose there is no possibility of rushing this fellow.”

Manfred shook his head.

“I do not wish to die,” he said, “and die I certainly should, for Noah Stedland is unpleasantly quick to shoot.”

Manfred’s prophecy was fulfilled two days later, when the influence of the anticyclone spread to London and a thin yellow mist descended on the city. It lifted in the afternoon, Manfred saw to his satisfaction, but gave no evidence of dispersing before nightfall.

===Chinese===
「我看，」两人回杰明街（Jermyn Street）住处的路上，冈萨雷斯说，「我看，跟这家伙硬拼是没有指望的。」

曼弗雷德摇头。

「我并不想死。」他说，「而硬拼我必死无疑，因为诺亚·斯特德兰出手快得叫人心里发毛。」

两天后，曼弗雷德的预言应验了：反气旋的势力扩到伦敦，一层薄薄的黄雾降在这座城上。曼弗雷德满意地看到，午后雾气抬升了，却毫无入夜前消散的迹象。

===Original===
Mr. Stedland’s office in Regent Street was small but comfortably furnished. On the glass door beneath his name was inscribed the magic word: “Financier,” and it is true that Stedland was registered as a moneylender and found it a profitable business; for what Stedland the moneylender discovered, Stedland the blackmailer exploited, and it was not an unusual circumstance for Mr. Stedland to lend at heavy interest money which was destined for his own pocket. In this way he could obtain a double grip upon his victim.

At half past two that afternoon his clerk announced a caller.

“Man or woman?”

“A man, sir,” said the clerk, “I think he’s from Molbury’s Bank.”

===Chinese===
斯特德兰先生在摄政街的办公室不大，陈设却颇为舒适。玻璃门上，他的名字底下铭着那个有魔力的字眼：「金融家」。事实上，斯特德兰登记在册的身份是放贷人，且深觉此业厚利可观；因为放贷人斯特德兰探得的消息，正好供讹诈犯斯特德兰去开发利用——斯特德兰先生放出高利贷、而那笔钱注定回流进他自己腰包的情形，也并非罕事。这样一来，他对猎物便有了一双钳子。

那天下午两点半，他的文书来报，有客求见。

「男的女的？」

「男的，先生。」文书说，「我看是从莫尔伯里银行来的。」

===Original===
“Do you know him?” asked Stedland.

“No, sir, but he came yesterday when you were out, and asked if you’d received the Bank’s balance sheet.”

Mr. Stedland took a cigar from a box on the table and lit it.

“Show him in,” he said, anticipating nothing more exciting than a dishonoured cheque from one of his clients.

The man who came in was obviously in a state of agitation. He closed the door behind him and stood nervously fingering his hat.

===Chinese===
「你认得他？」斯特德兰问。

「不认得，先生。不过昨天您外出时他来过，问您收到银行的资产负债表没有。」

斯特德兰先生从桌上的烟盒里取了一支雪茄，点上。

「请他进来。」他说，心里料想无非是哪个客户又开了张空头支票，再没有更热闹的事。

进来的那人显然心绪激荡。他回身关上门，局促地捻弄着帽子站在那里。

===Original===
“Sit down,” said Stedland. “Have a cigar, Mr.﻿—”

“Curtis, sir,” said the other huskily. “Thank you, sir, I don’t smoke.”

“Well, what do you want?” asked Stedland.

“I want a few minutes’ conversation with you, sir, of a private character.” He glanced apprehensively at the glass partition which separated Mr. Stedland’s office from the little den in which his clerks worked.

“Don’t worry,” said Stedland humorously. “I can guarantee that screen is soundproof. What’s your trouble?”

===Chinese===
「坐。」斯特德兰说，「来支雪茄，您是——？」

「柯蒂斯（Curtis），先生。」对方嗓音沙哑地说，「谢谢您，先生，我不抽烟。」

「好吧，你有什么事？」斯特德兰问。

「我想占用您几分钟，先生，说几句不便入耳的私话。」他提心吊胆地瞟了一眼那道玻璃隔断——它把斯特德兰先生的办公室与文书们伏案的小间隔开。

「放心。」斯特德兰打趣道，「那道隔屏我可以担保是隔音的。你有什么难处？」

===Original===
He scented a temporary embarrassment, and a bank clerk temporarily embarrassed might make a very useful tool for future use.

“I hardly know how to begin, Mr. Stedland,” said the man, seating himself on the edge of a chair, his face twitching nervously. “It’s terrible story, a terrible story.”

Stedland had heard about these terrible stories before, and sometimes they meant no more than that the visitor was threatened with bailiffs and was anxious to keep the news from the ears of his employers. Sometimes the confession was more serious﻿—money lost in gambling, and a desperate eleventh-hour attempt to make good a financial deficiency.

“Go on,” he said. “You won’t shock *me*!”

The boast was a little premature, however.

===Chinese===
他嗅出这是一时手头拮据，而一时拮据的银行职员，往后或许能派上大用场。

「我真不知从何说起，斯特德兰先生。」那人在椅子边沿上坐下，脸紧张地抽动着说，「是个可怕的故事，一个可怕的故事。」

这类可怕的故事，斯特德兰早先也听过；有时它们不过意味着来客被法警缠上了，急着不让风声传进东家的耳朵。有时这番坦白更要命些——赌输了钱，赶在最后关头铤而走险，要填上账目的亏空。

「讲下去，」他说，「你可吓不着*我*！」

不过，这句海口夸得略早了些。

===Original===
“It’s not about myself, but about my brother, John Curtis, who’s been cashier for twenty years, sir,” said the man nervously. “I hadn’t the slightest idea that he was in difficulties, but he was gambling on the Stock Exchange, and only today he has told me the news. I am in terrible distress about him, sir. I fear suicide. He is a nervous wreck.”

“What has he done?” asked Stedland impatiently.

“He has robbed the Bank, sir,” said the man in a hushed voice. “It wouldn’t matter if it had happened two years ago, but now, when things have been going so badly and we’ve had to stretch a point to make our balance sheet plausible, I shudder to think what the results will be.”

“Of how much has he robbed the Bank?” asked Stedland quickly.

“A hundred and fifty thousand pounds,” was the staggering reply, and Stedland jumped to his feet.

===Chinese===
「不是为我自己，先生，是为我哥哥约翰·柯蒂斯（John Curtis），他当出纳主任已经二十年了。」那人紧张地说，「我万万没有想到他会陷进困境，可他一直在证券交易所里投机，就在今天，他把这消息告诉了我。我为他愁肠寸断，先生。我怕他寻短见。他已经垮成一副神经的残骸了。」

「他干了什么？」斯特德兰不耐烦地问。

「他挪用了银行的钱，先生。」那人压低嗓音说，「这亏空若是出在两年前倒也罢了，可是眼下世道这样坏，我们的资产负债表已是勉力撑着才看得过去——一想到后果，我就浑身发抖。」

「挪用了多少？」斯特德兰紧接着问。

「十五万镑。」这答复石破天惊，斯特德兰霍地跳起身来。

===Original===
“A hundred and fifty thousand?” he said incredulously.

“Yes, sir. I was wondering whether you could speak for him; you are one of the most highly respected clients of the Bank!”

“Speak for him!” shouted Stedland, and then of a sudden he became cool. His quick brain went over the situation, reviewing every possibility. He looked up at the clock. It was a quarter to three.

“Does anybody in the Bank know?”

“Not yet, sir, but I feel it is my duty to the general manager to tell him the tragic story. After the Bank closes this afternoon I am asking him to see me privately and﻿—”

===Chinese===
「十五万镑？」他难以置信地说。

「是的，先生。我在想，您可否替他出面讲句话；您可是本行最受敬重的客户之一啊！」

「替他讲情！」斯特德兰嚷了起来，随即突然冷静下来。他敏捷的头脑把局面从头过了一遍，掂量了每一种可能。他抬头看了看钟。三点差一刻。

「银行里有人知道吗？」

「还没有，先生。可我总觉得，把这件惨事禀明总经理，是我对他的责任。今天下午银行关门后，我要请他单独见我，然后——」

===Original===
“Are you going back to the Bank now?” asked Stedland.

“Yes, sir,” said the man in surprise.

“Listen to me, my friend.” Stedland’s grey face was set and tense. He took a case from his pocket, opened it and extracted two notes. “Here are two notes for fifty,” he said. “Take those and go home.”

“But I’ve got to go to the Bank, sir. They will wonder﻿—”

“Never mind what they wonder,” said Stedland. “You’ll have a very good explanation when the truth comes out. Will you do this?”

===Chinese===
「你现在就回银行去？」斯特德兰问。

「是啊，先生。」那人愕然道。

「听我说，我的朋友。」斯特德兰那张灰脸绷得紧紧的。他从衣袋里取出一只票夹，打开，抽出两张钞票。「这是两张五十镑的，」他说，「拿上它们，回家去。」

「可我必须回银行去，先生。他们会奇怪——」

「他们奇怪不奇怪，不必你操心。」斯特德兰说，「等真相大白，你自有一套极好的说辞。这个忙你帮不帮？」

===Original===
The man took up the money reluctantly.

“I don’t quite know what you﻿—”

“Never mind what I want to do,” snapped Stedland. “That is to keep your mouth shut and go home. Do you understand plain English?”

“Yes, sir,” said the shaking Curtis.

===Chinese===
那人勉强把钱拿了起来。

「我不太明白，您这是要——」

「我要做什么，也不必你操心。」斯特德兰厉声道，「你只管闭上嘴，回家去。这句再明白不过的英国话，你可听得懂？」

「是，先生。」柯蒂斯抖着说。

===Original===
Five minutes later Mr. Stedland passed through the glass doors of Molbury’s Bank and walked straight to the counter. An air of calm pervaded the establishment and the cashier, who knew Stedland, came forward with a smile.

> “Unconscious of their awful doom,
 

 The little victims play.”

quoted Stedland to himself. It was a favourite quotation of his, and he had used it on many appropriate occasions.

===Chinese===
五分钟后，斯特德兰先生穿过莫尔伯里银行的玻璃门，径直走到柜台前。店堂里一派安恬，认得斯特德兰的出纳员含笑迎上前来。

> 「浑然不觉可怖的死劫将临，
 

 小小的受难者犹自嬉戏。」

斯特德兰自顾自念道。这是他心爱的一句引文，许多应景的场合都用过。

===Original===
He passed, a slip of paper across the counter, and the cashier looked at it and raised his eyebrows.

“Why, this is almost your balance, Mr. Stedland,” he said.

Stedland nodded.

“Yes, I am going abroad in a hurry,” he said. “I shall not be back for two years, but I am leaving just enough to keep the account running.”

It was a boast of Molbury’s that they never argued on such occasions as these.

===Chinese===
他隔着柜台递过去一张纸条，出纳员看了看，扬起了眉毛。

「哎呀，这差不多是您的全部存款了，斯特德兰先生。」他说。

斯特德兰点头。

「不错，我要赶着出国。」他说，「两年之内不会回来，不过我会留下一笔钱，够这账户转下去的。」

莫尔伯里银行素来引以为夸口的一点是：逢到这等场面，他们从不多问。

===Original===
“Then you will want your box?” said the cashier politely.

“If you please,” said Mr. Noah Stedland. If the Bank passed into the hands of the Receiver, he had no wish for prying strangers to be unlocking and examining the contents of the tin box he had deposited with the Bank, and to the contents of which he made additions from time to time.

Ten minutes later, with close on a hundred thousand pounds in his pockets, a tin box in one hand, the other resting on his hip pocket﻿—for he took no chances﻿—Mr. Stedland went out again on the street and into the waiting taxicab. The fog was cleared, and the sun was shining at Clapham when he arrived.

He went straight up to his study, fastened the door and unlocked the little safe. Into this he pushed the small box and two thick bundles of notes, locking the safe door behind him. Then he rang for the faithful Jope, unfastening the door to admit him.

“Have we another camp bed in the house?” he asked.

===Chinese===
「那么，您要用一下您的保险箱了？」出纳员客气地说。

「有劳。」诺亚·斯特德兰先生说。倘若银行落到破产接管人手里，他可不愿让好事的陌生人为他存在银行的那只铁箱开锁验看——那箱子里的东西，他还不时往里添呢。

十分钟后，斯特德兰先生重又走上街头，钻进候着的出租车：口袋里装着将近十万镑，一手提着铁箱，另一只手搭在后裤袋上——他从不冒险。到达克拉珀姆时，雾已散尽，阳光正好。

他径直上楼进了书房，闩好门，开了那口小保险柜。他把小铁箱和两厚沓钞票塞了进去，回手锁上柜门。然后他摇铃唤忠仆乔普，开了门放他进来。

「家里还有一张行军床吗？」他问。

===Original===
“Yes, sir,” said Jope.

“Well, bring it up here. I am going to sleep in my study tonight.”

“Anything wrong, sir?”

“Don’t ask jackass questions. Do as you’re told!”

Tomorrow, he thought, he would seek out a safer repository for his treasures. He spent that evening in his study and lay down to rest, but not to sleep, with a revolver on a chair by the side of his camp bed. Mr. Stedland was a cautious man. Despite his intention to dispense with sleep for one night, he was dozing when a sound in the street outside roused him.

===Chinese===
「有，先生。」乔普说。

「好，搬上来。今晚我睡书房。」

「出了什么事吗，先生？」

「别问蠢驴问题。叫你干什么就干什么！」

明天，他想，他要给这些宝贝寻个更稳妥的藏处。那一晚他在书房里度过，躺下歇息，却不是睡觉，行军床旁的椅子上搁着一把左轮枪。斯特德兰先生是个谨慎的人。尽管打定主意这一夜不睡，街上一个声响把他惊醒时，他还是在打盹。

===Original===
It was a familiar sound﻿—the clang of fire bells﻿—and apparently fire engines were in the street, for he heard the whine of motors and the sound of voices. He sniffed; there was a strong smell of burning, and looking up he saw a flicker of light reflected on the ceiling. He sprang out of bed to discover the cause. It was immediately discernible, for the fuse factory was burning merrily, and he caught a glimpse of firemen at work and a momentary vision of a hose in action. Mr. Stedland permitted himself to smile. That fire would be worth money to him, and there was no danger to himself.

And then he heard a sound in the hall below; a deep voice boomed an order, and he caught the chatter of Jope, and unlocked the door. The lights were burning in the hall and on the stairway. Looking over the banisters he saw the shivering Jope, with an overcoat over his pyjamas, expostulating with a helmeted fireman.

“I can’t help it,” the latter was saying, “I’ve got to get a hose through one of these houses, and it might as well be yours.”

Mr. Stedland had no desire to have a hose through his house, and thought he knew an argument which might pass the inconvenience on to his neighbour.

===Chinese===
那是个熟悉的声响——火警钟的铿锵——而且消防车显然已经开到了街上，因为他听见马达的哀鸣和嘈杂的人声。他吸了口气；空气里一股浓烈的焦煳味，抬头一看，天花板上闪动着一片反光。他翻身下床去查究竟。缘由一目了然：那座引信工厂烧得正欢，他瞥见消防队员在忙着救火，一瞥之间还望见一条水龙带正喷着水。斯特德兰先生允许自己笑了笑。这场火对他来说就是钱，而他自己毫无危险。

接着他听见楼下门厅里有动静；一个低沉的嗓门吼着下命令，还夹着乔普的絮叨，他便开了门。门厅和楼梯上灯都亮着。他凭栏下望，只见乔普睡衣外面罩着大衣，哆哆嗦嗦，正跟一个戴头盔的消防队员讲道理。

「我也没法子，」那消防队员正说着，「我总得把水龙带从这几家宅子里穿一家过去，反正穿哪家不是穿，就借贵府吧。」

斯特德兰先生可不想让水龙带穿过自家的宅子，而且他自信想得出一个说法，把这桩不便转嫁给邻居。

===Original===
“Just come up here a moment,” he said. “I want to speak to one of those firemen.”

The fireman came clumping up the stairs in his heavy boots, a fine figure of a man in his glittering brass.

“Sorry,” he said, “but I must get the hose﻿—”

“Wait a moment, my friend,” said Mr. Stedland with a smile. “I think you will understand me after a while. There are plenty of houses in this road, and a tenner goes a long way, eh? Come in.”

He walked back into his room and the fireman followed and stood watching as he unlocked the safe. Then:

“I didn’t think it would be so easy,” he said.

===Chinese===
「上来一下。」他说，「我要跟那位消防员说句话。」

那消防队员穿着沉重的靴子，咚咚咚地上了楼，一身锃亮的铜扣，好一条威风的汉子。

「抱歉，」他说，「可我必须把水龙带——」

「稍等片刻，我的朋友。」斯特德兰先生含笑说，「我想过一会儿你就明白我了。这条街上的宅子多得很，一张十镑钞票的路子宽得很，嗯？请进。」

他走回房里，消防队员跟了进来，站着看他开保险柜。然后：

「没想到会这么容易。」他说。

===Original===
Stedland swung round.

“Put up your hands,” said the fireman, “and don’t make trouble, or you’re going out, Noah. I’d just as soon kill you as talk to you.”

Then Noah Stedland saw that beneath the shade of the helmet the man’s face was covered with a black mask.

“Who﻿—who are you?” he asked hoarsely.

“I’m one of the Four Just Men﻿—greatly reviled and prematurely mourned. Death is my favourite panacea for all ills﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
斯特德兰猛地转身。

「举起手来。」消防队员说，「别找麻烦，不然你就完了，诺亚。杀你跟同你讲话，在我是一样痛快的事。」

这时诺亚·斯特德兰才看清，头盔的阴影底下，那张脸罩着一副黑色面罩。

「你——你是谁？」他哑着嗓子问。

「我是四义士之一——备遭诟骂，又被人过早地悼念。死亡是我最钟爱的万灵药，包治百病……」

===Original===
---

At nine o’clock in the morning Mr. Noah Stedland still sat biting his nails, a cold uneaten breakfast spread on a table before him.

To him came Mr. Jope wailing tidings of disaster, interrupted by Chief Inspector Holloway and a hefty subordinate who followed the servant into the room.

===Chinese===
---
早晨九点钟，诺亚·斯特德兰先生还坐在那里啃指甲，面前桌上摊着一顿凉了而未动的早饭。

乔普先生哭丧着脸前来报凶信，话音随即被人打断——总督察霍洛韦（Chief Inspector Holloway）带着一名魁梧的属下，跟着这仆人走进了房间。

===Original===
“Coming for a little walk with me, Stedland?” asked the cheery inspector, and Stedland rose heavily.

“What’s the charge?” he asked heavily.

“Blackmail,” replied the officer. “We’ve got evidence enough to hang you﻿—delivered by special messenger. You fixed that case against Storr too﻿—naughty, naughty!”

As Mr. Stedland put on his coat the inspector asked:

===Chinese===
「跟我出去散个小步吧，斯特德兰？」督察乐呵呵地问。斯特德兰沉重地站起身。

「什么罪名？」他沉声问。

「讹诈。」警官答道，「我们有足够的证据吊死你——专差递送的。斯托尔那桩案子，也是你做下的手脚——不乖啊，不乖！」

斯特德兰先生穿外衣的时候，督察问道：

===Original===
“Who gave you away?”

Mr. Stedland made no reply. Manfred’s last words before he vanished into the foggy street had been emphatic.

“If we wanted to kill you, the man called Curtis would have killed you this afternoon when we played on your cunning; we could have killed you as easily as we set fire to the factory. And if you talk to the police of the Four Just Men, we will kill you, even though you be in Pentonville with a regiment of soldiers round you.”

And somehow Mr. Stedland knew that his enemy spoke the truth. So he said nothing, neither there nor in the dock at the Old Bailey, and went to penal servitude without speaking.

===Chinese===
「是谁出卖了你？」

斯特德兰先生没有作答。曼弗雷德消失在雾蒙蒙的街上之前，留下的最后几句话毫不含糊。

「我们若要杀你，今天下午我们钓你上钩时，那个叫柯蒂斯的人早就杀了你；我们要弄死你，就像放火烧那座工厂一样容易。你若胆敢向警察提起四义士，我们就杀你——哪怕你蹲在彭顿维尔监狱（Pentonville）里，四周团团围着一团士兵。」

也不知怎么，斯特德兰先生知道他的仇人说的是真话。于是他一言不发——在那里不发，到了老贝利（the Old Bailey）的被告席上也不发——一声不响地去服了他的苦役。

## The Man with the Canine Teeth / 长着犬齿的人

===Original===

“Murder, my dear Manfred is the most accidental of crimes,” said Leon Gonsalez, removing his big shell-rimmed glasses and looking across the breakfast-table with that whimsical earnestness which was ever a delight to the handsome genius who directed the operations of the Four Just Men.

“Poiccart used to say that murder was a tangible expression of hysteria,” he smiled, “but why this grisly breakfast-table topic?”

Gonsalez put on his glasses again and returned, apparently, to his study of the morning newspaper. He did not wilfully ignore the question, but his mind, as George Manfred knew, was so completely occupied by his reflections that he neither heard the query nor, for the matter of that, was he reading the newspaper. Presently he spoke again.

“Eighty percent of the men who are charged with murder are making their appearance in a criminal court for the first time,” he said, “therefore, murderers as a class are not criminals﻿—I speak, of course, for the Anglo-Saxon murderer. Latin and Teutonic criminal classes supply sixty percent of the murderers in France, Italy and the Germanic States. They are fascinating people, George, fascinating!”

His face lighted up with enthusiasm, and George Manfred surveyed him with amusement.

“I have never been able to take so detached a view of those gentlemen,” he said. “To me they are completely horrible﻿—for is not murder the apotheosis of injustice?” he asked.

===Chinese===

“谋杀嘛，我亲爱的曼弗雷德（Manfred），是最偶然的一种犯罪。”莱昂·冈萨雷斯（Leon Gonsalez）摘下他那副大号角质框眼镜，隔着早餐桌望过去，脸上带着那种亦庄亦谐的认真神情——主持四义士（The Four Just Men）种种行动的那位俊雅天才，向来把这份神情当作一桩赏心乐事。

“波伊卡特（Poiccart）从前常说，谋杀不过是歇斯底里的一种有形表现。”他微笑道，“可为什么挑早餐桌上谈这么个阴森题目？”

冈萨雷斯重新戴上眼镜，表面上又回去研读晨报了。他并非有意怠慢这个问题，而是——如乔治·曼弗雷德所深知——他的心思整个叫自己的沉思占满了，既没听见这句问话，说句实在话，也压根没有在读报。不一会儿他又开了口。

“被控谋杀的人当中，百分之八十都是头一回踏进刑事法庭，”他说，“因此，杀人犯作为一个群体并不属于惯犯——当然，我说的是盎格鲁-撒克逊的杀人犯。在法国、意大利和德意志诸邦，拉丁及条顿罪犯阶层供出了那里百分之六十的杀人犯。这些人叫人着迷，乔治，着迷得很！”

他脸上焕发出热情的光彩，乔治·曼弗雷德饶有兴味地打量着他。

“我对这班先生向来没法这么超然，”他说，“在我看来，他们可怖至极——须知谋杀岂不正是不义之举的登峰造极？”他问道。


===Original===

“I suppose so,” said Gonsalez vacantly.

“What started this line of thought?” asked Manfred, rolling his serviette.

“I met a true murderer type last night,” answered the other calmly. “He asked me for a match and smiled when I gave it to him. A perfect set of teeth, my dear George, perfect﻿—except﻿—”

“Except?”

“The canine teeth were unusually large and long, the eyes deep set and amazingly level, the face anamorphic﻿—which latter fact is not necessarily criminal.”

“Sounds rather an ogre to me,” said Manfred.

===Chinese===

“大概是这样吧。”冈萨雷斯心不在焉地说。

“这番高论是打哪儿来的？”曼弗雷德一边卷餐巾一边问。

“昨晚我遇见了一个真正的杀人犯类型，”对方平静地答道，“他向我借火，我递过去时他笑了笑。一口好牙，我亲爱的乔治，完美无缺——只是——”

“只是？”

“犬齿格外粗长，双眼深陷而目光平稳得出奇，脸型有些变形——这后一点倒未必就是罪犯的标记。”

“听着倒像个食人妖。”曼弗雷德说。


===Original===

“On the contrary,” Gonsalez hastened to correct the impression, “he was quite good-looking. None but a student would have noticed the irregularity of the face. Oh no, he was most presentable.”

He explained the circumstances of the meeting. He had been to a concert the night before﻿—not that he loved music, but because he wished to study the effect of music upon certain types of people. He had returned with hieroglyphics scribbled all over his programme, and had sat up half the night elaborating his notes.

“He is the son of Professor Tableman. He is not on good terms with his father, who apparently disapproves of his choice of fiancée, and he loathes his cousin,” added Gonsalez simply.

Manfred laughed aloud.

“You amusing person! And did he tell you all this of his own free will, or did you hypnotise him and extract the information? You haven’t asked me what I did last night.”

Gonsalez was lighting a cigarette slowly and thoughtfully.

===Chinese===

“恰恰相反，”冈萨雷斯连忙纠正这种印象，“他生得相当体面。只有钻研过的人才看得出那张脸的不规整。哦不，他仪表堂皇得很。”

他讲了相遇的原委。前一晚他去听了一场音乐会——倒不是他热爱音乐，而是想研究音乐对某几类人产生的影响。他回来时节目单上已经涂满了天书般的符号，又熬了半个晚上细加整理笔记。

“他是泰布尔曼教授（Professor Tableman）的儿子。他同父亲不和——父亲显然不赞成他挑的未婚妻——他还厌恶他的堂兄。”冈萨雷斯淡淡地补了一句。

曼弗雷德放声大笑。

“你这个有趣的人！这些都是他自愿告诉你的，还是你催眠了他，把话掏出来的？你倒没问问我昨晚干了些什么。”

冈萨雷斯正慢条斯理、若有所思地点着一支烟。


===Original===

“He is nearly two metres﻿—to be exact, six feet two inches﻿—in height, powerfully built, with shoulders like that!” He held the cigarette in one hand and the burning match in the other to indicate the breadth of the young man. “He has big, strong hands and plays football for the United Hospitals. I beg your pardon, Manfred; where *were* you last night?”

“At Scotland Yard,” said Manfred; but if he expected to produce a sensation he was to be disappointed. Probably knowing his Leon, he anticipated no such result.

“An interesting building,” said Gonsalez. “The architect should have turned the western façade southward﻿—though its furtive entrances are in keeping with its character. You had no difficulty in making friends?”

“None. My work in connection with the Spanish Criminal Code and my monograph on Dactyology secured me admission to the chief.”

Manfred was known in London as “Señor Fuentes,” an eminent writer on criminology, and in their roles of Spanish scientists both men bore the most compelling of credentials from the Spanish Minister of Justice. Manfred had made his home in Spain for many years. Gonsalez was a native of that country, and the third of the famous four﻿—there had not been a fourth for twenty years﻿—Poiccart, the stout and gentle, seldom left his big garden in Cordova.

To him Leon Gonsalez referred when he spoke.

===Chinese===

“他差不多有两米——确切说，六英尺二英寸高，体格健壮，肩膀有这么宽！”他一手夹着烟、一手举着燃着的火柴，比划那年轻人的肩宽。“他双手粗大有力，在联合医院队踢足球。恕我失礼，曼弗雷德；你昨晚到底去哪儿了？”

“去苏格兰场了。”曼弗雷德说。不过他若指望引起轰动，那可要失望了。或许正因为他了解自己的莱昂，本来就料不到会有这种效果。

“一栋有意思的建筑，”冈萨雷斯说，“建筑师本该把西立面转到朝南——不过它那些躲躲闪闪的入口，倒同它的品性很相称。你交朋友没费什么劲吧？”

“毫不费劲。我在西班牙刑法典方面的研究，加上那部指纹学专著，让我得以获见他们的首脑。”

曼弗雷德在伦敦以“富恩特斯先生”（Señor Fuentes）之名闻名，是一位卓有声誉的犯罪学著作家；两人以西班牙科学家的身份行走，都持有西班牙司法部长签发的最具效力的证书。曼弗雷德在西班牙侨居多年，冈萨雷斯是该国本土人氏，而名震遐迩的四人中的第三位——二十年来已没有第四位了——波伊卡特，那位心宽体胖、性情温厚的，则很少离开他在科尔多瓦（Cordova）的大花园。

莱昂·冈萨雷斯开口时，指的正是他。


===Original===

“You must write and tell our dear friend Poiccart,” he said. “He will be interested. I had a letter from him this morning. Two new litters of little pigs have come to bless his establishment, and his orange trees are in blossom.”

He chuckled to himself, and then suddenly became serious.

“They took you to their bosom, these policemen?”

Manfred nodded.

“They were very kind and charming. We are lunching with one of the Assistant Commissioners, Mr. Reginald Fare, tomorrow. British police methods have improved tremendously since we were in London before, Leon. The fingerprint department is a model of efficiency, and their new men are remarkably clever.”

===Chinese===

“你务必写信告诉咱们亲爱的朋友波伊卡特，”他说，“他会感兴趣的。今天早晨我刚收到他的信。又有两窝小猪降生，来给他的家业添福了，橘树也开了花。”

他自己咯咯笑了两声，随即忽然正色。

“这些警察把你当自己人接纳了？”

曼弗雷德点了点头。

“他们非常和善，极有风度。明天我们要同一位助理警务总监，雷金纳德·费尔先生（Mr. Reginald Fare）共进午餐。自从咱们上次来伦敦，英国警方的办案手段进步惊人，莱昂。指纹部门堪称效率的典范，新进的这班人也聪明得很。”


===Original===

“They will hang us yet,” said the cheerful Leon.

“I think not!” replied his companion.

The lunch at the Ritz-Carlton was, for Gonsalez especially, a most pleasant function. Mr. Fare, the middle-aged Commissioner, was, in addition to being a charming gentleman, a very able scientist. The views and observations of Marro, Lombroso, Fere, Mantegazza and Ellis flew from one side of the table to the other.

“To the habitual criminal the world is an immense prison, alternating with an immense jag,” said Fare. “That isn’t my description but one a hundred years old. The habitual criminal is an easy man to deal with. It is when you come to the non-criminal classes, the murderers, the accidental embezzlers﻿—”

===Chinese===

“他们迟早要把咱们绞死。”乐天的莱昂说。

“我看不会！”同伴答道。

丽思卡尔顿酒店（Ritz-Carlton）的那顿午餐，对冈萨雷斯尤其是一桩美事。费尔先生，这位人到中年的总监，不但是位极有风度的绅士，还是一位造诣颇深的科学家。马罗、隆布罗索、费雷、曼特加扎和埃利斯诸家的见解与观察，在餐桌这一侧与那一侧之间飞来飞去。

“对惯犯来说，世界是一座硕大无朋的监狱，同一场硕大无朋的纵饮轮换交替，”费尔说，“这不是我的说法，是一百年前的旧论。惯犯好对付。难就难在那些本不属于罪犯阶层的人——杀人犯，偶然失足的挪用公款者——”


===Original===

“Exactly!” said Gonsalez. “Now my contention is﻿—”

He was not to express his view, for a footman had brought an envelope to the Commissioner, and he interrupted Gonsalez with an apology to open and read its contents.

“H’m!” he said. “That is a curious coincidence.﻿ ﻿…”

He looked at Manfred thoughtfully.

“You were saying the other night that you would like to watch Scotland Yard at work close at hand, and I promised you that I would give you the first opportunity which presented﻿—your chance has come!”

===Chinese===

“正是！”冈萨雷斯说，“而我的论点是——”

他的论点没能说出口：一名侍役给总监送来一只信封，总监道了声歉，便拆开来读。

“唔，”他说，“这可是个蹊跷的巧合。……”

他若有所思地望着曼弗雷德。

“前几天晚上你说过，想就近看看苏格兰场办案，我答应过你，头一个碰上的机会就给你留着——你的机会来了！”


===Original===

He had beckoned the waiter and paid his bill before he spoke again.

“I shall not disdain to draw upon your ripe experience,” he said, “for it is possible we may need all the assistance we can get in this case.”

“What is it?” asked Manfred, as the Commissioner’s car threaded the traffic at Hyde Park Corner.

“A man has been found dead in extraordinary circumstances,” said the Commissioner. “He holds rather a prominent position in the scientific world﻿—a Professor Tableman﻿—you probably know the name.”

===Chinese===

他招手唤来侍者，付清了账，才又开口。

“我也不吝借重你的丰富经验，”他说，“因为这案子我们或许需要一切到手的助力。”

“什么案子？”总监的车在海德公园角的车流间穿行时，曼弗雷德问道。

“有个人死了，情形极不寻常，”总监说，“他在科学界地位相当显赫——一位泰布尔曼教授——你多半听说过这个名字。”


===Original===

“Tableman?” said Gonsalez, his eyes opening wide. “Well, that is extraordinary! You were talking of coincidences, Mr. Fare. Now I will tell you of another.”

He related his meeting with the son of the Professor on the previous night.

“Personally,” Gonsalez went on, “I look upon all coincidences as part of normal intercourse. It is a coincidence that, if you receive a bill requiring payment, you receive two or more during the day, and that if you receive a cheque by the first post, be sure you will receive a cheque by your second or third post. Some day I shall devote my mind to the investigation of that phenomenon.”

“Professor Tableman lives in Chelsea. Some years ago he purchased his house from an artist, and had the roomy studio converted into a laboratory. He was a lecturer in physics and chemistry at the Bloomsbury University,” explained Fare, though he need not have done so, for Manfred recalled the name; “and he was also a man of considerable means.”

“I knew the Professor and dined with him about a month ago,” said Fare. “He had had some trouble with his son. Tableman was an arbitrary, unyielding old man, one of those types of Christians who worship the historical figures of the Old Testament but never seem to get to the second book.”

===Chinese===

“泰布尔曼？”冈萨雷斯说着，眼睛睁大了，“嘿，这可真是稀奇！费尔先生，您方才正说巧合来着。我再给您讲一个。”

他讲了前一夜遇见教授之子的事。

“就我个人而言，”冈萨雷斯接着说，“我把一切巧合都看作寻常人情往来的一部分。收到要付的账单便是如此：这一天你若收到一张催账单，必定还会再收到两张三张；头一班邮差送来了一张支票，那第二班、第三班邮差准还给你送支票来。哪天我要专门研究研究这一现象。”

“泰布尔曼教授住在切尔西。若干年前他从一个画家手里买下这栋房子，把宽敞的画室改成了实验室。他曾任布卢姆斯伯里大学（Bloomsbury University）的物理化学讲师，”费尔解说道——其实大可不必，因为曼弗雷德记得这个名字——“而且他家道殷实。”

“我认识这位教授，大约一个月前还同他吃过饭，”费尔说，“他跟儿子闹过些不痛快。泰布尔曼是个专断、固执的老头儿，属于那一类基督徒：崇奉《旧约》里的历史人物，却似乎永远读不到第二部书。”


===Original===

They arrived at the house, a handsome modern structure in one of the streets abutting upon King’s Road, and apparently the news of the tragedy had not leaked out, for the usual crowd of morbid loungers had not gathered. A detective was waiting for them, and conducted the Commissioner along a covered passageway running by the side of the house, and up a flight of steps directly into the studio. There was nothing unusual about the room save that it was very light, for one of the walls was a huge window and the sloping roof was also of glass. Broad benches ran the length of two walls, and a big table occupied the centre of the room, all these being covered with scientific apparatus, whilst two long shelves above the benches were filled with bottles and jars, apparently containing chemicals.

A sad-faced, good-looking young man rose from a chair as they entered.

“I am John Munsey,” he said, “the Professor’s nephew. You remember me, Mr. Fare? I used to assist my uncle in his experiments.”

Fare nodded. His eyes were occupied with the figure that lay upon the ground, between table and bench.

“I have not moved the Professor,” said the young man in a low voice. “The detectives who came moved him slightly to assist the doctor in making his examination, but he has been left practically where he fell.”

The body was that of an old man, tall and spare, and on the grey face was an unmistakable look of agony and terror.

===Chinese===

他们来到那栋宅子——国王路（King's Road）临街巷弄里一座漂亮的新式建筑——惨案的消息显然尚未走漏，因为平日那种病态的闲看人群还不曾围拢。一名侦探等着他们，领着总监沿房子一侧的有顶过道走去，上一段台阶，便径直进了工作室。这屋子没什么特别，只是非常亮堂：一面墙整堵是大窗，斜屋顶也镶着玻璃。两张宽工作台沿两面墙一字排开，屋子正中立着一张大台子，上面摆满了科学仪器；工作台上方的两条长架上塞满瓶瓶罐罐，看样子装的都是化学品。

一个眉目清秀、满面悲戚的青年从椅子上起身。

“我是约翰·芒西（John Munsey），”他说，“教授的侄子。您还记得我吧，费尔先生？我从前常帮叔叔做实验。”

费尔点了点头。他的目光正落在地上那具身影上，它横在台子与工作台之间。

“我没有移动教授，”青年低声说，“先到的侦探们把他稍稍挪动了一下，好让医生验看，但他基本还保持倒下时的原样。”

死者是个老人，又高又瘦，灰白的脸上凝着一种痛苦与惊怖的神情，一望便知。


===Original===

“It looks like a case of strangling,” said Fare. “Has any rope or cord been found?”

“No, sir,” replied the young man. “That was the view which the detectives reached, and we made a very thorough search of the laboratory.”

Gonsalez was kneeling by the body, looking with dispassionate interest at the lean neck. About the throat was a band of blue about four inches deep, and he thought at first that it was a material bandage of some diaphanous stuff, but on close inspection he saw that it was merely the discoloration of the skin. Then his keen eye rose to the table, near where the Professor fell.

“What is that?” he asked. He pointed to a small green bottle by the side of which was an empty glass.

“It is a bottle of crème de menthe,” said the youth; “my uncle took a glass usually before retiring.”

“May I?” asked Leon, and Fare nodded.

===Chinese===

“看样子是一桩勒杀案，”费尔说，“找到什么绳索或带子了吗？”

“没有，先生，”青年答道，“先前的侦探也是这个判断，我们把实验室彻底搜了一遍。”

冈萨雷斯跪在尸体旁，冷静而专注地端详那干瘦的脖颈。喉部有一圈约四英寸宽的青紫勒痕，起初他还当是某种轻纱料子缠的绷带，细看之下才发现不过是皮肤变了色。随后他敏锐的目光移向台子——教授倒下的地方就在近旁。

“那是什么？”他问，指着一只小绿瓶，旁边有一只空玻璃杯。

“是一瓶薄荷甜酒（crème de menthe），”青年说，“叔叔睡前照例要喝上一杯。”

“可以吗？”莱昂问；费尔点了点头。


===Original===

Gonsalez picked up the glass and smelt it, then held it to the light.

“This glass was not used for liqueur last night, so he was killed before he drank,” the Commissioner said. “I’d like to hear the whole story from you, Mr. Munsey. You sleep on the premises, I presume?”

After giving a few instructions to the detectives, the Commissioner followed the young man into a room which was evidently the late Professor’s library.

“I have been my uncle’s assistant and secretary for three years,” he said, “and we have always been on the most affectionate terms. It was my uncle’s practice to spend the morning in his library, the whole of the afternoon either in his laboratory or at his office at the University, and he invariably spent the hours between dinner and bedtime working at his experiments.”

“Did he dine at home?” asked Fare.

“Invariably,” replied Mr. Munsey, “unless he had an evening lecture or there was a meeting of one of the societies with which he was connected, and in that case he dined at the Royal Society’s Club in St. James’s Street.

===Chinese===

冈萨雷斯拿起杯子闻了闻，又举到光前细看。

“昨晚这只杯子没有盛过利口酒，可见他还没来得及喝就遇害了，”总监说，“我想请你把事情从头到尾讲一遍，芒西先生。你就住在这宅子里，我猜？”

向侦探们交代了几句之后，总监随青年走进一间屋子，那显然是已故教授的书房。

“三年来我一直给叔叔当助手兼秘书，我们相处得始终极为亲厚。叔叔的习惯是：上午整个在书房，下午或在实验室，或去大学的办公室，而晚饭后到就寝前的这几个钟头，他必定在做实验。”

“他在家用晚餐吗？”费尔问。

“向来如此，”芒西先生答，“除非他晚上有课，或者他参加的哪个学会正好开会，那种日子他就在圣詹姆斯街（St. James's Street）的皇家学会俱乐部用晚餐。


===Original===

“My uncle, as you probably know, Mr. Fare, has had a serious disagreement with his son, Stephen Tableman, and my cousin and very good friend. I have done my best to reconcile them, and when, twelve months ago, my uncle sent for me in this very room and told me that he had altered his will and left the whole of his property to me and had cut his son entirely from his inheritance, I was greatly distressed. I went immediately to Stephen and begged him to lose no time in reconciling himself with the old man. Stephen just laughed and said he didn’t care about the Professor’s money, and that, sooner than give up Miss Faber﻿—it was about his engagement that the quarrel occurred﻿—he would cheerfully live on the small sum of money which his mother left him. I came back and saw the Professor and begged him to restore Stephen to his will. I admit,” he half smiled, “that I expected and would appreciate a small legacy. I am following the same scientific course as the Professor followed in his early days, and I have ambitions to carry on his work. But the Professor would have none of my suggestion. He raved and stormed at me, and I thought it would be discreet to drop the subject, which I did. Nevertheless, I lost no opportunity of putting in a word for Stephen, and last week, when the Professor was in an unusually amiable frame of mind, I raised the whole question again and he agreed to see Stephen. They met in the laboratory; I was not present, but I believe that there was a terrible row. When I came in, Stephen had gone, and Mr. Tableman was livid with rage. Apparently, he had again insisted upon Stephen giving up his fiancée, and Stephen had refused point-blank.”

“How did Stephen arrive at the laboratory?” asked Gonsalez. “May I ask that question, Mr. Fare?”

The Commissioner nodded.

“He entered by the side passage. Very few people who come to the house on purely scientific business enter the house.”

“Then access to the laboratory is possible at all hours?”

“Until the very last thing at night, when the gate is locked,” said the young man. “You see, Uncle used to take a little constitutional before going to bed, and he preferred using that entrance.”

===Chinese===

“您多半也有所耳闻，费尔先生，我叔叔和他儿子斯蒂芬·泰布尔曼（Stephen Tableman）——也就是我堂弟、我的挚友——闹得很僵。我一直尽力劝和他们。十二个月前，就在这间屋里，叔叔把我叫去，告诉我他改了遗嘱，全部财产都留给我，把儿子彻底排除在继承之外，我听了心急如焚。我马上去找斯蒂芬，劝他抓紧时机同老头子和好。斯蒂芬只是一笑，说教授的钱他无所谓，还说只要能不放弃费伯小姐（Miss Faber）——他们吵架正是为了订婚的事——他情愿靠母亲留下的一小笔钱过日子。我回来见教授，求他恢复斯蒂芬的继承权。我承认，”他微微一笑，“我当时是指望、也乐意见到一小笔遗赠的。我走的是教授早年走的同一条科学路子，也有志于继承他的事业。可教授对我的提议根本不买账，冲我又吼又闹，我想还是知趣闭嘴为妙，也就照办了。不过，只要有机会，我总替斯蒂芬进言。上星期，教授心情出奇地和悦，我把整件事重新提了出来，他这才同意见斯蒂芬。他们是在实验室见的面；我没在场，但我知道那次吵得天翻地覆。我进去时斯蒂芬已经走了，泰布尔曼先生气得脸色铁青。显然，他又一次要斯蒂芬放弃未婚妻，斯蒂芬一口回绝了。”

“斯蒂芬是怎么进实验室的？”冈萨雷斯问，“我能提这个问题吗，费尔先生？”

总监点了点头。

“他从侧廊进来的。因纯科学事务来访的人，几乎从不进正宅的门。”

“那就是说，实验室随时都进得去？”

“到夜里最后一刻才锁栅门，”青年说，“您想，叔叔睡前总要散一小会儿步，他偏爱走那个入口。”

===Original===

“Was the gate locked last night?”

John Munsey shook his head.

“No,” he said quietly. “That was one of the first things I investigated. The gate was unfastened and ajar. It is not so much of a gate as an iron grille, as you probably observed.”

“Go on,” nodded Mr. Fare.

“Well, the Professor gradually cooled down, and for two or three days he was very thoughtful, and I thought a little sad. On Monday﻿—what is today? Thursday?﻿—yes, it was on Monday, he said to me: ‘John, let’s have a little talk about Steve. Do you think I have treated him very badly?’ ‘I think you were rather unreasonable, Uncle,’ I said. ‘Perhaps I was,’ he replied. ‘She must be a very fine girl for Stephen to risk poverty for her sake.’ That was the opportunity I had been praying for, and I think I urged Stephen’s case with an eloquence which he would have commended. The upshot of it was that the old man weakened and sent a wire to Stephen, asking him to see him last night. It must have been a struggle for the Professor to have got over his objection to Miss Faber; he was a fanatic on the question of heredity﻿—”

===Chinese===

“昨晚栅门锁了吗？”

约翰·芒西摇了摇头。

“没有，”他平静地说，“这是我最先查证的事项之一。栅门没闩上，虚掩着。那与其说是门，不如说是一扇铁栅，您二位进来时想必看见了。”

“继续讲。”费尔先生点头道。

“唔，教授慢慢消了气，有两三天心事重重，我看还有点伤感。星期一——今天是星期四，对吧？——对，是星期一，他对我说：‘约翰，咱们来聊聊史蒂夫吧。你说，我是不是待他太狠了？’‘我看您有点不讲情理，叔叔。’我说。‘也许是，’他答道，‘能让斯蒂芬甘愿为她受穷，这姑娘必定是个好姑娘。’这正是我求之不得的机会，我想我替斯蒂芬说的那番话，说得连教授本人也会称许。结局是老头子软了心，给斯蒂芬拍了电报，约他昨晚来见。教授能压下对费伯小姐的成见，于他想必是一场苦战——他在遗传这件事上是个狂热分子——”


===Original===

“Heredity?” interrupted Manfred quickly. “What was wrong with Miss Faber?”

“I don’t know,” shrugged the other, “but the Professor had heard rumours that her father had died in an inebriates’ home. I believe those rumours were baseless.”

“What happened last night?” asked Fare.

“I understand that Stephen came,” said Munsey. “I kept carefully out of the way; in fact, I spent my time in my room, writing off some arrears of correspondence. I came downstairs about half past eleven, but the Professor had not returned. Looking from this window you can see the wall of the laboratory, and as the lights were still on, I thought the Professor’s conversation had been protracted, and, hoping that the best results might come from this interview, I went to bed. It was earlier than I go as a rule, but it was quite usual for me to go to bed even without saying good night to the Professor.

“I was awakened at eight in the morning by the housekeeper, who told me that the Professor was not in his room. Here again, this was not an unusual circumstance. Sometimes the Professor would work very late in the laboratory and then throw himself into an armchair and go off to sleep. It was a habit of which I had remonstrated as plainly as I dared; but he was not a man who bore criticism with equanimity.

“I got into my dressing-gown and my slippers, and went along to the laboratory, which is reached, as you know, by the way we came here. It was then that I discovered him on the floor, and he was quite dead.”

===Chinese===

“遗传？”曼弗雷德急忙插话，“费伯小姐有什么不好？”

“我不知道，”对方耸了耸肩，“可教授听到些风言风语，说她父亲死在嗜酒者收容所里。我相信那些传闻纯属子虚。”

“昨晚出了什么事？”费尔问。

“据我所知，斯蒂芬来了，”芒西说，“我有意避了开去；实际上我一直在自己房里，补写拖欠的信件。十一点半前后我下了楼，可教授还没回屋里来。从这扇窗望得见实验室的墙，灯还亮着，我想教授这次谈话谈得久了些，又盼着这番会面能有最好的结果，我便上床睡了。这比我平日的钟点早了些，不过连晚安都不跟教授道一声就睡，在我也是常有的事。

“早晨八点，女管家把我叫醒，说教授不在他房里。这也不算反常。教授有时在实验室干到很晚，便一头栽进扶手椅睡过去。这个毛病，我平素能多委婉就多委婉地劝过；可他不是个能安然受谏的人。

“我披上晨衣、趿上拖鞋，往实验室去——路就是方才咱们来的这条。就在那儿，我发现他倒在地上，人已经没气了。”


===Original===

“Was the door of the laboratory open?” asked Gonsalez.

“It was ajar.”

“And the gate also was ajar?”

Munsey nodded.

“You heard no sound of quarrelling?”

“None.”

===Chinese===

“实验室的门开着吗？”冈萨雷斯问。

“虚掩着。”

“栅门也虚掩着？”

芒西点了点头。

“你没听见争吵的声音？”

“没有。”


===Original===

There was a knock, and Munsey walked to the door.

“It is Stephen,” he said, and a second later Stephen Tableman, escorted by two detectives, came into the room. His big face was pale, and when he greeted his cousin with a little smile, Manfred saw the extraordinary canines, big and cruel looking. The other teeth were of normal size, but these pointed fangs were notably abnormal.

Stephen Tableman was a young giant, and, observing those great hands of his, Manfred bit his lip thoughtfully.

“You have heard the sad news, Mr. Tableman?”

“Yes, sir,” said Stephen in a shaking voice. “Can I see my father?”

“In a little time,” said Fare, and his voice was hard. “I want you to tell me when you saw your father last.”

===Chinese===

有人敲门，芒西走过去开门。

“是斯蒂芬。”他说。片刻之后，斯蒂芬·泰布尔曼由两名侦探陪同走进屋来。他宽大的脸没有血色；当他向堂兄微微一笑以示招呼时，曼弗雷德看见了那对异乎寻常的犬齿——硕大，模样狰狞。其余牙齿尺寸如常，唯独这两颗尖牙反常得扎眼。

斯蒂芬·泰布尔曼是个年轻的巨汉；看着他那一双大手，曼弗雷德若有所思地咬住了嘴唇。

“你已得知这不幸的消息了，泰布尔曼先生？”

“是的，先生。”斯蒂芬声音发颤地说，“我能见见我父亲吗？”

“稍待片刻。”费尔说，语气生硬，“我要你告诉我，你最后一次见到你父亲是什么时候。”


===Original===

“I saw him alive last night,” said Stephen Tableman quickly. “I came by appointment to the laboratory, and we had a long talk.”

“How long were you there with him?”

“About two hours, as near as I can guess.”

“Was the conversation of a friendly character?”

“Very,” said Stephen emphatically. “For the first time since over a year ago”﻿—he hesitated﻿—“we discussed a certain subject rationally.”

“The subject being your fiancée, Miss Faber?”

===Chinese===

“昨晚我见到活着的他。”斯蒂芬·泰布尔曼立即答道，“我按约去了实验室，我们长谈了一场。”

“你在那儿陪了他多久？”

“约莫两个钟头，我估摸着。”

“谈话是友好的吗？”

“非常友好。”斯蒂芬加重语气说，“一年多来头一回”——他迟疑了一下——“我们心平气和地商量了一件事。”

“事情指的是你的未婚妻费伯小姐？”


===Original===

Stephen looked at the interrogator steadily.

“That was the subject, Mr. Fare,” he replied quietly.

“Did you discuss any other matters?”

Stephen hesitated.

“We discussed money,” he said. “My father cut off his allowance, and I have been rather short; in fact, I have been overdrawn at my bank, and he promised to make that right, and also spoke about﻿—the future.”

===Chinese===

斯蒂芬直视着问话的人。

“就是这件事，费尔先生。”他平静地答道。

“你们还谈了别的吗？”

斯蒂芬迟疑了。

“我们谈了钱，”他说，“父亲停了我的津贴，我手头一直相当拮据；说实在的，我在银行透支了。他答应把这笔补上，还谈到了——将来。”


===Original===

“About his will?”

“Yes, sir, he spoke about altering his will.” He looked across at Munsey, and again he smiled. “My cousin has been a most persistent advocate, and I can’t thank him half enough for his loyalty to me in those dark times,” he said.

“When you left the laboratory, did you go out by the side entrance?”

Stephen nodded.

===Chinese===

“谈到他的遗嘱了？”

“是的，先生，他说要改遗嘱。”他望向芒西，又笑了笑。“我这位堂兄一直是最执着的说客；在那段黑暗的日子里，他对我忠心耿耿，我怎么谢也谢不够。”他说。

“你离开实验室时，是从侧门出去的？”

斯蒂芬点了点头。


===Original===

“And did you close the door behind you?”

“My father closed the door,” he said. “I distinctly remember hearing the click of the lock as I was going up the alley.”

“Can the door be opened from outside?”

“Yes,” said Stephen, “there is a lock which has only one key, and that is in my father’s possession﻿—I think I am right, John?”

John Munsey nodded.

“So that, if he closed the door behind you, it could only be opened again by somebody in the laboratory﻿—himself, for example?”

===Chinese===

“出门时你随手把门带上了吗？”

“门是我父亲关的，”他说，“我清清楚楚记得，走上小巷时，身后传来锁舌咔哒一响。”

“那扇门能从外面打开吗？”

“能，”斯蒂芬说，“那把锁只有一把钥匙，在我父亲手里——我没记错吧，约翰？”

约翰·芒西点了点头。

“这么说，他若在你身后锁了门，就只有实验室里的人才能再把它打开——比方说他自己？”


===Original===

Stephen looked puzzled.

“I don’t quite understand the meaning of this enquiry,” he said. “The detective told me that my father had been found dead. What was the cause?”

“I think he was strangled,” said Fare quietly, and the young man took a step back.

“Strangled!” he whispered. “But he hadn’t an enemy in the world.”

“That we shall discover.” Fare’s voice was dry and businesslike. “You can go now, Mr. Tableman.”

After a moment’s hesitation the big fellow swung across the room through a door in the direction of the laboratory. He came back after an absence of a quarter of an hour, and his face was deathly white.

===Chinese===

斯蒂芬面露困惑。

“我不大明白这一连串盘问是什么意思，”他说，“侦探告诉我，我父亲被人发现死了。死因是什么？”

“我看他是被勒死的。”费尔平静地说；青年倒退了一步。

“勒死的！”他失声道，“可他在这个世上连一个仇人也没有啊。”

“有否仇人，我们会查个水落石出。”费尔的声音干巴巴的，公事公办。“你现在可以走了，泰布尔曼先生。”

那大个子迟疑了片刻，随即大步穿过房间，从一扇朝实验室方向的门出去了。他离开一刻钟后回来，脸色死一般苍白。


===Original===

“Horrible, horrible!” he muttered. “My poor father!”

“You are on the way to being a doctor, Mr. Tableman? I believe you are at the Middlesex Hospital,” said Fare. “Do you agree with me that your father was strangled?”

The other nodded.

“It looks that way,” he said, speaking with difficulty. “I couldn’t conduct an examination as if he had been﻿—somebody else, but it looks that way.”

The two men walked back to their lodgings. Manfred thought best when his muscles were most active. Their walk was in silence, each being busy with his own thoughts.

“You observed the canines?” asked Leon with quiet triumph after a while.

===Chinese===

“可怕，可怕！”他喃喃道，“我可怜的父亲！”

“你在学医吧，泰布尔曼先生？我记得你在米德尔塞克斯医院（Middlesex Hospital）。”费尔说，“你同意我的看法吗——令尊是被勒死的？”

对方点了点头。

“看样子是的，”他艰难地说，“我没法把他当作旁人来检查——可看样子是的。”

两人步行回寓所。曼弗雷德的头脑，在筋骨最为活动的时候最为灵光。一路无话，各人忙着各人的心事。

“注意到那对犬齿了吧？”过了一会儿，莱昂带着不动声色的得意问道。


===Original===

“I observed too his obvious distress,” said Manfred, and Leon chuckled.

“It is evident that you have not read friend Mantegazza’s admirable monograph on the ‘Physiology of Pain,’ ” he said smugly﻿—Leon was delightfully smug at times﻿—“nor examined his most admirable tables on the ‘Synonyms of Expression,’ or otherwise you would be aware that the expression of sorrow is indistinguishable from the expression of remorse.”

Manfred looked down at his friend with that quiet smile of his.

“Anybody who did not know you, Leon, would say that you were convinced that Professor Tableman was strangled by his son.”

“After a heated quarrel,” said Gonsalez complacently.

===Chinese===

“我还注意到他那显而易见的悲痛。”曼弗雷德说；莱昂咯咯笑了。

“看来你没读过咱们的朋友曼特加扎那部精彩的《痛觉生理学》专著，”他洋洋自得地说——莱昂有时候得意得实在可爱——“也没研读过他那套精妙之极的《表情同义词》图表；否则你就会知道，悲恸的表情与悔恨的表情，根本无从分辨。”

曼弗雷德低头看着自己的朋友，脸上挂着那副安静的微笑。

“莱昂，凡是跟你不相识的人，准会说你已经认定泰布尔曼教授是被他儿子勒死的了。”

“在一场火冒三丈的争吵之后。”冈萨雷斯怡然自得地补了一句。


===Original===

“When young Tableman had gone, you inspected the laboratory. Did you discover anything?”

“Nothing more than I expected to find,” said Gonsalez. “There were the usual air apparatus, the inevitable liquid-air still, the ever-to-be-expected electric crucibles. The inspection was superfluous, I admit, for I knew exactly how the murder was committed﻿—for murder it was﻿—the moment I came into the laboratory and saw the thermos flask and the pad of cotton wool.”

Suddenly he frowned and stopped dead.

“*Santa Miranda!*” he ejaculated. Gonsalez always swore by this nonexistent saint. “I had forgotten!”

He looked up and down the street.

===Chinese===

“小泰布尔曼走后，你把实验室查看了一遍。有发现吗？”

“发现的都不出我的所料，”冈萨雷斯说，“无非是些寻常的空气装置、少不了的液态空气蒸馏器、意料之中的电坩埚。这番查看实属多余，我承认，因为一走进实验室、看见那只保温瓶和那团棉絮，我就确切知道这桩谋杀是怎么干成的——那确凿是谋杀。”

他忽然皱起眉头，猛地站住了。

“*Santa Miranda*（圣米兰达）！”他失声喊道——冈萨雷斯起誓赌咒，总用这位并不存在的圣人。“我忘了！”

他朝街的两头张了张。


===Original===

“There is a place from whence we can telephone,” he said. “Will you come with me, or shall I leave you here?”

“I am consumed with curiosity,” said Manfred.

They went into the shop and Gonsalez gave a number. Manfred did not ask him how he knew it, because he too had read the number which was written on the telephone disc that stood on the late Professor’s table.

“Is that you, Mr. Munsey?” asked Gonsalez. “It is I. You remember I have just come from you? Yes, I thought you would recognise my voice. I want to ask you where are the Professor’s spectacles.”

There was a moment’s silence.

“The Professor’s spectacles?” said Munsey’s voice. “Why, they’re with him, aren’t they?”

===Chinese===

“那边有个能打电话的地方，”他说，“你跟我去，还是让我把你留在这儿？”

“我的好奇心已经烧得我按捺不住了。”曼弗雷德说。

两人进了那家店铺，冈萨雷斯报了个号码。曼弗雷德没有问他怎么知道号码的，因为已故教授桌上那只电话拨号盘旁写着的号码，他也看到了。

“是芒西先生吗？”冈萨雷斯问，“是我。您记得我刚从您那儿来吧？对，我就知道您听得出我的嗓音。我想请问，教授的眼镜在哪儿？”

电话那头静了片刻。

“教授的眼镜？”芒西的声音说，“不是在他身上吗？”


===Original===

“They were not on the body or near it,” said Gonsalez. “Will you see if they are in his room? I’ll hold the line.”

He waited, humming a little aria from *El Perro Chico*, a light opera which had its day in Madrid fifteen years before; and presently he directed his attention again to the instrument.

“In his bedroom, were they? Thank you very much.”

He hung up the receiver. He did not explain the conversation to Manfred, nor did Manfred expect him to, for Leon Gonsalez dearly loved a mystery. All he permitted himself to say was;

“Canine teeth!”

And this seemed to amuse him very much.

===Chinese===

“不在尸体上，也不在近旁，”冈萨雷斯说，“麻烦您看看是不是在他房里？我持机等您。”

他一边等，一边哼着《小狗》（*El Perro Chico*）里的一支小咏叹调——那部轻歌剧十五年前曾在马德里风行一时；过了一会儿，他把注意力又收回到话筒上。

“在他卧室里，是吗？多谢。”

他挂上听筒。他没有向曼弗雷德解释这番通话，曼弗雷德也并不指望他解释，因为莱昂·冈萨雷斯最钟爱悬念。他只容自己说了一句：

“犬齿！”

而这话似乎让他开心得很。

===Original===

When Gonsalez came to breakfast the next morning, the waiter informed him that Manfred had gone out early. George came in about ten minutes after the other had commenced breakfast, and Leon Gonsalez looked up.

“You puzzle me when your face is so mask-like, George,” he said. “I don’t know whether you’re particularly amused or particularly depressed.”

“A little of the one and a little of the other,” said Manfred, sitting down to breakfast. “I have been to Fleet Street to examine the files of the sporting press.”

“The sporting press?” repeated Gonsalez, staring at him, and Manfred nodded.

“Incidentally, I met Fare. No trace of poison has been found in the body, and no other sign of violence. They are arresting Stephen Tableman today.”

===Chinese===

第二天早晨冈萨雷斯下来用早餐，侍者告诉他，曼弗雷德一大早出去了。对方开始用餐约十分钟后，乔治才进来，莱昂·冈萨雷斯抬眼望去。

“你这张脸跟面具似的，真让我犯难，乔治，”他说，“我闹不清你是格外高兴呢，还是格外沮丧。”

“两样都占一点。”曼弗雷德说着坐下用早餐，“我去舰队街翻体育报刊的合订本了。”

“体育报刊？”冈萨雷斯盯住他，重复了一遍；曼弗雷德点了点头。

“顺道碰见了费尔。尸体里没查出丝毫毒迹，也没有其他暴力的痕迹。他们今天就要逮捕斯蒂芬·泰布尔曼。”


===Original===

“I was afraid of that,” said Gonsalez gravely. “But why the sporting press, George?”
