Manfred did not answer the question, but went on:

“Fare is quite certain that the murder was committed by Stephen Tableman. His theory is that there was a quarrel and that the young man lost his temper and choked his father. Apparently, the examination of the body proved that extraordinary violence must have been used. Every blood-vessel in the neck is congested. Fare also told me that at first the doctor suspected poison, but there is no sign of any drug to be discovered, and the doctors say that the drug that would cause that death with such symptoms is unknown. It makes it worse for Stephen Tableman because for the past few months he has been concentrating his studies upon obscure poisons.”

Gonsalez stretched back in his chair, his hands in his pockets.

“Well, whether he committed that murder or not,” he said after a while, “he is certain to commit a murder sooner or later. I remember once a doctor in Barcelona who had such teeth. He was a devout Christian, a popular man, a bachelor, and had plenty of money, and there seemed no reason in the world why he should murder anybody, and yet he did. He murdered another doctor who threatened to expose some error he made in an operation. I tell you, George, with teeth like that﻿—” He paused and frowned thoughtfully. “My dear George,” he said, “I am going to ask Fare if he will allow me the privilege of spending a few hours alone in Professor Tableman’s laboratory.”

===Chinese===

“我就怕这一手。”冈萨雷斯神色凝重地说，“可是翻体育报刊做什么，乔治？”

曼弗雷德不答这个问题，径自往下说：

“费尔确信这桩谋杀是斯蒂芬·泰布尔曼干的。他的推断是：先是一场争吵，年轻人动了怒，掐死了父亲。显然，验尸表明行凶时用的暴力骇人听闻——颈部每一条血管都淤血了。费尔还告诉我，起初医生疑心是毒杀，可查不出任何药物的迹象，医生们说，能造成这种死状和症状的毒药闻所未闻。这对斯蒂芬·泰布尔曼更加不利，因为近几个月来，他一直潜心钻研冷僻的毒物。”

冈萨雷斯仰身靠回椅背，两手插在口袋里。

“哼，这桩案子不论是不是他干的，”半晌他说道，“他早晚总要干出一桩杀人案来。我记得巴塞罗那从前有位医生，就长着那样的牙。他是个虔诚的基督徒，人缘极好，单身汉，又富有，天底下似乎没有任何理由要他去杀谁——可他杀了。他杀了另一位医生，因为那位威胁要揭发他一次手术上的失误。我告诉你，乔治，长着那样的牙——”他顿住，若有所思地皱起眉。“亲爱的乔治，”他说，“我要去问问费尔，能不能赏我个恩典，让我独自在泰布尔曼教授的实验室里待上几个钟头。”


===Original===

“Why on earth﻿—” began Manfred, and checked himself. “Why, of course, you have a reason, Leon. As a rule I find no difficulty in solving such mysteries as these. But in this case I am puzzled, though I have confidence that you have already unravelled what mystery there is. There are certain features about the business which are particularly baffling. Why should the old man be wearing thick gloves﻿—”

Gonsalez sprang to his feet, his eyes blazing.

“What a fool! What a fool!” he almost shouted. “I didn’t see those. Are you sure, George?” he asked eagerly. “He had thick gloves? Are you certain?”

Manfred nodded, smiling his surprise at the other’s perturbation.

“That’s it!” Gonsalez snapped his fingers. “I knew there was some error in my calculations! Thick woollen gloves, weren’t they?” He became suddenly thoughtful. “Now, I wonder how the devil he induced the old man to put ’em on?” he said half to himself.

===Chinese===

“这究竟为什么——”曼弗雷德开了个头，又刹住了，“哦，当然，你有你的道理，莱昂。这类谜案，我一向不难解开。可这一桩我却犯了迷糊，不过我相信，这里头有什么谜，你早已解开了。这桩事有几处特别令人费解的地方。老头儿干吗要戴着厚手套——”

冈萨雷斯霍地跳起身来，两眼放光。

“真是个蠢货！真是个蠢货！”他几乎喊出声来，“我竟没看见那双手套。你确定吗，乔治？”他急切地问，“他戴着厚手套？你能肯定？”

曼弗雷德点了点头，对同伴这份激动微感诧异，含笑望着他。

“就是它！”冈萨雷斯打了个响指，“我就说我准是算漏了什么！是厚毛线手套，对不对？”他忽然又沉思起来。“真见鬼，他到底是怎么哄老头儿把那玩意儿戴上的？”他半自言自语地说。


===Original===

The request to Mr. Fare was granted, and the two men went together to the laboratory. John Munsey was waiting for them.

“I discovered those spectacles by my uncle’s bedside,” he said as soon as he saw them.

“Oh, the spectacles?” said Leon absently. “May I see them?” He took them in his hand. “Your uncle was very shortsighted. How did they come to leave his possession, I wonder?”

“I think he went up to his bedroom to change; he usually did after dinner,” explained Mr. Munsey. “And he must have left them there. He usually kept an emergency pair in the laboratory, but for some reason or other he doesn’t seem to have put them on. Do you wish to be alone in the laboratory?” he asked.

“I would rather,” said Leon. “Perhaps you would entertain my friend whilst I look round?”

===Chinese===

向费尔先生提出的请求获了准，两人便一同前往实验室。约翰·芒西正等着他们。

“我发现那副眼镜在叔叔的床边。”他一见他们便说。

“哦，眼镜吗？”莱昂心不在焉地说，“能给我看看吗？”他把眼镜拿在手里。“您叔叔深度近视。眼镜怎么会离了他的身，我倒纳闷。”

“我想他是上楼到卧室更衣去了，他饭后照例要换衣服的。”芒西先生解释道，“眼镜准是落在了那儿。他通常在实验室备着一副应急的，可不知怎么，那天似乎没有戴上。您想独自待在实验室里？”他问。

“最好如此。”莱昂说，“我四下查看的工夫，也许您可以陪陪我这位朋友？”


===Original===

Left alone, he locked the door that communicated between the laboratory and the house, and his first search was for the spectacles that the old man usually wore when he was working.

Characteristically enough, he went straight to the place where they were﻿—a big galvanised ashpan by the side of the steps leading up to the laboratory. He found them in fragments, the horn rims broken in two places, and he collected what he could and returned to the laboratory, and, laying them on the bench, he took up the telephone.

The laboratory had a direct connection with the exchange, and after five minutes waiting, Gonsalez found himself in communication with Stephen Tableman.

“Yes, sir,” was the surprised reply. “My father wore his glasses throughout the interview.”

“Thank you, that is all,” said Gonsalez and hung up the phone.

===Chinese===

独处之后，他锁上了实验室与正屋相通的那扇门，头一件事便是搜寻老先生平日做实验戴的那副眼镜。

说来也有他的风格，他径直走向藏镜之处——实验室台阶旁一只镀锌铁皮灰箱。眼镜已经成了碎片，角质镜框断作两截；他把能拾的都拾起来，带回实验室摊在工作台上，然后拿起电话。

实验室有一条直通电话局的线路，等了五分钟，冈萨雷斯与斯蒂芬·泰布尔曼通上了话。

“是的，先生。”对方讶异地答道，“面谈的全程，我父亲都戴着眼镜。”

“多谢，问完了。”冈萨雷斯说完挂断了电话。


===Original===

Then he went to one of the apparatus in a corner of the laboratory and worked steadily for an hour and a half. At the end of that time he went to the telephone again. Another half hour passed, and then he pulled from his pocket a pair of thick woollen gloves, and unlocking the door leading to the house, called Manfred.

“Ask Mr. Munsey to come,” he said.

“Your friend is interested in science,” said Mr. Munsey as he accompanied Manfred along the passage.

“I think he is one of the cleverest in his own particular line,” said Manfred.

He came into the laboratory ahead of Munsey, and to his surprise, Gonsalez was standing near the table, holding in his hand a small liqueur glass filled with an almost colourless liquid. Almost colourless, but there was a blue tinge to it, and to Manfred’s amazement a faint mist was rising from its surface.

===Chinese===

随后他走到实验室角落里的一台仪器前，不紧不慢、专心致志地干了一个半钟头。干完之后，他又去打电话。又过了半个钟头，他从口袋里掏出一副厚毛线手套，开了通往正屋的门锁，唤来曼弗雷德。

“请芒西先生过来一下。”他说。

“您这位朋友对科学有兴趣。”芒西先生陪曼弗雷德走过过道时说。

“依我看，在他那一行里，他算得上数一数二的聪明人。”曼弗雷德说。

他先于芒西走进实验室；令他讶异的是，冈萨雷斯正站在桌旁，手里托着一只小利口酒杯，杯中盛着近乎无色的液体。近乎无色，却隐隐泛着一抹蓝；更教曼弗雷德骇然的是，液面上正袅袅升起一缕淡雾。


===Original===

Manfred stared at him, and then he saw that the hands of Leon Gonsalez were enclosed in thick woollen gloves.

“Have you finished?” smiled Mr. Munsey as he came from behind Manfred; and then he saw Leon and smiled no more. His face went drawn and haggard, his eyes narrowed, and Manfred heard his laboured breathing.

“Have a drink, my friend?” said Leon pleasantly. “A beautiful drink. You’d mistake it for crème de menthe or any old liqueur﻿—especially if you were a shortsighted, absentminded old man and somebody had purloined your spectacles.”

“What do you mean?” asked Munsey hoarsely. “I﻿—I don’t understand you.”

“I promise you that this drink is innocuous, that it contains no poison whatever, that it is as pure as the air you breathe,” Gonsalez went on.

“Damn you!” yelled Munsey, but before he could leap at his tormentor, Manfred had caught him and slung him to the ground.

===Chinese===

曼弗雷德盯住他，随即看见莱昂·冈萨雷斯那双手上，正套着厚厚的毛线手套。

“都看好了？”芒西先生从曼弗雷德身后转出来，含笑问道；接着他看见了莱昂，笑意顿敛。他的脸绷得扭曲枯槁，双眼眯起，曼弗雷德听见他呼吸粗重。

“喝一杯吗，我的朋友？”莱昂和颜悦色地说，“一杯妙极了的饮料。你会把它错当成薄荷甜酒，或是无论哪种利口酒——尤其当你是个深度近视、心不在焉的老先生，而又有谁恰好把你的眼镜顺走了的时候。”

“你这是什么意思？”芒西嗓音沙哑地问，“我——我不明白你的意思。”

“我向你担保，这杯东西无害，不含半点毒性，纯净得如同你呼吸的空气。”冈萨雷斯接着说。

“该死的！”芒西吼道；可他还没来得及扑向这个折磨他的人，曼弗雷德已一把将他拿住，掼倒在地。


===Original===

“I have telephoned for the excellent Mr. Fare, and he will be here soon, and also Mr. Stephen Tableman. Ah, here they are.”

There was a tap at the door.

“Will you open, please, my dear George? I do not think our young friend will move. If he does, I will throw the contents of this glass in his face.”

Fare came in, followed by Stephen, and with them an officer from Scotland Yard.

“There is your prisoner, Mr. Fare,” said Gonsalez. “And here is the means by which Mr. John Munsey encompassed the death of his uncle﻿—decided thereto, I guess, by the fact that his uncle had been reconciled with Stephen Tableman, and that the will which he had so carefully manoeuvred was to be altered in Stephen Tableman’s favour.”

“That’s a lie!” gasped John Munsey. “I worked for you﻿—you know I did, Stephen. I did my best for you﻿—”

===Chinese===

“我已打电话去请那位干练的费尔先生了，他很快就到，还有斯蒂芬·泰布尔曼先生也请了。啊，他们到了。”

门口响起轻轻的叩门声。

“亲爱的乔治，请你开门好吗？谅咱们这位小朋友也不敢乱动。他若动一动，我就把这杯东西泼到他脸上。”

费尔走了进来，斯蒂芬跟在后面，同来的还有一名苏格兰场警官。

“您的囚犯在那儿，费尔先生。”冈萨雷斯说，“而这，就是约翰·芒西先生送他叔父归西的那件东西——他之所以下了决心，我猜，是因为叔父已同斯蒂芬·泰布尔曼言归于好，而那份他处心积虑经营出的遗嘱，就要改成对斯蒂芬·泰布尔曼有利了。”

“那是谎话！”约翰·芒西喘着气喊道，“我是为你出力来着——你知道的，斯蒂芬。我为你尽了全力——”


===Original===

“All part of the general scheme of deception﻿—again I am guessing,” said Gonsalez. “If I am wrong, drink this. It is the liquid your uncle drank on the night of his death.”

“What is it?” demanded Fare quickly.

“Ask him,” smiled Gonsalez, nodding to the man.

John Munsey turned on his heels and walked to the door, and the police officer who had accompanied Fare followed him.

“And now I will tell you what it is,” said Gonsalez. “It is liquid air!”

“Liquid air!” said the Commissioner. “Why, what do you mean? How can a man be poisoned with liquid air?”

===Chinese===

“全都是那出欺人大戏里的一环——这又是我的猜测。”冈萨雷斯说，“若我猜错了，就把这杯喝下去。这正是你叔父死的那天夜里喝下去的东西。”

“那是什么？”费尔急问。

“问他吧。”冈萨雷斯朝那人一点头，微笑道。

约翰·芒西转过脚跟，径直朝门口走去；随费尔同来的那名警官跟了出去。

“现在我来告诉你们这是什么，”冈萨雷斯说，“是液态空气！”

“液态空气！”总监说，“这是怎么说？人怎么会教液态空气毒死？”


===Original===

“Professor Tableman was not poisoned. Liquid air is a fluid obtained by reducing the temperature of air to two hundred and seventy degrees below zero. Scientists use the liquid for experiments, and it is usually kept in a thermos flask, the mouth of which is stopped with cotton wool, because, as you know, there would be danger of a blow up if the air was confined.”

“Good God?” gasped Tableman in horror. “Then that blue mark about my father’s throat﻿—”

“He was frozen to death. At least his throat was frozen solid the second that liquid was taken. Your father was in the habit of drinking a liqueur before he went to bed, and there is no doubt that, after you had left, Munsey gave the Professor a glassful of liquid air and by some means induced him to put on gloves.”

“Why did he do that? Oh, of course, the cold,” said Manfred.

Gonsalez nodded.

“Without gloves he would have detected immediately the stuff he was handling. What artifice Munsey used we may never know. It is certain he himself must have been wearing gloves at the time. After your father’s death he then began to prepare evidence to incriminate somebody else. The Professor had probably put away his glasses preparatory to going to bed, and the murderer, like myself, overlooked the fact that the body was still wearing gloves.

===Chinese===

“泰布尔曼教授不是被毒死的。液态空气，是把空气的温度降到零下二百七十度而得到的一种流体。科学家拿这种液体做实验，通常盛在保温瓶里，瓶口用棉絮松松塞住——因为你们知道，空气若被封在里面，便有炸裂之虞。”

“天哪！”泰布尔曼倒吸一口冷气，惊骇万分，“那么我父亲喉间那圈青痕——”

“他是给冻死的。至少，那液体一入口，他的喉咙顷刻间就冻成了实心。你父亲睡前有喝一杯利口酒的习惯；毫无疑问，你走之后，芒西给教授满满递上一杯液态空气，又想法子哄他戴上了手套。”

“他干吗要那样做？哦，对了，是怕冷。”曼弗雷德说。

冈萨雷斯点了点头。

“若不戴手套，老先生立刻就会察觉自己端在手里的是什么货色。芒西使的什么伎俩，我们恐怕永远无从知晓。可以断定的是，他本人当时必定也戴着手套。你父亲死后，他便着手炮制栽赃他人的证据。教授多半是为就寝做预备，先把眼镜收妥了，而凶手同我一样，忽略了尸体的手上还戴着手套。”


===Original===

“My own theory,” said Gonsalez later, “is that Munsey has been working for years to oust his cousin from his father’s affections. He probably invented the story of the dipsomaniac father of Miss Faber.”

Young Tableman had come to their lodgings, and now Gonsalez had a shock. Something he said had surprised a laugh from Stephen, and Gonsalez stared at him.

“Your﻿—your teeth!” he stammered.

Stephen flushed.

“My teeth?” he repeated, puzzled.

“You had two enormous canines when I saw you last,” said Gonsalez. “You remember, Manfred?” he said, and he was really agitated. “I told you﻿—”

===Chinese===

“我个人的看法，”冈萨雷斯后来说道，“是芒西多年来一直在处心积虑，要把堂弟从老人的疼爱里挤出去。费伯小姐那位嗜酒如命的老父亲的传闻，多半也是他编造出来的。”

小泰布尔曼来到他们的寓所；这时冈萨雷斯吃了一惊。他不知说了句什么，逗得斯蒂芬笑出声来，冈萨雷斯直勾勾地盯着他。

“你——你的牙！”他结结巴巴地说。

斯蒂芬涨红了脸。

“我的牙？”他茫然地重复。

“我上回见你的时候，你还有两颗大得吓人的犬齿。”冈萨雷斯说，“你还记得吧，曼弗雷德？”他真的动了容，“我当时就告诉你——”


===Original===

He was interrupted by a burst of laughter from the young student.

“Oh, they were false,” he said awkwardly. “They were knocked out at a rugger match, and Benson, who’s a fellow in our dental department and is an awfully good chap, though a pretty poor dentist, undertook to make me two to fill the deficiency. They looked terrible, didn’t they? I don’t wonder your noticing them. I got two new ones put in by another dentist.”

“It happened on the thirteenth of September last year. I read about it in the sporting press,” said Manfred, and Gonsalez fixed him with a reproachful glance.

===Chinese===

话被年轻学生的一阵大笑打断。

“哦，那是假牙。”他不好意思地说，“是打橄榄球赛时给撞掉的。本森——我们牙科的一位同仁，人是顶好的，只是当牙医的手艺稀松——自告奋勇给我做了两颗，补上这个缺。看上去吓人得很，对不对？难怪你们会留意到。后来我另找了位牙医，装了两颗新的。”

“那是去年九月十三日的事。体育报刊上登过，我读到了。”曼弗雷德说；冈萨雷斯定定地望住他，目光里满是责备。


===Original===

“You see, my dear Leon﻿—” Manfred laid his hand on the other’s shoulder﻿—“I knew they were false, just as you knew they were canines.”

When they were alone, Manfred said:

“Talking about canines﻿—”

“Let us talk about something else,” snapped Leon.

===Chinese===

“你瞧，亲爱的莱昂，”曼弗雷德把手搭在同伴的肩上，“我早知道那是假牙，正如你早知道那是犬齿。”

等到两人独处时，曼弗雷德说：

“说到犬齿——”

“咱们换个话题吧。”莱昂没好气地说。

## The Man Who Hated Earthworms / 恨蚯蚓的人

===Original===
> “The death has occurred at Staines of Mr. Falmouth, late Superintendent of the Criminal Investigation Department. Mr. Falmouth will best be remembered as the Officer who arrested George Manfred, the leader of the Four Just Men gang. The sensational escape of this notorious man is perhaps the most remarkable chapter in criminal history. The ‘Four Just Men’ was an organisation which set itself to right acts of injustice which the law left unpunished. It is believed that the members were exceedingly rich men who devoted their lives and fortunes to this quixotic but wholly unlawful purpose. The gang has not been heard of for many years.”

Manfred read the paragraph from the *Morning Telegram* and Leon Gonsalez frowned.

“I have an absurd objection to being called a ‘gang,’ ” he said, and Manfred smiled quietly.

“Poor old Falmouth,” he reflected, “well, he knows! He was a nice fellow.”

“I liked Falmouth,” agreed Gonsalez. “He was a perfectly normal man except for a slight progenism﻿—”

Manfred laughed.

===Chinese===
> 「法尔茅斯（Falmouth）先生于斯泰恩斯（Staines）逝世，生前曾任刑事调查部警司。世人追念法尔茅斯，最难忘的莫过于他逮捕『四义士』（The Four Just Men）帮伙首领乔治·曼弗雷德（George Manfred）一事。这个恶名远扬之人的轰动越狱，堪称犯罪史上最惊心动魄的一章。『四义士』是一个以纠正法律未加惩处的不公为己任的组织。据信其成员皆是极富有之人，为这项堂吉诃德式却全然违法的事业，献上了各自的生命与财产。该帮伙已多年杳无音讯。」

曼弗雷德读着《晨间电讯报》（*Morning Telegram*）上的这段话，莱昂·冈萨雷斯（Leon Gonsalez）皱起了眉头。

「被人叫作『帮伙』，我有个荒唐的忌讳。」他说。曼弗雷德莞尔一笑。

「可怜的老法尔茅斯，」他感叹道，「唉，他这会儿什么都知道了！他是个好人。」

「我也喜欢法尔茅斯，」冈萨雷斯深表赞同，「他是个再正常不过的人，只除了微微有些凸颌（progenism）——」

曼弗雷德笑了。

===Original===
“Forgive me if I appear dense, but I have never been able to keep up with you in this particular branch of science,” he said, “what is a ‘progenism’?”

“The unscientific call it an ‘underhung jaw,’ ” explained Leon, “and it is mistaken for strength. It is only normal in Piedmont where the brachycephalic skull is so common. With such a skull, progenism is almost a natural condition.”

“Progenism or not, he was a good fellow,” insisted Manfred and Leon nodded. “With well-developed wisdom teeth,” he added slyly, and Gonsalez went red, for teeth formed a delicate subject with him. Nevertheless he grinned.

“It will interest you to know, my dear George,” he said triumphantly, “that when the famous Dr. Carrara examined the teeth of four hundred criminals and a like number of non-criminals﻿—you will find his detailed narrative in the monograph ‘Sullo Sviluppo Del Terzo Dente Morale Net Criminali’﻿—he found the wisdom tooth more frequently present in normal people.”

“I grant you the wisdom tooth,” said Manfred hastily. “Look at the bay! Did you ever see anything more perfect?”

They were sitting on a little green lawn overlooking Babbacombe Beach. The sun was going down and a perfect day was drawing to its close. High above the blue sea towered the crimson cliffs and green fields of Devon.

===Chinese===
「恕我愚钝，这一门学问，我从来追不上你，」他说，「什么是『凸颌』？」

「不谙科学的人管它叫『地包天』，」莱昂解释道，「人们还误把它当作坚毅的标志。只有在皮埃蒙特（Piedmont），它才算正常，那里短颅型的头骨极为普遍。配上那样的头骨，凸颌几乎是天然状态。」

「管他凸颌不凸颌，他是个好人。」曼弗雷德坚持道，莱昂点头称是。「而且智齿发育甚好。」他又狡黠地补了一句。冈萨雷斯涨红了脸——牙齿于他向来是个微妙的话题。不过他还是咧嘴笑了。

「亲爱的乔治，告诉你一桩趣闻，」他得意扬扬地说，「当年大名鼎鼎的卡拉拉博士（Dr. Carrara）检查过四百名罪犯与同等数目的非罪犯的牙齿——他的详细论述见诸专著『Sullo Sviluppo Del Terzo Dente Morale Net Criminali』（《论罪犯第三臼齿之发育》）——他发现智齿在正常人当中反而更为多见。」

「智齿之事，我认了。」曼弗雷德连忙说，「瞧这海湾！你可曾见过比这更完美的景致？」

两人坐在一方小小的绿茵草坪上，俯瞰巴巴科姆海滩（Babbacombe Beach）。夕阳西沉，完美的一天正走向尾声。德文郡（Devon）绯红的悬崖与苍翠的田野，高高耸峙在蔚蓝的大海之上。

===Original===
Manfred looked at his watch.

“Are we dressing for dinner?” he asked, “or has your professional friend Bohemian tastes?”

“He is of the new school,” said Leon, “rather superior, rather immaculate, very Balliol. I am anxious that you should meet him, his hands are rather fascinating.”

Manfred in his wisdom did not ask why.

“I met him at golf,” Gonsalez went on, “and certain things happened which interested me. For example, every time he saw an earthworm he stopped to kill it and displayed such an extraordinary fury in the assassination that I was astounded. Prejudice has no place in the scientific mind. He is exceptionally wealthy. People at the club told me that his uncle left him close on a million, and the estate of his aunt or cousin who died last year was valued at another million and he was the sole legatee. Naturally a good catch. Whether Miss Moleneux thinks the same I have had no opportunity of gauging,” he added after a pause.

===Chinese===
曼弗雷德看了看表。

「咱们晚饭要更衣盛装吗？」他问，「还是你那位专业人士朋友偏好波希米亚做派？」

「他是新派人物，」莱昂说，「颇为自命不凡，一尘不染，十足的巴利奥尔（Balliol，牛津名学院）气派。我正想让你见见他，他那双手颇为耐人寻味。」

曼弗雷德自有他的明智，没有问为什么。

「我是在打高尔夫时认识他的，」冈萨雷斯继续说，「随后发生了几件令我感兴趣的事。譬如，他每见一条蚯蚓，必停下来将其弄死，行凶时那股异乎寻常的狂怒令我骇然。偏见在科学的头脑里本无立足之地。他极为富有。俱乐部里的人告诉我，他叔父留给他的遗产将近百万；去年亡故的姑母——抑或表亲——名下的产业又估值百万，而他是唯一的继承人。自然是桩好亲事。莫利纽克斯小姐（Miss Moleneux）是否也作此想，我尚无缘探个究竟。」他顿了顿，又加了一句。

===Original===
“Good lord!” cried Manfred in consternation as he jumped up from his chair. “She is coming to dinner too, isn’t she?”

“And her mamma,” said Leon solemnly. “Her mamma has learnt Spanish by correspondence lessons, and insists upon greeting me with ‘*habla usted Espanol* Espanol?’ ”

The two men had rented Cliff House for the spring. Manfred loved Devonshire in April when the slopes of the hills were yellow with primroses and daffodils made a golden path across the Devon lawns. “Señor Fuentes” had taken the house after one inspection and found the calm and the peace which only nature’s treasury of colour and fragrance could bring to his active mind.

Manfred had dressed and was sitting by the wood fire in the drawing-room when the purr of a motorcar coming cautiously down the cliff road brought him to his feet and through the open French window.

Leon Gonsalez had joined him before the big limousine had come to a halt before the porch.

===Chinese===
「天哪！」曼弗雷德惊呼着从椅子上跳起来。「她今晚也要来赴宴，对不对？」

「还有她的妈妈。」莱昂郑重地说，「她妈妈上函授课学了西班牙语，坚持用『*habla usted Espanol*（您会说西班牙语吗）』来同我打招呼。」

两人为度春租下了悬崖别墅（Cliff House）。曼弗雷德钟爱四月的德文郡：那时山坡上樱草花铺出一片金黄，水仙在德文郡的草坪间踏出一条金色的小径。「富恩特斯先生」（Señor Fuentes）只看了一次房便把它租下，为他那颗活跃的心灵，觅得了唯有大自然这座色彩与芬芳的宝库方能带来的宁静与安谧。

曼弗雷德穿戴停当，正坐在客厅的柴火边，忽听得一辆汽车沿崖路小心驶下的低鸣，他当即起身，穿过敞开的法式落地长窗。

大型轿车尚未在门廊前停稳，莱昂·冈萨雷斯已来到他身旁。

===Original===
The first to alight was a man and George observed him closely. He was tall and thin. He was not bad looking, though the face was lined and the eyes deep set and level. He greeted Gonsalez with just a tiny hint of patronage in his tone.

“I hope we haven’t kept you waiting, but my experiments detained me. Nothing went right in the laboratory today. You know Miss Moleneux and Mrs. Moleneux?”

Manfred was introduced and found himself shaking hands with a grave-eyed girl of singular beauty.

Manfred was unusually sensitive to “atmosphere” and there was something about this girl which momentarily chilled him. Her frequent smile, sweet as it was and undoubtedly sincere, was as undoubtedly mechanical. Leon, who judged people by reason rather than instinct, reached his conclusion more surely and gave shape and definite description to what in Manfred’s mind was merely a distressful impression. The girl was afraid! Of what? wondered Leon. Not of that stout, complacent little woman whom she called mother, and surely not of this thin-faced academic gentleman in pince-nez.

Gonsalez had introduced Dr. Viglow and whilst the ladies were taking off their cloaks in Manfred’s room above, he had leisure to form a judgment. There was no need for him to entertain his guest. Dr. Viglow spoke fluently, entertainingly and all the time.

===Chinese===
头一个下车的是个男人，乔治细细打量着他。他身材瘦高，相貌不坏，虽然脸上刻着皱纹，双眼深陷、目光平直。他招呼冈萨雷斯时，语气里恰有一丝居高临下的味道。

「希望没让你们久等，我的实验把我耽搁住了。今天实验室里事事不顺。你们认识莫利纽克斯小姐和莫利纽克斯太太吧？」

介绍过后，曼弗雷德发现自己握着的，是一位眼神沉静、美得出众的姑娘的手。

曼弗雷德对「气氛」异乎寻常地敏感，而这姑娘身上有种东西，令他霎时心头发冷。她频频绽露的微笑，纵然甜美、无疑真诚，却也无疑带着机械味。莱昂以理智而非直觉判断人，结论下得更笃定，并把曼弗雷德心中那团只是令人不安的模糊印象，说得轮廓分明：这姑娘在害怕！怕什么呢？莱昂纳闷。不是怕那位被她唤作母亲、矮胖自得的女人，也断然不是怕这位戴夹鼻眼镜、瘦脸的学究绅士。

冈萨雷斯介绍了维格洛博士（Dr. Viglow）；趁太太小姐们在楼上曼弗雷德的房里解斗篷，他得以从容形成自己的判断。他也无须费心招待客人——维格洛博士口若悬河，妙趣横生，而且一刻不停。

===Original===
“Our friend here plays a good game of golf,” he said, indicating Gonsalez, “a good game of golf indeed for a foreigner. You two are Spanish?”

Manfred nodded. He was more thoroughly English than the doctor, did that gentleman but know, but it was as a Spaniard and armed, moreover, with a Spanish passport that he was a visitor to Britain.

“I understood you to say that your investigations have taken rather a sensational turn, Doctor,” said Leon and a light came into Dr. Viglow’s eyes.

“Yes,” he said complacently, and then quickly, “who told you that?”

“You told me yourself at the club this morning.”

The doctor frowned.

===Chinese===
「我们这位朋友高尔夫打得不坏，」他指了指冈萨雷斯，「对外国人来说，实在打得不坏。你们二位是西班牙人？」

曼弗雷德点了点头。他其实比这位博士更是个地道的英国人——倘若那位绅士知道的话——只是他眼下以西班牙人的身份、手持西班牙护照，客居英国。

「我记得您说过，博士，您的研究有了个相当轰动的转向。」莱昂说。维格洛博士眼里顿时放光。

「是的。」他怡然自得地说，随即急忙追问，「这话是谁告诉你的？」

「今天早上在俱乐部，是您亲口告诉我的。」

博士皱起了眉。

===Original===
“Did I?” he said and passed his hand across his forehead. “I can’t recollect that. When was this?”

“This morning,” said Leon, “but your mind was probably occupied with much more important matters.”

The young professor bit his lip and frowned thoughtfully.

“I ought not to have forgotten what happened this morning,” he said in a troubled tone.

He gave the impression to Manfred that one half of him was struggling desperately to overcome a something in the other half. Suddenly he laughed.

“A sensational turn!” he said. “Yes indeed, and I rather think that within a few months I shall not be without fame, even in my own country! It is, of course, terribly expensive. I was only reckoning up today that my typists’ wages come to nearly £60 a week.”

===Chinese===
「是吗？」他说着，抬手抹了抹前额。「我记不起来了。那是什么时候的事？」

「今天早上，」莱昂说，「不过您的心思，八成正被重要得多的大事占着呢。」

年轻的教授咬着嘴唇，若有所思地皱起眉来。

「今天早上发生的事，我本不该忘记的。」他语气惶然地说。

他给曼弗雷德的印象是：此人的一半正在拼命挣扎，要制服另一半里的某种东西。突然，他笑了。

「轰动的转向！」他说，「是啊，千真万确！我看要不了几个月，我不愁没有名望，即便在本国也不例外！当然，这花费大得吓人。我今天刚算过一笔账，光打字员的工资每周就将近 60 英镑。」

===Original===
Manfred opened his eyes at this.

“Your typists’ wages?” he repeated slowly. “Are you preparing a book?”

“Here are the ladies,” said Dr. Felix.

His manner was abrupt to rudeness and later when they sat round the table in the little dining-room Manfred had further cause to wonder at the boorishness of this young scientist. He was seated next to Miss Moleneux and the meal was approaching its end when most unexpectedly he turned to the girl and in a loud voice said:

“You haven’t kissed me today, Margaret.”

The girl went red and white and the fingers that fidgeted with the tableware before her were trembling when she faltered:

===Chinese===
曼弗雷德闻言睁大了眼睛。

「打字员的工资？」他缓缓重复道，「您是在准备写书？」

「太太小姐们来了。」菲利克斯博士（Dr. Felix）说。

他的举止唐突得近乎无礼。随后众人在小餐厅里围桌而坐时，曼弗雷德对这位年轻科学家的粗鄙又添了一层诧异。他坐在莫利纽克斯小姐身旁；宴席将近尾声之际，他竟出其不意地转向姑娘，高声说道：

「你今天还没吻我呢，玛格丽特（Margaret）。」

姑娘的脸红一阵白一阵，摆弄着面前餐具的手指直打颤，她期期艾艾地说：

===Original===
“Haven’t﻿—haven’t I, Felix?”

The bright eyes of Gonsalez never left the doctor. The man’s face had gone purple with rage.

“By God! This is a nice thing!” he almost shouted. “I’m engaged to you. I’ve left you everything in my will and I’m allowing your mother a thousand a year and you haven’t kissed me today!”

“Doctor!” It was the mild but insistent voice of Gonsalez that broke the tension. “I wonder whether you would tell me what chemical is represented by the formula Cl2O5.”

The doctor had turned his head slowly at the sound of Leon’s voice and now was staring at him. Slowly the strange look passed from his face and it became normal.

“Cl2O5 is Oxide of Chlorine,” he said in an even voice, and from thenceforward the conversation passed by way of acid reactions into a scientific channel.

===Chinese===
「没——没吻过吗，菲利克斯？」

冈萨雷斯那双明亮的眼睛一瞬也不曾离开那博士。那人的脸已气得发紫。

「天杀的！这可真是一桩妙事！」他几乎是吼出来的。「我同你订了婚。我在遗嘱里把一切都留给了你，还许你母亲一年一千镑，你竟然今天还没吻我！」

「博士！」打破这紧张气氛的，是冈萨雷斯温和而不容分说的嗓音。「可否请您告诉我，化学式 Cl2O5 代表的是哪种化学物质？」

听得莱昂的声音，博士缓缓转过头来，此刻正直勾勾地盯着他。那怪异的神色慢慢从他脸上褪去，恢复了常态。

「Cl2O5 是氯氧化物。」他语调平缓地说。此后，话题便经由酸反应转入了科学的轨道。

===Original===
The only person at the table who had not been perturbed by Viglow’s outburst had been the dumpy complacent lady on Manfred’s right. She had tittered audibly at the reference to her allowance, and when the hum of conversation became general she lowered her voice and leant toward Manfred.

“Dear Felix is so eccentric,” she said, “but he is quite the nicest, kindest soul. One must look after one’s girls, don’t you agree, señor?”

She asked this latter question in very bad Spanish and Manfred nodded. He shot a glance at the girl. She was still deathly pale.

“And I am perfectly certain she will be happy, much happier than she would have been with that impossible person.”

She did not specify who the “impossible person” was, but Manfred sensed a whole world of tragedy. He was not romantic, but one look at the girl had convinced him that there was something wrong in this engagement. Now it was that he came to a conclusion which Leon had reached an hour before, that the emotion which dominated the girl was fear. And he pretty well knew of whom she was afraid.

Half an hour later when the tail light of Dr. Viglow’s limousine had disappeared round a corner of the drive the two men went back to the drawing-room and Manfred threw a handful of kindling to bring the fire to a blaze.

===Chinese===
席间唯一没有被维格洛这通发作搅得心神不宁的，是曼弗雷德右手边那位矮胖自得的女客。听到提及自己的津贴，她还出声地吃吃笑了；待众人的谈笑嗡然四起，她便压低嗓音，朝曼弗雷德倾过身来。

「亲爱的菲利克斯就是这么古怪，」她说，「可他实在是顶好、顶和善的一个人。自家姑娘总得有人照应，您说是不是，先生？」

这后半句是用极蹩脚的西班牙语问的。曼弗雷德点了点头。他朝姑娘飞快地瞥了一眼。她仍旧死一般苍白。

「而且我完全确信她会幸福的，比跟那个不可救药的人在一起，幸福得多。」

她没有点明那个「不可救药的人」是谁，但曼弗雷德嗅出了满满一世界的悲剧。他不是个浪漫的人，可只消看这姑娘一眼，他便确信这门亲事有些不对劲。此时，他得出了一个莱昂一小时前便已达成的结论：支配着这姑娘的情绪，是恐惧。而她怕的是谁，他心里也差不多有数了。

半小时后，维格洛博士轿车的尾灯绕过车道拐角消失不见，两人回到客厅，曼弗雷德投进一把引柴，把火烧得旺起来。

===Original===
“Well, what do you think?” said Gonsalez, rubbing his hands together with evidence of some enjoyment.

“I think it’s rather horrible,” replied Manfred, settling himself in his chair. “I thought the days when wicked mothers forced their daughters into unwholesome marriages were passed and done with. One hears so much about the modern girl.”

“Human nature isn’t modern,” said Gonsalez briskly, “and most mothers are fools where their daughters are concerned. I know you won’t agree but I speak with authority. Mantegazza collected statistics of 843 families﻿—”

Manfred chuckled.

“You and your Mantegazza!” he laughed. “Did that infernal man know everything?”

“Almost everything,” said Leon. “As to the girl,” he became suddenly grave. “She will not marry him of course.”

===Chinese===
「怎么样，你有何高见？」冈萨雷斯说，一面搓着双手，颇有几分乐在其中。

「我觉得这事相当可怕。」曼弗雷德在椅子里坐定，答道，「我还以为恶毒的母亲逼女儿嫁入不良姻缘的年代，早已过去、一了百了呢。如今『摩登女郎』的说法可听得太多了。」

「人性可不摩登，」冈萨雷斯干脆利落地说，「大凡涉及女儿，多数母亲都是蠢人。我知道你不会同意，但我此言有据。曼特加扎（Mantegazza）搜集过 843 个家庭的统计资料——」

曼弗雷德扑哧一笑。

「你呀，张口闭口你的曼特加扎！」他笑道，「那个可恶的家伙当真无所不知？」

「几乎无所不知。」莱昂说，「至于那姑娘，」他忽然正色道，「她当然不会嫁给他。」

===Original===
“What is the matter with him?” asked Manfred. “He seems to have an ungovernable temper.”

“He is mad,” replied Leon calmly and Manfred looked at him.

“Mad?” he repeated incredulously. “Do you mean to say that he is a lunatic?”

“I never use the word in a spectacular or even in a vulgar sense,” said Gonsalez, lighting a cigarette carefully. “The man is undoubtedly mad. I thought so a few days ago and I am certain of it now. The most ominous test is the test of memory. People who are on the verge of madness or entering its early stages do not remember what happened a short time before. Did you notice how worried he was when I told him of the conversation we had this morning?”

“That struck me as peculiar,” agreed Manfred.

“He was fighting,” said Leon, “the sane half of his brain against the insane half. The doctor against the irresponsible animal. The doctor told him that if he had suddenly lost his memory for incidents which had occurred only a few hours before, he was on the high way to lunacy. The crazy half of the brain told him that he was such a wonderful fellow that the rules applying to ordinary human beings did not apply to him. We will call upon him tomorrow to see his laboratory and discover why he is paying £60 a week for typists,” he said. “And now, my dear George, you can go to bed. I am going to read the excellent but often misguided Lombroso on the male delinquent.”

===Chinese===
「他有什么毛病？」曼弗雷德问，「他的脾气似乎暴得没法管。」

「他疯了。」莱昂平静地答道。曼弗雷德望着他。

「疯了？」他不肯相信地重复道，「你是说，他是个疯子？」

「我用这个词，从不取其耸人听闻、乃至流俗的意义。」冈萨雷斯说着，小心翼翼地点上一支烟，「这个人无疑疯了。几天前我便有此想，如今更是确信无疑。最不祥的检验，是记忆的检验。濒临疯狂、或初入疯狂之境的人，记不得片刻之前发生的事。你可曾留意，当我提起我们今早那番谈话时，他是何等惶然？」

「我也觉得此事蹊跷。」曼弗雷德深有同感。

「他方才是在交战，」莱昂说，「头脑里清醒的一半对抗疯狂的一半。博士对抗那不负责任的畜生。博士这一半告诫他：他若突然记不起区区几小时前发生的事，那他正走在通向疯人院的康庄大道上。疯狂的那一半则告诉他：他是个如此了不起的人物，约束凡夫俗子的规矩，于他一概不适用。明天我们去拜访他，参观他的实验室，弄明白他为何每周付给打字员 60 英镑。」他说，「好了，亲爱的乔治，你可以去睡了。我要去读隆布罗索（Lombroso）论男性罪犯的大作了——此人高明归高明，却常常误人。」

===Original===
Dr. Viglow’s laboratory was a new red building on the edge of Dartmoor. To be exact, it consisted of two buildings, one of which was a large army hut which had been recently erected for the accommodation of the doctor’s clerical staff.

“I haven’t met a professor for two or three years,” said Manfred as they were driving across the moor, en route to pay their call, “nor have I been in a laboratory for five. And yet within the space of a few weeks I have met two extraordinary professors, one of whom I admit was dead. Also I have visited two laboratories.”

Leon nodded.

“Some day I will make a very complete examination of the phenomena of coincidence,” he said.

When they reached the laboratory they found a post-office van, backed up against the main entrance, and three assistants in white overalls were carrying post bags and depositing them in the van.

“He must have a pretty large correspondence,” said Manfred in wonder.

===Chinese===
维格洛博士的实验室，是达特穆尔（Dartmoor）边上一幢崭新的红房子。确切地说，它由两座建筑构成，其中一座是新近搭建的大型军用棚屋，用来安置博士的文书人员。

「我已经两三年没见过教授了，」驱车穿越荒原、前去拜访的路上，曼弗雷德说，「也有五年没进过实验室了。可不出几个星期，我竟结识了两位非同寻常的教授——我承认，其中一位已经死了——还参观了两座实验室。」

莱昂点了点头。

「改天，我要对巧合现象作一番极其透彻的考察。」他说。

抵达实验室时，他们看见一辆邮车正车尾抵着正门，三名穿白色罩衣的助手正把一袋袋邮件往车上搬。

「他的书信往来想必大得惊人。」曼弗雷德讶异地说。
===Original===
The doctor, in a long white overall, was standing at the door as they alighted from their car, and greeted them warmly.

“Come into my office,” he said, and led the way to a large airy room which was singularly free from the paraphernalia which Gonsalez usually associated with such workrooms.

“You have a heavy post,” said Leon and the doctor laughed quietly.

“They are merely going to the Torquay post office,” he said. “I have arranged for them to be despatched when﻿—” he hesitated, “when I am sure. You see,” he said, speaking with great earnestness, “a scientist has to be so careful. Every minute after he has announced a discovery he is tortured with the fear that he has forgotten something, some essential, or has reached a too hasty conclusion. But I think I’m right,” he said, speaking half to himself. “I’m sure I’m right, but I must be even more sure!”

He showed them round the large room, but there was little which Manfred had not seen in the laboratory of the late Professor Tableman. Viglow had greeted them genially, indeed expansively, and yet within five minutes of their arrival he was taciturn, almost silent, and did not volunteer information about any of the instruments in which Leon showed so much interest, unless he was asked.

===Chinese===
他们下车时，博士穿一件白色长罩衣站在门口，热忱地迎接他们。

「到我的办公室来。」他说着，领路走进一间宽敞通风的屋子——这屋子竟出奇地没有那些冈萨雷斯惯常与此类工作室联想到一起的瓶瓶罐罐。

「您的邮件真不少。」莱昂说。博士淡淡一笑。

「它们不过是运往托基（Torquay）邮局。」他说，「我已安排妥当，等到——」他迟疑了一下，「等到我确信无误时再行投递。您瞧，」他极为恳切地说，「科学家不得不慎之又慎。宣布一项发现之后的每一分钟，他都在受煎熬，唯恐自己遗漏了什么、遗漏了某个要紧之处，或是下了过于仓促的结论。不过我想我是对的。」他半是自语地说，「我确信我是对的，但我必须更加确信！」

他领他们参观了那间大屋，但其中少有曼弗雷德在已故的泰布尔曼教授（Professor Tableman）实验室里不曾见过的东西。维格洛迎接他们时和蔼可亲，甚至热情洋溢；可他们到了还不到五分钟，他便沉默寡言起来，近乎一言不发。对莱昂极感兴趣的那些仪器，除非人家问起，他一句也不主动介绍。

===Original===
They came back to his room and again his mood changed and he became almost gay.

“I’ll tell you,” he said, “by Jove, I’ll tell you! And no living soul knows this except myself, or realises or understands the extraordinary work I have been doing.”

His face lit up, his eyes sparkled and it seemed to Manfred that he grew taller in this moment of exaltation. Pulling open a drawer of a table which stood against the wall he brought out a long porcelain plate and laid it down. From a wire-netted cupboard on the wall he took two tin boxes and with an expression of disgust which he could not disguise, turned the contents upon the slab. It was apparently a box full of common garden mould and then Leon saw to his amazement a wriggling little red shape twisting and twining in its acute discomfort. The little red fellow sought to hide himself and burrowed sinuously into the mould.

“Curse you! Curse you!” The doctor’s voice rose until it was a howl. His face was twisted and puckered in his mad rage. “How I hate you!”

If ever a man’s eyes held hate and terror, they were the eyes of Dr. Felix Viglow.

===Chinese===
他们回到他的房间，他的情绪又变了，变得近乎欢快。

「我告诉你们，」他说，「老天作证，我要告诉你们！这件事除了我自己，没有一个活人知晓；也没有人意识到、或理解我一直在从事的这项非凡工作。」

他容光焕发，双眼发亮，曼弗雷德只觉得他在这一阵亢奋之中都高大了不少。他拉开靠墙一张桌子的抽屉，取出一块长条瓷板放下；又从墙上蒙着铁丝网的柜子里取出两只铁皮盒，带着一副掩饰不住的嫌恶神情，把盒中之物倒在瓷板上。那看上去分明是一盒寻常的园土，随即莱昂惊愕地看到，一个小小的红色身影正在土里痛苦地扭动缠绞。那红色的小家伙想把自己藏起来，蜿蜒地钻进了土中。

「该死的！该死的！」博士的嗓门越抬越高，直到变成一声嚎叫。他的脸在疯狂的暴怒中扭曲皱缩。「我多么恨你！」

倘若说有谁的眼中盛着仇恨与恐怖，那便是菲利克斯·维格洛博士（Dr. Felix Viglow）的双眼。

===Original===
Manfred drew a long breath and stepped back a pace the better to observe him. Then the man calmed himself and peered down at Leon.

“When I was a child,” he said in a voice that shook, “I hated them and we had a nurse named Martha, a beastly woman, a wicked woman, who dropped one down my neck. Imagine the horror of it!”

Leon said nothing. To him the earthworm was a genus of chaetopod in the section *oligochaeta* and bore the somewhat, pretentious name of *lumbricus terrestris*. And in that way, Dr. Viglow, eminent naturalist and scientist, should have regarded this beneficent little fellow.

“I have a theory,” said the doctor. He was calmer now and was wiping the sweat from his forehead with a handkerchief, “that in cycles every type of living thing on the earth becomes in turn the dominant creature. In a million years’ time man may dwindle to the size of an ant and the earthworm, by its super-intelligence, its cunning and its ferocity, may be preeminent in the world! I have always thought that,” he went on when neither Leon nor Manfred offered any comment. “It is still my thought by day and my dream by night. I have devoted my life to the destruction of this menace.”

Now the earthworm is neither cunning nor intelligent and is moreover notoriously devoid of ambition.

===Chinese===
曼弗雷德长吸一口气，退后一步，好把他瞧个真切。随后那人平静下来，低头打量莱昂。

「我还是个孩子的时候，」他声音发颤地说，「就恨它们。我们家有个保姆，叫玛莎（Martha），一个下流的女人，一个恶毒的女人，她把一条蚯蚓塞进了我的后领。想想那有多可怕！」

莱昂一言不发。在他看来，蚯蚓不过是寡毛纲（*oligochaeta*）之下一属的毛足虫，顶着一个略显自命的学名 *lumbricus terrestris*（陆正蚓）。以这样的眼光看待这个有益的小生灵，本是维格洛博士——杰出的博物学家与科学家——分内应有之义。

「我有一种理论，」博士说。他此刻平静了些，正用手帕拭去前额的汗，「地球上的每一种生物，都会周期性地轮番成为主宰。再过一百万年，人类也许会缩得同蚂蚁一般大小，而蚯蚓凭其超等的智能、狡诈与凶残，或将称雄世界！我一向作此想。」见莱昂和曼弗雷德都不置一词，他继续说道，「这至今仍是我白昼之所思、夜间之所梦。我已将毕生奉献于铲除这一祸害。」

然而蚯蚓既不狡诈，也不聪明，更出了名地胸无大志。

===Original===
The doctor again went to the cupboard and took out a wide-necked bottle filled with a greyish powder. He brought it back and held it within a few inches of Leon’s face.

“This is the work of twelve years,” he said simply. “There is no difficulty in finding a substance which will kill these pests, but this does more.”

He took a scalpel and tilting the bottle brought out a few grains of the powder on the edge of it. This he dissolved in a twenty-ounce measure which he filled with water. He stirred the colourless fluid with a glass rod, then lifting the rod he allowed three drops to fall upon the mould wherein the little creature was hidden. A few seconds passed, there was a heaving of the earth where the victim was concealed.

“He is dead,” said the doctor triumphantly and scraped away the earth to prove the truth of his words. “And he is not only dead, but that handful of earth is death to any other earthworm that touches it.”

He rang a bell and one of his attendants came in.

“Clear away that,” he said with a shudder and walked gloomily to his desk.

===Chinese===
博士又走到柜子前，取出一只盛满灰色粉末的广口瓶，拿回来举到离莱昂的脸只有几英寸的地方。

「这是十二年的心血。」他淡淡地说，「要找一种能杀死这些害虫的东西并不难，可这一种的本事远不止于此。」

他取过一把解剖刀，倾斜瓶身，用刀刃带出几粒粉末，倒进一只注了水的二十盎司量杯里溶开。他用玻璃棒搅动那无色的液体，随后举起玻璃棒，让三滴溶液滴在藏着那小生灵的泥土上。几秒钟过去，那受害者藏身之处的泥土翻涌起来。

「它死了。」博士得意扬扬地说，随手扒开泥土，以证实自己的话不虚。「它不单死了，那一小撮土也成了毒土，别的蚯蚓谁碰谁死。」

他摇了摇铃，一名侍从走了进来。

「把它收走。」他打了个寒噤说，随即郁郁地走向自己的书桌。

===Original===
Leon did not speak all the way back to the house. He sat curled up in the corner of the car, his arms lightly folded, his chin on his breast. That night without a word of explanation he left the house, declining Manfred’s suggestion that he should walk with him and volunteering no information as to where he was going.

Gonsalez walked by the cliff road, across Babbacombe Downs and came to the doctor’s house at nine o’clock that night. The doctor had a large house and maintained a big staff of servants, but amongst his other eccentricities was the choice of a gardener’s cottage away from the house as his sleeping place at night.

It was only lately that the doctor had chosen this lonely lodging. He had been happy enough in the big old house which had been his father’s, until he had heard voices whispering to him at night and the creak of boards and had seen shapes vanishing along the dark corridors, and then in his madness he had conceived the idea that his servants were conspiring against him and that he might any night be murdered in his bed. So he had the gardener turned out of his cottage, had refurnished the little house, and there, behind locked doors, he read and thought and slept the nights away. Gonsalez had heard of this peculiarity and approached the cottage with some caution, for a frightened man is more dangerous than a wicked man. He rapped at the door and heard a step across the flagged floor.

“Who is that?” asked a voice.

“It is I,” said Gonsalez and gave the name by which he was known.

After hesitation the lock turned and the door opened.

===Chinese===
回程一路上，莱昂一言不发。他蜷坐在车厢角落里，双臂轻拢，下巴抵在胸前。当夜，他一句解释也没有便出了门：曼弗雷德提议陪他走走，被他婉拒；至于去哪儿，他一个字也不肯吐露。

冈萨雷斯沿崖路步行，穿过巴巴科姆高地（Babbacombe Downs），当晚九点钟来到博士的宅邸。博士的宅子很大，仆役成群，但他的种种怪癖之一，便是放着大宅不住，偏挑了主宅之外的一座园丁小屋作夜间寝处。

博士选定这处冷清的居所，还是不久前的事。他原本在父亲传下的老宅里住得安安适适，直到夜里听见有声音向他窃窃低语，听见地板吱呀作响，看见人影沿黑暗的走廊倏然隐没；于是在疯病里，他认定仆人们正密谋对付他，说不定哪天夜里就会让他死在床上。于是他撵走了园丁，把小屋重新布置一番，从此在反锁的门后读书、思索、一觉睡到天亮。冈萨雷斯听说过这个怪癖，走近小屋时便加了些小心——一个吓破了胆的人，比一个恶人更危险。他敲了敲门，听见石板地上响起一阵脚步。

「是谁？」门里一个声音问。

「是我。」冈萨雷斯说，报上了自己在此地为人所知的名字。

迟疑片刻，锁一转，门开了。

===Original===
“Come in, come in,” said Viglow testily and locked the door behind him. “You have come to congratulate me, I am sure. You must come to my wedding too, my friend. It will be a wonderful wedding, for there I shall make a speech and tell the story of my discovery. Will you have a drink? I have nothing here, but I can get it from the house. I have a telephone in my bedroom.”

Leon shook his head.

“I have been rather puzzling out your plan, Doctor,” he said, accepting the proffered cigarette, “and I have been trying to connect those postal bags which I saw being loaded at the door of your laboratory with the discovery which you revealed this afternoon.”

Dr. Viglow’s narrow eyes were gleaming with merriment and he leant back in his chair and crossed his legs, like one preparing for a pleasant recital.

“I will tell you,” he said. “For months I have been in correspondence with farming associations, both here and on the Continent. I have something of a European reputation,” he said, with that extraordinary immodesty which Leon had noticed before. “In fact, I think that my treatment for phylloxera did more to remove the scourge from the vineyards of Europe than any other preparation.”

Leon nodded. He knew this to be the truth.

===Chinese===
「进来，进来。」维格洛没好气地说，随手在身后把门锁上。「你准是来道贺的。我的朋友，我的婚礼你也务必到场。那将是场盛大的婚礼，因为我要在婚礼上发表演说，讲出我发现的故事。来一杯吗？我这儿什么都没有，不过可以打发人到大宅去取。我卧室里装了一部电话。」

莱昂摇了摇头。

「我一直在费神琢磨您的计划，博士，」他接过递来的香烟，说，「也一直想把我在您实验室门口看着装车的那些邮袋，同您今天下午亮出的那项发现，牵到一块儿。」

维格洛博士细窄的眼睛里闪着愉悦的光，他往椅背上一靠，跷起二郎腿，活像个正待娓娓道来的说书人。

「我这就告诉你。」他说，「几个月来，我一直在同国内及欧洲大陆的农业协会通信。我在欧洲也算薄有声名。」他说这话时，带着莱昂先前便领教过的那种异乎寻常的自负。「说实在的，我认为欧洲葡萄园的根瘤蚜之祸得以扫除，我的药剂功劳最大，别的方子谁也比不上。」

莱昂点了点头。他知道这是实情。

===Original===
“So you see, my word is accepted in matters dealing with agriculture. But I found after one or two talks with our own stupid farmers that there is an unusual prejudice against destroying”﻿—he did not mention the dreaded name but shivered﻿—“and that of course I had to get round. Now that I am satisfied that my preparation is exact, I can release the packets in the post office. In fact, I was just about to telephone to the postmaster telling him that they could go off﻿—they are all stamped and addressed﻿—when you knocked at the door.”

“To whom are they addressed?” asked Leon steadily.

“To various farmers﻿—some fourteen thousand in all in various parts of the country and Europe, and each packet has printed instructions in English, French, German and Spanish. I had to tell them that it was a new kind of fertiliser or they may not have been as enthusiastic in the furtherance of my experiment as I am.”

“And what are they going to do with these packets when they get them?” asked Leon quietly.

“They will dissolve them and spray a certain area of their land﻿—I suggested ploughed land. They need only treat a limited area of earth,” he explained. “I think these wretched beasts will carry infection quickly enough. I believe,” he leant forward and spoke impressively, “that in six months there will not be one living in Europe or Asia.”

“They do not know that the poison is intended to kill﻿—earthworms?” asked Leon.

===Chinese===
「所以你明白，在农业事务上，我的话是作数的。可我跟我们自己那些蠢农夫谈过一两次之后便发现，人们对灭除——」他没有说出那个令他胆寒的名字，只打了个寒噤，「抱有一种异乎寻常的偏见，这当然得设法绕过去。如今我既已确信我的药剂精确无误，便可以把那些包裹投放邮局了。说真的，你敲门的时候，我正要打电话通知邮政局长放行呢——包裹全都贴好邮票、写好地址了。」

「包裹都寄给谁？」莱昂不动声色地问。

「寄给四面八方的农夫——本国与欧洲各地，总共约一万四千人——每个包裹里都附有英文、法文、德文和西班牙文印刷的说明书。我只得告诉他们这是一种新型肥料，否则，他们对我这项实验的热心劲儿，未必赶得上我。」

「他们收到包裹之后，打算怎么处置？」莱昂轻声问。

「他们会把药剂溶开，喷洒在自家土地的一定区域——我建议用犁过的地。他们只需处理有限的一小块土地。」他解释道，「我想这些可恶的畜生会把疫病传得足够快。我相信，」他身子前倾，语气郑重，「不出六个月，欧亚两洲便再无一条活口。」

「他们并不知道，那毒药是用来杀——蚯蚓的？」莱昂问。

===Original===
“No, I’ve told you,” snapped the other. “Wait, I will telephone the postmaster.”

He rose quickly to his feet, but Leon was quicker and gripped him arm.

“My dear friend,” he said, “you must not do this.”

Dr. Viglow tried to withdraw his arm.

“Let me go,” he snarled. “Are you one of those devils who are trying to torment me?”

In ordinary circumstances, Leon would have been strong enough to hold the man, but Viglow’s strength was extraordinary and Gonsalez found himself thrust back into the chair. Before he could spring up, the man had passed through the door and slammed and locked it behind him.

===Chinese===
「不知道，我不是说过了么。」对方厉声道，「等等，我这就给邮政局长打电话。」

他霍地站起，莱昂却更快，一把攥住他的手臂。

「我亲爱的朋友，」他说，「这件事，您千万做不得。」

维格洛博士想抽回手臂。

「放开我。」他咬牙切齿地说，「你也是那些想折磨我的魔鬼中的一员？」

换作寻常时候，莱昂的力气足以制住此人，但维格洛的膂力非凡，冈萨雷斯竟被推得跌坐回椅子里。不等他跳起身，那人已夺门而出，砰地摔上门，反锁了。

===Original===
The cottage was on one floor and was divided into two rooms by a wooden partition which Viglow had erected. Over the door was a fanlight, and pulling the table forward Leon sprang on to the top and with his elbow smashed the flimsy frame.

“Don’t touch that telephone,” he said sternly. “Do you hear?”

The doctor looked round with a grin. “You are a friend of those devils!” he said, and his hand was on the receiver when Leon shot him dead.

---

===Chinese===
小屋只有一层，被维格洛立起的一道木隔断隔成两间。门上方开着一扇气窗，莱昂把桌子拖到门前，一跃而上，用胳膊肘击碎了那单薄的窗框。

「不许碰那部电话。」他厉声说，「听见没有？」

博士咧嘴回头一笑。「原来你是那些魔鬼的同伙！」他说。他的手刚搭上听筒，莱昂便一枪要了他的命。

---

===Original===
Manfred came back the next morning from his walk and found Gonsalez pacing the lawn, smoking an extra long cigar.

“My dear Leon,” said Manfred as he slipped his arm in the other’s. “You did not tell me.”

“I thought it best to wait,” said Leon.

“I heard quite by accident,” Manfred went on. “The story is that a burglar broke into the cottage and shot the doctor when he was telephoning for assistance. All the silverware in the outer room has been stolen. The doctor’s watch and pocketbook have disappeared.”

“They are at this moment at the bottom of Babbacombe Bay,” said Leon. “I went fishing very early this morning before you were awake.”

They paced the lawn in silence for a while and then:

===Chinese===
第二天早晨，曼弗雷德散步归来，见冈萨雷斯正在草坪上踱步，嘴里抽着一支特长的雪茄。

「亲爱的莱昂，」曼弗雷德挽起同伴的手臂说，「你可没有告诉我。」

「我想还是等等再说为好。」莱昂说。

「我纯属碰巧听说的。」曼弗雷德接着说，「外面的说法是：一个窃贼闯进小屋，趁博士打电话求救时开枪打死了他。外间的银器尽数被盗，博士的怀表和皮夹也不翼而飞。」

「它们此刻正躺在巴巴科姆湾（Babbacombe Bay）的水底。」莱昂说，「今天一大早，趁你还没醒，我去钓了阵鱼。」

两人在草坪上默默踱了片刻，随后——

===Original===
“Was it necessary?” asked Manfred.

“Very necessary,” said Leon gravely. “You have to realise first of all that although this man was mad, he had discovered not only a poison but an infection.”

“But, my dear fellow,” smiled Manfred, “was an earthworm worth it?”

“Worth more than his death,” said Leon. “There isn’t a scientist in the world who does not agree that if the earthworm was destroyed the world would become sterile and the people of this world would be starving in seven years.”

Manfred stopped in his walk and stared down at his companion.

===Chinese===
「有这个必要吗？」曼弗雷德问。

「非常有必要。」莱昂正色道，「首先你必须认识到：这个人虽然疯了，他发现的可不只是一种毒药，而是一种传染。」

「可是，亲爱的朋友，」曼弗雷德微笑道，「为几条蚯蚓，值得吗？」

「比他的死有价值得多。」莱昂说，「天底下的科学家，没有一个不认同：蚯蚓一旦灭尽，世界将变得贫瘠，不出七年，世上的人便要挨饿。」

曼弗雷德停下脚步，低头凝视着同伴。

===Original===
“Do you really mean that?”

Leon nodded.

“He is the one necessary creature in God’s world,” he said soberly. “It fertilises the land and covers the bare rocks with earth. It is the surest friend of mankind that we know, and now I am going down to the post office with a story which I think will be sufficiently plausible to recover those worm poisoners.”

Manfred mused a while, then he said:

“I’m glad in many ways﻿—in every way,” he corrected. “I rather liked that girl, and I’m sure that impossible person isn’t so impossible.”

===Chinese===
「你这话是当真的？」

莱昂点了点头。

「它是上帝的世界里独一无二、不可或缺的造物。」他郑重地说，「它使土地肥沃，给光秃的岩石披上泥土。它是我们所知的人类最可靠的朋友。现在我要去一趟邮局，带上我认为足够可信的一套说辞，把那些灭蚓的毒药追回来。」

曼弗雷德沉吟半晌，说道：

「我在许多方面都感到庆幸——应该说，在一切方面。」他更正道，「我相当喜欢那姑娘，而且我敢肯定，那个不可救药的人，并不那么不可救药。」

## The Man Who Died Twice / 死过两次的人

===Original===

The interval between Acts II and III was an unusually long one, and the three men who sat in the stage box were in such harmony of mind that none of them felt the necessity for making conversation. The piece was a conventional crook play and each of the three had solved the “mystery” of the murder before the drop fell on the first act. They had reached the same solution (and the right one) without any great mental effort.

Fare, the Police Commissioner, had dined with George Manfred and Leon Gonsalez (he addressed them respectively as “Señor Fuentes” and “Señor Mandrelino” and did not doubt that they were natives of Spain, despite their faultless English) and the party had come on to the theatre.

Mr. Fare frowned as at some unpleasant memory and heard a soft laugh. Looking up, he met the dancing eyes of Leon.

“Why do you laugh?” he asked, half smiling in sympathy.

===Chinese===

第二幕与第三幕之间的幕间格外漫长，坐在舞台包厢里的三个人心意相通到这般地步，谁也不觉得有开口交谈的必要。演的是一出老套的犯罪戏，还没等第一幕落幕，三人便都已破解了那桩谋杀的“谜底”。他们不费什么脑筋，便得出了同一个答案（而且是正确的那个）。

警务总监费尔（Fare）同乔治·曼弗雷德（George Manfred）和莱昂·冈萨雷斯（Leon Gonsalez）共进了晚餐——他分别管这两位叫「富恩特斯先生」（Señor Fuentes）和「曼德雷利诺先生」（Señor Mandrelino），而且尽管两人说着一口无懈可击的英语，他也从不怀疑他们是西班牙本乡本土的人——饭后一行人便来了剧院。

费尔先生皱起眉头，像是想起了什么不愉快的往事，接着便听见一声轻轻的笑。他抬起眼，正遇上莱昂那双神采飞动的眼睛。

“你笑什么？”他问道，感同身受似的也漾出几分笑意。

===Original===

“At your thoughts,” replied the calm Gonsalez.

“At my thoughts!” repeated the other, startled,

“Yes,” Leon nodded, “you were thinking of the Four Just Men.”

“Extraordinary!” exclaimed Fare.

“It is perfectly true. What is it, telepathy?”

Gonsalez shook his head. As to Manfred, he was gazing abstractedly into the stalls.

===Chinese===

“笑你的心事。”泰然自若的冈萨雷斯答道。

“笑我的心事！”对方愕然，跟着重复了一遍。

“没错，”莱昂点头道，“你刚才正在想四义士。”

“神了！”费尔叫出声来。

“千真万确。这是什么道理，心灵感应么？”

冈萨雷斯摇了摇头。至于曼弗雷德，他正心不在焉地望着正厅前排出神。

===Original===

“No, it was not telepathy,” said Leon, “it was your facial expression.”

“But I haven’t mentioned those rascals, how﻿—”

“Facial expression,” said Leon, revelling in his pet topic, “especially an expression of the emotions, comes into the category of primitive instincts﻿—they are not ‘willed.’ For example, when a billiard player strikes a ball he throws and twists his body after the ball﻿—you must have seen the contortions of a player who has missed his shot by a narrow margin? A man using scissors works his jaw, a rower moves his lips with every stroke of the oar. These are what we call ‘automatisms.’ Animals have these characteristics. A hungry dog approaching meat pricks his ears in the direction of his meal﻿—”

“Is there a particular act of automatism produced by the thought of the Four Just Men?” asked the Commissioner, smiling.

Leon nodded.

“It would take long to describe, but I will not deceive you. I less read than guessed your thoughts by following them. The last line in the last act we saw was uttered by a ridiculous stage parson who says: ‘Justice! There is a justice beyond the law!’ And I saw you frown. And then you looked across the stalls and nodded to the editor of the *Megaphone*. And I remembered that you had written an article on the Four Just Men for that journal﻿—”

===Chinese===

“不，这不是心灵感应，”莱昂说，“是你的面部表情。”

“可我一个字也没提过那帮恶棍，怎么——”

“面部表情，”莱昂说，对自己的拿手题目津津乐道，“尤其是情绪的表达，属于原始本能一类——它不受‘意志’支配。比方说，台球手击出一球，身子会追着球又倾又拧——差之毫厘没打准的球手会扭成什么怪相，你总见识过吧？使剪刀的人下巴会跟着使劲，划船的人每划一桨嘴唇都要嗫动。这些便是我们所谓的‘自动化动作’。动物也有这类特性：饿狗朝肉走去时，耳朵便朝吃食的方向竖起来——”

“那么，一想到四义士，会不会引出某种特定的自动化动作？”总监含笑问道。

莱昂点了点头。

“细说起来话长，但我不想瞒你。我对你的心思，与其说是读出来的，不如说是循着痕迹猜出来的。方才那最后一幕的收场台词，出自一个可笑的舞台牧师之口：‘正义！法律之外另有正义！’我看见你皱了眉。随后你朝正厅前排那边望过去，朝《传声筒报》（*Megaphone*）的主编点了点头。我又记起你曾给那家报纸写过一篇论四义士的文章——”

===Original===

“A little biography on poor Falmouth who died the other day,” corrected Fare. “Yes, yes, I see. You were right, of course. I was thinking of them and their pretensions to act as judges and executioners when the law fails to punish the guilty, or rather the guilty succeed in avoiding conviction.”

Manfred turned suddenly.

“Leon,” he spoke in Spanish, in which language the three had been conversing off and on during the evening. “View the cavalier with the diamond in his shirt﻿—what do you make of him?” The question was in English.

Leon raised his powerful opera glasses and surveyed the man whom his friend had indicated.

“I should like to hear him speak,” he said after a while. “See how delicate his face is and how powerful are his jaws﻿—almost prognathic, for the upper maxilla is distinctly arrested. Regard him, señor, and tell me if you do not agree that his eyes are unusually bright?”

Manfred took the glasses and looked at the unconscious man.

===Chinese===

“不过是替前些日子去世的可怜的法尔茅斯立了篇小传罢了。”费尔更正道，“对，对，我明白了。当然，你猜得没错。我正在想他们，想他们那副自任法官兼行刑手的做派——每逢法律未能惩治罪人，或者不如说，罪人侥幸逃脱了定罪之时，他们便要越俎代庖。”

曼弗雷德倏地转过身来。

“莱昂，”他用西班牙语开了口——这一晚三人时不时便用这种语言交谈。“瞧那位衬衫上缀着钻石的阔公子——你看他是什么路数？”这句问话却是用英语说的。

莱昂举起他那副倍数极高的观剧镜，打量起朋友指给他看的那个人。

“我倒想听听他说话。”过了片刻他说，“瞧他这张脸何等细嫩，牙关又何等有力——几乎到了突颌的程度，因为上颌骨明显发育不足。您端详端详他，先生，说说您是否同意，他的眼睛亮得异乎寻常？”

曼弗雷德接过观剧镜，端详起那个浑然不觉的对象。

===Original===

“They are swollen﻿—yes, I see they are bright.”

“What else do you see?”

“The lips are large and a little swollen too, I think,” said Manfred.

Leon took the glasses and turned to the Commissioner.

“I do not bet, but if I did I would wager a thousand pesetas that this man speaks with a harsh cracked voice.”

Fare looked from his companion to the object of their scrutiny and then back to Leon.

===Chinese===

“眼睛是浮肿的——唔，我看得出是发亮。”

“你还看出什么了？”

“嘴唇厚，我想也有些肿。”曼弗雷德说。

莱昂收回观剧镜，转向总监。

“我从不赌钱，可若是要赌，我愿押上一千比塞塔，赌这个人说话嗓音沙哑破裂。”

费尔的目光从同伴身上移到他们端详的那个人身上，又移回莱昂脸上。

===Original===

“You are perfectly right,” he said quietly. “His name is Ballam and his voice is extraordinarily rough and harsh. What is he?”

“Vicious,” replied Gonsalez. “My dear friend, that man is vicious, a bad man. Beware of the bright eyes and the cracked voice, señor! They stand for evil!”

Fare rubbed his nose irritably, a trick of his.

“If you were anybody else I should be very rude and say that you knew him or had met him,” he said, “but after your extraordinary demonstration the other day I realise there must be something in physiognomy.”

He referred to a visit which Leon Gonsalez and Manfred had paid to the record department of Scotland Yard. There, with forty photographs of criminals spread upon the table before him Gonsalez, taking them in order, had enumerated the crimes with which their names were associated. He only made four errors and even they were very excusable.

“Yes, Gregory Ballam is a pretty bad lot,” said the Commissioner thoughtfully. “He has never been through our hands, but that is the luck of the game. He’s as shrewd as the devil and it hurts me to see him with a nice girl like Genee Maggiore.”

===Chinese===

“你说得分毫不差。”他轻声说，“他名叫巴拉姆（Ballam），嗓音粗厉得出奇。此人如何？”

“邪恶。”冈萨雷斯答道，“我亲爱的朋友，那是个恶徒，坏种一个。当心那双亮眼睛和那副哑嗓子，先生！它们是罪恶的标志！”

费尔恼火地揉了揉鼻子——这是他的老习惯。

“换个人说这话，我就要不客气地顶回去，说他准是认识此人，或是打过照面。”他说，“可自从那天你那场惊人的表演之后，我算明白了，观相术这一套必有几分道理。”

他指的是莱昂·冈萨雷斯和曼弗雷德前些日子对苏格兰场档案部的一次造访。当时桌上摊着四十张罪犯照片，冈萨雷斯挨张看过，一一数说出与这些名字相关联的罪名。他只错了四处，而且那四处也还情有可原。

“是啊，格雷戈里·巴拉姆（Gregory Ballam）可不是什么好东西。”总监若有所思地说，“他从没落到我们手里，不过那只是他手气好。此人精得像魔鬼，眼看他和吉妮·马焦雷（Genee Maggiore）这样乖巧的姑娘搅在一处，真叫我痛心。”

===Original===

“The girl who is sitting with him?” asked Manfred, interested.

“An actress,” murmured Gonsalez. “You observe, my dear George, how she turns her head first to the left and then to the right at intervals, though there is no attraction in either direction. She has the habit of being seen﻿—it is not vanity, it is merely a peculiar symptom of her profession.”

“What is his favourite vanity?” asked Manfred and the Commissioner smiled. “You know our Dickens, eh?” he asked, for he thought of Manfred as a Spaniard. “Well, it would be difficult to tell you what Gregory Ballam does to earn his respectable income,” he said more seriously. “I think he is connected with a moneylender’s business and runs a few profitable sidelines.”

“Such as﻿—” suggested Manfred.

Mr. Fare was not, apparently, anxious to commit himself. “I’ll tell you in the strictest confidence,” he said. “We believe, and have good cause to believe, that he has a hop joint which is frequented by wealthy people. Did you read last week about the man, John Bidworth, who shot a nursemaid in Kensington Gardens and then shot himself?”

Manfred nodded.

===Chinese===

“就是坐在他身边的那个姑娘？”曼弗雷德来了兴致。

“是个演员。”冈萨雷斯喃喃道，“你注意到了吗，我亲爱的乔治，她每隔一会儿就把头先转向左、再转向右，可两边并没有什么值得看的。她这是落下了‘给人看’的习惯——不是虚荣，不过是她那一行的特殊症候罢了。”

“他最得意的虚荣又是什么？”曼弗雷德问，总监不禁莞尔。“你读过我们的狄更斯，嗯？”他问，因为他一直把曼弗雷德当成西班牙人。“唉，格雷戈里·巴拉姆靠什么挣他那份体面的收入，还真是难说清楚。”他的口气郑重起来，“我想他挂靠着一项放贷的生意，另外还经营几样有利可图的副业。”

“比方说——”曼弗雷德提示道。

费尔先生显然不急着把话说死。“我只告诉你一人，你知我知。”他说，“我们有理由相信——很好的理由——他开着一处有钱人常去的鸦片馆。上礼拜那个在肯辛顿公园（Kensington Gardens）开枪打死保姆、随后自戕的人，约翰·比德沃思（John Bidworth），你读到过么？”

曼弗雷德点了点头。

===Original===

“He was quite a well-connected person, wasn’t he?” he asked.

“He was very well connected,” replied Fare emphatically. “So well connected that we did not want to bring his people into the case at all. He died the next day in hospital and the surgeons tell us that he was undoubtedly under the influence of some Indian drug and that in his few moments of consciousness he as much as told the surgeon in charge of the case that he had been on a jag the night before and had finished up in what he called an opium house, and remembered nothing further till he woke up in the hospital. He died without knowing that he had committed this atrocious crime. There is no doubt that under the maddening influence of the drug he shot the first person he saw.”

“Was it Mr. Ballam’s opium house?” asked Gonsalez, interested.

The curtain rose at that moment and conversation went on in a whisper.

“We don’t know﻿—in his delirium he mentioned Ballam’s name. We have tried our best to find out. He has been watched. Places at which he has stayed any length of time have been visited, but we have found nothing to incriminate him.”

Leon Gonsalez had a favourite hour and a favourite meal at which he was at his brightest. That hour was at nine o’clock in the morning and the meal was breakfast. He put down his paper the next morning and asked:

===Chinese===

“那人可是个门路很硬的人物，对吧？”他问。

“岂止门路硬。”费尔加重语气答道，“硬到我们压根不想把他的家里人牵进案子。他第二天就死在了医院里，外科医生告诉我们，他当时毫无疑问中了某种印度毒药的劲儿，在神志清醒的短短片刻里，他差不多等于告诉了主治医生：头天夜里他烂醉了一场，最后到了一家他自己所谓的鸦片馆，之后直到在医院里醒来，便一概不记得了。他到死也不知道自己犯下过这桩骇人的罪行。毫无疑问，他在药物致狂的作用下，见到头一个人便开了枪。”

“那是巴拉姆先生的鸦片馆么？”冈萨雷斯关切地问。

恰在此时幕启了，谈话改成了耳语。

“我们不得而知——他谵妄之中提过巴拉姆的名字。我们已竭力查证。此人被监视过，他久留之处也都查访遍了，可始终没找到任何能给他定罪的东西。”

莱昂·冈萨雷斯有一个最得意的时辰和一顿最得意的饭食，每逢其时他的才思最为焕发。那便是早晨九点钟和早餐。第二天早晨，他放下报纸，问道：

===Original===

“What is crime?”

“Professor,” said Manfred solemnly, “I will tell you. It is the departure from the set rules which govern human society.”

“You are conventional,” said Gonsalez. “My dear George, you are always conventional at nine o’clock in the morning! Now, had I asked you at midnight you would have told me that it is any act which wilfully offends and discomforts your neighbour. If I desired to give it a narrow and what they call in this country a legal interpretation I would add, ‘contrary to the law.’ There must be ten thousand crimes committed for every one detected. People associated crime only with those offences which are committed by a certain type of illiterate or semi-illiterate lunatic or half-lunatic, glibly dubbed a ‘criminal.’ Now, here is a villainous crime, a monumental crime. He is a man who is destroying the souls of youth and breaking hearts ruthlessly! Here is one who is dragging down men and women from the upward road and debasing them in their own eyes, slaying ambition and all beauty of soul and mind in order that he should live in a certain comfort, wearing a clean dress shirt every evening of his life and drinking expensive and unnecessary wines with his expensive and indigestible dinner.”

“Where is this man?” asked Manfred.

“He lives at 993 Jermyn Street, in fact he is a neighbour,” said Leon.

===Chinese===

“什么是犯罪？”

“教授，”曼弗雷德一本正经地说，“我来告诉你。犯罪就是背离了维系人类社会的既定规则。”

“你可真是恪守成规。”冈萨雷斯说，“我亲爱的乔治，一到早晨九点钟，你总免不了恪守成规！倘若我在半夜里问你，你就会告诉我：凡存心冒犯、搅扰旁人的行为皆是犯罪。假如我想给它下一个狭义的、也就是此地所谓的法律解释，我还得补上一句：‘违犯法律。’每查获一桩罪行，必有上万桩已然犯下。世人一想到犯罪，想到的只是某一类不识字或半识字的疯子、半疯子所犯的过犯，顺口给他们扣一顶‘罪犯’的帽子。可是你瞧，眼前就有一桩穷凶极恶的罪，一桩登峰造极的罪。有这么一个人，正在摧残青年的灵魂，冷酷地碎人心肠！有这么一个人，正把男男女女从向上的路上拖拽下来，叫他们自轻自贱，扼杀他们的雄心和灵魂心智的一切美好，只为他自己过得某种安适——一辈子每晚穿干净的礼服衬衫，就着那顿昂贵而难消化的晚饭，喝昂贵而无必要的美酒。”

“此人在哪里？”曼弗雷德问。

“他住在杰明街 993 号，说起来还是位邻居。”莱昂说。

===Original===

“You’re speaking of Mr. Ballam?”

“I’m speaking of Mr. Ballam,” said Gonsalez gravely. “Tonight I am going to be a foreign artist with large rolls of money in my pockets and an irresistible desire to be amused. I do not doubt that sooner or later Mr. Ballam and I will gravitate together. Do I look like a detective, George?” he asked abruptly.

“You look more like a successful pianist,” said George and Gonsalez sniffed.

“You can even be offensive at nine o’clock in the morning,” he said.

There are two risks which criminals face (with due respect to the opinions of Leon Gonsalez, this word criminal is employed by the narrator) in the pursuit of easy wealth. There is the risk of detection and punishment which applies to the big as well as to the little delinquent. There is the risk of losing large sums of money invested for the purpose of securing even larger sums. The criminal who puts money in his business runs the least risk of detection. That is why only the poor and foolish come stumbling up the stairs which lead to the dock at the Old Bailey, and that is why the big men, who would be indignant at the very suggestion that they were in the category of lawbreakers, seldom or never make their little bow to the Judge.

Mr. Gregory Ballam stood for and represented certain moneyed interests which had purchased at auction three houses in Montague Street, Portland Place. They were three houses which occupied an island site. The first of these was let out in offices, the ground floor being occupied by a lawyer, the first floor by a wine and spirit merchant, the second being a very plain suite, dedicated to the business hours of Mr. Gregory Ballam. This gentleman also rented the cellar, which by the aid of lime-wash and distemper had been converted into, if not a pleasant, at any rate a neat and cleanly storage place. Through this cellar you could reach (amongst other places) a brand-new garage, which had been built for one of Mr. Ballam’s partners, but in which Mr. Ballam was not interested at all.

===Chinese===

“你说的是巴拉姆先生？”

“我说的是巴拉姆先生。”冈萨雷斯正色道，“今夜我要扮一个外国艺术家，兜里揣着大卷钞票，怀着一股按捺不住的寻欢之兴。我不怀疑，迟早巴拉姆先生和我自会相互吸引到一处。我看上去像侦探么，乔治？”他猝然问道。

“你更像一位功成名就的钢琴家。”乔治说。冈萨雷斯嗤了一声。

“你居然也能在早晨九点钟出口伤人。”他说。

世上的罪犯在追逐不义之财时，要面对两种风险（尽管对莱昂·冈萨雷斯的高见未敢怠慢，“罪犯”一词乃是叙述者所用的）。一种是被侦破、受惩治的风险，大案犯小案犯一体适用。另一种是折损大笔本钱的风险——那笔钱原是为套取更大的数目才投下去的。肯往自己“生意”里砸钱的罪犯，被侦破的风险最小。正因如此，只有穷人和蠢货才会跌跌撞撞地登上老贝利（the Old Bailey）那通向被告席的台阶；也正因如此，那些大人物——只要有人暗示他们属于违法者之列便会勃然作色的大人物——却难得、或者说从不向法官先生欠身一礼。

格雷戈里·巴拉姆先生代表并代理着某些财团的利益，后者在拍卖会上买下了波特兰街（Portland Place）蒙塔古街（Montague Street）上的三幢房子。那是三幢占着一方岛式地块的屋子。头一幢分租出去做写字楼，底层归一位律师，二楼归一位酒商，三楼是一套十分朴素的套间，专供格雷戈里·巴拉姆先生的营业时间之用。这位先生还租下了地下室——借着石灰水和涂料，那里被改造成了一处算不上舒适、至少也整洁干净的储藏之地。穿过这间地下室可以通到（除别处而外）一间崭新的车库，那是为巴拉姆先生的一位合伙人盖的，巴拉姆先生本人对它毫无兴趣。

===Original===

None but the workmen who had been employed in renovation knew that it was possible also to walk from one house to the other, either through the door in the cellar which had existed when the houses were purchased, or through a new door in Mr. Ballam’s office.

The third house, that at the end of the island site, was occupied by the International Artists’ Club, and the police had never followed Mr. Ballam there because Mr. Ballam had never gone there, at least not by the front door. The Artists’ Club had a “rest room” and there were times when Mr. Ballam had appeared, as if by magic, in that room, had met a select little party and conducted them through a well-concealed pass-door to the ground floor of the middle house. The middle house was the most respectable looking of the three. It had neat muslin curtains at all its windows and was occupied by a venerable gentleman and his wife.

The venerable gentleman made a practice of going out to business every morning at ten o’clock, his shiny silk hat set jauntily on the side of his head, a furled umbrella under his arm and a buttonhole in his coat. The police knew him by sight and local constables touched their helmets to him. In the days gone by when Mr. Raymond, as he called himself, had a luxurious white beard and earned an elegant income by writing begging letters and interviewing credulous and sympathetic females, he did not have that name or the reputation which he enjoyed in Montague Street. But now he was clean-shaven and had the appearance of a retired admiral and he received £4 a week for going out of the house every morning at ten o’clock, with his silk hat set at a rakish angle, and his furled umbrella and his neat little boutonniere. He spent most of the day in the Guildhall reading-room and came back at five o’clock in the evening as jaunty as ever.

And his day’s work being ended, he and his hard-faced wife went to their little attic room and played cribbage and their language was certainly jaunty but was not venerable.

On the first floor, behind triple black velvet curtains, men and women smoked day and night. It was a large room, being two rooms which had been converted into one and it had been decorated under Mr. Ballam’s eye. In this room nothing but opium was smoked. If you had a fancy for hashish you indulged yourself in a basement apartment. Sometimes Mr. Ballam himself came to take a whiff of the dream-herb, but he usually reserved these visits for such occasions as the introduction of a new and profitable client. The pipe had no ill-effect upon Mr. Ballam. That was his boast. He boasted now to a new client, a rich Spanish artist who had been picked up by one of his jackals and piloted to the International Artists’ Club.

===Chinese===

只有雇来翻修房屋的工人知道，从一幢屋子走到另一幢也是走得通的——或走地下室里那扇买下房子时便已存在的门，或走巴拉姆先生办公室里一扇新开的门。

第三幢，即岛式地块尽头的那幢，由国际艺术家俱乐部（International Artists’ Club）占用；警察从未跟踪巴拉姆先生到过那里，因为巴拉姆先生从没去过——至少没走正门。艺术家俱乐部有一间“休息室”，巴拉姆先生时不时会像变戏法似的出现在那间屋里，会晤一小拨经挑选的客人，再领着他们穿过一扇遮掩得极好的暗门，进到当中那幢房子的底层。当中那幢是三幢里外貌最体面的，所有窗户都挂着整洁的细布窗帘，住着一位德高望重的老先生和他的太太。

这位德高望重的老先生每天早晨十点钟必定出门“上班”，亮闪闪的丝质大礼帽潇洒地歪戴在头一侧，腋下夹着收拢的雨伞，衣襟上别一朵纽扣花。警察都认得他这张脸，本地的警员见了都要碰一碰头盔致意。想当年，这位自称雷蒙德先生（Mr. Raymond）的人物蓄着一部考究的白胡须，靠写乞讨信、约见那些轻信而心软的太太小姐们，挣着一份可观的收入，那时他还不叫这个名字，也没有他在蒙塔古街如今享有的这份清誉。如今他剃光了胡子，一副退役海军上将的派头，每星期领四英镑，酬劳便是每天早晨十点钟走出家门——歪戴着丝帽，夹着收拢的雨伞，佩着小巧的襟花。白天他大半泡在市政厅（Guildhall）的阅览室里，傍晚五点钟回家，照样神采奕奕。

一天的“工作”既毕，他便和他那位面色冷硬的太太回到他们的小阁楼间，打克里比奇纸牌。两口子的谈吐倒确实轻快得很，可一点也不“德高望重”。

二楼上，三重黑丝绒帘幕后面，男男女女日夜吞吐着烟气。那是间大屋子，由两间打通而成，装潢布置皆在巴拉姆先生亲自照看之下。在这间屋里，抽的清一色是鸦片。倘若偏爱哈希什（大麻膏），自有地下室的一套房间供你受用。巴拉姆先生有时也亲自来吸上一口梦草，不过这类驾临通常留待招徕有利可图的新主顾的场合。烟枪对巴拉姆先生毫无不良影响——这是他的自夸。此刻他正向一位新主顾夸口：一个有钱的西班牙艺术家，叫他的一名爪牙物色到手，一路引到了国际艺术家俱乐部。

===Original===

“Nor on me,” said the newcomer, waving away a yellow-faced Chinaman who ministered to the needs of the smokers. “I always bring my own smoke.”

Ballam leant forward curiously as the man took a silver box from his pocket and produced therefrom a green and sticky-looking pill.

“What is that?” asked Ballam curiously.

“It is a mixture of my own, *cannabis indica*, opium and a little Turkish tobacco mixed. It is even milder than opium and the result infinitely more wonderful.”

“You can’t smoke it here,” said Ballam, shaking his head. “Try the pipe, old man.”

But the “old man”﻿—he was really young in spite of his grey hair﻿—was emphatic.

===Chinese===

“对我也没有。”来客说着，挥手打发走一个面色蜡黄的中国侍者——此人专司伺候烟客的需索。“我从来都是自带烟抽。”

那人从口袋里取出一只银盒，从中拈出一粒绿乎乎、黏答答的药丸，巴拉姆好奇地探过身去。

“那是什么？”巴拉姆好奇地问。

“是我自己调配的方子：*cannabis indica*（印度大麻）、鸦片，再掺一点土耳其烟丝。它比鸦片还要温和，效力却奇妙无穷。”

“这玩意儿在这儿可抽不得。”巴拉姆摇头道，“抽枪吧，老兄。”

可是这位“老兄”——尽管一头灰发，其实人还年轻——态度斩钉截铁。
===Original===

“It doesn’t matter,” he said, “I can smoke at home. I only came out of curiosity,” and he rose to go.

“Don’t be in a hurry,” said Ballam hastily. “See here, we’ve got a basement downstairs where the hemp pipes go﻿—the smokers up here don’t like the smell﻿—I’ll come down and try one with you. Bring your coffee.”

The basement was empty and selecting a comfortable divan Mr. Ballam and his guest sat down.

“You can light this with a match, you don’t want a spirit stove,” said the stranger.

Ballam, sipping his coffee, looked dubiously at the pipe which Gonsalez offered.

“There was a question I was going to ask you,” said Leon. “Does running a show like this keep you awake at nights?”

===Chinese===

“不要紧，”他说，“我在家里也抽得。我不过是出于好奇来的。”说罢起身要走。

“别急着走啊。”巴拉姆忙道，“听我说，我们楼下有间地下室，专供抽大麻烟枪——楼上这几位不喜欢那股味儿——我陪你下去抽一斗。把你的咖啡也端上。”

地下室里空无一人，巴拉姆先生和客人拣了一张舒服的软榻坐下。

“这个用火柴就能点着，用不着酒精炉。”那生客说。

巴拉姆一面呷着咖啡，一面将信将疑地打量冈萨雷斯递过来的烟枪。

“有个问题我本想请教你。”莱昂说，“开着这么一摊生意，夜里睡得着觉么？”

===Original===

“Don’t be silly,” said Mr. Ballam, lighting his pipe slowly and puffing with evident enjoyment. “This isn’t bad stuff at all. Keep me awake at nights? Why should it?”

“Well,” answered Leon. “Lots of people go queer here, don’t they? I mean it ruins people smoking this kind of stuff.”

“That’s their look out,” said Mr. Ballam comfortably. “They get a lot of fun. There’s only one life and you’ve got to die once.”

“Some men die twice,” said Leon soberly. “Some men who under the influence of a noxious drug go fantee and wake to find themselves murderers. There’s a drug in the East which the natives call ‘bal.’ It turns men into raving lunatics.”

“Well, that doesn’t interest me,” said Ballam impatiently. “We must hurry up with this smoke. I’ve a lady coming to see me. Must keep an appointment, old man,” he laughed.

“On the contrary, the introduction of this drug into a pipe interests you very much,” said Leon, “and in spite of Miss Maggiore’s appointment﻿—”

===Chinese===

“别傻了。”巴拉姆先生说着，慢条斯理地点起烟枪，津津有味地吞云吐雾，“这货色一点也不赖。叫我夜里睡不着？凭什么？”

“是么。”莱昂答道，“在这儿抽着抽着就抽坏了的人可不少，对吧？我是说，这类东西抽久了是要毁人的。”

“那是他们自己的事。”巴拉姆先生安然道，“他们乐也乐够了。人只有一条命，死也不过一回。”

“有的人却要死两回。”莱昂正色道，“有些人中了毒药发起狂来，一觉醒来发现自己成了杀人凶手。东方有一种药，土人管它叫‘巴尔’（bal）。它能叫人变成狂吼乱叫的疯子。”

“得了，那与我无关。”巴拉姆不耐烦地说，“咱们赶紧把这斗抽完。有位女士要来见我。约会可不能失约，老兄。”他笑道。

“恰恰相反，把这药掺进烟枪里，与你的干系大得很。”莱昂说，“任凭马焦雷小姐有什么约会——”

===Original===

The other started.

“What the hell are you talking about?” he asked crossly.

“In spite of that appointment I must break the news to you that the drug which turns men into senseless beasts is more potent than any you serve in this den.”

“What’s it to do with me?” snarled Ballam.

“It interests you a great deal,” said Leon coolly, “because you are at this moment smoking a double dose!”

With a howl of rage Ballam sprang to his feet and what happened after that he could not remember. Only something seemed to split in his head, and a blinding light flashed before his eyes and then a whole century of time went past, a hundred years of moving time and an eternity of flashing lights, of thunderous noises, of whispering voices, of ceaseless troubled movement. Sometimes he knew he was talking and listened eagerly to hear what he himself had to say. Sometimes people spoke to him and mocked him and he had a consciousness that he was being chased by somebody.

===Chinese===

对方猛地一震。

“你他妈的胡说些什么？”他怒冲冲地问。

“任凭有那个约会，我也必须让你知道一个消息：世上那种把人变成没头没脑的禽兽的药，可比你在这贼窝里供应的任何货色都要霸道。”

“与我什么相干？”巴拉姆咆哮道。

“与你的干系大得很。”莱昂冷冷地说，“因为此时此刻，你抽下去的已是双倍的剂量！”

巴拉姆怒吼一声跳将起来，此后的事他再也记不起了。只觉得脑子里仿佛有什么东西崩裂了，一道炫目的白光在眼前炸开，随后整整一个世纪的光阴打了过去——流动不息的一百年，一场无穷无尽：闪灭不定的光，轰轰隆隆的声响，窃窃私语的人声，无休无止的纷扰颠簸。有时他知道自己正在说话，便急切地想听清自己要说些什么；有时人们对他讲话，嘲笑他，他则隐隐觉得有什么人正在追捕自己。

===Original===

How long this went on he could not judge. In his half-bemused condition he tried to reckon time but found he had no standard of measurement. It seemed years after that he opened his eyes with a groan, and put his hand to his aching head. He was lying in bed. It was a hard bed and the pillow was even harder. He stared up at the whitewashed ceiling and looked round at the plain distempered walls. Then he peered over the side of the bed and saw that the floor as of concrete. Two lights were burning, one above a table and one in a corner of the room where a man was sitting reading a newspaper. He was a curious-looking man and Ballam blinked at him.

“I am dreaming,” he said aloud and the man looked up.

“Hello! Do you want to get up?”

Ballam did not reply. He was still staring, his mouth agape. The man was in uniform, in a dark, tight-fitting uniform. He wore a cap on his head and a badge. Round his waist was a shiny black belt and then Ballam read the letters on the shoulder-strap of the tunic.

“A.W.,” he repeated, dazed. “A.W.”

What did “A.W.” stand for? And then the truth flashed on him.

===Chinese===

这样熬了多久，他无从判断。半昏沉之中他试图推算时间，却发现没有任何可以度量的标准。仿佛隔了许多年，他才呻吟一声睁开眼睛，伸手按住胀痛的脑袋。他躺在床上。床板很硬，枕头更硬。他仰望着粉刷过的天花板，环顾四壁素净的墙。随后他从床沿探头下望，看见地面是水泥的。屋里亮着两盏灯，一盏在桌子上方，一盏在屋角——那里坐着个男人，正在读报。那人生得模样古怪，巴拉姆朝他直眨眼。

“我在做梦。”他出声说道。那人抬起了头。

“嗬！想起床了？”

巴拉姆没有答话。他还在直勾勾地盯着，嘴张得老大。那人穿着制服，深色的紧身制服，头上戴一顶便帽，缀着徽章，腰间束一条乌黑发亮的皮带。这时巴拉姆才看清那制服肩带上的字母。

“A.W.，”他茫然地念道，“A.W.。”

“A.W.”是哪两个字的缩写？随即，真相电光般掠过他的脑际。

===Original===

Assistant Warder! He glared round the room. There was one window, heavily barred and covered with thick glass. On the wall was pasted a sheet of printed paper. He staggered out of bed and read, still open-mouthed:

> “Regulations for His Majesty’s Prisons.”

He looked down at himself. He had evidently gone to bed with his breeches and stockings on and his breeches were of coarse yellow material and branded with faded black arrows. He was in prison! How long had he been there?

“Are you going to behave today?” asked the warder curtly. “We don’t want any more of those scenes you gave us yesterday!”

“How long have I been here?” croaked Ballam.

===Chinese===

助理看守（Assistant Warder）！他狠狠扫视这间屋子。只有一扇窗，装着粗重的铁栅，还蒙着厚玻璃。墙上贴着一张印刷的纸。他摇摇晃晃地下了床，依旧张着嘴读道：

> “《皇家监狱管理条例》。”

他低头看了看自己。显然他是和衣睡下的，马裤长袜都没脱，那马裤是粗劣的黄色料子，打着褪了色的黑色箭头印记。他在监狱里！他在这儿多久了？

“今天肯安分点吗？”看守没好气地问，“昨天你闹的那一出，我们可不想再来一回！”

“我在这儿多久了？”巴拉姆声音沙哑地问。

===Original===

“You know how long you’ve been here. You’ve been here three weeks, yesterday.”

“Three weeks!” gasped Ballam. “What is the charge?”

“Now don’t come that game with me, Ballam,” said the warder, not unkindly. “You know I’m not allowed to have conversations with you. Go back and sleep. Sometimes I think you are as mad as you profess to be.”

“Have I been﻿—bad?” asked Ballam.

“Bad?” The warder jerked up his head. “I wasn’t in the court with you, but they say you behaved in the dock like a man demented, and when the Judge was passing sentence of death﻿—”

“My God!” shrieked Ballam and fell back on the bed, white and haggard. “Sentenced to death!” He could hardly form the words. “What have I done?”

===Chinese===

“你在这儿多久，自己心里有数。到昨天，整三个礼拜。”

“三个礼拜！”巴拉姆倒抽一口气，“罪名是什么？”

“行了，别跟我来这一套，巴拉姆。”看守说，语气并不刻薄，“你知道不许我跟你多谈。回床上去睡。有时候我倒觉得，你是真疯，疯得跟你自称的一模一样。”

“我是不是——闹过乱子？”巴拉姆问。

“闹乱子？”看守猛地扬起头，“开审的时候我没在场，可听说你在被告席上闹得像失了心疯，等法官宣判死刑的时候——”

“我的上帝！”巴拉姆惨叫一声跌回床上，面无人色，形容枯槁。“判了死刑！”他几乎吐不出字来，“我干了什么？”

===Original===

“You killed a young lady, you know that,” said the warder. “I’m surprised at you, trying to come it over me after the good friend I’ve been to you, Ballam. Why don’t you buck up and take your punishment like a man?”

There was a calendar above the place where the warder had been sitting.

“Twelfth of April,” read Ballam and could have shrieked again, for it was the first day of March that he met that mysterious stranger. He remembered it all now. *Bal!* The drug that drove men mad.

He sprang to his feet.

“I want to see the governor! I want to tell them the truth! I’ve been drugged!”

“Now you’ve told us all that story before,” said the warder with an air of resignation. “When you killed the young lady﻿—”

===Chinese===

“你杀了一位年轻小姐，这你还不知道么。”看守说，“我可真没想到，我待你这么够朋友，你还想蒙我，巴拉姆。怎么不拿出点男人样子，硬硬气气领你的罪？”

看守方才坐的位置上方挂着一本日历。

“四月十二日。”巴拉姆念了出来，险些又叫出声——他遇见那个神秘生客，还是三月一日的事。这下他全想起来了。*Bal*（巴尔）！那逼人发狂的药。

他一跃而起。

“我要见监狱长！我要把真相告诉他们！我是被人下了药的！”

“你那套话我们早就听遍了。”看守一脸无可奈何，“你杀那位年轻小姐的时候——”

===Original===

“What young lady?” shrieked Ballam. “Not Maggiore! Don’t tell me﻿—”

“You know you killed her right enough,” said the warder. “What’s the good of making all this fuss? Now go back to bed, Ballam. You can’t do any good by kicking up a shindy this night of all nights in the world.”

“I want to see the governor! Can I write to him?”

“You can write to him if you like,” and the warder indicated the table.

Ballam staggered up to the table and sat down shakily in a chair. There was half a dozen sheets of blue notepaper headed in black: “H.M. Prison, Wandsworth, SW1.”

He was in Wandsworth prison! He looked round the cell. It did not look like a cell and yet it did. It was so horribly bare and the door was heavy looking. He had never been in a cell before and of course it was different to what he had expected.

===Chinese===

“什么年轻小姐？”巴拉姆尖声叫道，“不是马焦雷！别告诉我——”

“你心里明白，就是她，没错。”看守说，“这么吵吵嚷嚷有什么用处？回床上去，巴拉姆。天底下偏偏挑这么一个晚上闹个天翻地覆，你闹不出好来。”

“我要见监狱长！我能给他写信吗？”

“你愿意写就写吧。”看守朝桌子指了指。

巴拉姆踉跄着走到桌前，颤巍巍地坐进椅子。桌上摆着半打蓝色信纸，黑色字头印着：“H.M. Prison, Wandsworth, SW1.”（旺兹沃思王家监狱，西南一区）。

他竟在旺兹沃思监狱！他环顾这间牢房。它不像牢房，却又偏偏像。它赤裸得怕人，那扇门看着就厚重。他从没进过牢房，自然与预想的大不一样。

===Original===

A thought struck him.

“When﻿—when am I to be punished?” he said chokingly.

“Tomorrow!”

The word fell like a sentence of doom and the man fell forward, his head upon his arms and wept hysterically. Then of a sudden he began to write with feverish haste, his face red with weeping.

His letter was incoherent. It was about a man who had come to the club and had given him a drug and then he had spent a whole eternity in darkness seeing lights and being chased by people and hearing whispering voices. And he was not guilty. He loved Genee Maggiore. He would not have hurt a hair of her head.

He stopped here to weep again. Perhaps he was dreaming? Perhaps he was under the influence of this drug. He dashed his knuckles against the wall and the shock made him wince.

===Chinese===

他猛然想起一件事。

“什么时候——什么时候受刑？”他哽咽着说。

“明天！”

这个字眼落下来，如同一纸催命的判决。他扑倒下去，头埋进臂弯里，歇斯底里地痛哭。突然间他又抓起笔狂写起来，哭红的脸上透着狂热。

他的信写得语无伦次。说的是有个人到俱乐部来，给他下了药，随后他在黑暗里熬了整整一世，看见光，被人追，听见窃窃的耳语。他是无辜的。他爱吉妮·马焦雷。她头上的一根头发他也不肯伤。

写到这里他又停下来哭。也许他是在做梦？也许他还带着那药的劲儿？他用指节往墙上撞，那阵剧痛叫他缩了一下。

===Original===

“Here, none of that,” said the warder sternly. “You get back to bed.”

Ballam looked at his bleeding knuckles. It was true! It was no dream! It was true, true!

He lay on the bed and lost consciousness again and when he awoke the warder was still sitting in his place reading. He seemed to doze again for an hour, although in reality it was only for a few minutes, and every time he woke something within him said: “This morning you die!”

Once he sprang shrieking from the bed and had to be thrown back.

“If you give me any more trouble I’ll get another officer in and we’ll tie you down. Why don’t you take it like a man? It’s no worse for you than it was for her,” said the warder savagely.

After that he lay still and he was falling into what seemed a longer sleep when the warder touched him. When he awoke he found his own clothes laid neatly by the side of the bed upon a chair and he dressed himself hurriedly.

===Chinese===

“喂，不许来这套。”看守厉声道，“回床上去。”

巴拉姆看着自己渗血的指节。是真的！不是梦！是真的，千真万确！

他躺回床上，又失去了知觉。再醒来时，看守仍坐在原处读报。他似乎又打了一个钟头的盹，其实不过几分钟，而每次醒来，心里总有个声音在说：“今天早晨你就得死！”

有一回他尖叫着从床上弹起来，硬叫人按了回去。

“你再找麻烦，我就再叫一个看守进来，把你捆起来。怎么就不能像个男子汉挺过去？你受的这点罪，不比她受的重。”看守狠狠地说。

此后他躺着不动，正坠入一场似乎更长的沉睡时，看守碰了碰他。醒来时，他发现自己的衣服整整齐齐地摆在床边的椅子上，便慌忙穿好。

===Original===

He looked around for something.

“Where’s the collar?” he asked trembling.

“You don’t need a collar,” the warder’s voice had a certain quality of sardonic humour.

“Pull yourself together,” said the man roughly. “Other people have gone through this. From what I’ve heard you ran an opium den. A good many of your clients gave us a visit. They had to go through with it, and so must you.”

He waited, sitting on the edge of the bed, his face in his hands and then the door opened and a man came in. He was a slight man with a red beard and a mop of red hair.

The warder swung the prisoner round.

===Chinese===

他四下里找什么东西。

“领子呢？”他哆嗦着问。

“你用不着领子。”看守的声音里透着一丝冷嘲的幽默。

“振作点。”那人粗声说，“别人也都经过这一遭。听说你开着一家鸦片馆。你那些主顾，不少都来我们这儿做过客。他们熬过来了，你也一样。”

他坐在床沿上等着，双手捂着脸。随后门开了，进来一个人，瘦小个子，红胡子，一头蓬乱的红发。

看守把囚犯拨转过来。

===Original===

“Put your hands behind you,” he said and Ballam sweated as he felt the strap grip his wrists.

Then light was extinguished. A cap was drawn over his face and he thought he heard voices behind him. He wasn’t fit to die, he knew that. There always was a parson in a case like this. Someone grasped his arm on either side and he walked slowly forward through the door across a yard and through another door. It was a long way and once his knees gave under him but he stood erect. Presently they stopped.
