“Stand where you are,” said a voice and he found a noose slipped round his neck and waited, waited in agony, minutes, hours it seemed. He took no account of time and could not judge it. Then he heard a heavy step and somebody caught him by the arm.

“What are you doing here, governor?” said a voice.

The bag was pulled from his head. He was in the street. It was night and he stood under the light of a streetlamp. The man regarding him curiously was a policeman.

===Chinese===

“把手背到身后。”他说。皮带扣上手腕的那一刻，巴拉姆出了一身冷汗。

接着，灯灭了。一顶帽子罩到他脸上，他恍惚听见身后有人说话。他不配去死，这他自己知道。这种场合总少不了一位牧师。两边各有一个人攥住他的手臂，他缓缓向前走，穿过一道门，横过一个院子，又穿过一道门。路很长，有一回他膝盖都软了，可他仍站得笔直。不久他们停下了。

“站原地别动。”一个声音说。他觉得一条绞索套上了脖颈，便等着，在极度的煎熬里等着，像是几分钟，又像是几个钟头。他全然不计时间，也无法计量。随后他听见一阵沉重的脚步，有人一把抓住了他的手臂。

“你在这儿干什么呢，老先生？”一个声音说。

头上的罩子拽了下来。他站在街上。是夜里，他立在一盏路灯的光下。正打量着他的那个人，是个警察。

===Original===

“Got a bit of rope round your neck, too, somebody tied your hands. What is it﻿—a holdup case?” said the policeman as he loosened the straps. “Or is it a lark?” demanded the representative of the law. “I’m surprised at you, an old gentleman like you with white hair!”

Gregory Ballam’s hair had been black less than seven hours before when Leon Gonsalez had drugged his coffee and had brought him through the basement exit into the big yard at the back of the club.

For here was a nice new garage as Leon had discovered when he prospected the place, and here they were left uninterrupted to play the comedy of the condemned cell with blue sheets of prison notepaper put there for the occasion and a copy of Prison Regulations which was donated quite unwittingly by Mr. Fare, Commissioner of Police.

===Chinese===

“脖子上还套着一截绳子，手也叫人捆上了。这是怎么回事——遭了抢劫？”警察一面给他解皮带一面说，“还是闹着玩呢？”这位法律的代表追问道，“您这么一位白发苍苍的老先生，居然也来这个，真叫我意外！”

不到七个钟头之前，格雷戈里·巴拉姆的头发还是黑的——那时莱昂·冈萨雷斯在他的咖啡里下了药，把他从地下室的出口弄出去，送进了俱乐部后面的大院子。

原来这里有一间崭新的车库——莱昂先前踩点时就发现好了——就在这儿，他们不受打扰地演完了这出死牢喜剧：为这场好戏备下的蓝色监狱信纸，还有一册《监狱管理条例》，那可是警务总监费尔先生在浑然不知中捐出来的。

## The Man Who Hated Amelia Jones / 恨阿梅莉亚·琼斯的人

===Original===

There was a letter that came to Leon Gonsalez, and the stamp bore the image and superscription of Alphonse XIII. It was from a placid man who had written his letter in the hour of siesta, when Cordova slept, and he had scribbled all the things which had come into his head as he sat in an orange bower overlooking the lordly Guadalquivir, now in yellow spate.

“It is from Poiccart,” said Leon.

“Yes?” replied George Manfred, half asleep in a big armchair before the fire.

That and a green-shaded reading lamp supplied the illumination to their comfortable Jermyn Street flat at the moment.

“And what,” said George, stretching himself, “what does our excellent friend Poiccart have to say?”

“A blight has come upon his onions,” said Leon solemnly and Manfred chuckled and then was suddenly grave.

===Chinese===

有一封信寄到莱昂·冈萨雷斯（Leon Gonsalez）手上，邮票上印着阿方索十三世（Alphonse XIII）的头像和铭记。写信的是个心境恬淡的人，趁午睡时分、科尔多瓦（Cordova）全城酣睡之际落笔；他坐在一座俯瞰瓜达尔基维尔河（the Guadalquivir）的橙树凉棚里——那条气派的大河眼下正值黄浊的涨水期——把掠过脑际的种种念头都信手写了下来。

“是波伊卡特（Poiccart）来的。”莱昂说。

“哦？”乔治·曼弗雷德（George Manfred）半睡半醒地应了一声，他正瘫在炉火前一把大扶手椅里。

此刻，他们杰明街那套舒适公寓里的照明，便全指望这炉火和一盏绿罩读书灯了。

“那么，”乔治伸了个懒腰，“我们那位顶好的朋友波伊卡特有什么可说的？”

“一场灾祸降临了他的洋葱。”莱昂一本正经地说。曼弗雷德扑哧一笑，随即又忽然严肃起来。

===Original===

There was a time when the name of these three, with one who now lay in the Bordeaux cemetery, had stricken terror to the hearts of evildoers. In those days the Four Just Men were a menace to the sleep of many cunning men who had evaded the law, yet had not evaded this ubiquitous organisation, which slew ruthlessly in the name of Justice.

Poiccart was growing onions! He sighed and repeated the words aloud.

“And why not?” demanded Leon. “Have you read of the *Three Musketeers*?”

“Surely,” said Manfred, with a smile at the fire.

===Chinese===

想当年，这三个人，外加如今长眠于波尔多（Bordeaux）公墓的一位，他们的名号曾叫恶人闻风丧胆。那时候，四义士（The Four Just Men）是许多狡徒睡眠的大患——这些人躲过了法律，却躲不开这个无处不在的组织；它以正义之名，杀伐决绝，绝不容情。

波伊卡特竟在种洋葱！他叹了口气，把这句话高声又念了一遍。

“那有什么不可以？”莱昂质问道，“你读过《三个火枪手》（Three Musketeers）吗？”

“当然读过。”曼弗雷德说，含笑望着炉火。

===Original===

“In what book, may I ask?” demanded Leon.

“Why, in *The Three Musketeers*, of course,” replied Manfred in surprise.

“Then you did wrong,” said Leon Gonsalez promptly. “To love the Three Musketeers, you must read of them in *The Iron Mask*. When one of them has grown fat and is devoting himself to his raiment, and one is a mere courtier of the King of France, and the other is old and full of sorrow for his lovesick child. Then they become human, my dear Manfred, just as Poiccart becomes human when he grows onions. Shall I read you bits?”

“Please,” said Manfred, properly abashed.

===Chinese===

“那么请问，是在哪本书里读的？”莱昂追问。

“怎么，当然是《三个火枪手》里。”曼弗雷德愕然答道。

“那你可就错了，”莱昂·冈萨雷斯当即说道，“要爱这三个火枪手，你必须到《铁面人》（The Iron Mask）里去读他们：那时，一个已经发福，一心讲究穿着；一个不过是法兰西国王驾前的一名廷臣；另一个老了，为害着相思病的孩儿满怀悲苦。到那时他们才算活人，我亲爱的曼弗雷德，正如波伊卡特种起洋葱来才算活人。要不要我念几段给你听听？”

“请吧。”曼弗雷德说，当真有些讪然。

===Original===

> “H’m,” read Gonsalez, “ ‘I told you about the onions, George. I have some gorgeous roses. Manfred would love them﻿ ﻿… do not take too much heed of this new blood test, by which the American doctor professes that he can detect degrees of relationship﻿ ﻿… the new little pigs are doing exceedingly well. There is one that is exceptionally intelligent and contemplative. I have named him George.’ ”

George Manfred by the fire squirmed in his chair and chuckled.

> “ ‘This will be a very good year for wine, I am told,’ ” Leon read on, “ ‘but the oranges are not as plentiful as they were last year﻿ ﻿… do you know that the fingerprints of twins are identical? Curiously enough the fingerprints of twins of the anthropoid ape are dissimilar. I wish you would get information on this subject﻿ ﻿…’ ”

===Chinese===

> “唔，”冈萨雷斯念道，“‘洋葱的事我已经跟你说过啦，乔治。我这里有几株玫瑰好极了。曼弗雷德准会喜欢……那种新的验血法别太当真，就是美国医生声称凭它能验出亲缘远近的那一种……新添的小猪崽都长得很不坏。有一头格外聪明，格外爱沉思。我给它起名叫乔治。’”

炉火边的乔治·曼弗雷德在椅子里扭来扭去，呵呵直笑。

> “‘听说今年会是个酿酒的好年头，’”莱昂接着往下念，“‘可是橙子不如去年结得多……你可知道，双胞胎的指纹是一模一样的？说来奇怪，类人猿双胞胎的指纹却各不相同。我望你设法弄些这方面的资料来……’”

===Original===

He read on, little scraps of domestic news, fleeting excursions into scientific side-issues, tiny scraps of gossip﻿—they filled ten closely written pages.

Leon folded the letter and put it in his pocket.

“Of course he’s not right about the fingerprints of twins being identical. That was one of the illusions of the excellent Lombroso. Anyway the fingerprint system is unsatisfactory.”

“I never heard it called into question,” said George in surprise. “Why isn’t it satisfactory?”

Leon rolled a cigarette with deft fingers, licked down the paper and lit the ragged end before he replied.

“At Scotland Yard, they have, let us say, one hundred thousand fingerprints. In Britain there are fifty million inhabitants. One hundred thousand is exactly one five-hundredth of fifty millions. Suppose you were a police officer and you were called to the Albert Hall where five hundred people were assembled and told that one of these had in his possession stolen property and you received permission to search them. Would you be content with searching one and giving a clean bill to the rest?”

===Chinese===

他一路念下去，都是些持家的琐碎消息，间或岔出去几笔科学的枝节话题，再夹几则小小的闲话——密密麻麻写满了十页纸。

莱昂把信折好，放进口袋。

“双胞胎的指纹一模一样，他这话当然不对。那是出色的隆布罗梭（Lombroso）的迷误之一。总而言之，指纹这套办法并不完善。”

“我从没听人这么质疑过，”乔治诧异道，“它怎么不完善？”

莱昂用灵巧的手指卷了支烟，舔湿压平烟纸，点着毛糙的烟头，这才回答。

“在苏格兰场，比方说，存着十万枚指纹。全英国有五千万人口。十万恰好是五千万的五百分之一。假如你是个警官，奉命到阿尔伯特音乐厅去——那里聚着五百个人——上头告诉你其中一人身上带着赃物，并且准你搜查他们。只搜一个人，就给其余人开清白证明，你肯吗？”

===Original===

“Of course not,” said Manfred, “but I don’t see what you mean.”

“I mean that until the whole of the country and every country in Europe adopts a system by which every citizen registers his fingerprints and until all the countries have an opportunity of exchanging those fingerprints and comparing them with their own, it is ridiculous to say that no two prints are alike.”

“That settles the fingerprint system,” said Manfred, sotto voce.

“Logically it does,” said the complacent Leon, “but actually it will not, of course.”

There was a long silence after this and then Manfred reached to a case by the side of the fireplace and took down a book.

===Chinese===

“当然不肯，”曼弗雷德说，“可我不明白你想说明什么。”

“我的意思是：除非本国以及欧洲每一个国家都采用一套让每个公民登记指纹的制度，除非各国有机会交换这些指纹、与各自的存档比对，否则断言天下没有两枚相同的指纹，就是荒唐话。”

“这下指纹制度算完蛋了。”曼弗雷德压低嗓音说。

“从逻辑上讲是完蛋了，”莱昂洋洋自得地说，“不过实际上，当然完不了。”

此后是长长一阵沉默。然后曼弗雷德伸手到壁炉边的一只匣子上，取下一本书来。

===Original===

Presently he heard the creak of a chair as Gonsalez rose and the soft “pad” of a closing door. Manfred looked up at the clock and, as he knew, it was half past eight.

In five minutes Leon was back again. He had changed his clothing and, as Manfred had once said before, his disguise was perfect. It was not a disguise in the accepted understanding of the word, for he had not in any way touched his face, or changed the colour of his hair.

Only by his artistry he contrived to appear just as he wished to appear, an extremely poor man. His collar was clean, but frayed. His boots were beautifully polished, but they were old and patched. He did not permit the crudity of a heel worn down, but had fixed two circular rubber heels just a little too large for their foundations.

“You are an old clerk battling with poverty, and striving to the end to be genteel,” said Manfred.

Gonsalez shook his head.

“I am a solicitor who, twenty years ago, was struck off the rolls and ruined because I helped a man to escape the processes of the law. An ever so much more sympathetic role, George. Moreover, it brings people to me for advice. One of these nights you must come down to the public bar of the Cow and Compasses and hear me discourse upon the Married Woman’s Property Act.”

===Chinese===

不多时，他听见冈萨雷斯起身时椅子的一响，还有房门轻轻“嗒”地合上的声音。曼弗雷德抬头看了看钟——果如他所知，八点半了。

五分钟后莱昂又回来了。他换了装束，而且——正如曼弗雷德从前说过的那样——他的化装完美无缺。那又不是这个词通常意义上的化装，因为他丝毫没有碰自己的脸，也没有改变头发的颜色。

全凭一身演技，他硬是让自己成了他想显现的样子：一个穷到了家的人。领子干净，然而磨了边。靴子擦得漂漂亮亮，然而又旧又打着补丁。他不屑于后跟磨偏那种拙劣的破绽，而是钉了两片圆形橡胶后跟，比鞋底稍稍大出一圈。

“你是个跟贫穷苦斗的老账房，拼到最后一口气也要撑住体面。”曼弗雷德说。

冈萨雷斯摇了摇头。

“我是个事务律师，二十年前因为帮一个人逃脱法律的罗网，被除了名，前程尽毁。这角色叫人同情得多啦，乔治。再说，这还能把人引到我这里来讨主意。哪天晚上，你务必光临「母牛与圆规」（the Cow and Compasses）的大众酒吧，听我宣讲《已婚妇女财产法》（Married Woman’s Property Act）。”

===Original===

“I never asked you what you were before,” said George. “Good hunting, Leon, and my respectful salutations to Amelia Jones!”

Gonsalez was biting his lips thoughtfully and looking into the fire and now he nodded.

“Poor Amelia Jones?” he said softly.

“You’re a wonderful fellow,” smiled Manfred, “only you could invest a charwoman of middle age with the glamour of romance.”

Leon was helping himself into a threadbare overcoat.

“There was an English poet once﻿—it was Pope, I think﻿—who said that everybody was romantic who admired a fine thing, or did one. I rather think Amelia Jones has done both.”

===Chinese===

“我倒从没问过你从前是干什么的，”乔治说，“祝你狩猎愉快，莱昂，并向阿梅莉亚·琼斯（Amelia Jones）致以敬意！”

冈萨雷斯若有所思地咬着嘴唇，凝望炉火，这时点了点头。

“可怜的阿梅莉亚·琼斯？”他轻声说。

“你真是个妙人，”曼弗雷德微笑道，“也只有你，能给一个中年的打杂女工披上传奇的光彩。”

莱昂正往身上套一件磨得露线的旧大衣。

“从前有位英国诗人——我想是蒲柏（Pope）——说过，凡是欣赏一桩美事、或者亲手做成一桩美事的人，都是浪漫的人。我看阿梅莉亚·琼斯两样都占全了。”

===Original===

The Cow and Compasses is a small public-house in Treet Road, Deptford. The gloomy thoroughfare was well-nigh empty, for it was a grey cold night when Leon turned into the bar. The uninviting weather may have been responsible for the paucity of clients that evening, for there were scarcely half a dozen people on the sanded floor when he made his way to the bar and ordered a claret and soda.

One who had been watching for him started up from the deal form on which she had been sitting and subsided again when he walked toward her with glass in hand.

“Well, Mrs. Jones,” he greeted her, “and how are you this evening?”

She was a stout woman with a white worn face and hands that trembled spasmodically.

“I am glad you’ve come, sir,” she said.

She held a little glass of port in her hand, but it was barely touched. It was on one desperate night when in an agony of terror and fear this woman had fled from her lonely home to the light and comfort of the public-house that Leon had met her. He was at the time pursuing with the greatest caution a fascinating skull which he had seen on the broad shoulders of a Covent Garden porter. He had tracked the owner to his home and to his place of recreation and was beginning to work up to his objective, which was to secure the history and the measurements of this unimaginative bearer of fruit, when the stout charwoman had drifted into his orbit. Tonight she evidently had something on her mind of unusual importance, for she made three lame beginnings before she plunged into the matter which was agitating her.

===Chinese===

「母牛与圆规」是德普特福德（Deptford）特里特路（Treet Road）上一家小酒馆。莱昂拐进店堂时，那条阴沉沉的大街上几乎空无一人，因为那是个灰色阴冷的夜晚。天气面目可憎，那晚客人稀少多半得归咎于它：他走到柜台前要一杯红酒苏打时，铺着沙土的地板上拢共不到半打人。

一个一直在守候他的人从松木长凳上霍地站起，待他端着杯子朝她走去，又坐了回去。

“哟，琼斯太太，”他招呼道，“今晚您好吗？”

她是个敦实的女人，一张操劳而苍白的脸，两只手一阵一阵痉挛似的发抖。

“您来了我就踏实了，先生。”她说。

她手里握着一小杯波特酒，却几乎没有沾唇。莱昂与她相识，是在一个绝望已极的夜晚：这女人被恐惧和惊惶煎熬得死去活来，从孤零零的家里逃到酒馆的灯火与人气里来避难。他当时正以极度的审慎，追踪一颗迷人的颅骨——那颅骨是他早先在考文特花园一个搬运工的宽阔肩膀上瞧见的。他一路跟踪颅骨的主人到了住处、到了消遣的去处，正开始步步逼近目标——也就是弄到这位想象力贫乏的水果搬运工的身世和各项测量数据——这位敦实的打杂女工却漂进了他的轨道。今晚她心里显然压着一件非同小可的事，因为她结结巴巴起了三回头，才一头扎进那桩搅得她六神无主的事情里去。

===Original===

“Mr. Lucas” (this was the name Gonsalez had given to the habitués of the Cow and Compasses), “I want to ask you a great favour. You’ve been very kind to me, giving me advice about my husband, and all that. But this is a big favour and you’re a very busy gentleman, too.”

She looked at him appealingly, almost pleadingly.

“I have plenty of time just now,” said Gonsalez.

“Would you come with me into the country tomorrow?” she asked. “I want you to﻿—to﻿—to see somebody.”

“Why surely, Mrs. Jones,” said Gonsalez.

“Would you be at Paddington Station at nine o’clock in the morning? I would pay your fare,” she went on fervently. “Of course, I shouldn’t allow you to go to any expense﻿—I’ve got a bit of money put by.”

===Chinese===

“卢卡斯先生”（这是冈萨雷斯报给「母牛与圆规」常客们的名字），“我想求您帮个大忙。您一直待我很好，帮我对付我男人的事，还教了这教了那。可这回是个大忙，您又是个顶忙的绅士。”

她望着他，眼神里满是恳求，几乎是哀告。

“我眼下有的是工夫。”冈萨雷斯说。

“明天您肯陪我到乡下去一趟吗？”她问。“我要您去——去——去见一个人。”

“当然肯，琼斯太太。”冈萨雷斯说。

“明早九点，您到帕丁顿车站行不行？车钱由我来付，”她热切地接着说，“当然啦，我怎么能让您破费——我自己积攒了一点钱。”

===Original===

“As to that,” said Leon, “I’ve made a little money myself today, so don’t trouble about the fare. Have you heard from your husband?”

“Not from him,” she shook her head, “but from another man who has just come out of prison.”

Her lips trembled and tears were in her eyes.

“He’ll do it, I know he’ll do it,” she said, with a catch in her voice, “but it’s not me that I’m thinking of.”

Leon opened his eyes.

“Not you?” he repeated.

===Chinese===

“说到车钱，”莱昂说，“我今天自己也挣了几个，您就不必费心了。您男人有信来吗？”

“不是他的信，”她摇摇头，“是另一个刚从监狱里出来的人捎来的信儿。”

她嘴唇颤抖，眼里含着泪。

“他会干的，我知道他会干的，”她说，嗓音哽了一下，“可我心里想的并不是我自己。”

莱昂睁大了眼睛。

“不是您？”他重复道。

===Original===

He had suspected the third factor, yet he had never been able to fit it in the scheme of this commonplace woman.

“No, sir, not me,” she said miserably. “You know he hates me and you know he’s going to do me in the moment he gets out, but I haven’t told you why.”

“Where is he now?” asked Leon.

“Devizes Gaol, he’s gone there for his discharge. He’ll be out in two months.”

“And then he’ll come straight to you, you think?”

She shook her head.

===Chinese===

他早就疑心有这么个第三方因素，却始终没能把它安放进这个寻常女人的格局里。

“不是的，先生，不是我自己，”她凄凄惶惶地说，“您知道他恨我，您知道他一放出来就要干掉我，可我一直没告诉您为什么。”

“他现在在哪儿？”莱昂问。

“迪韦齐斯（Devizes）监狱，他转到那儿办出狱的手续。两个月后就放出来了。”

“然后他就会照直来找您，您是这么想的？”

她摇了摇头。

===Original===

“Not he,” she said bitterly. “That ain’t his way. You don’t know him, Mr. Lucas. But nobody does know him like I do. If he’d come straight to me it’d be all right, but he’s not that kind. He’s going to kill me, I tell you, and I don’t care how soon it comes. He wasn’t called Bash Jones for nothing. I’ll get it all right!” she nodded grimly. “He’ll just walk into the room and bash me without a word and that’ll be the end of Amelia Jones. But I don’t mind, I don’t mind,” she repeated. “It’s the other that’s breaking my heart and has been all the time.”

He knew it was useless to try to persuade her to tell her troubles, and at closing time they left the bar together.

“I’d ask you home only that might make it worse, and I don’t want to get you into any kind of bother, Mr. Lucas,” she said.

He offered his hand. It was the first time he had done so, and she took it in her big limp palm and shook it feebly.

“Very few people have shaken hands with Amelia Jones,” thought Gonsalez, and he went back to the flat in Jermyn Street to find Manfred asleep before the fire.

===Chinese===

“他才不会呢，”她恨恨地说，“那不是他的路数。您不了解他，卢卡斯先生。可这世上也没人比我更了解他。他要肯照直冲我来倒好了，他不是那路人。他会杀了我的，我跟您说，早一天晚一天我都不在乎。巴什·琼斯（Bash Jones）这名字，人家可不是白叫的。我这条命横竖是交代了！”她狠狠地点点头。“他只会一声不吭地闯进屋来，把我一顿痛殴，阿梅莉亚·琼斯就这么完了事。可我不怕，我不怕，”她又说了一遍，“叫我心碎的是另一个人，从头到尾都是另一个人。”

他知道，劝她倾吐苦衷是徒劳的。到了打烊时分，两人一同走出了酒馆。

“我本想请您到家里坐坐，就怕那样反倒更糟，我也不想把您搅进任何麻烦里去，卢卡斯先生。”她说。

他伸出手去。这是他头一回这样做。她把那只手握进自己宽大绵软的手掌里，轻轻地握了握。

“跟阿梅莉亚·琼斯握过手的人，可没有几个。”冈萨雷斯心想。他回到杰明街的公寓，发现曼弗雷德已在炉火前睡着了。

===Original===

He was waiting at Paddington Station the next morning in a suit a little less shabby, and to his surprise Mrs. Jones appeared dressed in better taste than he could have imagined was possible. Her clothes were plain but they effectively disguised the class to which she belonged. She took the tickets for Swindon and there was little conversation on the journey. Obviously she did not intend to unburden her mind as yet.

The train was held up at Newbury whilst a slow up-train shunted to allow a school special to pass. It was crowded with boys and girls who waved a cheery and promiscuous greeting as they passed.

“Of course!” nodded Leon. “It is the beginning of the Easter holidays. I had forgotten.”

At Swindon they alighted and then for the first time the woman gave some indication as to the object of their journey.

“We’ve got to stay on this platform,” she said nervously. “I’m expecting to see somebody, and I’d like you to see her, too, Mr. Lucas.”

===Chinese===

第二天早晨，他穿着一套稍稍不那么寒酸的衣裳，在帕丁顿车站等候。出乎他的意料，琼斯太太露面时的穿戴之得体，远超他所能想象的限度。她的衣裳朴素，却把她所属的阶层掩饰得滴水不漏。她买了去斯温登（Swindon）的车票，旅途上交谈寥寥。显然，她还不打算倾吐心事。

列车在纽伯里（Newbury）被拦停，好让一列慢腾腾的上行车调线让路，放一趟学校专列过去。专列里挤满了少男少女，错车而过时，朝这边欢天喜地、见人就挥地打着招呼。

“可不是嘛！”莱昂点点头。“复活节假期开始了。我倒把这给忘了。”

到了斯温登，两人下车。这时，那女人才头一回稍稍透露出此行的目的。

“咱们得待在这个月台上，”她紧张兮兮地说，“我在等一个人，我也想让您见见她，卢卡斯先生。”

===Original===

Presently another special ran into the station and the majority of the passengers in this train also were children. Several alighted at the junction, apparently to change for some other destination than London, and Leon was talking to the woman, who he knew was not listening, when he saw her face light up. She left him with a little gasp and walked quickly along the platform to greet a tall, pretty girl wearing the crimson and white hat-ribbon of a famous West of England school.

“Why, Mrs. Jones, it is so kind of you to come down to see me. I wish you wouldn’t take so much trouble. I should be only too happy to come to London,” she laughed. “Is this a friend of yours?”

She shook hands with Leon, her eyes smiling her friendliness.

“It’s all right, Miss Grace,” said Mrs. Jones, agitated. “I just thought I’d pop down and have a look at you. How are you getting on at school, miss?”

“Oh, splendidly,” said the girl. “I’ve won a scholarship.”

“Isn’t that lovely!” said Mrs. Jones in an awestricken voice. “You always was wonderful, my dear.”

===Chinese===

不多时，又有一趟专列开进站来，这趟车上的乘客大半也是孩子。有几个人在这个枢纽站下了车，看样子是要转车去伦敦以外的地方。莱昂正跟那女人说话——他明知她一句也没听进——忽见她脸上豁然一亮。她轻轻啊了一声撇下他，快步沿着月台迎上前去，迎接一个高挑漂亮的姑娘；姑娘帽上系着英国西部一所名校的绯白两色帽带。

“哎呀，琼斯太太，您特地跑下来看我，真是太好了。我希望您别这么劳神。我到伦敦去，再乐意不过了。”她笑起来。“这位是您的朋友吗？”

她同莱昂握了握手，眼睛里盈盈的笑意透着友善。

“没什么，格蕾丝（Grace）小姐，”琼斯太太激动不安地说，“我就是想着突然跑下来瞧瞧你。你在学校里过得怎么样，小姐？”

“哦，好得很，”姑娘说，“我得了一份奖学金。”

“这可真了不得！”琼斯太太用一种敬畏的声调说。“你一向都是了不起的，亲爱的。”

===Original===

The girl turned to Leon.

“Mrs. Jones was my nurse, you know, years and years ago, weren’t you, Mrs. Jones?”

Amelia Jones nodded.

“How is your husband? Is he still unpleasant?”

===Chinese===

姑娘转向莱昂。

“琼斯太太从前是我的保姆呢，那是好多年好多年以前了，是不是，琼斯太太？”

阿梅莉亚·琼斯点了点头。

“您丈夫怎么样？还是那么不叫人痛快吗？”

===Original===

“Oh, he ain’t so bad, miss,” said Mrs. Jones bravely. “He’s a little trying at times.”

“Do you know, I should like to meet him.”

“Oh no, you wouldn’t, miss,” gasped Amelia. “That’s only your kind heart. Where are you spending your holidays, miss?” she asked.

“With some friends of mine at Clifton, Molly Walker, Sir George Walker’s daughter.”

The eyes of Amelia Jones devoured the girl and Leon knew that all the love in her barren life was lavished upon this child she had nursed. They walked up and down the platform together and when her train came in Mrs. Jones stood at the carriage door until it drew out from the station and then waited motionless looking after the express until it melted in the distance.

===Chinese===

“哦，他没那么坏，小姐，”琼斯太太硬撑着说，“就是有时候有点难缠。”

“说真的，我倒想见见他。”

“哦不，小姐，您不会想见的，”阿梅莉亚倒抽一口气说。“那不过是您一副好心肠罢了。您假期打算在哪儿过呀，小姐？”她问。

“跟我几位朋友一起过，在克利夫顿（Clifton），莫莉·沃克家，就是乔治·沃克爵士的女儿。”

阿梅莉亚·琼斯的目光贪恋地吞饮着姑娘的一举一动。莱昂明白了：她那荒芜一生里全部的爱，都毫无保留地倾洒在这个她亲手带大的孩子身上。几人在月台上来回踱着；姑娘的火车进站时，琼斯太太立在车门旁，直到列车驶出车站，仍一动不动地伫立着，目送那趟快车消失在远方。

===Original===

“I’ll never see her again!” she muttered brokenly. “I’ll never see her again! Oh, my God!”

Her face was drawn and ghastly in its pallor and Leon took her arm.

“You must come and have some refreshment, Mrs. Jones. You are very fond of that young lady?”

“Fond of her?” She turned upon him. “Fond of her? She﻿—she is my daughter!”

They had a carriage to themselves going back to Town and Mrs. Jones told her story.

===Chinese===

“我再也见不着她了！”她泣不成声地喃喃着。“我再也见不着她了！哦，上帝啊！”

她的脸抽紧了，白得吓人，莱昂挽住她的手臂。

“您得去用些茶点，琼斯太太。您很疼那位年轻小姐吧？”

“疼她？”她猛地转过身来对着他。“疼她？她——她是我的女儿啊！”

回城的车厢里只有他们两个，琼斯太太讲起了她的故事。

===Original===

“Grace was three years old when her father got into trouble,” she said. “He had always been a brute and I think he’d been under the eyes of the police since he was a bit of a kid. I didn’t know this when I married him. I was nursemaid in a house that he’d burgled and I was discharged because I’d left the kitchen door ajar for him, not knowing that he was a thief. He did one long lagging and when he came out he swore he wouldn’t go back to prison again, and the next time if there was any danger of an alarm being raised, he would make it a case of murder. He and another man got into touch with a rich bookmaker on Blackheath. Bash used to do his dirty work for him, but they quarrelled and Bash and his pal burgled the house and got away with nearly nine thousand pounds.

===Chinese===

“格蕾丝三岁那年，她爹惹上了官司，”她说，“他一向是个恶棍，我想他打从还是个小毛孩起，就在警察的眼睛里挂了号。我嫁他的时候不知道这些。我在一户人家当保姆，那家让他给偷了，我因为给他把厨房门留了道缝，就叫人家辞退了——那时候我不知道他是个贼。他蹲过一回长牢，出来时赌咒发誓，说再也不进监狱了；还说下一回，只要有人有拉响警报的危险，他就干脆弄出一条人命来。他跟另一个人搭上了布莱克希思（Blackheath）一个有钱的马票商。巴什从前净替他干脏活，后来两人闹翻了，巴什和他的同伙偷了那人家，卷走了将近九千镑。”

===Original===

“It was a big race day and Bash knew there’d be a lot of money in notes that had been taken on the racecourse and that couldn’t be traced. I thought he’d killed this man at first. It wasn’t his fault that he hadn’t. He walked into the room and bashed him as he lay in bed﻿—that was Bash’s way﻿—that’s how he got his name. He thought there’d be a lot of enquiries and gave me the money to look after. I had to put the notes into an old beer jar half full of sand, ram in the cork and cover the cork and the neck with candle-wax so that the water couldn’t get through, and then put it in the cistern which he could reach from one of the upstairs rooms at the back of the house. I was nearly mad with fear because I thought the gentleman had been killed, but I did as I was told and sunk the jar in the cistern. That night Bash and his mate were getting away to the north of England when they were arrested at Euston Station. Bash’s friend was killed, for he ran across the line in front of an engine, but they caught Bash and the house was searched from end to end. He got fifteen years’ penal servitude and he would have been out two years ago if he hadn’t been a bad character in prison.

“When he was in gaol I had to sit down and think, Mr. Lucas, and my first thought was of my child. I saw the kind of life that she was going to grow up to, the surroundings, the horrible slums, the fear of the police, for I knew that Bash would spend a million if he had it in a few weeks. I knew I was free of Bash for at least twelve years and I thought and I thought and at last I made up my mind.

===Chinese===

“那天是大赛马日，巴什知道家里会有大把赛马场上收进来的钞票，没法追查踪迹。起先我还当他把那位先生打死了呢。没打死，可不是他心慈手软。他闯进房去，照着躺在床上的人一顿痛殴——巴什就是这么个做法——他这名字就是这么来的。他料到事后必有一番大追查，就把钱交给我保管。我只好把那些钞票塞进一只装了半罐沙子的旧啤酒罐，把软木塞砸实，再用蜡烛油把塞子和罐颈封了个严实，好叫水渗不进去，然后把它放进水箱——从屋子后头楼上一间屋里就够得着那水箱。我吓得几乎疯了，因为我认定那位先生已经死了；可我还是照吩咐做了，把罐子沉进了水箱。当天夜里，巴什和他同伙正要逃往英格兰北部，在尤斯顿车站落了网。巴什的同伙送了命——他从一台机车前面横穿铁道，给撞死了——巴什倒叫他们抓住了，那座房子从头到尾搜了个底朝天。他判了十五年苦役；要不是他在牢里也是个劣迹昭彰的坏分子，两年前就放出来了。”

“他坐了牢，我就只好坐下来前前后后地想，卢卡斯先生，我头一个想到的就是我的孩子。我看得清清楚楚，她将来要过的是什么样的日子：那种环境，那些可怕的贫民窟，对警察的恐惧——因为我知道，巴什手里就是有一百万，不出几个星期也能花个精光。我知道至少有十二年，巴什缠不上我。我想啊想啊，想啊想啊，最后拿定了主意。”

===Original===

“It was twelve months after he was in gaol that I dared get the money, for the police were still keeping their eye on me as the money had not been found. I won’t tell you how I bought grand clothes so that nobody would suspect I was a working woman or how I changed the money.

“I put it all into shares. I’m not well educated, but I read the newspapers for months, the columns about money. At first I was puzzled and I could make no end to it, but after a while I got to understand and it was in an Argentine company that I invested the money, and I got a lawyer in Bermondsey to make a trust of it. She gets the interest every quarter and pays her own bills﻿—I’ve never touched a penny of it. The next thing was to get my little girl out of the neighbourhood, and I sent her away to a home for small children﻿—it broke my heart to part with her﻿—until she was old enough to go into a school. I used to see her regularly and when, after my first visit, I found she had almost forgotten who I was, I pretended that I’d been her nurse﻿—and that’s the story.”

===Chinese===

“他坐牢十二个月以后，我才敢去动那笔钱，因为赃款一直没有找到，警察还盯着我呢。我怎么买了体面的衣裳、叫谁也疑心不到我是个做工的女人，那笔钱又是怎么换开的——这些我就不告诉您了。”

“我把钱全买了股票。我没受过多少教育，可是我一连几个月读报纸，读那些讲钱经的栏目。起初我满头雾水，怎么也理不出个头绪；过了一阵子，我渐渐看懂了，就把钱投进了一家阿根廷公司，又请伯蒙德赛（Bermondsey）的一个律师立了个信托。她每个季度领利息，自己付自己的账——那里头我一个子儿也没沾过。接下来一件事，是把我的小姑娘带出这个街区：我把她送进了一家幼童养育院——骨肉分离，把我的心都撕碎了——直住到她够上学的年纪。我从前按时去看她；头一回探望之后，我发现她差不多已经忘了我是谁，我就装作从前的身份不过是她的保姆——事情的全部，就是这些。”

===Original===

Gonsalez was silent.

“Does your husband know?”

“He knows I spent the money,” said the woman staring blankly out of the window. “He knows that the girl is at a good school. He’ll find out,” she spoke almost in a whisper. “He’ll find out!”

So that was the tragedy! Leon was struck dumb by the beauty of this woman’s sacrifice. When he found his voice again, he asked:

“Why do you think he will kill you? These kind of people threaten.”

===Chinese===

冈萨雷斯沉默着。

“您丈夫知道吗？”

“他知道我花掉了那笔钱，”女人说，茫然地凝望着窗外，“他知道那姑娘在一所好学校里。他会查出来的，”她说话的声音低得几乎是耳语，“他会查出来的！”

原来悲剧是在这里！这女人牺牲之壮美，惊得莱昂哑口无言。等他重新找回声音，他问：

“您为什么认定他会杀您？这号人，向来只会嘴上吓唬吓唬。”

===Original===

“Bash doesn’t threaten as a rule,” she interrupted him. “It’s the questions he’s been asking people who know me. People from Deptford who he’s met in prison. Asking what I do at nights, what time I go to bed, what I do in the daytime. That’s Bash’s way.”

“I see,” said Leon. “Has anybody given him the necessary particulars?” he asked.

She shook her head.

“They’ve done their best for me,” she said. “They are bad characters and they commit crimes, but there’s some good hearts amongst them. They have told him nothing.”

“Are you sure?”

“I’m certain. If they had he wouldn’t be still asking. Why, Toby Brown came up from Devizes a month ago and told me Bash was there and was still asking questions about me. He’d told Toby that he’d never do another lagging and that he reckoned he’d be alive up to Midsummer Day if they caught him.”

===Chinese===

“巴什往常可不兴吓唬人，”她打断他，“是他一直在盘问那些认识我的人——德普特福德的人，他在监狱里结识的。问我夜里做什么，几点上床，白天都干些什么。巴什就是这个路数。”

“我明白了，”莱昂说，“那么，必要的情况有人提供给他吗？”他问。

她摇了摇头。

“他们为我算是仁至义尽了，”她说，“他们都是些坏分子，也确实作奸犯科，可他们中间也有好心肠的人。他们什么也没有告诉他。”

“您能肯定？”

“我十成肯定。要是有人告诉过他，他就不会还在打听了。可不是嘛，一个月前托比·布朗从迪韦齐斯过来，告诉我巴什在那儿，还在打听我的事。他跟托比说，他这辈子再也不蹲大牢了；还说，就算他们抓住他，他算准自己也能活到仲夏日。”

===Original===

Leon went up to his flat that night exalted.

“What have you been doing with yourself?” asked Manfred. “I for my part have been lunching with the excellent Mr. Fare.”

“And I have been moving in a golden haze of glory! Not my own, no, not my own, Manfred,” he shook his head, “but the glory of Amelia Jones. A wonderful woman, George. For her sake I am going to take a month’s holiday, during which time you can go back to Spain and see our beloved Poiccart and hear all about the onions.”

“I would like to go back to Madrid for a few days,” said Manfred thoughtfully. “I find London particularly attractive, but if you really are going to take a holiday﻿—where are you spending it, by the way?”

“In Devizes Gaol,” replied Gonsalez cheerfully, and Manfred had such faith in his friend that he offered no comment.

Leon Gonsalez left for Devizes the next afternoon. He arrived in the town at dusk and staggered unsteadily up the rise toward the marketplace. At ten o’clock that night a police constable found him leaning against a wall at the back of the Bear Hotel, singing foolish songs, and ordered him to move away. Whereupon Leon addressed him in language for which he was at the time (since he was perfectly sober) heartily ashamed. Therefore he did appear before a bench of magistrates the next morning, charged with being drunk, using abusive language and obstructing the police in the execution of their duty.

===Chinese===

那天夜里，莱昂回到公寓，心神为之昂扬。

“你这一向都在忙些什么？”曼弗雷德问。“我自己呢，同那位出色的费尔（Fare）先生吃了顿午饭。”

“而我，是在一团金色的荣光里走动！不是我自己的光，不是，曼弗雷德，不是我自己的，”他摇摇头，“是阿梅莉亚·琼斯的光。一个了不起的女人，乔治。为了她，我要去度一个月的假；这期间呢，你可以回西班牙去看看我们亲爱的波伊卡特，把洋葱的种种近况听个够。”

“我倒确实想回马德里待上几天，”曼弗雷德若有所思地说，“我觉得伦敦格外迷人。不过，你要真去休假——顺便问一句，你打算上哪儿去度假？”

“迪韦齐斯监狱。”冈萨雷斯喜滋滋地答道。曼弗雷德对这位朋友信心十足，竟没有半句多话。

第二天下午，莱昂·冈萨雷斯动身去了迪韦齐斯。他黄昏时分进了城，脚下虚浮、踉踉跄跄地顺着上坡往集市广场蹭。当晚十点钟，一名巡警发现他倚在熊旅店（the Bear Hotel）的后墙根，唱着傻里傻气的歌，便勒令他走开。莱昂当即回敬了他一通话——那种话，他当时（鉴于他其实滴酒未沾）可是打心眼里羞愧。于是第二天上午，他果真被押上了治安法官的法庭，罪名是酗酒、出言辱骂并阻挠警察执行公务。

===Original===

“This is hardly a case which can be met by imposing a fine,” said the staid chairman of the Bench. “Here is a stranger from London who comes into this town and behaves in a most disgusting manner. Is anything known against the man?”

“Nothing, sir,” said the gaoler regretfully.

“You will pay a fine of twenty shillings or go to prison for twenty-one days.”

“I would much rather go to prison than pay,” said Leon truthfully.

So they committed him to the local gaol as he had expected. Twenty-one days later, looking very brown and fit, he burst into the flat and Manfred turned with outstretched hands.

===Chinese===

“此案恐怕不是一笔罚款所能了结的，”治安庭那位持重的庭长说，“一个伦敦来的外乡人，进到本镇地面上，行止竟如此不堪。此人有何劣迹在案吗？”

“没有，先生。”狱卒不无惋惜地说。

“着令缴纳罚金二十先令，否则入狱二十一天。”

“我宁可坐牢，也不交钱。”莱昂说的句句是实话。

于是，正如他所料，他被押进了本地监狱。二十一天后，他晒得黝黑、精神抖擞地闯进公寓，曼弗雷德转过身来，向他伸出双手。

===Original===

“I heard you were back,” said Leon joyously. “I’ve had a great time! They rather upset my calculations by giving me three weeks instead of a month, and I was afraid that I’d get back before you.”

“I came back yesterday,” said George and his eyes strayed to the sideboard.

Six large Spanish onions stood in a row and Leon Gonsalez doubled up with mirth. It was not until he had changed into more presentable garments that he told of his experience.

“Bash Jones had undoubtedly homicidal plans,” he said. “The most extraordinary case of facial anamorphosis I have seen. I worked with him in the tailor’s shop. He is coming out next Monday.”

“He welcomed you, I presume, when he discovered you were from Deptford?” said Manfred dryly.

Leon nodded.

===Chinese===

“听说你回来了，”莱昂欢天喜地地说，“我过了段好日子！他们只关了我三个星期，没有关满一个月，倒把我的盘算搅乱了，我还生怕赶在你前头回来呢。”

“我昨天回来的。”乔治说，眼睛却溜向了餐具柜。

六只硕大的西班牙洋葱一字排开，莱昂·冈萨雷斯笑得直不起腰来。直到换上一身体面些的衣裳，他才讲起自己的经历。

“巴什·琼斯毫无疑问怀着杀人的计划，”他说，“这是我见过的最奇特的一例面部变形（facial anamorphosis）。我在监狱缝工场跟他一起干活。他下周一出狱。”

“他发现你是德普特福德人之后，想必对你热络得很吧？”曼弗雷德干巴巴地说。

莱昂点了点头。

===Original===

“He intends to kill his wife on the third of the month, which is the day after he is released,” he said.

“Why so precise?” asked Manfred in surprise.

“Because that is the only night she sleeps in the house alone. There are usually two young men lodgers who are railwaymen and these do duty until three in the morning on the third of every month.”

“Is this the truth or are you making it up?” asked Manfred.

“I did make it up,” admitted Gonsalez. “But this is the story I told and he swallowed it eagerly. The young men have no key, so they come in by the kitchen door which is left unlocked. The kitchen door is reached by a narrow passage which runs the length of Little Mill Street and parallel with the houses. Oh yes, he was frightfully anxious to secure information, and he told me that he would never come back to gaol again except for a short visit. An interesting fellow. I think he had better die,” said Leon, with some gravity. “Think of the possibilities for misery, George. This unfortunate girl, happy in her friends, well-bred﻿—”

===Chinese===

“他打算本月三号动手杀他的妻子，也就是他获释的第二天。”他说。

“为什么掐得这么准？”曼弗雷德诧异地问。

“因为那是唯一一夜她独自睡在屋里。家里平时住着两个年轻房客，都是铁路上的人，每月三号当班要到凌晨三点。”

“这是实情，还是你编造的？”曼弗雷德问。

“是我编造的，”冈萨雷斯坦白道，“可这正是我讲给他听的故事，他听得如获至宝、深信不疑。那两个年轻人没有钥匙，所以从厨房门进来，那扇门是不上锁的。去厨房门要先走一条窄窄的夹道，那夹道贯通小米尔街（Little Mill Street）全长，与两排房子平行。哦没错，他急不可耐地想套取情报。他还告诉我，他这辈子再也不会进监狱了——除非是来小坐片刻。一个有意思的家伙。我看他还是死了的好，”莱昂说，语气有几分郑重，“想想那种种苦痛的可能吧，乔治。这个不幸的姑娘，有好友相伴，又有教养——”

===Original===

“Would you say that,” smiled Manfred, “with Bash for a father?”

“Well-bred, I repeat,” said Gonsalez firmly. “Breeding is merely a quality acquired through lifelong association with gentlefolk. Put the son of a duke in the slums and he’ll grow up a peculiar kind of slum child, but a slum child nevertheless. Think of the horror of it. Dragging this child back to the kennels of Deptford, for that will be the meaning of it, supposing this Mr. Bash Jones does not kill his wife. If he kills her then the grisly truth is out. No, I think we had better settle this Mr. Bash Jones.”

“I agree,” said Manfred, puffing thoughtfully at his cigar, and Leon Gonsalez sat down at the table with Browning’s poems open before him and read, pausing now and again to look thoughtfully into space as he elaborated the method by which Bash Jones should die.

On the afternoon of the third, Mrs. Amelia Jones was called away by telegram. She met Leon Gonsalez at Paddington Station.

===Chinese===

“她父亲是巴什，这话你也说得出口？”曼弗雷德微笑道。

“有教养，我再说一遍，”冈萨雷斯斩钉截铁地说，“教养，不过是终身与体面人厮混熏染出来的一种品性。把公爵的儿子丢进贫民窟，他会长成一种别具一格的贫民窟孩子，但终究还是贫民窟孩子。想想那有多可怕吧。假如这位巴什·琼斯先生不杀他的妻子，那么此中意味就是：把这孩子拖回德普特福德的狗窝里去。如果他杀了她，那骇人的真相便会大白于天下。不，我看我们最好还是把这位巴什·琼斯先生料理掉。”

“我赞成。”曼弗雷德说着，若有所思地喷着雪茄烟。莱昂·冈萨雷斯在桌边坐下，面前摊开勃朗宁（Browning）的诗集，一边读，一边不时停下来出神地望向半空，细细推敲让巴什·琼斯送命的办法。

三号下午，阿梅莉亚·琼斯太太被一封电报支开了。她在帕丁顿车站见到了莱昂·冈萨雷斯。

===Original===

“You have brought your key with you, Mrs. Jones?”

“Yes, sir,” said the woman in surprise, then, “Do you know that my husband is out of prison?”

“I know, I know,” said Gonsalez, “and because he is free I want you to go away for a couple of nights. I have some friends in Plymouth. They will probably meet you at the station and if they do not meet you, you must go to this address.”

He gave her an address of a boardinghouse that he had secured from a Plymouth newspaper.

“Here is some money. I insist upon your taking it. My friends are very anxious to help you.”

She was in tears when he left her.

===Chinese===

“钥匙您随身带着吧，琼斯太太？”

“带着呢，先生，”女人诧异地说，随即又道，“您可知道，我男人出狱了？”

“我知道，我知道，”冈萨雷斯说，“正因为他自由了，我才要您出门避上两晚。我在普利茅斯有几个朋友。他们多半会到车站接您；要是没接着，您就到这个地址去。”

他给了她一家寄宿公寓的地址，那是他从一份普利茅斯报纸上弄来的。

“这儿有点钱。您务必收下。我的朋友们都急着想帮您一把。”

他离开她时，她已经泪眼婆娑。

===Original===

“You are sure you have locked up your house?” said Leon at parting.

“I’ve got the key here, sir.”

She opened her bag and he noticed that now her hands trembled all the time.

“Let me see,” said Leon taking the bag in his hand and peering at the interior in his shortsighted way. “Yes, there it is.”

He put in his hand, brought it out apparently empty and closed the bag again.

“Goodbye, Mrs. Jones,” he said, “and don’t lose courage.”

===Chinese===

“您家里确实都锁好了？”临别时莱昂说。

“钥匙就在这儿呢，先生。”

她打开手提包，他注意到，她的手这会儿一直抖个不停。

“我瞧瞧。”莱昂说着接过提包，用他近视的目光朝包里端详。“嗯，在这儿呢。”

他把手伸进去，抽出来时看样子空空如也，随即将包又合上了。

“再会，琼斯太太，”他说，“可别泄了勇气。”

===Original===

When dark fell Leon Gonsalez arrived in Little Mill Street carrying a bulky something in a black cloth bag. He entered the house unobserved, for the night was wet and gusty and Little Mill Street crouched over its scanty fires.

He closed the door behind him and with the aid of his pocket lamp found his way to the one poor bedroom in the tiny house. He turned down the cover, humming to himself, then very carefully he removed the contents of the bag, the most important of which was a large glass globe.

Over this he carefully arranged a black wig and searched the room for articles of clothing which might be rolled into a bundle. When he had finished his work, he stepped back and regarded it with admiration. Then he went downstairs, unlocked the kitchen door, and to make absolutely certain crossed the little yard and examined the fastening of the gate which led from the lane. The lock apparently was permanently out of order and he went back satisfied.

In one corner of the room was a clothes hanger, screened from view by a length of cheap cretonne. He had cleared this corner of its clothing to make up the bundle on the bed. Then he sat down in a chair and waited with the patience which is the peculiar attribute of the scientist.

The church bells had struck two when he heard the back gate creak, and rising noiselessly took something from his pocket and stepped behind the cretonne curtain. It was not a house in which one could move without sound, for the floorboards were old and creaky and every stair produced a creak. But the man who was creeping from step to step was an artist and Leon heard no other sound until the door slowly opened and a figure came in.

===Chinese===

夜幕四合，莱昂·冈萨雷斯来到小米尔街，手里拎着一只黑布袋，装着件鼓鼓囊囊的物事。他神不知鬼不觉地进了屋，因为那晚又湿又风紧，小米尔街正蜷缩在自家星星点点的炉火旁。

他反手掩上房门，借着袖珍手电的光，摸到小屋里唯一一间寒素的卧室。他哼着小调翻开被褥，然后小心翼翼地取出袋子里的东西，其中最要紧的，是一只大玻璃球。

他在玻璃球上细心地布好一顶黑色假发，又在屋里搜罗可以卷作一卷的衣物。活计做罢，他退后一步，满怀欣赏地端详自己的成果。随后他下楼去，开了厨房门上的锁；为保万无一失，又穿过小院子，查看那扇由小巷进来的院门闩得牢不牢。那把锁显然早就坏了，他满意地回身进屋。

房间一角立着一个挂衣架，用一幅廉价的印花布遮着。他已经把那一角的衣裳清了空，卷成了床上那一卷。然后他在一把椅子上坐下，以科学家独有的耐心，静静等候。

教堂的钟敲过两点，他听见后院门吱呀一响，便无声无息地起身，从口袋里取出一样东西，闪身藏到印花布帘后面。这可不是一所能悄没声儿走动的房子：地板老旧，一踩一呻吟，每级楼梯都要吱嘎作声。可是那个一级一级往上蹭的人是个行家，直到房门缓缓推开、一条人影闪了进来，莱昂再没有听见半点声息。

===Original===

It moved with stealthy steps across the room and stood for a few seconds by the side of the bulky figure in the bed. Apparently he listened and was satisfied. Then Leon saw a stick rise and fall.

Bash Jones did not say a word until he heard the crash of the broken glass. Then he uttered an oath and Leon heard him fumble in his pocket for his matches. The delay was fatal. The chlorine gas, compressed at a pressure of many atmospheres, surged up around him. He choked, turned to run and fell, and the yellow gas rolled over him in a thick and turgid cloud.

Leon Gonsalez stepped from his place of concealment and the dying man staring up saw two enormous glass eyes and the snout-like nozzle of the respirator and went bewildered to his death.

Leon collected the broken glass and carefully wrapped the pieces in his bag. He replaced the clothes with the most extraordinary care and put away the wig and tidied the room before he opened the window and the door. Then he went to the front of the house and opened those windows too. A southwester was blowing and by the morning the house would be free from gas.

Not until he was in the back yard did he remove the gas mask he wore and place that too in the bag.

===Chinese===

那人影蹑手蹑脚地穿过房间，在床上那团臃肿的人形旁边立了几秒钟。他显然侧耳听了听，觉得满意。接着，莱昂看见一根手杖扬起，落下。

巴什·琼斯一声也没吭，直到听见玻璃砸碎的脆响。他咒骂了一声，莱昂听见他在口袋里摸索火柴。这一耽搁，便断送了他的性命。压缩在好多个大气压下的氯气，在他周身轰然涌起。他呛得透不过气，转身要逃，一头栽倒；黄色的毒气翻滚漫过他，凝成浓浊不堪的一团。

莱昂·冈萨雷斯从藏身之处跨了出来。那垂死的人仰面凝望，看见两只硕大无朋的玻璃眼睛，和防毒面具上猪嘴似的凸出部，于是一片茫然地奔赴了死亡。

莱昂收拢碎玻璃，小心翼翼地把碎片包好，收进袋里。他以超乎寻常的细心把衣物归回原位，收好假发，把房间收拾停当，然后才打开窗子和房门。他又走到房子前部，把那几扇窗也一一打开。外面刮着西南风，到早晨，屋里的毒气便会散尽。

直到进了后院，他才摘下脸上戴着的防毒面具，也把它放进了袋子。

===Original===

An hour later he was in his own bed in a deep, untroubled sleep.

Mrs. Jones slept well that night, and in a dainty cubicle somewhere in the west of England a slim girlish figure in pyjamas snuggled into her pillow and sighed happily.

But Bash Jones slept soundest of all.

===Chinese===

一小时后，他已睡进自己的床里，睡得深沉安稳，了无梦扰。

那天夜里，琼斯太太睡得很好；在英格兰西部某地一间精致的小卧室里，一个纤细的少女身影穿着睡衣，惬意地偎进枕头，幸福地叹了口气。

而巴什·琼斯，睡得比谁都沉。

## The Man Who Was Happy / 幸福的人

===Original===

On a pleasant evening in early summer, Leon Gonsalez descended from the top of a motor-omnibus at Piccadilly Circus and walking briskly down the Haymarket, turned into Jermyn Street apparently oblivious of the fact that somebody was following on his heels.

Manfred looked up from his writing as his friend came in, and nodded smilingly as Leon took off his light overcoat and made his way to the window overlooking the street.

“What are you searching for so anxiously, Leon?” he asked.

“Jean Prothero, of 75 Barside Buildings, Lambeth,” said Leon, not taking his eyes from the street below. “Ah, there he is, the industrious fellow!”

“Who is Jean Prothero?”

===Chinese===

初夏一个晴和的傍晚，莱昂·冈萨雷斯（Leon Gonsalez）在皮卡迪利广场（Piccadilly Circus）从一辆公共汽车的顶层下来，脚步轻快地沿海马克特（Haymarket）一路走去，拐进杰明街（Jermyn Street），对有人正紧紧跟在身后这件事，看样子浑然不觉。

朋友进屋时，曼弗雷德（Manfred）从手头的文稿上抬起头来；莱昂脱下薄外套，径直走向俯临街面的窗前，他便含笑点了点头。

“你在那么起劲地找什么，莱昂？”他问。

“兰贝斯（Lambeth）巴赛德公寓（Barside Buildings）75 号的让·普罗瑟罗（Jean Prothero）。”莱昂答道，眼睛没有离开楼下的街道，“啊，他在那儿呢，这个勤快人！”

“让·普罗瑟罗是谁？”

===Original===

Gonsalez chuckled.

“A very daring man,” evaded Leon, “to wander about the West End at this hour.” He looked at his watch. “Oh no, not so daring,” he said, “everybody who is anybody is dressing for dinner just now.”

“A ladder larcenist?” suggested Manfred, and Leon chuckled again.

“Nothing so vulgar,” he said. “By ladder larcenist I presume you mean the type of petty thief who puts a ladder against a bedroom window whilst the family are busy at dinner downstairs, and makes off with the odd scraps of jewellery he can find?”

Manfred nodded.

===Chinese===

冈萨雷斯轻声笑了。

“一个胆子很大的人，”莱昂避而不答，“敢在这个时辰到西区（West End）来逛。”他看了看表。“哦不，也算不得大胆，”他说，“眼下但凡有头有脸的人物，都在更衣预备晚宴呢。”

“爬梯窃贼？”曼弗雷德猜道，莱昂又笑起来。

“没那么俗气，”他说，“你说的爬梯窃贼，想必是指那类小贼：趁一家人在楼下用晚餐，把梯子往卧室窗上一搭，再把能找到的零星首饰席卷而去？”

曼弗雷德点了点头。

===Original===

“That is the official description of this type of criminal,” he agreed.

Leon shook his head.

“No, Mr. Prothero is interesting,” he said. “Interesting for quite another reason. In the first place, he is a bald-headed criminal, or potential criminal, and as you know, my dear George, criminals are rarely bald. They are coarse-haired, and they are thin-haired: they have such personal eccentricities as parting their hair on the wrong side, but they are seldom bald. The dome of Mr. Prothero’s head is wholly innocent of hair of any kind. He is the second mate of a tramp steamer engaged in the fruit trade between the Canary Islands and Southampton. He has a very pretty girl for a wife and, curiously enough, a ladder larcenist for a brother-in-law, and I have excited his suspicion quite unwittingly. Incidentally,” he added as though it were a careless afterthought, “he knows that I am one of the Four Just Men.”

Manfred was silent. Then:

“How does he know that?” he asked quietly.

===Chinese===

“官方对这类罪犯的描述正是如此。”他表示同意。

莱昂摇了摇头。

“不，普罗瑟罗先生这个人才有意思，”他说，“有意思全在另一层缘故。头一件，他是个秃头罪犯——或者说潜在的罪犯；而你知道，我亲爱的乔治，罪犯极少秃顶。他们头发粗硬，头发稀疏，有这样那样的个人怪癖，比如头路偏偏分在错的一边，可很少秃顶。普罗瑟罗先生那颗头颅寸草不生。他是一艘跑水果运输的不定期货轮上的二副，往返于加那利群岛（Canary Islands）与南安普敦（Southampton）之间。他娶了个标致得很的姑娘做太太，说来也怪，内兄偏偏是个爬梯窃贼，而我完全出于无心，惹起了他的疑心。顺带说一句，”他补了一句，像是随口想起的闲话，“他知道我是四义士（The Four Just Men）之一。”

曼弗雷德沉默了。片刻后：

“他是怎么知道的？”他不动声色地问。

===Original===

Leon had taken off his coat and had slipped his arms into a faded alpaca jacket; he did not reply until he had rolled and lit an untidy Spanish cigarette.

“Years ago, when there was a hue and cry after that pernicious organisation, whose name I have mentioned, an organisation which, in its humble way, endeavoured to right the injustice of the world and to mete out to evildoers the punishment which the ponderous machinery of the Law could not inflict, you were arrested, my dear George, and consigned to Chelmsford Gaol. From there you made a miraculous escape, and on reaching the coast you and I and Poiccart were taken aboard the yacht of our excellent friend the Prince of the Asturias, who honoured us by acting as the fourth of our combination.”

Manfred nodded.

“On that ship was Mr. Jean Prothero,” said Leon. “How he came to be on the yacht of His Serene Highness I will explain at a later stage, but assuredly he was there. I never forget faces, George, but unfortunately I am not singular in this respect, Mr. Prothero remembered me, and seeing me in Barside Buildings﻿—”

“What were you doing in Barside Buildings?” asked Manfred with a faint smile.

===Chinese===

莱昂已经脱下外衣，把胳膊套进一件褪色的羊驼呢上衣；他卷好一支歪歪扭扭的西班牙香烟，点上吸着了，这才作答。

“多年以前，我方才提到的那个为祸不浅的组织正被闹得满城风雨、四处缉拿——那是个以其谦逊的方式致力于匡正世间不公、把法律这部笨重机器施加不到的惩罚加诸恶人的组织——我亲爱的乔治，你就在那时被捕，被押进了切姆斯福德监狱（Chelmsford Gaol）。你从那里奇迹般脱身，一到海岸，你、我和波伊卡特（Poiccart）便被接上了我们那位极好的朋友阿斯图里亚斯亲王（the Prince of the Asturias）的游艇，亲王屈尊充任了我们四人组合中的第四人。”

曼弗雷德点了点头。

“那条船上就有让·普罗瑟罗先生，”莱昂说，“他怎么会到亲王殿下的游艇上去，我日后再作解释，但他确凿就在船上。我从不忘记面孔，乔治，可惜在这方面并非我独一份，普罗瑟罗先生也记住了我，而他在巴赛德公寓看见了我——”

“你到巴赛德公寓去做什么？”曼弗雷德微微笑着问。

===Original===

“In Barside Buildings,” replied Leon impressively, “are two men unknown to one another, both criminals, and both colour-blind!”

Manfred put down his pen and turned, prepared for a lecture on criminal statistics, for he had noticed the enthusiasm in Gonsalez’s voice.

“By means of these two men,” said Leon joyously, “I am able to refute the perfectly absurd theories which both Mantegazza and Scheml have expounded, namely, that criminals are never colour-blind. The truth is, my dear George, both these men have been engaged in crime since their early youth. Both have served terms of imprisonment, and what is more important, their fathers were colour-blind and criminals!”

“Well, what about Mr. Prothero?” said Manfred, tactfully interrupting what promised to be an exhaustive disquisition upon optical defects in relation to congenital lawlessness.

“One of my subjects is Prothero’s brother-in-law, or rather, half-brother to Mrs. Prothero, her own father having been a blameless carpenter, and lives in the flat overhead. These flats are just tiny dwelling places consisting of two rooms and a kitchen. The builders of Lambeth tenements do not allow for the luxury of a bathroom. In this way I came to meet Mrs. Prothero whilst overcoming the reluctance of her brother to talk about himself.”

===Chinese===

“巴赛德公寓里，”莱昂郑重其事地答道，“住着两个互不相识的人，都是罪犯，而且都是色盲！”

曼弗雷德放下笔转过身来，准备听一堂关于犯罪统计学的讲授，因为他已经听出冈萨雷斯话音里的兴头。

“多亏这两个人，”莱昂喜滋滋地说，“我得以驳倒曼特加扎（Mantegazza）和舍姆尔（Scheml）两人都阐发过的那套荒谬绝伦的理论，即罪犯绝不会是色盲。事实是，我亲爱的乔治，这两个人从少年时代起便混迹罪途，都蹲过监狱，更要紧的是，他们的父亲也是色盲，也是罪犯！”

“那么，普罗瑟罗先生究竟是怎么回事？”曼弗雷德说，得体地打断了一篇眼看要长篇大论下去的宏论——论的是视觉缺陷与天生无法无天的关系。

“我的研究对象之一便是普罗瑟罗的内兄——更确切地说，是普罗瑟罗夫人（Mrs. Prothero）的同母异父兄弟，她自己的父亲是个清白本分的木匠——他就住在楼上那套房间里。这种公寓房不过是些小小的寓所，两间屋外带一个厨房。兰贝斯公寓楼的营造商可不预备浴室这等奢侈。我就是这样结识了普罗瑟罗夫人——当时我正设法打消她兄弟谈自己的勉强。”

===Original===

“And you met Prothero, too, I presume,” said Manfred patiently.

“No, I didn’t meet him, except by accident. He passed on the stairs and I saw him give me a swift glance. His face was in the shadow and I did not recognise him until our second meeting, which was today. He followed me home. As a matter of fact,” he added, “I have an idea that he followed me yesterday, and only came today to confirm my place of residence.”

“You’re a rum fellow,” said Manfred.

“Maybe I’ll be rummer,” smiled Leon. “Everything depends now,” he said thoughtfully, “upon whether Prothero thinks that I recognised him. If he does﻿—”

Leon shrugged his shoulders.

===Chinese===

“那么我想，普罗瑟罗本人你也见过了。”曼弗雷德耐心地说。

“没有，我没同他照面，只除了偶然一回。他在楼梯上与我交错而过，我看见他朝我飞快地瞟了一眼。他脸在暗影里，直到第二次相见我才认出他来——那就是今天。他一路尾随我回了家。其实，”他补充道，“我琢磨着他昨天就跟踪过我，今天再来，不过是要坐实我的住处。”

“你真是个怪人。”曼弗雷德说。

“也许还会更怪呢。”莱昂微笑道。“如今一切都系于一点，”他若有所思地说，“就看普罗瑟罗是不是认定我认出了他。他若认定了——”

莱昂耸了耸肩。

===Original===

“Not for the first time have I fenced with death and overcome him,” he said lightly.

Manfred was not deceived by the flippancy of his friend’s tone.

“As bad as that, eh?” he said, “and more dangerous for him, I think,” he added quietly. “I do not like the idea of killing a man because he has recognised us﻿—that course does not seem to fit in with my conception of justice.”

“Exactly,” said Leon briskly, “and there will be no need, I think. Unless, of course﻿—” he paused.

“Unless what?” asked Manfred.

“Unless Prothero really does love his wife, in which case it may be a very serious business.”

===Chinese===

“我同死亡交过手并制服了它，这已不是头一遭。”他轻描淡写地说。

朋友这份满不在乎的语气，瞒不过曼弗雷德。

“糟到那个地步了，嗯？”他说，“而且我想，对他来说更危险。”他平静地补了一句。“我可不喜欢因为一个人认出了咱们就杀掉他——那样的做法似乎不合我对正义的观念。”

“正是，”莱昂干脆利落地说，“而且我想，不至于走到那一步。当然，除非——”他顿住了。

“除非什么？”曼弗雷德问。

“除非普罗瑟罗当真爱他的妻子——真是那样，事情就可能非常严重了。”

===Original===

The next morning he strolled into Manfred’s bedroom carrying the cup of tea which the servant usually brought, and George stared up at him in amazement.

“What is the matter with you, Leon, haven’t you been to bed?”

Leon Gonsalez was dressed in what he called his “pyjama outfit”﻿—a grey flannel coat and trousers, belted at the waist, a silk shirt open at the neck and a pair of light slippers constituted his attire, and Manfred, who associated this costume with all-night studies, was not astonished when Leon shook his head.

“I have been sitting in the dining-room, smoking the pipe of peace,” he said.

“All night?” said Manfred in surprise. “I woke up in the middle of the night and I saw no light.”

“I sat in the dark,” admitted Leon. “I wanted to hear things.”

===Chinese===

第二天早晨，他端着仆人平日照例送来的那杯茶，踱进曼弗雷德的卧室，乔治讶异地仰头望着他。

“你是怎么了，莱昂，一夜没上床？”

莱昂·冈萨雷斯穿着他自称的“睡衣行头”——灰色法兰绒上衣配长裤，腰间束一条带子，真丝衬衫敞着领口，脚上一双轻便拖鞋——曼弗雷德素来把这一身穿戴同通宵用功联想在一起，所以莱昂摇头时他并不惊讶。

“我一直坐在餐室里，抽着和平烟斗。”他说。

“一整夜？”曼弗雷德诧异地说，“我半夜醒来，没看见一点灯光。”

“我坐在黑地里，”莱昂承认道，“我想听见点动静。”

===Original===

Manfred stirred his tea thoughtfully. “Is it as bad as that? Did you expect﻿—”

Leon smiled.

“I didn’t expect what I got,” he said. “Will you do me a favour, my dear George?”

“What is your favour?”

“I want you not to speak of Mr. Prothero for the rest of the day. Rather, I wish you to discuss purely scientific and agricultural matters, as becomes an honest Andalusian farmer, and moreover to speak in Spanish.”

===Chinese===

曼弗雷德若有所思地搅着茶。“事情竟糟到这个地步？你原先料到——”

莱昂微微一笑。

“我原先没料到会听见我听见的那些。”他说，“你能帮我一个忙吗，我亲爱的乔治？”

“帮什么忙？”

“我要你今天余下的时间里一个字也别提普罗瑟罗先生。非但如此，我还希望你只谈纯粹的科学和农业话题，方才配得上一个正经的安达卢西亚农夫的身份，而且，要说西班牙语。”

===Original===

Manfred frowned.

“Why?” and then: “I’m sorry, I can’t get out of the habit of being mystified, you know, Leon. Spanish and agriculture it shall be, and no reference whatever to Prothero.”

Leon was very earnest and Manfred nodded and swung out of bed.

“May I talk of taking a bath?” he asked sardonically.

===Chinese===

曼弗雷德皱起了眉头。

“为什么？”随即又说：“对不起，你知道，莱昂，我这动不动就被你弄得云里雾里的毛病是改不掉了。行，西班牙语加农业，绝口不提普罗瑟罗。”

莱昂态度极其恳切，曼弗雷德点点头，翻身下了床。

“那我可以说说洗澡的事吗？”他讥诮地问。

===Original===

Nothing particularly interesting happened that day. Once Manfred was on the point of referring to Leon’s experience, and divining the drift of his thought, Leon raised a warning finger.

Gonsalez could talk about crime, and did. He talked of its more scientific aspects and laid particular stress upon his discovery of the colour-blind criminal. But of Mr. Prothero he said no word.

After they had dined that night, Leon went out of the flat and presently returned.

“Thank heaven we can now talk without thinking,” he said.

He pulled a chair to the wall and mounted it nimbly. Above his head was a tiny ventilator fastened to the wall with screws. Humming a little tune he turned a screwdriver deftly and lifted the little grille from its socket, Manfred watching him gravely.

===Chinese===

那一天没发生什么特别有意思的事。有一回曼弗雷德正要提起莱昂的那番遭遇，莱昂猜到他念头转向何处，便竖起一根手指以示警告。

冈萨雷斯能谈犯罪，也确实谈了。他谈的是犯罪较为科学的一面，尤其着力渲染他发现色盲罪犯一事。可是关于普罗瑟罗先生，他一字不提。

那天晚上两人用过晚餐，莱昂出门去了一趟，不多时便回来了。

“谢天谢地，现在咱们说话可以不用过脑子了。”他说。

他把一把椅子拖到墙边，轻捷地登了上去。头顶上是一个用螺钉固定在墙上的小通气窗。他嘴里哼着小调，手上麻利地转动螺丝刀，把小格栅从槽座里卸了下来，曼弗雷德神情严肃地在旁看着。

===Original===

“Here it is,” said Leon. “Pull up a chair, George.”

“It” proved to be a small flat brown box four inches by four in the centre of which was a black vulcanite depression.

“Do you recognise him?” said Leon. “He is the detectaphone﻿—in other words a telephone receiver fitted with a microphonic attachment.”

“Has somebody been listening to all we’ve been saying?”

Leon nodded.

“The gentleman upstairs has had a dull and dreary day. Admitting that he speaks Spanish, and that I have said nothing which has not illuminated that branch of science which is my particular hobby,” he added modestly, “he must have been terribly bored.”

===Chinese===

“喏，就是这个。”莱昂说，“拖把椅子过来，乔治。”

那个“它”原来是个四英寸见方的扁平棕色小盒，正中凹着一块黑色硬胶。

“认得它吗？”莱昂说，“这是侦听器（detectaphone）——换句话说，就是一枚装了扩音附件的电话听筒。”

“有人一直在偷听我们说的每一句话？”

莱昂点了点头。

“楼上那位先生这一天可过得单调乏味。就算他懂西班牙语，就算我说的每一句话无不为我的特别爱好所属的那门科学增辉添彩，”他谦虚地补了一句，“他也一定闷得要命。”

===Original===

“But﻿—” began Manfred.

“He is out now,” said Gonsalez. “But to make perfectly sure﻿—”

With deft fingers he detached one of the wires by which the box was suspended in the ventilator shaft.

“Mr. Prothero came last night,” he explained. “He took the room upstairs, and particularly asked for it. This I learnt from the head waiter﻿—he adores me because I give him exactly three times the tip which he gets from other residents in these service flats, and because I tip him three times as often. I didn’t exactly know what Prothero’s game was, until I heard the tap-tap of the microphone coming down the shaft.”

He was busy re-fixing the grille of the ventilator﻿—presently he jumped down.

===Chinese===

“可是——”曼弗雷德刚要开口。

“他这会儿出去了，”冈萨雷斯说，“不过为万全起见——”

他手指灵巧，解开了把小盒悬吊在通气井里的一根电线。

“普罗瑟罗先生是昨晚来的，”他解释道，“他住进了楼上的房间，而且特意开口指名要那一间。这是我从领班侍者那里打听来的——他崇拜我，因为我给他的赏钱不多不少，恰好是这些服务公寓里其他住客所给的三倍，而且我赏钱的次数也比人家多上三倍。普罗瑟罗究竟打的什么算盘，我起初还不甚了然，直到我听见扩音器顺着通气井传下来的嗒嗒声。”

他忙着把通气窗的格栅装回原处——不多时便跳下椅来。

===Original===

“Would you like to come to Lambeth today? I do not think there is much chance of our meeting Mr. Prothero. On the other hand, we shall see Mrs. Prothero shopping at eleven o’clock in the London Road, for she is a methodical lady.”

“Why do you want me to see her?” asked Manfred.

He was not usually allowed to see the workings of any of Leon’s schemes until the dramatic denouement, which was meat and drink to him, was near at hand.

“I want you, with your wide knowledge of human nature, to tell me whether she is the type of woman for whom a bald-headed man would commit murder,” he said simply, and Manfred stared at him in amazement.

“The victim being﻿—?”

“Me!” replied Gonsalez, and doubled up with silent laughter at the blank look on Manfred’s face.

===Chinese===

“今天想不想跟我去一趟兰贝斯？我看撞见普罗瑟罗先生的机会不大。倒是正好，我们能在十一点钟见到普罗瑟罗夫人在伦敦路（London Road）买东西，因为她是位行事一板一眼的太太。”

“你为什么要我去看她？”曼弗雷德问。

莱昂的种种谋划，他素来要等到那戏剧性的结局临近——那结局于他好比佳肴美酒，是莫大的享受——才获准看一看其中的门道。

“我要凭你那渊博的人性知识，告诉我她是不是那种能让一个秃头男人为之行凶杀人的女人。”他直截了当地说，曼弗雷德惊愕地瞪着他。

“遇害的是——？”

“我！”冈萨雷斯答道，看见曼弗雷德一脸茫然，他无声地笑得前仰后合。

===Original===

It was four minutes to eleven exactly when Manfred saw Mrs. Prothero. He felt the pressure of Leon’s hand on his arm and looked.

“There she is,” said Leon.

A girl was crossing the road. She was neatly, even well-dressed for one of her class. She carried a market bag in one gloved hand, a purse in the other.

“She’s pretty enough,” said Manfred.

The girl had paused to look in a jeweller’s window and Manfred had time to observe her. Her face was sweet and womanly, the eyes big and dark, the little chin firm and rounded.

===Chinese===

整十一点差四分的时候，曼弗雷德看见了普罗瑟罗夫人。他觉出莱昂的手按在自己臂上，便望了过去。

“她来了。”莱昂说。

一个年轻女子正穿过马路。以她那个阶层而论，她穿得整洁，甚至称得上体面。她一只戴手套的手提着采买用的提袋，另一只手拿着钱袋。

“她够标致的了。”曼弗雷德说。

姑娘在一家珠宝店的橱窗前驻足张望，曼弗雷德便有了端详她的工夫。她面容甜美温柔，一双眼睛又大又黑，小巧的下巴圆润而坚毅。

===Original===

“What do you think of her?” said Leon.

“I think she’s rather a perfect specimen of young womanhood,” said Manfred.

“Come along and meet her,” said the other, and took his arm.

The girl looked round at first in surprise, and then with a smile. Manfred had an impression of flashing white teeth and scarlet lips parted in amusement. Her voice was not the voice of a lady, but it was quiet and musical.

“Good morning, Doctor,” she said to Leon. “What are you doing in this part of the world so early in the morning.”

===Chinese===

“你看她如何？”莱昂说。

“我看她简直是年轻女性中近乎完美的典范。”曼弗雷德说。

“来，见见她。”另一位说着挽起他的手臂。

姑娘回过头来，先是诧异，随即含笑。曼弗雷德得到的印象是一口皓齿闪闪发亮，猩红的双唇因笑意而绽开。她的嗓音算不上贵妇人的嗓音，却轻柔而悦耳。

“早安，医生。”她对莱昂说，“这么一大早，您怎么到这一带来啦。”

===Original===

“Doctor,” noted Manfred.

The adaptable Gonsalez assumed many professions for the purpose of securing his information.

“We have just come from Guy’s Hospital. This is Dr. Selbert,” he introduced Manfred. “You are shopping, I suppose?”

She nodded.

“Really, there was no need for me to come out, Mr. Prothero being away at the Docks for three days,” she replied.

===Chinese===

“医生。”曼弗雷德暗暗记下这个称呼。

随机应变的冈萨雷斯，为了打探想要的消息，会顶上种种职业头衔。

“我们刚从盖伊医院（Guy's Hospital）过来。这位是塞尔伯特医生（Dr. Selbert）。”他把曼弗雷德介绍给她，“您是来买东西的，我猜？”

她点了点头。

“其实我用不着出门，普罗瑟罗先生去船坞要待上三天呢。”她答道。

===Original===

“Have you seen your brother this morning?” asked Leon.

A shadow fell over the girl’s face.

“No,” she said shortly.

Evidently, thought Manfred, she was not particularly proud of her relationship. Possibly she suspected his illicit profession, but at any rate she had no desire to discuss him, for she changed the subject quickly.

They talked for a little while, and then with an apology she left them and they saw her vanish through the wide door of a grocer’s store.

===Chinese===

“今天早上见过您兄弟吗？”莱昂问。

一片阴影掠过姑娘的脸。

“没有。”她简短地说。

显然，曼弗雷德心想，她并不怎么以这层亲眷为荣。她可能疑心他干的是非法营生，但无论如何她无意谈论他，因为她很快把话头岔开了。

他们又谈了一小会儿，随后她道了声歉离开两人，他们望着她的身影消失在一家食品杂货店的宽门里。

===Original===

“Well, what do you think of her?”

“She is a very beautiful girl,” said Manfred quietly.

“The kind of girl that would make a bald-headed criminal commit a murder?” asked Leon, and Manfred laughed.

“It is not unlikely,” he said, “but why should he murder you?”

“*Nous verrons*,” replied Leon.

===Chinese===

“怎么样，你看她如何？”

“她是个非常美丽的姑娘。”曼弗雷德平静地说。

“是那种能让秃头罪犯去犯一桩谋杀案的女人么？”莱昂问，曼弗雷德笑了。

“这并非没有可能，”他说，“可他为什么要谋害你呢？”

“*Nous verrons*（咱们走着瞧）。”莱昂答道。

===Original===

When they returned to their flat in the afternoon the mail had been and there were half a dozen letters. One bearing a heavy crest upon the envelope attracted Manfred’s attention.

“Lord Pertham,” he said, looking at the signature. “Who is Lord Pertham?”

“I haven’t a *Who’s Who* handy, but I seem to know the name,” said Leon. “What does Lord Pertham want?”

“I’ll read you the note,” said Manfred.

> “ ‘**Dear Sir**,’ ” it read.
> “ ‘Our mutual friend Mr. Fare of Scotland Yard is dining with us tonight at Connaught Gardens, and I wonder whether you would come along? Mr. Fare tells me that you are one of the cleverest criminologists of the century, and as it is a study which I have made particularly my own, I shall be glad to make your acquaintance.’ ”

It was signed “Pertham” and there was a postscript running﻿—

> “Of course, this invitation also includes your friend.”

===Chinese===

下午两人回到公寓，邮件已经送到，共有六封信。其中一封信封上压着厚重的纹章，引起了曼弗雷德的注意。

“珀瑟姆勋爵（Lord Pertham）。”他看着落款说。“珀瑟姆勋爵是谁？”

“我手边没有《名人录》（*Who's Who*），不过这名字我似乎耳熟。”莱昂说，“珀瑟姆勋爵有什么事？”

“我把信念给你听。”曼弗雷德说。

> “‘**亲爱的先生**，’”信上写道。
> “‘我们共同的朋友、苏格兰场（Scotland Yard）的费尔先生（Mr. Fare）今晚将到康诺特花园（Connaught Gardens）与我们共进晚餐，不知您可愿拨冗前来？费尔先生告诉我，您是本世纪最聪明的犯罪学家之一，而犯罪学恰是我潜心研治、视作己有的学问，我很乐意结识您。’”

信的落款是“珀瑟姆”，还有一行附言——

> “当然，此项邀请亦包括您的朋友在内。”

===Original===

Manfred rubbed his chin.

“I really do not want to dine fashionably tonight,” he said.

“But I do,” said Leon promptly. “I have developed a taste for English cooking, and I seem to remember that Lord Pertham is an epicurean.”

Promptly at the hour of eight they presented themselves at the big house standing at the corner of Connaught Gardens and were admitted by a footman who took their hats and coats and showed them into a large and gloomy drawing-room.

A man was standing with his back to the fire﻿—a tall man of fifty with a mane of grey hair, that gave him an almost leonine appearance.

He came quickly to meet them.

===Chinese===

曼弗雷德摩挲着下巴。

“今晚我实在不想去赶那种时髦的饭局。”他说。

“可我想去。”莱昂当即说，“我已经吃出了英国菜的门道，而且我似乎记得，珀瑟姆勋爵是个讲究口腹之道的饕客。”

八点整，两人准时到康诺特花园街角那座大宅登门，一位男仆引他们进去，接过帽子和外套，把他们领进一间宽大而阴沉的客厅。

一个男人背朝壁炉站着——五十岁上下的高个子，一头灰发浓密如鬃，几乎让他生出几分雄狮般的气概。

他快步迎上前来。

===Original===

“Which is Mr. Fuentes?” he asked, speaking in English.

“I am Signor Fuentes,” replied Manfred, with a smile, “but it is my friend who is the criminologist.”

“Delighted to meet you both﻿—but I have an apology to make to you;” he said, speaking hurriedly. “By some mischance﻿—the stupidity of one of my men﻿—the letter addressed to Fare was not posted. I only discovered it half an hour ago. I hope you don’t mind.”

Manfred murmured something conventional and then the door opened to admit a lady.

“I want to present you to her ladyship,” said Lord Pertham.

===Chinese===

“哪位是富恩特斯先生（Mr. Fuentes）？”他用英语问道。

“在下就是富恩特斯先生。”曼弗雷德含笑答道，“不过犯罪学家是敝友。”

“见到二位十分高兴——不过我有一桩事要向你们致歉；”他说话很急，“不知怎么出了岔子——我的一个仆人蠢笨——那封写给费尔的信没有付邮。我半小时前才发现。但愿二位不要介意。”

曼弗雷德客套了两句，接着门开了，进来一位女士。

“我想把二位引见给夫人。”珀瑟姆勋爵说。

===Original===

The woman who came in was thin and vinegary, a pair of pale eyes, a light-lipped mouth and a trick of frowning deprived her of whatever charm Nature had given to her.

Leon Gonsalez, who analysed faces automatically and mechanically, thought, “Spite﻿—suspicion﻿—uncharity﻿—vanity.”

The frown deepened as she offered a limp hand.

“Dinner is ready, Pertham,” she said, and made no attempt to be agreeable to her guests.

It was an awkward meal. Lord Pertham was nervous and his nervousness might have communicated itself to the two men if they had been anything but what they were. This big man seemed to be in terror of his wife﻿—was deferential, even humble in her presence, and when at last she swept her sour face from the room he made no attempt to hide his sigh of relief.

===Chinese===

进来的那位太太又瘦又酸，一双浅色的眼睛，一张薄唇的嘴，再加上动辄蹙眉的习惯，把天生赋予她的那点魅力剥夺了个干净。

莱昂·冈萨雷斯——他分析面孔向来不假思索、习惯成自然——心中想道：“刻毒——猜疑——不仁——虚荣。”

她伸出一只软塌塌的手，眉头蹙得更紧了。

“晚饭备好了，珀瑟姆。”她说，全无意对客人作出亲和的姿态。

这顿饭吃得别扭。珀瑟姆勋爵局促不安，若这两位不是他们这般人物，他这份不安早传染过去了。这个大个子似乎怕老婆怕得要命——在她面前唯唯诺诺，甚至低声下气，等到她终于板着那张酸脸拂袖出屋，他毫不掩饰地长舒了一口气。

===Original===

“I am afraid we haven’t given you a very good dinner,” he said. “Her ladyship has had a little﻿—er﻿—disagreement with my cook.”

Apparently her ladyship was in the habit of having little disagreements with her cook, for in the course of the conversation which followed he casually mentioned certain servants in his household who were no longer in his employ. He spoke mostly of their facial characteristics, and it seemed to Manfred, who was listening as intently as his companion, that his lordship was not a great authority upon the subject. He spoke haltingly, made several obvious slips, but Leon did not correct him. He mentioned casually that he had an additional interest in criminals because his own life had been threatened.

“Let us go up and join my lady,” he said after a long and blundering exposition of some phase of criminology which Manfred could have sworn he had read up for the occasion.

They went up the broad stairs into a little drawing-room on the first floor. It was empty. His lordship was evidently surprised.

“I wonder﻿—” he began, when the door opened and Lady Pertham ran in. Her face was white and her thin lips were trembling.

===Chinese===

“恐怕我们这顿饭没能让二位吃好，”他说，“夫人跟我的厨子小小地——呃——生了一场意见。”

看来夫人跟厨子生点小意见是家常便饭，因为在随后的谈话中，他随口提到了家里几个已不再雇用的仆人。他谈的主要是他们的相貌特征，而在听得同伴侣一样专注的曼弗雷德看来，勋爵大人在这个题目上算不得多大的权威。他讲得磕磕绊绊，还明显出了几处纰漏，莱昂却并未纠正他。他又顺口提到，自己对罪犯还多一层关切，因为他自己的性命曾遭人威胁。

在就犯罪学的某个方面发了一通冗长而笨拙的议论之后——曼弗雷德敢发誓，这套东西全是他为今晚这场合临时恶补来的——他说：“咱们上楼去陪夫人吧。”

他们登上宽绰的楼梯，走进二楼一间小客厅。客厅空着。勋爵大人显然吃了一惊。

“怪了——”他刚开口，门开了，珀瑟姆夫人跑了进来。她脸色煞白，薄嘴唇直打颤。

===Original===

“Pertham,” she said rapidly, “I’m sure there’s a man in my dressing-room.”

“In your dressing-room?” said Lord Pertham, and ran out quickly.

The two men would have followed him, but he stopped halfway up the stairs and waved them back.

“You had better wait with her ladyship,” he said. “Ring for Thomas, my love,” he said.

Standing at the foot of the stairs they heard him moving about. Presently they heard a cry and the sound of a struggle. Manfred was halfway up the stairs when a door slammed above. Then came the sound of voices and a shot, followed by a heavy fall.

===Chinese===

“珀瑟姆，”她急促地说，“我敢肯定，我的化妆室里有个人。”

“在你的化妆室里？”珀瑟姆勋爵说着，疾奔了出去。

两人本要跟上去，他在楼梯半截处停住脚步，挥手叫他们退回。

“你们二位最好留下陪夫人。”他说，“按铃叫托马斯（Thomas），亲爱的。”他又说。

两人站在楼梯脚下，听见他在上面走动。不多时传来一声喊叫和扭打的声音。曼弗雷德刚奔到楼梯半腰，楼上砰的一声关上了门。接着是人声和一声枪响，随后是重重倒地的声响。

===Original===

Manfred flung himself against the door from whence the sound came.

“It’s all right,” said Lord Pertham’s voice.

A second later he unlocked the door and opened it.

“I’m afraid I’ve killed this fellow.”

The smoking revolver was still in his hand. In the middle of the floor lay a poorly dressed man and his blood stained the pearl-grey carpet.

===Chinese===

曼弗雷德朝着声音传出的那扇门猛冲过去。

“没事了。”珀瑟姆勋爵的声音说。

一秒钟后，他开锁推开了门。

“恐怕我把这家伙打死啦。”

那支冒着烟的左轮手枪还在他手里。地板正中躺着一个衣衫寒酸的男人，他的血染污了珍珠灰的地毯。

===Original===

Gonsalez walked quickly to the body and turned it over. At the first sight he knew that the man was dead. He looked long and earnestly in his face, and Lord Pertham said:

“Do you know him?”

“I think so,” said Gonsalez quietly. “He is my colour-blind criminal,” for he had recognised the brother of Mrs. Prothero.

They walked home to their lodgings that night leaving Lord Pertham closeted with a detective-inspector, and Lady Pertham in hysterics.

Neither man spoke until they reached their flat, then Leon, with a sigh of content, curled up in the big armchair and pulled lovingly at an evil-smelling cigar.

===Chinese===

冈萨雷斯快步走到尸体旁，把它翻了个身。第一眼他就看出人已经死了。他久久而专注地端详那张脸，珀瑟姆勋爵开口道：

“你认识他吗？”

“我想是的。”冈萨雷斯平静地说，“他是我那个色盲罪犯。”——他已认出这是普罗瑟罗夫人的兄弟。

当晚两人步行回寓，身后留下的是与一位探长关门密谈的珀瑟姆勋爵，和歇斯底里发作的珀瑟姆夫人。

回到公寓之前，两人谁也没有说话。进了门，莱昂才心满意足地叹了口气，蜷进那把大扶手椅，爱不释手地抽起一支气味呛人的雪茄。

===Original===

“Leon!”

He took no notice.

“Leon!”

Leon shifted his head round and met George’s eye.

“Did anything about that shooting tonight strike you as peculiar?”

===Chinese===

“莱昂！”

他不予理会。

“莱昂！”

莱昂这才转过头来，迎上乔治的目光。

“今晚那桩枪击案，你没觉得有什么地方蹊跷吗？”

===Original===

“Several things,” said Leon.

“Such as?”

“Such as the oddness of the fate that took Slippery Bill﻿—that was the name of my burglar﻿—to Lord Pertham’s house. It was not odd that he should commit the burglary, because he was a ladder larcenist, as you call him. By the way, did you look at the dead man’s hand?” he asked, twisting round and peering across the table at Manfred.

“No, I didn’t,” said the other in surprise.

“What a pity﻿—you would have thought it still more peculiar. What are the things you were thinking of?”

===Chinese===

“有好几处呢。”莱昂说。

“比如说？”

“比如命运蹊跷，竟把滑头比尔（Slippery Bill）——那是我那窃贼的名字——引到了珀瑟姆勋爵的宅子里。他去行窃本身倒不奇怪，因为他正是你所谓的爬梯窃贼。顺便问一句，你看死者的那双手了吗？”他一边问，一边扭身隔着桌子端详曼弗雷德。

“没看。”另一位诧异地说。

“真可惜——你若看了，准会觉得更加古怪。你方才想的是哪几件事？”

===Original===

“I was wondering why Lord Pertham carried a revolver. He must have had it in his pocket at dinner.”

“That is easily explained,” said Gonsalez. “Don’t you remember his telling us that his life had been threatened in anonymous letters?”

Manfred nodded.

“I had forgotten that,” he said. “But who locked the door?”

“The burglar, of course,” said Leon and smiled. And by that smile Manfred knew that he was prevaricating. “And talking of locked doors﻿—”

===Chinese===

“我在纳闷，珀瑟姆勋爵为什么随身带着左轮手枪。吃晚饭时，那枪必定就在他口袋里。”

“这好解释。”冈萨雷斯说，“你不记得他说过，有人写匿名信威胁他的性命么？”

曼弗雷德点了点头。

“这事我忘了。”他说，“可是，门是谁锁的？”

“当然是窃贼锁的。”莱昂说着微微一笑。凭这一笑，曼弗雷德便知道他在支吾。“说起锁门这件事——”

===Original===

He went into his room and returned with two little instruments that looked like the gongs of electric bells, except that there was a prong sticking up from each.

He locked the sitting-room door and placed one of these articles on the floor, sticking the spike into the bottom of the door so that it was impossible to open without exercising pressure upon the bell. He tried it and there was a shrill peal.

“That’s all right,” he said, and turned to examine the windows.

“Are you expecting burglars?”

“I am rather,” said Leon, “and really I cannot afford to lose my sleep.”

===Chinese===

他走进自己的房间，回来时手里拿着两样小器具，样子像电铃的铃盖，只是每一只上面都竖着一根尖刺。

他锁上起居室的门，把其中一件放在地板上，尖刺抵住门扇下沿，这样一来，门就非得压到铃上才开得了。他试了一下，铃发出一串刺耳的响声。

“这就妥了。”他说着转身去检查窗户。

“你这是防着窃贼上门？”

“还真是有点儿，”莱昂说，“再说，我的睡眠实在损失不起。”

===Original===

Not satisfied with the fastening of the window he pushed in a little wedge, and performed the same office to the second of the windows looking upon the street.

Another door, leading to Manfred’s room from the passage without, he treated as he had served the first.

In the middle of the night there was a frantic ring from one of the bells. Manfred leapt out of bed and switched on the light. His own door was fast and he raced into the sitting-room, but Gonsalez was there before him examining the little sentinel by the door. The door had been unlocked. He kicked away the alarm with his slippered foot.

“Come in, Lord Pertham,” he said. “Let’s talk this matter over.”

There was a momentary silence, then the sound of a slippered foot, and a man came in. He was fully dressed and hatless, and Manfred, seeing the bald head, gasped.

===Chinese===

他嫌窗子的插销不够牢靠，又塞进一枚小楔子；临街的第二扇窗，他也照样处置了一遍。

还有一扇门，从外面的过道通到曼弗雷德的房间，他照头一扇的做法办理了。

半夜里，其中一只铃发疯似的响了起来。曼弗雷德跳下床，打开灯。他自己那扇门纹丝未动，他便冲进起居室，冈萨雷斯却已先到，正在察看门边那个小哨兵。门上的锁已被打开。他穿着拖鞋的脚把警报器一脚踢开。

“请进，珀瑟姆勋爵。”他说，“咱们把这件事好好谈谈。”

片刻的静默之后，响起趿拉着拖鞋的脚步声，一个男人走了进来。他衣履齐全，头上无帽，曼弗雷德一见那颗光秃秃的脑袋，不由得倒抽一口凉气。

===Original===

“Sit down and make yourself at home, and let me relieve you of that lethal weapon you have in your pocket, because this matter can be arranged very amicably,” said Leon.

It was undoubtedly Lord Pertham, though the great mop of hair had vanished, and Manfred could only stare as Leon’s left hand slipped into the pocket of the midnight visitor and drew forth a revolver which he placed carefully on the mantelshelf.

Lord Pertham sank into a chair and covered his face with his hands. For a while the silence was unbroken.

“You may remember the Honourable George Fearnside,” began Leon, and Manfred started.

“Fearnside? Why, he was on the Prince’s yacht﻿—”

===Chinese===

“请坐，别拘束，权当在自己家里一样，再让我替你卸下口袋里那件要命的家伙，因为这件事完全可以和和气气地了结。”莱昂说。

这毫无疑问正是珀瑟姆勋爵，只是那一头浓密的头发不见了。莱昂的左手探进这位午夜来客的衣袋，抽出一支左轮手枪，小心地把它搁在壁炉台上，曼弗雷德只有干瞪眼的份。

珀瑟姆勋爵跌坐进椅子里，双手捂住了脸。有一阵子，寂静无人打破。

“你或许还记得可敬的乔治·弗恩赛德（the Honourable George Fearnside）吧。”莱昂开了头，曼弗雷德猛地一怔。

“弗恩赛德？咦，他当时就在亲王的游艇上——”

===Original===

“He was on the Prince’s yacht,” agreed Gonsalez, “and we thoroughly believed that he did not associate us with escaping malefactors, but apparently he knew us for the Four Just Men. You came into your title about six years ago, didn’t you, Pertham?”

The bowed figure nodded. Presently he sat up﻿—his face was white and there were black circles about his eyes.

“Well, gentlemen,” he said, “it seems that instead of getting you, you have got me. Now what are *you* going to do?”

Gonsalez laughed softly.

“For myself,” he said, “I am certainly not going in the witness-box to testify that Lord Pertham is a bigamist and for many years has been leading a double life. Because that would mean I should also have to admit certain uncomfortable things about myself.”

===Chinese===

“他当时确在亲王的游艇上。”冈萨雷斯附和道，“我们那时满心以为，他不会把我们同越狱在逃的重犯联想到一起，可他显然早就认出我们是四义士。你承袭爵位是六年前的事吧，珀瑟姆？”

那低垂的身影点了点头。过了一会儿，他坐直身子——脸色惨白，眼圈发黑。

“唉，二位，”他说，“看来不是你们落在我手里，倒是我落在你们手里了。那么，*你们*打算怎么办？”

冈萨雷斯轻轻笑了。

“至于我嘛，”他说，“我肯定不会上证人席去作证，说珀瑟姆勋爵是个重婚者、多年来一直过着双重生活。因为那就意味着，我也得承认某些关于我自己的、叫人难堪的事情。”

===Original===

The man licked his lips and then:

“I came to kill you,” he said thickly.

“So we gather,” said Manfred. “What is this story, Leon?”

“Perhaps his lordship will tell us,” said Gonsalez.

Lord Pertham looked round for something.

“I want a glass of water,” he said, and it was Leon who brought it.

===Chinese===

那人舔了舔嘴唇，然后说：

“我是来杀你的。”他声音发滞地说。

“我们也看出来了。”曼弗雷德说，“这究竟是怎么一段故事，莱昂？”

“也许勋爵大人愿意亲自讲给我们听。”冈萨雷斯说。

珀瑟姆勋爵环顾四周，像在找什么。

“我想要一杯水。”他说，端水来的正是莱昂。

===Original===

“It is perfectly true,” said Lord Pertham after a while. “I recognised you fellows as two of the Four Just Men. I used to be a great friend of His Highness, and it was by accident that I was on board the yacht when you were taken off. His Highness told me a yarn about some escapade, but when I got to Spain and read the newspaper account of the escape I was pretty certain that I knew who you were. You probably know something about my early life, how I went before the mast as a common sailor and travelled all over the world. It was the kind of life which satisfied me more than any, other, for I got to know people and places and to know them from an angle which I should never have understood in any other way. If you ever want to see the world, travel in the fo’c’sle,” he said with a half-smile.

“I met Martha Grey one night in the East End of London at a theatre. When I was a seaman I acted like a seaman. My father and I were not on the best of terms and I never wanted to go home. She sat by my side in the pit of the theatre and ridiculous as it may seem to you I fell in love with her.”

===Chinese===

“全是真的。”过了一会儿，珀瑟姆勋爵说，“我认出了你们两个是四义士中人。我从前同亲王殿下过从甚密，你们被接上船的时候，我恰好在艇上，纯属偶然。亲王殿下拿一桩什么冒失出逃的逸闻搪塞了我，可等我到了西班牙，读到报上关于那次脱逃的报道，我就相当有把握，自己认出了你们是谁。我的早年生活你们大概略知一二：我怎样做了普通水手，跑遍了世界各地。那种生活比任何别种生活都更让我满足，因为我由此认识了各色人等、各个地方，而且是从一个换用任何别的方式都无从体会的角度去认识它们。你们若真想见识世界，那就到水手舱里去旅行。”他半带笑意地说。

“一天晚上，我在伦敦东区（East End）的一家戏院里认识了玛莎·格雷（Martha Grey）。当水手的时候，我过的就是水手的日子。我和父亲处得不好，也从不想回家。她坐在戏院正厅后排我旁边的座位上，你们听着也许觉得荒唐，我却爱上了她。”

===Original===

“You were then married?” said Leon, but the man shook his head.

“No,” he said quickly. “Like a fool I was persuaded to marry her ladyship about three months later, after I had got sick of the sea and had come back to my own people. She was an heiress and it was a good match for me. That was before my father had inherited his cousin’s money. My life with her ladyship was a hell upon earth. You saw her tonight and you can guess the kind of woman she is. I have too great a respect for women and live too much in awe of them to exercise any control over her viperish temper and it was the miserable life I lived with her which drove me to seek out Martha.

===Chinese===

“你们那时就结婚了？”莱昂说，但那人摇了摇头。

“没有。”他很快地说，“我像个傻子似的，大约三个月后受人劝动，娶了夫人——那是在我厌倦了海上生涯、回到自己人中间之后。她是位财产继承人，这门亲事对我不坏。那是我父亲继承他堂兄的钱之前的事。我同夫人的共同生活简直是人间地狱。你们今晚见过她，也能猜出她是怎样的女人。我对女人敬重太过、畏惧太深，全然管束不了她那毒蛇般的脾气，而正是同她过的这种愁苦日子，逼得我去寻访玛莎。

===Original===

“Martha is a good girl,” he said, and there was a glitter in his eye as he challenged denial. “The purest, the dearest, the sweetest woman that ever lived. It was when I met her again that I realised how deeply in love I was, and as with a girl of her character there was no other way﻿—I married her.

===Chinese===

“玛莎是个好姑娘，”他说，眼里闪着光，一副容不得人否认的神气，“是世上最纯洁、最可亲、最温柔的女人。再见到她时，我才明白自己爱得有多深，而以她那样品性的姑娘，没有别的路可走——我娶了她。

===Original===

“I had fever when I was on a voyage to Australia and lost all my hair. That was long before I met Martha. I suppose it was vanity on my part, but when I went back to my own life and my own people, as I did for a time after that, I had a wig made which served the double purpose of concealing my infirmity and preventing my being recognised by my former shipmates.

“As the little hair I had had gone grey I had the wig greyed too, had it made large and poetical﻿—” he smiled sadly, “to make my disguise more complete. Martha didn’t mind my bald head. God bless her!” he said softly, “and my life with her has been a complete and unbroken period of happiness. I have to leave her at times to manage my own affairs and in those times I pretend to be at sea, just as I used to pretend to her ladyship that business affairs called me to America to explain my absence from her.”

===Chinese===

“有一年航行去澳大利亚，我害了热病，头发掉了个精光。那远在我认识玛莎之前。我猜那是我虚荣心作祟，可当我回去过原先的生活、回到自己人当中时——那之后我确曾回去过一阵——我叫人做了一顶假发，一举两得：既遮住了我这缺陷，又免得让旧日的船友认出来。

“既然我原来那点头发也已花白，我便让假发也做成灰白，做得又大又富诗意——”他惨然一笑，“好把我的伪装做得更足。玛莎并不嫌我秃顶。上帝保佑她！”他柔声说，“我和她共同生活的这些年，是一段圆满无缺的幸福时光。我有时不得不离开她去料理自己的事务，那种时候我便推说出了海——正如从前我向夫人谎称有商务要赶赴美洲，好交代我不在她跟前的日子。”

===Original===

“The man you shot was Martha’s half-brother, of course,” said Gonsalez, and Lord Pertham nodded.

“It was just ill luck which brought him to my house,” he said, “sheer bad luck. In the struggle my wig came off, he recognised me and I shot him,” he said simply. “I shot him deliberately and in cold blood, not only because he threatened to wreck my happiness, but because for years he has terrorised his sister and has been living on her poor earnings.”

Gonsalez nodded.

“I saw grey hair in his hands and I guessed what had happened,” he said.

===Chinese===

“你枪杀的那个人，自然就是玛莎的同母异父兄弟。”冈萨雷斯说，珀瑟姆勋爵点了点头。

“把他引到我家纯属倒霉，”他说，“十足的坏运气。厮打中我的假发脱落，他认出了我，我便开了枪。”他语气平平地说。“我是存心开的枪，冷着心开的枪，不只因为他威胁要毁掉我的幸福，还因为这些年来，他一直恐吓自己的妹妹，靠她那点可怜的进项坐吃。”

冈萨雷斯点了点头。

“我看见他手里攥着灰白的头发，便猜出了事由。”他说。

===Original===

“Now what are you going to do?” asked the Earl of Pertham.

Leon was smoking now.

“What are *you* going to do?” he asked in retort. “Perhaps you would like me to tell you?”

“I should,” said the man earnestly.

“You are going to take your bigamous wife abroad just as soon as this inquest is over, and you are going to wait a reasonable time and then persuade your wife to get a divorce. After which you will marry your Mrs. Prothero in your own name,” said Gonsalez.

===Chinese===

“那么现在，你们打算怎么办？”珀瑟姆伯爵问道。

这会儿莱昂正抽着烟。

“*你*打算怎么办？”他反问道，“兴许你乐意让我告诉你？”

“乐意。”那人恳切地说。

“等这场验尸一了结，你就带你那位重婚娶下的夫人出国去；然后等上一段合乎情理的时间，劝你夫人去办离婚；之后，你便用自己的本名，迎娶你的普罗瑟罗夫人。”冈萨雷斯说。

===Original===

“Leon,” said Manfred after Fearnside had gone back to the room above, the room he had taken in the hope of discovering how much Gonsalez knew, “I think you are a thoroughly unmoral person. Suppose Lady Pertham does not divorce his lordship?”

Leon laughed.

“There is really no need for her to divorce Lord Pertham,” he said, “for his lordship told us a little lie. He married his Martha first, deserted her and went back to her. I happen to know this because I have already examined both registers, and I know there was a Mrs. Prothero before there was a Lady Pertham.”

“You’re a wonderful fellow, Leon,” said Manfred admiringly.

“I am,” admitted Leon Gonsalez.

===Chinese===

弗恩赛德回到楼上那个房间去之后——他租下那间房，本是指望用来探明冈萨雷斯到底知道多少——曼弗雷德说：“莱昂，我看你是个彻头彻尾不讲道德的人。倘若珀瑟姆夫人不肯与勋爵大人离婚呢？”

莱昂笑了。

“其实，她根本用不着跟珀瑟姆勋爵离婚，”他说，“因为勋爵大人对我们撒了个小谎。他先娶的是他的玛莎，后来弃她而去，如今又回到了她身边。我碰巧知道这一点，因为两本婚姻登记册我都查过了，我知道，先有普罗瑟罗夫人，后有珀瑟姆夫人。”

“你真是个妙人，莱昂。”曼弗雷德叹服地说。

“可不是嘛。”莱昂·冈萨雷斯承认。

## The Man Who Loved Music / 热爱音乐的人

===Original===

The most striking characteristics of Mr. Homer Lynne were his deep and wide sympathies, and his love of Tschaikovsky’s *1812*. He loved music generally, but his neighbours in Pennerthon Road, Hampstead, could testify with vehemence and asperity to his preference for that great battle piece. It had led from certain local unpleasantness to a police-court application, having as its object the suppression of Mr. Homer Lynne as a public nuisance, and finally to the exchange of lawyers’ letters and the threat of an action in the High Court.

That so sympathetic and kindly a gentleman should utterly disregard the feelings and desires of his neighbours, that he should have in his bedroom the largest gramophone that Hampstead had ever known, and a gramophone, moreover, fitted with an automatic arm, so that no sooner was the record finished than the needle was switched to the outer edge of the disc and began all over again, and that he should choose the midnight hour for his indulgence, were facts as strange as they were deplorable.

Mr. Lynne had urged at the police court that the only method he had discovered for so soothing his nerves that he could ensure himself a night’s sleep, was to hear that thunderous piece.

That Mr. Lynne was sympathetic at least three distressed parents could testify.
