He was a theatrical agent with large interests in South America; he specialised in the collection of “turns” for some twenty halls large and small, and the great artists who had travelled through the Argentine and Mexico, Chile and Brazil, had nothing but praise for the excellent treatment they had received at the hands of those Mr. Lynne represented. It was believed, and was in truth, a fact, that he was financially interested in quite a number of these places of amusement, which may have accounted for the courtesy and attention which the great performers received on their tour.

===Chinese===

霍默·林恩先生（Mr. Homer Lynne）身上最惹眼的特质，一是他那深厚而博大的同情心，二是他对柴可夫斯基《1812》的热爱。他热爱一切音乐，但汉普斯特德（Hampstead）彭纳顿路（Pennerthon Road）的左邻右舍却可以激愤而尖刻地作证：他偏爱那首伟大的战斗乐曲。这偏好先是在当地惹出种种不快，继而弄出一纸治安法庭的诉状，意在把霍默·林恩先生当作公共妨害加以取缔，最后发展到律师函件往来不绝，威胁要闹上高等法院。

这样一位富于同情、和蔼可亲的绅士，竟全然不顾邻里的感受与心愿；他的卧室里竟摆着汉普斯特德有史以来最大的一台留声机，而且那留声机还装有自动唱臂，一张唱片刚完，唱针便自动移回唱片外缘，从头再放一遍；他竟偏偏挑午夜时分来纵情享受——这些事实既可怪，又可叹。

林恩先生在治安法庭上力陈：他平生发现的唯一能使神经足够安宁、保得一夜安眠的法子，就是听那首雷鸣般的乐曲。

林恩先生富于同情心，至少有三对伤心断肠的父母可以作证。他是个演出经纪人，在南美拥有庞大的利益；他专门为二十来家大大小小的剧场搜罗“节目”，凡是足迹遍及阿根廷、墨西哥、智利和巴西的大牌艺人，无不交口称赞自己在林恩先生所代理的各方手中受到的优厚待遇。人们相信——事实上也确是如此——他在相当多的这类娱乐场子里都投了股，这或许可以解释大牌艺人们巡回演出时何以处处受到礼遇与照拂。

===Original===

He also sent out a number of small artists﻿—microscopically small artists whose names had never figured on the play bills of Britain. They were chosen for their beauty, their sprightliness and their absence of ties.

“It’s a beautiful country,” Mr. Homer Lynne would say.

He was a grave, smooth man, clean-shaven, save for a sign of grey side-whiskers, and people who did not know him would imagine that he was a successful lawyer, with an ecclesiastical practice.

“It’s a beautiful country,” he would say, “but I don’t know whether I like sending a young girl out there. Of course, you’ll have a good salary, and live well﻿—have you any relations?”

===Chinese===

他也往外输送一批小艺人——小得要用显微镜才看得清的小艺人，她们的名字从没在英国的戏单上露过脸。挑中她们，是因为她们的美貌、活泼，和无亲无故。

“那是个美丽的国度。”霍默·林恩先生总爱这么说。

他是个神情庄重、八面光润的人，脸刮得干干净净，只留一点灰白的连鬓胡茬；不认识他的人准会以为他是个事业有成的律师，经办教会方面的业务。

“那是个美丽的国度，”他会说，“只是要不要送个年轻姑娘到那儿去，我心里还拿不准。当然，你会拿到可观的薪水，日子过得舒坦——你有什么亲人吗？”

===Original===

If the girl produced a brother or a father, or even a mother, or an intimate maiden aunt, Mr. Lynne would nod and promise to write on the morrow, a promise which he fulfilled, regretting that he did not think the applicant would quite suit his purpose﻿—which was true. But if she were isolated from these connections, if there were no relations to whom she would write, or friends who were likely to pester him with enquiries, her first-class passage was forthcoming﻿—but not for the tour which the great artists followed, nor for the bigger halls where they would be likely to meet. They were destined for smaller halls, which were not so much theatre as cabaret.

Now and again, on three separate occasions to be exact, the applicant for an engagement would basely deceive him. She would say she had no relations, and lo! there would appear an inquisitive brother, or, as in the present case, a father.

On a bright morning in June, Mr. Lynne sat in his comfortable chair, his hands folded, regarding gravely a nervous little man who sat on the other side of the big mahogany desk balancing his bowler hat on his knees.

===Chinese===

倘若姑娘能拿出一个兄弟或父亲，甚至母亲，或是一位过从亲密的未婚姑母，林恩先生便点点头，答应第二天写信回音；这诺言他也照办，信中遗憾地表示，他认为申请者恐怕不合他的用途——这倒是真话。可她若与这些牵连全然隔绝，没有可以写信的亲眷，也没有可能缠着他打探消息的朋友，那她的头等舱船票便指日可待——但走的不是大牌艺人跑的那条巡演路线，去的也不是他们可能相逢的大剧场。她们命里注定要去的是些小场子，与其说是剧场，不如说是卡巴莱歌舞场。

偶尔——确切说，前后有三回——来谋职的姑娘会卑鄙地欺骗他。她说自己无亲无故，可瞧吧！转眼便冒出来一个好打听的兄弟，或者，就像眼前这桩案子，一个父亲。

六月一个晴朗的早晨，林恩先生坐在他那张舒适的椅子里，双手交叠，神情庄重地打量着一个紧张不安的小个子男人——那人坐在大红木办公桌的另一侧，膝头上端端正正地托着一顶圆顶礼帽。

===Original===

“Rosie Goldstein,” said Mr. Lynne thoughtfully, “I seem to remember the name.”

He rang a bell and a dark young man answered.

“Bring me my engagement book, Mr. Mandez,” said Mr. Lynne.

“You see how it is, Mr. Lynne,” said the caller anxiously﻿—he was unmistakably Hebraic and very nervous. “I hadn’t any idea that Rosie had gone abroad until a friend of hers told me that she had come here and got an engagement.”

===Chinese===

“罗茜·戈德斯坦（Rosie Goldstein），”林恩先生若有所思地说，“这名字我好像记得。”

他按了按铃，进来应命的是个黑皮肤的年轻人。

“把我的约见簿拿来，曼德斯先生（Mr. Mandez）。”林恩先生说。

“您瞧这叫怎么回事，林恩先生，”来客急切地说——他一望便知是犹太人，紧张得很。“罗茜出了国，我本来一无所知，直到她的一位朋友告诉我，说她上这儿来找到了一份演出合同。”

===Original===

“I see,” said Mr. Lynne. “She did not tell you she was going.”

“No, sir.”

The dark young man returned with the book and Mr. Lynne turned the pages leisurely, running his finger down a list of names.

“Here we are,” he said. “Rosie Goldstein. Yes, I remember the girl now, but she told me she was an orphan.”

===Chinese===

“原来如此，”林恩先生说，“她走的时候没告诉你。”

“没有，先生。”

黑皮肤年轻人拿着簿子回来了，林恩先生慢条斯理地翻着，手指顺着一列名字往下捋。

“找到了，”他说，“罗茜·戈德斯坦。嗯，我想起这姑娘了，可她跟我说过，她是个孤儿。”

===Original===

Goldstein nodded.

“I suppose she thought I’d stop her,” he said with a sigh of relief. “But as long as I know where she is, I’m not so worried. Have you her present address?”

Lynne closed the book carefully and beamed at the visitor.

“I haven’t her present address,” he said cheerfully, “but if you will write her a letter and address it to me, I will see that it goes forward to our agents in Buenos Aires: they, of course, will be able to find her. You see, there are a large number of halls in connection with the circuit, and it is extremely likely that she may be performing upcountry. It is quite impossible to keep track of every artist.”

===Chinese===

戈德斯坦（Goldstein）点了点头。

“我琢磨她是怕我拦她，”他说着，如释重负地叹了口气。“不过只要知道她在哪儿，我就不那么揪心了。您有她现在的地址吗？”

林恩小心翼翼地合上簿子，满面春风地望着来客。

“她现在的地址，我手上倒没有，”他爽快地说，“不过您不妨给她写封信，寄到我这里，我保证转给我们布宜诺斯艾利斯（Buenos Aires）的代理商，他们自然找得到她。您知道，这条巡演线上挂着一大批场子，她很可能正在内地演出。每个艺人的行踪都要一一掌握，那是根本办不到的。”

===Original===

“I understand that, sir,” said the grateful little Jew.

“She ought to have told you,” said the sympathetic Lynne shaking his head.

He really meant that she ought to have told him.

“However, we’ll see what can be done.”

He offered his plump hand to the visitor, and the dark young man showed him to the door.

===Chinese===

“这个我明白，先生。”千恩万谢的小个子犹太人说。

“她本该告诉您的。”富有同情心的林恩摇着头说。

他心里真正的意思是：她本该告诉他。

“不过，我们会想想办法的。”

他向来访者伸出自己丰满的手，那黑皮肤年轻人把客人送出门去。

===Original===

Three minutes later Mr. Lynne was interviewing a pretty girl who had the advantage of stage experience﻿—she had been a member of a beauty chorus in a travelling revue. And when the eager girl had answered questions relating to her stage experiences, which were few, Mr. Lynne came to the real crux of the interview.

“Now what do your father and mother say about this idea of your accepting this engagement to go abroad?” he asked with his most benevolent smile.

“I have no father or mother,” said the girl, and Mr. Lynne guessed from the momentary quiver of the lips that she had lost one of these recently.

“You have brothers, perhaps?”

“I have no brothers,” she answered, shaking her head. “I haven’t any relations in the world, Mr. Lynne. You will let me go, won’t you?” she pleaded.

===Chinese===

三分钟后，林恩先生开始面试一个漂亮姑娘——她占着有舞台经验的优势，曾在一个巡回歌舞剧团的美女合唱队里当过队员。急切的姑娘回答了关于她舞台经历的种种问题——其实寥寥无几——之后，林恩先生便谈到了这次面谈的真正关键。

“那么，对于你接受这份出国演出合同的想法，你父亲母亲是什么态度？”他带着最慈祥的笑容问道。

“我没有父亲，也没有母亲。”姑娘说。林恩先生从她嘴唇一时的颤动猜到，两位亲人中的一位是最近才失去的。

“那么，也许有兄弟？”

“我没有兄弟，”她摇着头答道，“我在这世上一个亲人也没有，林恩先生。您会让我去的，对不对？”她恳求道。

===Original===

Mr. Lynne would let her go. If the truth be told, the minor “artists” he sent to the South American continent were infinitely more profitable than the great performers whose names were household words in London.

“I will write you tomorrow,” he said conventionally.

“You will let me go?”

He smiled.

“You are certain to go, Miss Hacker. You need have no fear on the subject. I will send you on the contract﻿—no, you had better come here and sign it.”

===Chinese===

林恩先生当然会让她去。说实话，他送往南美大陆的那些末流“艺人”，比那些名字在伦敦家喻户晓的大牌艺人有利可图得多。

“我明天给你写信。”他照例客套地说。

“您会让我去的？”

他微微一笑。

“你一定去得成，哈克小姐（Miss Hacker）。这事你完全不必担心。我会把合同给你送去——不，你还是自己到这儿来签字的好。”

===Original===

The girl ran down the stairs into Leicester Square, her heart singing. An engagement at three times bigger than the biggest salary she had ever received! She wanted to tell everybody about it, though she did not dream that in a few seconds she would babble her happiness to a man who at that moment was a perfect stranger.

He was a foreign-looking gentleman, well dressed and good-looking. He had the kind of face that appeals to children﻿—an appeal that no psychologist has ever yet analysed.

She met him literally by accident. He was standing at the bottom of the stairs as she came down, and missing her footing she tell forward into his arms.

“I am ever so sorry,” she said with a smile.

“You don’t look very sorry,” smiled the man. “You look more like a person who had just got a very nice engagement to go abroad.”

===Chinese===

姑娘一路跑下楼梯，冲进莱斯特广场（Leicester Square），心里唱着歌。一份演出合同，薪水竟是她平生拿过的最大数目的三倍！她恨不得见人就说，却做梦也没想到，不出几秒钟，她就会把自己的喜事一股脑儿倒给一个此刻还素不相识的人。

那是位外国模样的绅士，衣冠楚楚，相貌堂堂。他生着那种讨孩子喜欢的脸——这种吸引力，至今还没有哪位心理学家分析出来。

说来真是撞上的缘分。她下楼时，他正站在楼梯脚，她一脚踩空，整个人朝前摔进了他怀里。

“实在对不起，”她带着笑说。

“你看上去可不太像抱歉的样子，”那人微笑道，“倒更像是一个刚刚揽到一份出国美差的人。”

===Original===

She stared at him.

“However did you know that?”

“I know it because﻿—well, I know,” he laughed, and apparently abandoning his intention of going upstairs, he turned and walked with her into the street.

“Yes, I am,” she nodded. “I’ve had a wonderful opportunity. Are you in the profession?”

“No, I’m not in the profession,” said Leon Gonsalez, “if you mean the theatrical profession, but I know the countries you’re going to rather well. Would you like to hear something about the Argentine?”

===Chinese===

她瞪大了眼睛望着他。

“您怎么会知道的？”

“我知道，是因为——嗯，我就是知道。”他笑了，看样子打消了上楼的原意，转身陪她走到街上。

“是的，我要出国，”她点头道，“我碰上了一个了不起的机会。您是干演艺这一行的吗？”

“不，我不在行里，”莱昂·冈萨雷斯（Leon Gonsalez）说，“如果你说的是演戏这一行的话。不过你要去的那些国家，我相当熟悉。想不想听听阿根廷的情形？”

===Original===

She looked at him dubiously.

“I should very much,” she hesitated, “but I﻿—”

“I’m going to have a cup of tea, come along,” said Leon good-humouredly.

Though she had no desire either for tea or even for the interview (though she was dying to tell somebody) the magnetic personality of the man held her, and she fell in by his side. And at that very moment Mr. Lynne was saying to the dark-skinned man:

“Fonso! She’s a beaut!” and that staid man kissed the bunched tips of his fingers ecstatically.

===Chinese===

她将信将疑地打量他。

“我很想听，”她迟疑道，“可是我——”

“我正要去喝杯茶，一起来吧。”莱昂和颜悦色地说。

虽然她既不想喝茶，甚至也无意攀谈（尽管她憋着想找人说说），这个人身上磁石般的魅力却牢牢吸住了她，她便挨着他并肩走去。而就在那一刻，林恩先生正对那黑皮肤男人说：

“丰索（Fonso）！这妞儿是个美人！”那位一本正经的先生狂喜地吻了吻并拢的指尖。

===Original===

This was the third time Leon Gonsalez had visited the elegant offices of Mr. Homer Lynne in Panton Street.

Once there was an organisation which was called the Four Just Men, and these had banded themselves together to execute justice upon those whom the law had missed, or passed by, and had earned for themselves a reputation which was worldwide. One had died, and of the three who were left, Poiccart (who had been called the brains of the four) was living quietly in Seville. To him had come a letter from a compatriot in Rio, a compatriot who did not identify him with the organisation of the Four Just Men, but had written vehemently of certain abominations. There had been an exchange of letters, and Poiccart had discovered that most of these fresh English girls who had appeared in the dance halls of obscure towns had been imported through the agency of the respectable Mr. Lynne, and Poiccart had written to his friends in London.

“Yes, it’s a beautiful country,” said Leon Gonsalez, stirring his tea thoughtfully. “I suppose you’re awfully pleased with yourself.”

===Chinese===

这是莱昂·冈萨雷斯第三次踏进霍默·林恩先生在帕顿街（Panton Street）那间气派的办公室。

从前有一个组织，名叫四义士（The Four Just Men），这几个人结成一体，专门对法律遗漏或放过的恶人执行正义，为自己赢得了传遍全世界的名声。一人已经死去，剩下的三人中，波伊卡特（Poiccart）——人称四人中的智囊——正在塞维利亚（Seville）过着安静的日子。一封信从里约（Rio）的一位同胞手里寄到他这儿；那位同胞并不知道他与四义士组织的渊源，只是激愤地写到了某些骇人的罪恶。书信往来几番之后，波伊卡特查明：那些出现在偏僻城镇舞厅里的英国新姑娘，大多数都是经由那位体面的林恩先生的手引进去的。于是波伊卡特给伦敦的朋友们写了信。

“是啊，那是个美丽的国度，”莱昂·冈萨雷斯若有所思地搅着茶说，“我猜你这会儿正得意得很吧。”

===Original===

“Oh, it’s wonderful,” said the girl. “Fancy, I’m going to receive £12 a week and my board and lodging. Why, I shall be able to save almost all of it.”

“Have you any idea where you will perform?”

The girl smiled.

“I don’t know the country,” she said, “and it’s dreadfully ignorant of me, but I don’t know one single town in the Argentine.”

“There aren’t many people who do,” smiled Leon, “but you’ve heard of Brazil, I suppose?”

===Chinese===

“哦，太好了，”姑娘说，“想想看，我每星期拿 12 镑，还包吃包住。哎呀，这笔钱我几乎全能攒下来。”

“你知道自己要去哪儿演出吗？”

姑娘笑了。

“我不了解那个国家，”她说，“我这人无知得要命，阿根廷的一个城镇我都说不上来。”

“说得上来的人也不多，”莱昂微笑道，“不过巴西你总听说过吧？”

===Original===

“Yes, it’s a little country in South America,” she nodded, “I know that.”

“Where the nuts come from,” laughed Leon. “No, it’s not a little country in South America: it’s a country as wide as from here to the centre of Persia, and as long as from Brighton to the equator. Does that give you any idea?”

She stared at him.

And then he went on, but confined himself to the physical features of the subcontinent. Not once did he refer to her contract﻿—that was not his object. That object was disclosed, though not to her, when he said:

“I must send you a book. Miss Hacker: it will interest you if you are going to the Argentine. It is full of very accurate information.”

===Chinese===

“听说过，那是南美的一个小国，”她点点头，“这个我知道。”

“出坚果的那地方，”莱昂笑道，“不，它可不是南美的一个小国：那国家的宽度，抵得上从这里到波斯腹地；长度，抵得上从布莱顿（Brighton）到赤道。这你有概念了吗？”

她怔怔地望着他。

接着他讲下去，但只限于谈那片次大陆的山川地貌，对她的合同只字未提——那不是他的目的。他的目的，是在这句话里露出来的，虽说不是透露给她：

“我得寄给你一本书，哈克小姐：你要去阿根廷，它会让你感兴趣的。书里全是极为准确的知识。”

===Original===

“Oh, thank you,” she said gratefully. “Shall I give you my address?”

That was exactly what Leon had been fishing for. He put the scrap of paper she had written on into his pocketbook, and left her.

George Manfred, who had acquired a two-seater car, picked him up outside the National Gallery, and drove him to Kensington Gardens, the refreshment buffet of which, at this hour of the day, was idle. At one of the deserted tables Leon disclosed the result of his visit.

“It was singularly fortunate that I should have met one of the lambs.”

“Did you see Lynne himself?”

Leon nodded.

===Chinese===

“哦，谢谢您，”她感激地说，“要我把地址留给您吗？”

这正是莱昂一心要钓的东西。她写好的那张纸片，他夹进记事本，便与她作别。

乔治·曼弗雷德（George Manfred）新近置了一辆双座小汽车，在国家美术馆（National Gallery）外捎上他，载他到肯辛顿公园（Kensington Gardens）；这个时辰，园中的茶点部冷冷清清。在一张无人的小桌旁，莱昂说出了此行的收获。

“能撞见羊群里的一只小羊羔，实在是走运得很。”

“林恩本人你见到了？”

莱昂点了点头。

===Original===

“After I left the girl I went up and made a call. It was rather difficult to get past the Mexican gentleman﻿—Mandez I think his name is﻿—into the sanctum but eventually Lynne saw me.”

He chuckled softly:

“I do not play on the banjo; I declare this to you, my dear George, in all earnestness. The banjo to me is a terrible instrument﻿—”

“Which, means,” said Manfred with a smile, “that you described yourself as a banjo soloist who wanted a job in South America.”

“Exactly,” said Leon, “and I need hardly tell you that I was not engaged. The man is interesting, George.”

“All men are interesting to you, Leon,” laughed Manfred, putting aside the coffee he had ordered, and lighting a long, thin cigar.

===Chinese===

“离开那姑娘后，我上楼去拜访了一趟。想闯过那位墨西哥先生——我记得他叫曼德斯——进到圣所一般的内室，颇费了些周折，不过林恩终究还是见了我。”

他轻声笑了：

“我不弹班卓琴；我亲爱的乔治，我向你郑重声明这一点。班卓琴在我听来是件可怕的乐器——”

“这也就是说，”曼弗雷德微笑道，“你把自己说成了一个想在南美找饭碗的班卓琴独奏师。”

“一点不错，”莱昂说，“结果人家没用我，这我都不必说了。这个人很有意思，乔治。”

“天底下的人在你眼里个个有意思，莱昂。”曼弗雷德笑着把要来的咖啡推开，点上一支细长的雪茄。

===Original===

“I should have loved to tell him that his true vocation was arson. He has the face of the true incendiary, and I tell you George, that Lombroso was never more accurate than when he described that type. A fair, dear, delicate skin, a plump, babylike face, hair extraordinarily fine: you can pick them out anywhere.”

He caressed his chin and frowned.

“Callous destruction of human happiness also for profit. I suppose the same type of mind would commit both crimes. It is an interesting parallel. I should like to consult our dear friend Poiccart on that subject.”

===Chinese===

“我真想告诉他，他真正的天职是纵火。他长着一张地道纵火犯的脸，我告诉你，乔治，龙勃罗梭（Lombroso）描述这一类型时，从未比这更准。白皙娇嫩的皮肤，丰满的孩子脸，头发细得出奇：这种人你走到哪儿都能一眼挑出来。”

他摩挲着下巴，皱起了眉头。

“为牟利而冷酷地毁掉人的幸福，我想犯这两种罪的是同一类头脑。这个对照很有意思。真想就这个题目请教请教我们亲爱的朋友波伊卡特。”

===Original===

“Can he be touched by the law?” asked Manfred. “Is there no way of betraying him?”

“Absolutely none,” said Leon shortly. “The man is a genuine agent. He has the names of some of the best people on his books and they all speak loudly in his praise. The lie that is half a lie is easier to detect than the criminal who is half honest. If the chief cashier of the Bank of England turned forger, he would be the most successful forger in the world. This man has covered himself at every point. I had a talk with a Jewish gentleman﻿—a pathetic old soul named Goldstein, whose daughter went abroad some seven or eight months ago. He has not heard from her, and he told me that Lynne was very much surprised to discover that she had any relations at all. The unrelated girl is his best investment.”

“Did Lynne give the old man her address?”

Leon shrugged his shoulders.

===Chinese===

“法律动得了他吗？”曼弗雷德问，“就没法子告发他？”

“完全动不了，”莱昂简短地说，“此人是货真价实的经纪人。他名册上挂着一些最体面的名字，那些人都扯着嗓子夸他。半真半假的谎，反倒比半诚半实的罪犯更容易识破。英格兰银行的总出纳若改行造假，必是天下最得手的造假者。这个人把每个环节都遮得严严实实。我同一位犹太老先生谈过——是个可怜兮兮的老好人，姓戈德斯坦，女儿七八个月前出了国。他至今没收到女儿一封信，他告诉我，林恩发现她居然有亲戚时，惊讶得不得了。无亲无故的姑娘，正是他最划算的投资。”

“林恩把地址给那老头儿了吗？”

莱昂耸了耸肩。

===Original===

“There are a million square miles in the Argentine﻿—where is she? Cordoba, Tucuman, Mendoza, San Louis, Santa Fe, Rio Cuario, those are a few towns. And there are hundreds of towns where this girl may be dancing, towns which have no British or American Consul. It’s rather horrible, George.”

Manfred looked thoughtfully across the green spaces in the park.

“If we could be sure,” said Gonsalez softly. “It will take exactly two months to satisfy us, and I think it would be worth the money. Our young friend will leave by the next South American packet, and you, some time ago, were thinking of returning to Spain. I think I will take the trip.”

George nodded.

“I thought you would,” he said. “I really can’t see how we can act unless you do.”

===Chinese===

“阿根廷有一百万平方英里——她会在哪儿？科尔多瓦（Cordoba）、图库曼（Tucuman）、门多萨（Mendoza）、圣路易斯（San Louis）、圣菲（Santa Fe）、里奥夸尔托（Rio Cuario），这不过是几个城镇。这样的姑娘也许正在几百个城镇里跳舞，那些城镇连英国领事或美国领事都没有。这事相当可怕，乔治。”

曼弗雷德若有所思地望着公园里一片片的绿地。

“但愿咱们能拿得准，”冈萨雷斯轻声说，“要让自己心满意足，恰恰得花两个月，我认为这钱花得值。我们那位年轻朋友将搭下一班南美邮船动身，而你呢，前些日子不是正想着回西班牙去。这一趟，我看就由我来走。”

乔治点了点头。

“我就知道你会去，”他说，“你要不去，我实在想不出咱们还能怎么办。”

===Original===

Miss Lilah Hacker was amazed when she boarded the *Braganza* at Boulogne to discover that she had as fellow passenger the polite stranger who had lectured so entertainingly on the geography of South America.

To the girl her prospect was rosy and bright. She was looking forward to a land of promise, her hopes for the future were at zenith, and if she was disappointed a little that the agreeable Gonsalez did not keep her company on the voyage, but seemed forever preoccupied, that was a very unimportant matter.

It was exactly a month from the day she put foot on the *Braganza* that her hope and not a little of her faith in humanity were blasted by a stout Irishman whose name was Rafferty, but who had been born in the Argentine. He was the proprietor of a dance hall called “La Plaza” in a cattle town in the interior. She had been sent there with two other girls wiser than she, to entertain the half-breed vaqueros who thronged the town at night, and for whom “La Plaza” was the principal attraction.

===Chinese===

莉拉·哈克小姐（Miss Lilah Hacker）在布洛涅（Boulogne）登上“布拉干萨号”（the *Braganza*）时，惊讶地发现同船的乘客里，竟有那位把南美地理讲得妙趣横生的客气陌生人。

在这姑娘眼里，前途一片玫瑰色的光明。她盼望着那片应许之地，对未来的期望正悬在天顶；讨人喜欢的冈萨雷斯一路上不陪她做伴，似乎总是心事重重，这虽让她略感失望，却不过是件微不足道的小事。

从她踏上“布拉干萨号”那天起，恰好过了一个月，她的希望，连同她对人性不小的一部分信心，便被一个矮胖的爱尔兰人击得粉碎。此人名叫拉弗蒂（Rafferty），却生在阿根廷。他在内地一座牧牛镇上开着一间叫“拉普拉扎”的舞厅。她被派到那里，同去的还有两个比她有心眼的姑娘，任务是取悦入夜便涌进镇里的混血牧牛郎——对这些人来说，“拉普拉扎”就是头一号的去处。

===Original===

“You’ve got to get out of them ways of yours,” said Rafferty, twisting his cigar from one side of his mouth to the other. “When Señor Santiago wanted you to sit on his knee last night, you made a fuss, I’m told.”

“Of course, I did,” said the girl indignantly. “Why, he’s coloured!”

“Now see here,” said Mr. Rafferty, “there ain’t no coloured people in this country. Do you get that? Mr. Santiago is a gentleman and he’s got stacks of money, and the next time he pays you a little attention, you’ve got to be pleasant, see?”

“I’ll do nothing of the sort,” said the girl, pale and shaking, “and I’m going straight back to Buenos Aires tonight.”

===Chinese===

“你那些个臭讲究，都得给我改了，”拉弗蒂说，嘴里的雪茄从一边捻到另一边，“听说昨晚圣地亚哥先生（Señor Santiago）要你坐他腿上，你还闹了脾气。”

“我当然要闹，”姑娘愤愤地说，“他可是个有色人种！”

“你听好了，”拉弗蒂先生说，“这个国家压根儿没有什么有色人种。听明白没有？圣地亚哥先生是位体面人，钱多得成堆，下回他再对你献点殷勤，你就得给人家好脸子，懂吗？”

“我才不干这种事，”姑娘脸色煞白、浑身发抖地说，“今晚我就直接回布宜诺斯艾利斯去。”

===Original===

“Oh you are, are you?” Rafferty smiled broadly. “That’s an idea you can get out of your head, too.”

Suddenly he gripped her by the arm.

“You’re going up to your room, now,” he said, “and you’re going to stay there till I bring you out tonight to do your show, and if you give me any of your nonsense﻿—you’ll be sorry!”

He pushed her through the rough unpainted door of the little cell which was termed bedroom, and he paused in the doorway to convey information (and there was a threat in the course of it) which left her white and staring.

She came down that night and did her performance and to her surprise and relief did not excite even the notice of the wealthy Mr. Santiago, a half-bred Spaniard with a yellow face, who did not so much as look at her.

===Chinese===

“哦，你要回去，是吗？”拉弗蒂咧嘴一笑，“这个念头，你也趁早从脑袋里掏出去吧。”

他突然一把攥住她的胳膊。

“现在你就上楼回你屋里去，”他说，“给我老老实实待着，等晚上我带你出来演你的节目；你要是再跟我来那些混账花样——有你好受的！”

他把她推进那间称作卧室的小格子间的粗板门——门上连漆都没刷——又立在门口交代了几句（话里夹着威胁），说得她面无人色、目光发直。

当晚她下楼演了节目，令她又惊又宽慰的是，那位阔气的圣地亚哥先生——一个黄脸膛的西班牙混血儿——竟连正眼也没瞧她一下。

===Original===

Mr. Rafferty was also unusually bland and polite.

She went to her room that night feeling more comfortable. Then she discovered that her key was gone, and she sat up until one o’clock in the morning waiting for she knew not what. At that hour came a soft footfall in the passage; somebody tried the handle of her door, but she had braced a chair under the handle. They pushed and the rickety chair creaked; then there was a sound like a stick striking a cushion, and she thought she heard somebody sliding against the wooden outer wall of the room. A tap came to her door.

“Miss Hacker,” the voice said. She recognised it immediately. “Open the door quickly. I want to get you away.”

===Chinese===

拉弗蒂先生也一反常态地和气客气。

那天夜里她回到房里，心里松快了些。接着她发现钥匙不见了，便一直枯坐到凌晨一点，等着连她自己也不知道的什么。就在那个时辰，过道里传来轻软的脚步声；有人拧她的门把手，好在她在把手底下顶了一把椅子。那人推了推，破椅子咯吱作响；接着是一声闷响，像棍子打在软垫上，她仿佛还听见有人贴着房间的木外墙滑了下去。有人敲了敲她的门。

“哈克小姐，”那声音说。她立刻听出来了。“快开门。我要带你离开这儿。”

===Original===

With a trembling hand she removed the chair, and the few little articles of furniture she had piled against the door, and opened it. By the light of the candle which was burning in her room she recognised the man who had been her fellow passenger on the *Braganza*.

“Come quietly,” he said. “There is a back stair to the compound. Have you a cloak? Bring it, because you have a sixty-mile motor journey before we come to the railway.”

As she came through the door she saw the upturned toes of somebody who was lying in the passage, and with a shudder she realised that was the thumping sound she had heard.

They reached the big yard behind the “Plaza” crowded with the dusty motorcars of ranchers and their foremen, who had come into town for the evening, and passed out through the doorway. A big car was standing in the middle of the road, and to this he guided her. She threw one glance back at Rafferty’s bar. The windows blazed with light, the sound of the orchestra came faintly through the still night air, then she dropped her head on her hands and wept.

Leon Gonsalez had a momentary pang of contrition, for he might have saved her all this.

===Chinese===

她哆哆嗦嗦地挪开椅子，撤下堵门的那几件小家具，开了门。借着屋里燃着的烛光，她认出了这个在“布拉干萨号”上与她同船的人。

“悄悄跟我走，”他说，“后面有楼梯通到场院。有斗篷吗？带上，因为到火车站之前，咱们还得坐六十英里的汽车。”

出门的时候，她看见过道里躺着个人，鞋尖朝上翘着；她浑身一颤，明白方才那声闷响是什么了。

两人来到“拉普拉扎”后头的大场院，院里挤满了牧场主和工头们沾满尘土的汽车——他们是进城来消磨夜晚的——随后穿过门洞走出去。一辆大汽车停在路当中，他引着她上了车。她回头朝拉弗蒂的酒吧望了一眼：窗子里灯火通明，乐队的声响隐隐透过寂静的夜气传来。随后她把头埋进双手，哭了起来。

莱昂·冈萨雷斯心头掠过一阵悔意，因为这一切本可以让她免受的。

===Original===

It was two months exactly from the day he had left London, when he came running up the stairs of the Jermyn Street flat and burst in upon Manfred.

“You’re looking fit and fine, Leon,” said George, jumping up and gripping his hand. “You didn’t write and I ever expected that you would. I only got back from Spain two days ago.”

He gave the news from Seville and then:

“You proved the case?”

“To our satisfaction,” said Leon grimly. “Though you would not satisfy the law that Lynne was guilty. It is, however, a perfectly clear case. I visited his agent when I was in Buenos Aires, and took the liberty of rifling his desk in his absence. I found several letters from Lynne and by their tone there can be no doubt whatever that Lynne is consciously engaged in this traffic.”

They looked at one another.

===Chinese===

他离开伦敦恰好两个月之后，一天，他跑上杰明街（Jermyn Street）公寓的楼梯，一阵风似的闯到曼弗雷德面前。

“你看上去气色好极了，莱昂，”乔治说着跳起身来，紧紧握住他的手，“你没写信来，我也从没指望你会写。前天我才从西班牙回来。”

他讲了讲塞维利亚的消息，随后：

“案子坐实了？”

“咱们自己是满意了，”莱昂冷冷地说，“虽说要叫法律认定林恩有罪，还差得远。不过，这是一桩再清楚不过的案子。我在布宜诺斯艾利斯时拜访了他的代理商，趁他不在，冒昧翻检了他的书桌。我找到林恩的几封来信，就那口气而论，林恩自觉经营这桩买卖，毫无可疑。”

两人对望了一眼。

===Original===

“The rest is simple,” said Manfred, “and I will leave you to work out the details, my dear Leon, with every confidence that Mr. Homer Lynne will be very sorry indeed that he departed from the safe and narrow way.”

There was no more painstaking, thorough or conscientious workman that Leon Gonsalez. The creation of punishment was to him a work of love. No General ever designed the battle with a more punctilious regard to the minutest detail than Leon.

Before the day was over he had combed the neighbourhood in which Mr. Lynne lived of every vital fact. It was then that he learnt of Mr. Lynne’s passion for music. The cab which took Leon back to Jermyn Street did not go fast enough: he literally leapt into the sitting-room, chortling his joy.

“The impossible is possible, my dear George,” he cried, pacing about the apartment like a man demented. “I thought I should never be able to carry my scheme into effect, but he loves music, George! He adores the tuneful phonograph!”

“A little ice water, I think,” suggested Manfred gently.

===Chinese===

“剩下的就简单了，”曼弗雷德说，“细节由你去筹划，我亲爱的莱昂。我完全相信，霍默·林恩先生定会为自己偏离了安稳的正道而追悔莫及。”

论心细、周全、尽责，无人能出莱昂·冈萨雷斯之右。设计惩罚于他是一件赏心乐事。任凭哪位将军部署战役，也及不上莱昂对最细微之处的那份讲究。

当天还没过完，他已经把林恩先生居住的街区梳篦了一遍，凡属要紧的事实尽入囊中。也正是在这时候，他得知了林恩先生对音乐的痴迷。载莱昂回杰明街的马车走得不够快：他简直是蹦进客厅的，嘴里咯咯地笑个不停。

“不可能的变成可能的了，我亲爱的乔治！”他嚷道，像个着了魔的人似的在房间里踱来踱去，“我原以为我的计划永远无法实施，可他爱音乐，乔治！他崇拜那会唱歌的留声机！”

“我看，该给你来点冰水了。”曼弗雷德温言劝道。

===Original===

“No, no,” said Leon, “I am not hot, I am cool: I am ice itself! And who would expect such good luck? Tonight we will drive to Hampstead and we will hear his concert.”

It was a long time before he gave a coherent account of what he had learnt. Mr. Lynne was extremely unpopular in the neighbourhood, and Leon explained why.

Manfred understood better that night, when the silence of the sedate road in which Mr. Lynne’s detached house was situated was broken by the shrill sound of trumpets, and the rolling of drums, the clanging of bells, the simulated boom of cannon﻿—all the barbarian musical interjection which has made *1812* so popular with unmusical people.

“It sounds like a real band,” said Manfred in surprise.

A policeman strolled along, and seeing the car standing before the house, turned his head with a laugh.

===Chinese===

“不，不，”莱昂说，“我不是发热，我是冷静：我自己就是冰！谁料得到有这样的好运？今晚咱们就驱车去汉普斯特德，听他的音乐会去。”

过了好半天，他才把打听到的事有条有理地讲了一遍。林恩先生在街坊里极不得人心，莱昂说明了缘由。

当晚曼弗雷德就有了更真切的体会：林恩先生那幢独栋住宅所在的清静街道，寂静被刺耳的号声打破——鼓声隆隆，钟声铿锵，还有以假乱真的炮声轰鸣——全是那些让不懂音乐的人也对《1812》着迷不已的野蛮音乐插段。

“听着真像一支货真价实的乐队。”曼弗雷德惊讶地说。

一名警察踱了过来，看见汽车停在那宅子前面，笑着扭过头来。

===Original===

“It’s an awful row, isn’t it?”

“I wonder it doesn’t wake everybody up,” suggested Manfred.

“It does,” replied the policeman, “or it did until they got used to it. It’s the loudest gramophone in the world, I should think: like one of those things you have at the bottom of the tube stairs, to tell the people to move on. A stentaphone, isn’t it?”

“How long does this go on? All night?” asked Manfred.

“For about an hour, I believe,” said the policeman. “The gentleman who lives in that house can’t go to sleep without music. He’s a bit artistic, I think.”

===Chinese===

“吵得够呛，是吧？”

“真奇怪，这居然没把所有人都吵醒。”曼弗雷德说。

“吵醒啦，”警察答道，“或者说从前吵醒过，后来大家听惯了。我看这是全世界最响的留声机：就跟地铁楼梯口底下叫人往前走的那种大喇叭一个样。该叫扩音喇叭，对吧？”

“这要响多久？一整夜？”曼弗雷德问。

“大概一个钟头吧，我估摸，”警察说，“住那宅子的那位先生，不听音乐就睡不着。我看他还有点艺术气。”

===Original===

“He is,” said Leon grimly.

The next day he found out that four servants were kept in the establishment, three of whom slept on the premises. Mr. Lynne was in the habit of returning home every evening at about ten o’clock, except on Fridays when he went out of Town.

Wednesday evening was the cook’s night out, and it was also the night when Mr. Lynne’s butler and general factotum was allowed an evening off. There remained the housemaid, and even she presented no difficulty. The real trouble was that all these people would return to the house or the neighbourhood at eleven o’clock. Leon decided to make his appointment with Mr. Lynne for Friday night, on which day he usually went to Brighton. He watched the genial man leave Victoria, and then he called up Lynne’s house.

“Is that Masters?” he asked, and a man’s voice answered him.

“Yes, sir,” was the reply.

===Chinese===

“他确实是。”莱昂冷冷地说。

第二天他打听清楚，那宅子里雇着四个仆人，其中三个睡在宅内。林恩先生有个习惯，每晚十点钟左右回家，只有星期五例外——那天他要出城去。

星期三晚上是厨娘的歇班夜，也是林恩先生的贴身男仆兼万事总管获准休假的晚上。剩下一名女仆，而连她也不成问题。真正的麻烦在于，这些人到十一点钟都会回到宅子里或宅子附近。莱昂决定把与林恩先生的“约会”定在星期五夜里——那天他照例去布莱顿。他眼看着这位和气的先生离开维多利亚车站（Victoria），然后给林恩家挂了电话。

“是马斯特斯（Masters）吗？”他问，一个男人回了话。

“是的，先生。”对方答道。

===Original===

“It is Mr. Mandez here,” said Leon, imitating the curious broken English of Lynne’s Mexican assistant. “Mr. Lynne is returning to the house tonight on very important business, and he does not want any of the servants to be there.”

“Indeed, sir,” said Masters and showed no surprise. Evidently these instructions had been given before. Leon had expected some difficulty here, and had prepared a very elaborate explanation which it was not necessary to give.

“He wouldn’t like me to stay, sir?”

“Oh, no,” said Leon. “Mr. Lynne particularly said that nobody was to be in the house. He wants the side door and the kitchen door left unlocked,” he added as an afterthought. It was a brilliant afterthought if it came off, and apparently it did.

“Very good, sir,” said Masters.

===Chinese===

“我是曼德斯先生，”莱昂说，模仿着林恩那位墨西哥助手古怪蹩脚的英语，“林恩先生今晚有要事回宅，他不要任何仆人留在屋里。”

“是吗，先生。”马斯特斯说，毫不惊讶。显然这类吩咐以前就有过。莱昂原以为这里会遇到些麻烦，还备好了一套十分周详的说辞，结果根本用不上。

“他不乐意我留下吗，先生？”

“哦，不，”莱昂说，“林恩先生特别交代，屋里不许留人。他还要侧门和厨房门都不上锁。”他又补了一句，像是临时想起。这句临时添上的话若能兑现，堪称神来之笔，而看样子它兑现了。

“好的，先生。”马斯特斯说。

===Original===

Leon went straight from the telephone call-box, where he had sent the message, to the counter and wrote out a wire, addressed to Lynne, Hotel Ritz, Brighton. The message ran:

> “The girl Goldstein has been discovered at Santa Fe. Terrible row. Police have been making enquiries. Have very important information for you. I am waiting for you at your house.”

He signed it Mandez.

“He will get the wire at eight. There is a train back at nine. That should bring him to Hampstead by half past ten,” said Leon when he had rejoined Manfred who was waiting for him outside the post office. “We will be there an hour earlier: that is as soon as it is dark.”

===Chinese===

莱昂从发话的电话亭径直走到柜台，拟了一封电报，收报人是布莱顿丽兹酒店的林恩。电文如下：

> “戈德斯坦家的姑娘已在圣菲（Santa Fe）找到。闹出大乱子。警方正四处查问。有极重要的消息奉告。现于府上恭候。”

落款是曼德斯。

“他八点钟能收到电报。九点有一班回程车。十点半该能到汉普斯特德，”莱昂回到在邮局外等他的曼弗雷德身边时说，“咱们提前一个钟头到，也就是说天一擦黑就去。”

===Original===

They entered the house without the slightest difficulty. Manfred left his two-seater outside a doctor’s house, a place where an unattended car would not be noticed, and went on foot to Lynne’s residence. It was a large detached house, expensively furnished, and as Leon had expected, the servants had gone. He located Lynne’s room, a big apartment at the front of the house.

“There is his noise box,” said Leon pointing to a handsome cabinet near the window. “Electrical, too. Where does that wire lead?”

He followed the flex to a point above the head of the bed, where it terminated in what looked like a hanging bell push.

Leon was momentarily puzzled and then a light dawned upon him.

“Of course, if he has this infernal noise to make him go to sleep, the bell push switches off the music and saves him getting out of bed.”

===Chinese===

两人不费吹灰之力进了宅子。曼弗雷德把双座车停在一户医生家门前——那种地方，一辆没人看管的车不会惹眼——再步行到林恩的住宅。这是幢宽敞的独栋住宅，陈设豪奢；不出莱昂所料，仆人们都走了。他找到了林恩的房间，是宅子前部一套宽敞的屋子。

“他那台吵闹匣子在那儿，”莱昂指着窗边一台漂亮的柜机说，“还是电动的。那根电线通到哪儿去？”

他顺着软电线一路追到床头上方，电线的尽头是个像悬挂式电铃按钮的东西。

莱昂一时纳闷，随即恍然大悟。

“当然了，他既然要靠这地狱般的吵闹入睡，那按钮一按就关掉音乐，省得他下床。”

===Original===

He opened the lid of the gramophone cabinet and examined the record.

“*1812*,” he chuckled. He lifted the needle from the disc, turned the switch and the green table revolved. Then he walked to the head of the bed and pushed the knob of the bell push. Instantly the revolutions stopped.

“That is it,” he nodded, and turned over the soundbox, letting the needle rest upon the edge of the record.

“That,” he pointed to a bronze rod which ran from the centre to the side of the disc and fitted to some adjustment in the soundbox, “is the repeater. It is an American invention which I saw in Buenos Aires, but I haven’t seen many on this side. When the record is finished the rod automatically transfers the needle to the beginning of the record.”

“So that it can go on and on and on,” said Manfred interested. “I don’t wonder our friend is unpopular.”

===Chinese===

他掀开留声机柜的盖子，查看唱片。

“《1812》。”他轻声笑道。他抬起唱针，拧开开关，绿色的转盘转了起来。随后他走到床头，按了按按钮。转盘应声停住。

“就是它。”他点点头，翻转唱头，让唱针搭在唱片边上。

“那个，”他指着一根从唱片中央延到盘沿、接在唱头一处调节装置上的青铜杆说，“是自动复奏杆。是美国人的发明，我在布宜诺斯艾利斯见过，可咱们这一边还不多见。唱片一放完，这根杆子就自动把唱针挪回唱片开头。”

“好让它一遍一遍又一遍地放下去，”曼弗雷德饶有兴致地说，“怪不得咱们这位朋友不得人心。”

===Original===

Leon was looking round the room for something and at last he found what he was seeking. It was a brass clothes peg fastened to a door which led to a dressing-room. He put all his weight on the peg but it held firm.

“Excellent,” he said, and opened his bag. From this he took a length of stout cord and skilfully knotted one end to the clothes hook. He tested it but it did not move. From the bag he took a pair of handcuffs, unlocked and opened them and laid them on the bed. Then he took out what looked to be a Field-Marshal’s baton. It was about fourteen inches long, and fastened around were two broad strips of felt; tied neatly to the baton were nine pieces of cord which were fastened at one end to the cylinder. The cords were twice the length of the handle and were doubled over neatly and temporarily fastened to the handle by pieces of twine.

Leon looked at one end of the baton and Manfred saw a red seal.

===Chinese===

莱昂在屋里四下找什么，终于找到了要找的东西：一枚黄铜衣帽钩，钉在一扇通往更衣室的门上。他把全身分量都压上去，那钩子纹丝不动。

“好极了。”他说着打开提包。他从包里取出一截结实的绳子，利索地把一头拴在衣帽钩上。他试了试，纹丝不动。又从包里拿出一副手铐，开锁张开口，搁在床上。接着取出一杆东西，看着像元帅的权杖。约莫十四英寸长，杖身上绕着两条宽毡带；整整齐齐系在杖上的，是九条细绳，一端都拴在杖身上。绳子比握柄长出一倍，齐齐对折，暂时用细麻线扎在柄上。

莱昂看了看权杖的一头，曼弗雷德看见一枚红色封印。

===Original===

“What on earth is that, Leon?”

Leon showed him the seal, and Manfred read:

> “Prison Commission.”

“That,” said Leon, “is what is colloquially known as the ‘cat.’ In other words, the ‘cat of nine tails.’ It is an authentic instrument which I secured with some difficulty.”

He cut the twine that held the cords to the handle and let the nine thongs fall straight. Manfred took them into his hands and examined them curiously. The cords were a little thinner than ordinary window line, but more closely woven: at the end of each thong there was a binding of yellow silk for about half an inch.

===Chinese===

“这到底是什么，莱昂？”

莱昂把封印给他看，曼弗雷德读到：

> “监狱委员会。”

“这东西，”莱昂说，“就是行话里俗称‘猫儿鞭’的——换句话说，‘九尾鞭’。是一件地道货，我费了些周折才弄到手。”

他割断把绳条扎在柄上的麻线，让九条绳条直直垂下。曼弗雷德把它们拿在手里，好奇地检视。这些绳条比寻常的拉窗绳略细，但编得更紧密：每条绳梢都缠着约半英寸的黄丝线。

===Original===

Leon took the weapon in his hands and sent the cords whistling round his head.

“Made in Pentonville Gaol,” he explained, “and I’m afraid I’m not as expert as the gentleman who usually wields it.”

The dusk grew to darkness. The two men made their way downstairs and waited in the room leading from the hall.

At half past ten exactly they heard a key turn in the lock and the door close.

“Are you there, Mandez?” called the voice of Mr. Lynne, and it sounded anxious.

He took three steps towards the door and then Gonsalez stepped out.

“Good evening, Mr. Lynne,” he said.

===Chinese===

莱昂双手握住这件刑具，抡得绳条在头顶嗖嗖生风。

“彭顿维尔监狱（Pentonville Gaol）的制品，”他解释道，“只怕我的手法，不及平日挥它的那位先生娴熟。”

暮色渐渐沉成黑夜。两人下楼，守在门厅通进去的那个房间里。

十点半整，他们听见钥匙转动锁孔，门关上了。

“你在吗，曼德斯？”林恩先生的嗓音喊道，听来焦灼。

他朝门口走了三步，冈萨雷斯闪身而出。

“晚上好，林恩先生。”他说。

===Original===

The man switched on the light.

He saw before him a figure plainly dressed, but who it was he could not guess, for the intruder’s face was covered by a white semi-diaphanous veil.

“Who are you? What do you want?” gasped Lynne.

“I want you,” said Leon shortly. “Before we go any further, I will tell you this, Mr. Lynne, that if you make an outcry, if you attempt to attract attention from outside, it will be the last sound you ever make.”

“What do you want of me?” asked the stout man shakily, and then his eyes fell upon Manfred similarly veiled and he collapsed into the hall chair.

===Chinese===

那人拧亮了灯。

他眼前立着一个人，衣着朴素，可究竟是谁却猜不透，因为来客的脸罩在一幅半透明白纱后面。

“你们是谁？想干什么？”林恩喘着气问。

“我要的就是你，”莱昂简短地说，“往下进行之前，我先告诉你一句，林恩先生：你若喊叫，若想招来外头的注意，那就是你这辈子发出的最后一声。”

“你要我怎么样？”胖子哆哆嗦嗦地问，随即他的目光落在同样罩着面纱的曼弗雷德身上，整个人瘫进了门厅的椅子里。

===Original===

Manfred gripped his arm and led him upstairs to his bedroom. The blinds were pulled and the only light came from a small table-lamp by the side of the bed.

“Take off your coat,” said Manfred.

Mr. Lynne obeyed.

“Now your waistcoat.”

The waistcoat was discarded.

“Now I fear I shall have to have your shirt,” said Gonsalez.

“What are you going to do?” asked the man hoarsely.

===Chinese===

曼弗雷德攥住他的胳膊，把他带上楼送进卧室。百叶窗都拉下了，唯一的光亮来自床边一盏小小的台灯。

“把外衣脱了。”曼弗雷德说。

林恩先生照办了。

“再脱马甲。”

马甲也脱下了。

“恐怕连衬衫也得劳驾。”冈萨雷斯说。

“你们要干什么？”那人声音沙哑地问。

===Original===

“I will tell you later.”

The stout man, his face twitching, stood bare to the waist, and offered no resistance when Manfred snapped the handcuffs on him.

They led him to the door where the hat peg was, and deftly Leon slipped the loose end of the rope through the links and pulled his manacled hands tightly upwards.

“Now we can talk,” said Gonsalez. “Mr. Lynne, for some time you have been engaged in abominable traffic. You have been sending women, who sometimes were no more than children, to South America, and the penalties for that crime are, as you know, a term of imprisonment and this.”

===Chinese===

“回头再告诉你。”

胖子脸上肌肉抽搐着，光着上身站在那里；曼弗雷德啪地给他扣上手铐时，他也没有反抗。

两人把他带到装有衣帽钩的那扇门前，莱昂手法利落，把绳子的活头穿过铐环，将他戴铐的双手紧紧向上拽起。

“现在咱们可以谈谈了，”冈萨雷斯说，“林恩先生，一段时间以来，你一直在经营一桩可憎的买卖。你把那些有时不过是孩子的女人送往南美，而你知道，这桩罪名的刑罚——一是徒刑，二就是这个。”

===Original===

He picked the baton from where he had placed it, and shook out the loose cords. Mr. Lynne gazed at them over his shoulder, with a fascinated stare.

“This is colloquially known as the ‘cat of nine tails,’ ” said Gonsalez, and sent the thongs shrilling round his head.

“I swear to you I never knew﻿—” blubbered the man. “You can’t prove it﻿—”

===Chinese===

他从先前搁放的地方拿起那杆刑具，抖开松散的绳条。林恩先生扭头望着它们，目光像被魇住了一般。

“这就是俗称‘九尾鞭’的刑具。”冈萨雷斯说着，抡得绳条绕头发出锐啸。

“我发誓我一直不知道——”那人涕泪交流地哭喊，“你们证明不了——”

===Original===

“I do not intend proving it in public,” said Leon carefully. “I am here merely to furnish proof to you that you cannot break the law and escape punishment.”

And then it was that Manfred started the gramophone revolving, and the blare of trumpets and the thunder of drums filled the room with strident harmony.

The same policeman to whom Manfred and Gonsalez had spoken a few nights previously paced slowly past the house and stopped to listen with a grin. So, too, did a neighbour.

“What a din that thing makes,” said the aggrieved householder.

===Chinese===

“我不打算当众举证，”莱昂从容地说，“我到这里来，只是要向你本人证明一件事：触犯法律者，休想逃脱惩罚。”

就在这时，曼弗雷德开动了留声机，嘹亮的号声与隆隆的鼓声灌满房间，汇成一片刺耳的交响。

几天前同曼弗雷德和冈萨雷斯搭过话的那名警察，慢悠悠地巡到宅前，停住脚咧嘴听了听。一位邻居也停了下来。

“那玩意儿吵得多凶。”这位满腹委屈的住户说。

===Original===

“Yes it does,” admitted the policeman. “I think he wants a new record. It sounds almost as though somebody was shrieking their head off, doesn’t it?”

“It always sounds like that to me,” grumbled the neighbour, and went on.

The policeman smiled and resumed his beat, and from behind the windows of Mr. Lynne’s bedroom came the thrilling cadences of the “Marseillaise” and the boom of guns, and a shrill thin sound of fear and pain for which Tschaikovsky was certainly not responsible.

===Chinese===

“可不是嘛，”警察承认道，“我看他该换张新唱片了。听着简直像有人在扯着嗓子拼命尖叫，是不是？”

“在我听来一向就是这个动静。”邻居嘟囔着走了。

警察笑了笑，继续巡逻去了。林恩先生卧室的窗户后面，传出《马赛曲》激荡的乐句和大炮的轰鸣——还有一种又尖又细的、出于恐惧与疼痛的声响，那可绝不是柴可夫斯基的责任。

## The Man Who Was Plucked / 被榨干的人

===Original===

On Sunday night Martaus Club is always crowded with the smartest of the smart people who remain in Town over the weekend. Martaus Club is a place of shaded lights, of white napery, of glittering silver and glass, of exotic flowers, the tables set about the walls framing a parallelogram of shining floor.

Young men and women, and older folks too, can be very happy in Martaus Club—at a price. It is not the size of the “note” which Louis, the head waiter, initials, nor the amazing cost of wine, nor the half-a-crown strawberries, that breaks a man.

John Eden could have footed the bill for all he ate or drank or smoked in Martaus, and in truth the club was as innocent as it was gay. A pack of cards had never been found within its portals. Louis knew every face and the history behind the face, and could have told within a few pounds just what was the bank balance of every habitué. He did not know John Eden, who was the newest of members, but he guessed shrewdly.

John Eden had danced with a strange girl, which was unusual at Martaus, for you bring your own dancing partner, and never under any circumstances solicit a dance with a stranger.

===Chinese===

礼拜天晚上，马尔托俱乐部（Martaus Club）里总是挤满了周末留在城里的上流人物中最时髦的那一拨人。马尔托俱乐部里灯光半明，白色桌布，银器玻璃器皿熠熠生辉，异国花卉处处摆设，餐桌沿墙排开，围出一方长方形的晶亮舞池。

年轻的男男女女，连同年长些的人们，在马尔托俱乐部里都可以过得非常快活——只要出得起价钱。叫人倾家荡产的，倒不是领班路易（Louis）签押的那张“账单”数目多大，也不是酒价贵得惊人，更不是半克朗一份的草莓。

约翰·伊登（John Eden）在马尔托的吃、喝、烟，本来都付得起账，而说实在的，这家俱乐部寻欢归寻欢，清白也是真清白：门墙之内，一副纸牌也从未查出过。路易认得每一张面孔，也认得面孔背后的来历，随便哪位常客的银行存款有多少，他都能说得上下不差几镑。约翰·伊登是会员里最新的一个，他不认识，但他精明地猜了个八九不离十。

约翰·伊登同一位陌生姑娘跳了舞，这在马尔托可是件新鲜事，因为来这里的人都自带舞伴，无论如何也不会开口邀一个生人共舞。

===Original===

But Welby was there. Jack knew him slightly, though he had not seen him for years. Welby was the mirror of fashion and apparently a person of some importance. When he came across to him. Jack felt rather like a country cousin. He had been eight years in South Africa, and he felt rather out of it, but Welby was kindness itself, and then and there insisted upon introducing him to Maggie Vane. A beautiful girl, beautifully gowned, magnificently jewelled—her pearl necklace cost £20,000—she rather took poor Jack’s breath away, and when she suggested that they should go to Bingley’s, he would not have dreamt of refusing.

As he passed out through the lobby Louis, the head waiter, with an apology, brushed a little bit of fluff from his dress-coat, and said in a voice, inaudible, save to Jack:

“Don’t go to Bingley’s,” which was of course a preposterous piece of impertinence, and Jack glared at him.

===Chinese===

可是韦尔比（Welby）在场。杰克（Jack）同他有过一面之缘，虽说多年没有见过了。韦尔比是时尚的一面镜子，看样子还是个颇有身份的人物。他走过来招呼时，杰克自觉活像个乡下亲戚。他在南非待了八年，自觉大大落伍于潮流，可韦尔比和气到十分，当场便执意要引见他去认识玛吉·维恩（Maggie Vane）。一位美丽的姑娘，衣装雅丽，珠翠盈头——单是那串珍珠项链就值 20,000 英镑——可怜的杰克一时连气都喘不过来，待她提议两人到宾利家（Bingley's）去坐坐，他是做梦也想不到拒绝的。

他穿过门厅出去时，领班路易一面赔着不是，一面替他掸去晚礼服上的一星绒屑，用一种除了杰克谁也听不见的声音说：

“别去宾利家。”这自然是荒唐无礼之至的一句话，杰克狠狠瞪了他一眼。

===Original===

He was at Bingley’s until six o’clock in the morning, and left behind him cheques which would absorb every penny he had brought back from Africa, and a little more. He had come home dreaming of a little estate with a little shooting and a little fishing, and the writing of that book of his on big-game hunting, and all his dreams went out when the croupier, with a smile on his bearded lips, turned a card with a mechanical:

“*Le Rouge gagnant et couleur.*”

He had never dreamt Bingley’s was a gambling-house, and it certainly had not that appearance when he went in. It was only when this divine girl introduced him to the inner room, where they played *trente-et-quarante* and he saw how high the stakes were running, that he began to feel nervous. He sat by her side at the table and staked modestly and won. And continued to win—until he increased his stakes.

They were very obliging at Bingley’s. They accepted cheques, and, indeed, had cheque forms ready to be filled in.

===Chinese===

他在宾利家一直待到清晨六点，留下一叠支票，足把他从非洲带回的每一个便士尽数吸干，还倒贴了一些。他回国时满怀梦想：置一份小小的田产，猎些野物，钓些鱼，再写完他那部猎兽的大书；而当那蓄须的荷官唇边挂着笑，机械地翻出一张牌，口中念道——他的千般梦想便都熄灭了：

“*Le Rouge gagnant et couleur.*”（“红方胜，兼同色。”）

他做梦也不曾想到宾利家是个赌窟，进去的时候，那地方看上去也确实毫无赌窟的样子。直到这位天仙似的姑娘引他进了里间——里间玩的是 *trente-et-quarante*（“三十与四十”赌戏）——他看见赌注下得那样大，才开始心里发慌。他在赌台边坐在她身旁，下注下得节制，赢了；而且一路赢下去——直到他加大了赌注。

宾利家的人再殷勤不过。他们收支票，而且确实备好了现成的支票格式，只等填写。

===Original===

Jack Eden came back to the flat he had taken in Jermyn Street, which was immediately above that occupied by Manfred and Leon Gonsalez, and wrote a letter to his brother in India  …

Manfred heard the shot and woke up. He came out into the sitting-room in his pyjamas to find Leon already there, looking at the ceiling, the whitewash of which was discoloured by a tiny red patch which was growing larger.

Manfred went out on to the landing and found the proprietor of the flats in his shirt and trousers, for he had heard the shot from his basement apartment.

“I thought it was in your room, sir,” he said, “it must be in Mr. Eden’s apartment.”

Going up the stairs he explained that Mr. Eden was a new arrival in the country. The door of his flat was locked, but the proprietor produced a key which opened it. The lights were burning in the sitting-room, and one glance told Manfred the story. A huddled figure was lying across the table, from which the blood was dripping and forming a pool on the floor.

===Chinese===

杰克·伊登回到他在杰明街（Jermyn Street）租下的公寓——恰在曼弗雷德（Manfred）与莱昂·冈萨雷斯（Leon Gonsalez）住所的正上方——给他远在印度的兄弟写了一封信……

曼弗雷德听见枪声，惊醒了。他穿着睡衣走进客厅，发觉莱昂已经在那里，正仰面望着天花板——天花板的白灰上洇开了一小片红斑，而且还在渐渐扩大。

曼弗雷德出门到楼梯平台上，撞见公寓房东只穿着衬衫长裤——他从地下室的住处也听见了枪声。

“我还当是在您房里响的呢，先生，”他说，“准是在伊登先生屋里。”

上楼时他解释说，伊登先生是刚回国的人。公寓的门锁着，房东却掏出一把钥匙开了锁。客厅里灯还亮着，曼弗雷德一眼便看明了一切。一团蜷缩的身影伏在桌上，鲜血正顺着桌沿滴落，在地板上积成一洼。

===Original===

Gonsalez handled the man scientifically.

“He is not dead,” he said. “I doubt if the bullet has touched any vital organ.”

The man had shot himself in the breast; from the direction of the wound Gonsalez was fairly sure that the injuries were minor. He applied a first-aid dressing and together they lifted him on to a sofa. Then when the wound was dressed, Gonsalez looked round and saw the telltale letter.

“Pinner,” he said, holding the letter up, “I take it that you do not want to advertise the fact that somebody attempted to commit suicide in one of your flats?”

===Chinese===

冈萨雷斯以极科学的手法检视伤者。

“他没有死，”他说，“子弹未必伤着了什么要害器官。”

那人是朝自己胸口开的枪；从创口的方向判断，冈萨雷斯颇有把握伤势轻微。他做了急救包扎，两人合力把他抬上一张沙发。包扎停当，冈萨雷斯环顾四周，看见了那封泄底的书信。

“平纳（Pinner），”他举起那封信说，“我揣想，你总不愿意把有人在你公寓里企图自杀的事张扬出去吧？”

===Original===

“That is the last thing in the world I want,” said the flat proprietor, fervently.

“Then I’m going to put this letter in my pocket. Will you telephone to the hospital and say there has been an accident. Don’t talk about suicide. The gentleman has recently come back from South Africa; he was packing his pistol, and it exploded.”

The man nodded and left the room hurriedly.

Gonsalez went to where Eden was lying and it was at that moment the young man’s eyes opened. He looked from Manfred to Gonsalez with a puzzled frown.

“My friend,” said Leon, in a gentle voice as he leant over the wounded man, “you have had an accident, you understand? You are not fatally injured; in fact, I think your injury is a very slight one. The ambulance will come for you and you will go to a hospital and I will visit you daily.”

===Chinese===

“那是天底下我最不愿意的事。”公寓房东恳切地说。

“那么这封信我就揣进自己口袋了。请你给医院挂个电话，就说出了个意外。别提自杀。这位先生刚从南非回来，收拾手枪的时候，枪走了火。”

那人点点头，急匆匆走了出去。

冈萨雷斯走到伊登躺着的地方，恰在那一刻，年轻人睁开了眼睛。他皱着眉头，满脸困惑，看看曼弗雷德，又看看冈萨雷斯。

“我的朋友，”莱昂俯身向着伤者，语声温和地说，“你出了一桩意外，明白么？你没有伤在致命处；说实在的，我看你的伤很轻。救护车会来接你，你要进医院，我每天都会去看你。”

===Original===

“Who are you?” whispered the man.

“I am a neighbour of yours,” smiled Leon.

“The letter!” Eden gasped the words and Leon nodded.

“I have it in my pocket,” he said, “and I will restore it to you when you are recovered. You understand that you have had an accident?”

Eden nodded.

A quarter of an hour later the hospital ambulance rolled up to the door, and the would-be suicide was taken away.

===Chinese===

“你是谁？”那人喃喃地问。

“我是你的一个邻居。”莱昂微笑道。

“那封信！”伊登喘着气说出这几个字，莱昂点了点头。

“它在我口袋里，”他说，“等你痊愈我便还给你。你明白你出了一场意外吧？”

伊登点了点头。

一刻钟后，医院的救护车辚辚驶到门前，这位自杀未遂者被抬走了。

===Original===

“Now,” said Leon, when they were back in their own room, “we will discover what all this is about,” and very calmly he slit open the envelope and read.

“What is it?” asked Manfred.

“Our young friend came back from South Africa with £7,000, which he had accumulated in eight years of hard work. He lost it in less than eight hours at a gambling-house which he does not specify. He has not only lost all the money he has but more, and apparently has given cheques to meet his debts.”

Leon scratched his chin.

“That necessitates a further examination of his room. I wonder if the admirable Mr. Pinner will object?”

===Chinese===

“好了，”回到自己房里后，莱昂说，“咱们来看看这究竟是怎么回事。”他从容不迫地裁开封套，读了起来。

“写了什么？”曼弗雷德问。

“咱们这位年轻朋友从南非带回 7,000 英镑，那是他八年辛劳攒下的。不到八个钟头，他就在一家他没有点名的赌窟里输了个干净。他不但输光了身边所有的钱，还赔上了没有的，而且显然开了支票来抵赌债。”

莱昂挠了挠下巴。

“这就免不了再查一遍他的房间。不知那位可敬的平纳先生会不会见怪？”

===Original===

The admirable Mr. Pinner was quite willing that Leon should anticipate the inevitable visit of the police. The search was made, and Leon found a chequebook for which he had been looking, tucked away in the inside pocket of Jack Eden’s dress-suit, and brought it down to his room.

“No names,” he said disappointedly. “Just ‘cash’ on every counterfoil. All, I should imagine, to the same person. He banks with the Third National Bank of South Africa, which has an office in Throgmorton Street.”

He carefully copied the numbers of the cheques—there were ten in all.

===Chinese===

那位可敬的平纳先生十分情愿让莱昂抢在警察必然上门之前先搜一遍。搜查做过了，莱昂找到了他一直在找的那本支票簿，正塞在杰克·伊登晚礼服的内袋里，便把它拿回楼下自己房中。

“没有抬头人名，”他失望地说，“每张存根上只写着‘现金’。我猜想，全都是开给同一个人的。他开户的银行是南非第三国民银行（Third National Bank of South Africa），在斯罗格莫顿街（Throgmorton Street）设有办事处。”

他仔细抄下那些支票的号码——总共有十张。

===Original===

“First of all,” he said, “as soon as the post office is open we will send a telegram to the bank stopping the payment of these. Of course he can be sued, but a gambling debt is not recoverable at law, and before that happens we shall see many developments.”

The first development came the next afternoon. Leon had given instructions that anybody who called for Mr. Eden was to be shown up to him, and at three o’clock came a very smartly dressed young man who aspirated his *h*’s with suspicious emphasis. “Is this Mr. Eden’s flat?”

“Is this Mr. Eden’s flat?”

“No, it isn’t,” said Gonsalez. “It is the flat of myself and my friend who are acting for Mr. Eden.”

The visitor frowned suspiciously at Leon.

“Acting for him?” he said. “Well, you can perhaps give me a little information about some cheques that have been stopped. My governor went to get a special clearance this morning, and the bank refused payment. Does Mr. Eden know all about this?”

===Chinese===

“头一件事，”他说，“等邮局一开门，我们就给银行拍电报，止付这些支票。当然他可能吃官司，可赌债在法律上是追不回来的，而真到那一步之前，我们还有的是好戏看。”

第二天下午，头一场好戏就来了。莱昂早已吩咐下去：凡是为伊登先生来的客人，一律领上楼见他。三点钟便来了一个衣着极为时髦的年轻人，说起话来把词首的 *h* 音咬得重得可疑。“这是伊登先生的公寓吗？”

“这是伊登先生的公寓吗？”

“不是，”冈萨雷斯说，“这是我和我朋友的公寓，我们两人代伊登先生办事。”

来客朝莱昂狐疑地皱起了眉。

“代他办事？”他说，“那好，几张被止付的支票，你或许能给我一点消息。我们老板今天上午去办理特别兑付，银行拒付了。这事伊登先生全知道吗？”

===Original===

“Who is your governor?” asked Leon pleasantly.

“Mr. Mortimer Birn.”

“And his address?”

The young man gave it. Mr. Mortimer Birn was apparently a bill-discounter, and had cashed the cheques for a number of people who did not want to pass them through their banks. The young man was very emphatic as to the cheques being the property of a large number of people.

“And they all came to Mr. Birn. What a singular coincidence,” agreed Leon.

“I’d rather see Mr. Eden, if you don’t mind,” said the emissary of Mr. Mortimer Birn, and his tone was unpleasant.

===Chinese===

“你们的老板是谁？”莱昂和颜悦色地问。

“莫蒂默·伯恩先生（Mr. Mortimer Birn）。”

“他的地址呢？”

年轻人报了地址。莫蒂默·伯恩先生明摆着是个票据贴现商，替不少不愿让支票过自家银行的人兑过现钱。年轻人一口咬定，那些支票是好许多人的产业。

“而它们全都落到了伯恩先生手里。多么罕见的巧合。”莱昂随声附和道。

“你要是不见怪，我倒宁愿见伊登先生本人。”莫蒂默·伯恩先生的这位使者说，语气很不客气。

===Original===

“You cannot see him because he has met with an accident,” said Leon. “But I will see your Mr. Birn.”

He found Mr. Birn in a very tiny office in Glasshouse Street. The gentleman’s business was not specified either on the doorplate or on the painted window, but Leon smelt “moneylender” the moment he went into his office.

The outer office was unoccupied when he entered. It was a tiny dusty cupboard of a place with just room enough to put a diminutive table, and the space was further curtailed by a wooden partition, head high, which served to exclude the unfortunate person who occupied the room from draughts and immediate observation. A door marked private led to Mr. Birn’s holy of holies and from this room came the sound of loud voices.

===Chinese===

“你见不到他，因为他遭了一场意外，”莱昂说，“不过你们的伯恩先生，我倒可以见一见。”

他在格拉斯豪斯街（Glasshouse Street）一间极小的办公室里找到了伯恩先生。这位先生的营生，门牌上没有写，玻璃窗上的字号也没有写，可莱昂一走进他的办公室，就嗅出了“放印子钱”的气味。

他进去时外间空无一人。那是小得像个积灰橱柜似的一块地方，刚够摆一张小小的桌子，空间还被一道齐头高的木隔板又截去一块——这隔板的用处，是把待在这屋里的倒霉人同过堂风和旁人就近的注目隔开。一扇写着“闲人免进”的门，通向伯恩先生的至圣所，那间屋里正传出高声的说话。

===Original===

Gonsalez listened.

“… come without telephoning, hey? She always comes in the morning, haven’t I told you a hundred times?” roared one voice.

“She doesn’t know me,” grumbled the other.

“She’s only got to see your hair …”

It was at that moment that the young man who called at Jermyn Street came out of the room. Gonsalez had a momentary glimpse of two men. One was short and stout, the other was tall, but it was his bright red hair that caught Leon’s eye. And then Mr. Birn’s clerk went back to the room and the voices ceased. When Gonsalez was ushered into the office, only the proprietor of the establishment was visible.

===Chinese===

冈萨雷斯侧耳听去。

“……不先打个电话就来，嘿？她向来都是上午来，我没跟你说过一百遍了？”一个声音吼道。

“她不认得我嘛。”另一个咕哝道。

“她只消瞧见你那头头发……”

恰在那一刻，到杰明街登门的那位年轻人从那间屋里走了出来。冈萨雷斯一眼瞥见了两个人。一个矮胖，一个高个儿，可攫住莱昂目光的，是那人火红的头发。随后伯恩先生的文书回房去了，说话声戛然而止。冈萨雷斯被让进办公室时，看得见的只剩这家买卖的老板一个人。

===Original===

Birn was a stout bald man, immensely affable. He told Leon the same story as his clerk had told.

“Now, what is Eden going to do about these cheques?” asked Birn at last.

“I don’t think he’s going to meet them,” said Leon gently. “You see, they are gambling debts.”

“They are cheques,” interrupted Birn, “and a cheque is a cheque whether it’s for a gambling debt or a sack of potatoes.”

===Chinese===

伯恩是个秃顶的胖子，和气得不得了。他对莱昂说的那一套，同他文书说的如出一辙。

“那么，这些支票伊登打算怎么办？”伯恩终于问道。

“我看他不打算兑付，”莱昂轻声说，“你要明白，那是赌债。”

“它们是支票，”伯恩打断道，“支票就是支票，管它是抵赌债的还是抵一麻袋土豆的。”

===Original===

“Is that the law?” asked Leon, “and if it is, will you write me a letter to that effect, in which case you will be paid.”

“Certainly I will,” said Mr. Birn. “If that’s all you want, I’ll write it now.”

“Proceed,” said Leon, but Mr. Birn did not write the letter.

Instead he talked about his lawyers; grew virtuously indignant on the unsportsmanlike character of people who repudiated debts of honour (how he came to be satisfied that the cheques represented gambling losses, he did not explain) and ended the interview a little apoplectically. And all the time Leon was speculating upon the identity of the third man he had seen and who had evidently left the room through one of the three doors which opened from the office.

===Chinese===

“法律是这么说的吗？”莱昂问，“如果是，可否请你照这个意思给我写一封信？那样的话，你就会得到偿付。”

“这个当然，”伯恩先生说，“你要的若不过如此，我现在就写。”

“请写吧。”莱昂说。可伯恩先生并没有写那封信。

他反而大谈起他的律师来；又正气凛然地愤慨了一通，痛骂那些赖掉“荣誉之债”的人不讲究运动家的风格（至于他自己凭什么认定那些支票是赌输的钱，他没有交代）；这场会见到末了，他几乎动了肝火。而莱昂自始至终，都在揣度他瞥见的第三个人的身份——那人分明是从办公室开出的三扇门中某一扇离开的。

===Original===

Leon went down the narrow stairs into the street, and as he stepped on to the pavement, a little car drove up and a girl descended. She did not look at him, but brushing past ran up the stairs. She was alone, and had driven her own luxurious coupe. Gonsalez, who was interested, waited till she came out, which was not for twenty minutes, and she was obviously distressed.

Leon was curious and interested. He went straight on to the hospital where they had taken Eden, and found the young man sufficiently recovered to be able to talk.

His first words betrayed his anxiety and his contrition.

“I say, what did you do with that letter? I was a fool to—”

“Destroyed it,” said Leon, which was true. “Now, my young friend, you’ve got to tell me something. Where was the gambling-house to which you went?”

It took a long time to persuade Mr. John Eden that he was not betraying a confidence and then he told him the whole story from beginning to end.

===Chinese===

莱昂下了窄窄的楼梯来到街上，脚才踏上门阶，一辆小汽车驶到门前，下来一位姑娘。她没有看他，擦身而过，径自跑上楼去。她是独自一人，开着自己那辆豪华的双门小轿车。冈萨雷斯来了兴味，一直等到她出来——这一等便是二十分钟——她分明是心绪纷乱。

莱昂又好奇又关切。他径直赶到收治伊登的那家医院，发现年轻人已经恢复得可以说话了。

他开口头一句话，就露出了他的焦虑与悔恨。

“我说，那封信你们是怎么处置的？我真是糊涂，竟——”

“销毁了。”莱昂说——这话不假。“好了，我的年轻朋友，你得告诉我一件事。你去的那个赌窟在什么地方？”

费了好一番口舌，才让约翰·伊登先生相信自己并非在出卖什么人的秘密，于是他把事情从头到尾讲了一遍。

===Original===

“So it was a lady who took you there, eh?” said Leon thoughtfully.

“She wasn’t in it,” said John Eden quickly. “She was just a visitor like myself. She told me she had lost five hundred pounds.”

“Naturally, naturally,” said Leon. “Is she a fair lady with very blue eyes, and has she a little car of her own?”

The man looked surprised.

“Yes, she drove me in her car,” he said, “and she is certainly fair and has blue eyes. In fact, she’s one of the prettiest girls I’ve ever seen. You needn’t worry about the lady, sir,” he said shaking his head. “Poor girl, she was victimised, if there was any victimisation.”

===Chinese===

“这么说，带你去那儿的是位女士，嗯？”莱昂若有所思地说。

“她没掺在里头，”约翰·伊登急忙说，“她同我一样，只是个客人。她跟我说她输了 500 英镑。”

“自然，自然，”莱昂说，“她是不是一位白肤金发、眼睛碧蓝、自己有辆小汽车的女士？”

那人露出惊讶之色。

“是啊，是她开自己的车送的我，”他说，“她也确实是白肤金发、眼睛碧蓝。说实在的，她是我平生见过的最标致的姑娘之一。这位女士您不必担心，先生，”他摇着头说，“可怜的姑娘，要说有人受了骗，她自己才是受骗的那个。”

===Original===

“196 Paul Street, Mayfair, I think you said.”

“I’m certain it was Paul Street, and almost as sure it was 196,” said Eden. “But I hope you’re not going to take any action against them, because it was my own fault. Aren’t you one of the two gentlemen who live in the flat under me?” he asked suddenly.

Leon nodded.

“I suppose the cheques have been presented and some of them have come back.”

“They have not been presented yet, or at any rate they have not been honoured,” said Leon. “And had you shot yourself, my young friend, they would not have been honoured at all, because your bank would have stopped payment automatically.”

===Chinese===

“梅费尔（Mayfair）保罗街（Paul Street）196 号，我想你是这么说的。”

“保罗街我是拿得稳的，196 号也差不多有八成准，”伊登说，“可我希望您不要对他们采取什么行动，因为错在我自己。您不就是住在我楼下那套公寓里的两位先生之一吗？”他突然问。

莱昂点了点头。

“我想支票已经提示兑付了，有些还退了票。”

“它们还没有提示，或者说无论如何还没有兑付，”莱昂说，“而你要是把自己打死了，我的年轻朋友，它们就一概兑付不成了，因为你的银行会自动止付。”

===Original===

Manfred dined alone that night. Leon had not returned, and there had been no news from him until eight o’clock, when there came a District Messenger with a note asking Manfred to give the bearer his dress clothes and one or two articles which he mentioned.

Manfred was too used to the ways of Leon Gonsalez to be greatly surprised. He packed a small suitcase, sent the messenger boy off with it and he himself spent the evening writing letters.

At half past two he heard a slight scuffle in the street outside, and Leon came in without haste, and in no wise perturbed, although he had just emerged from a rough-and-tumble encounter with a young man who had been watching the house all the evening for his return.

===Chinese===

那天晚上曼弗雷德独自用的晚餐。莱昂没有回来，也一直没有他的音讯，直到八点钟，来了一个区信差（District Messenger），捎来一张便条，请曼弗雷德把晚礼服和便条上点到的几件东西交给来人。

对莱昂·冈萨雷斯的行事路数，曼弗雷德早已见惯，并不怎么吃惊。他收拾好一只小提箱，打发信差男孩带走，自己则整个晚上都在写信。

两点半，他听见外头街上有一阵短促的扭打，莱昂不慌不忙地走进来，神色毫不慌乱，尽管他方才同一个小伙子混战了一场——那人整个晚上都守着这房子，专等他回来。

===Original===

He was not in evening dress, Manfred noticed, but was wearing the clothes he had on when he went out in the morning.

“You got your suitcase all right?”

“Oh yes, quite,” replied Leon.

He took a short stick from his trousers pocket, a stick made of rhinoceros hide, and called in South Africa a “sjambok.” It was about a foot and a half in length, but it was a formidable weapon and was one of the articles which Leon had asked for. He examined it in the light.

“No, I didn’t cut his scalp,” he said. “I was afraid I had.”

“Who was this?”

===Chinese===

曼弗雷德注意到，他没穿晚礼服，身上还是早晨出门时那套衣裳。

“你的提箱收到无误吧？”

“哦，收到了，完好无缺。”莱昂答道。

他从裤袋里掏出一截短鞭，那是犀牛皮做的，在南非叫做“sjambok”（犀皮鞭）。约有一英尺半长，却是件了不得的兵器，也是莱昂点名要来的东西之一。他把它拿到灯下端详。

“没破他的头皮，”他说，“我原先还怕破了呢。”

“那是何人？”

===Original===

Before he replied, Leon put out the light, pulled back the curtains from the open window and looked out. He came back, replaced the curtains, and put the light on again.

“He has gone away, but I do not think we have seen the last of that crowd,” he said.

He drank a glass of water, sat down by the table and laughed.

“Do you realise, my dear Manfred,” he said, “that we have a friend in Mr. Fare, the Police Commissioner, and that he occasionally visits us?”

“I realise that very well,” smiled Manfred. “Why, have you seen him?”

Leon shook his head.

===Chinese===

答话之前，莱昂先熄了灯，把敞开的窗子前的窗帘拉到一旁，向外张望。他回身把窗帘复原，再点起灯来。

“他走了，不过我看，这一伙人咱们还没打完交道。”他说。

他喝了一杯水，在桌边坐下，笑了起来。

“你可意识到，我亲爱的曼弗雷德，”他说，“咱们在警察总监费尔先生（Mr. Fare）那里有一位朋友，而这位朋友偶尔还要来看望咱们？”

“这个我清楚得很。”曼弗雷德微笑道，“怎么，你见到他了？”

莱昂摇了摇头。

===Original===

“No, only other people have seen him and have associated me with the Metropolitan Constabulary. I had occasion to interview our friend Mr. Bingley, and he and those who are working with him are perfectly satisfied that I am what is known in London as a ‘split,’ in other words a detective, and it is generally believed that I am engaged in the business of suppressing gambling-houses. Hence the mild attention I have received and hence the fact, as I recognised when I was on my way back to Jermyn Street today—luckily I had forgotten to tell the cabman where to stop and he passed the watchers before I could stop him—that I am under observation.”

He described his visit to the hospital and his interview with Mr. Birn.

“Birn, who of course is Bingley, is the proprietor of three, and probably more, big gambling-houses in London, at least he is the financial power behind them. I should not imagine that he himself frequents any of them. The house in Mayfair was, of course, shut up tonight and I did not attempt to locate it. They were afraid that our poor friend would inform the police. But oh, my dear Manfred, how can I describe to you the beauties of that lovely house in Bayswater Road, where all that is fashionable and wealthy in London gathers every night to try its luck at baccarat?”

===Chinese===

“没有，见着他的只是别人，还把我和首都警察队伍挂上了钩。我有事要访一访咱们的朋友宾利先生（Mr. Bingley），如今他和他的同伙深信不疑，我就是伦敦黑话里所谓的‘split’（‘条子’，换句话说就是警探），而且一般都认定我是干查禁赌窟这一营生的。所以我才受了那点小小的关照；也所以——今天在回杰明街的路上我察觉到了——我已被人盯上了，幸亏我忘了告诉车夫在哪里停车，没等我叫停，他已经驶过了盯梢的人。”

他讲了自己去医院探望的经过，以及同伯恩先生的一番会谈。

“伯恩——他自然就是宾利——名下开着三家、多半还不止三家伦敦的大赌窟，至少，他是背后的财东。我看他自己哪家都不常去。梅费尔那处今晚自然是闭门谢客了，我也不曾去找。他们生怕咱们那位可怜的朋友去报官。可是啊，我亲爱的曼弗雷德，贝斯沃特路（Bayswater Road）上那座可爱的宅子，我该怎么向你形容它的妙处呢——伦敦一切时髦阔绰的人物，夜夜聚在那里碰运气赌巴卡拉（baccarat）？”

===Original===

“How did you get there?” asked Manfred.

“I was taken,” replied Gonsalez simply. “I went to dinner at Martaus Club. I recognised Mr. Welby and greeted him as an old friend. I think he really believed that he had met me before I went to the Argentine and made my pile, and of course, he sat down with me and we drank liqueurs, and he introduced me to a most beautiful girl, with a most perfectly upholstered little runabout.”

“You weren’t recognised?”

Leon shook his head.

“The moustache which I put on my face was indistinguishable from the real thing,” he said not without pride. “I put it on hair by hair, and it took me two hours to manufacture. When it was done you would not have recognised me. I danced with the beautiful Margaret and—” He hesitated.

“You made love to her,” said Manfred admiringly.

===Chinese===

“你是怎么进去的？”曼弗雷德问。

“我是被人带去的。”冈萨雷斯答得干脆。“我到马尔托俱乐部去用晚餐。我认出了韦尔比先生，把他当老朋友一般招呼。我看他真是相信，在我去阿根廷发迹之前就同我有过交往；于是当然，他挨着我坐下，我们喝着利口酒，他把我引见给一位绝色佳人——还连带一辆软垫装得分外考究的小跑车。”

“没被认出来？”

莱昂摇了摇头。

“我贴在脸上的那撇小胡子，同真的一模一样、无从分辨，”他说，不无得意。“我一根一根粘上去的，足足做了两个钟头。做成之后，连你也认不出我来。我同美丽的玛格丽特（Margaret）跳了舞，后来——”他迟疑了一下。

“你向她大献殷勤了。”曼弗雷德叹服地说。

===Original===

Leon shrugged.

“My dear Manfred, it was necessary,” he said solemnly. “And was it not fortunate that I had in my pocket a diamond ring which I had brought back from South America—it cost me 110 guineas in Regent Street this afternoon—and how wonderful that it should fit her. She wasn’t feeling at her best, either, until that happened. That was the price of my admission to the Bayswater establishment. She drove me there in her car. It was a visit not without profit,” he said modestly. He put his hand in his pocket and pulled out a stiff bundle of notes.

Manfred was laughing softly.

Leon was the cleverest manipulator of cards in Europe. His long delicate fingers, the amazing rapidity with which he could move them, his natural gift for palming, would have made his fortune either as a conjurer or a cardsharper.

===Chinese===

莱昂耸了耸肩。

“我亲爱的曼弗雷德，那是非如此不可，”他郑重其事地说，“何况多巧，我口袋里恰好有一枚从南美带回来的钻石戒指——今天下午在摄政街（Regent Street）花了我 110 几尼——而它居然正合她的手指，你说妙不妙。在那之前，她本来还有些恹恹的呢。那便是我进贝斯沃特那处的门票钱。她开自己的车送我去的。这一趟也并非全无收获。”他谦逊地说。他把手伸进口袋，掏出一沓挺括的钞票。

曼弗雷德在低声轻笑。

莱昂是欧洲第一等的操牌妙手。他那修长灵巧的手指，手指移动时那惊人的速度，还有掌心里藏牌的天赋，无论做魔术师还是做赌场老千，都足以使他发迹。

===Original===

“The game was baccarat and the cards were dealt from a box by an intelligent croupier,” explained Leon. “Those which were used were thrown into a basin. Those in the stack were, of course, so carefully arranged that the croupier knew the sequence of them all. To secure a dozen cards from the basin was a fairly simple matter. To stroll from the room and rearrange them so that they were alternately against and for the bank, was not difficult, but to place them on the top of those he was dealing, my dear Manfred, I was an artist!”

Leon did not explain what form his artistry took, nor how he directed the attention of croupier and company away from the “deck” for that fraction of a second necessary—the croupier seldom took his hands from the cards—but the results of his enterprise were to be found in the thick stack of notes which lay on the table.

===Chinese===

“赌的是巴卡拉，牌由一个乖觉的荷官从牌盒里发出来，”莱昂解说道，“用过的牌丢进一只盆里。牌叠里的牌自然排得井井有条，荷官对全部的次序了然于心。从盆里弄出一打牌来，不算什么难事。踱出房去，把它们重新排过，输庄赢庄相间，也不费力；可是要把它们放到他正发着的那叠牌顶上——我亲爱的曼弗雷德，这一手，我是个艺术家！”

莱昂没有解释他的艺术用的是何等手法，也没有解释他如何在那必要的一眨眼工夫里，把荷官和满座赌客的注意力从“牌叠”上引开——荷官是难得把手从牌上挪开的——但他这番营生的进项，全在桌上摊着的那厚厚一沓钞票里了。

===Original===

He took off his coat and put on his old velvet jacket, pacing the room with his hands in his pockets.

“Margaret Vane,” he said softly. “One of God’s most beautiful works, George, flawless, gifted, and yet if she is what she appears, something so absolutely loathsome that …”

He shook his head sadly.

“Does she play a big part, or is she just a dupe?” asked Manfred.

Leon did not answer at once.

“I’m rather puzzled,” he said slowly. He related his experience in Mr. Birn’s office, the glimpse of the red-haired man and Mr. Birn’s fury with him.

===Chinese===

他脱下外衣，换上那件旧丝绒上衣，两手插在口袋里，在屋里踱来踱去。

“玛格丽特·维恩（Margaret Vane），”他轻声说，“上帝最美的造物之一啊，乔治——毫无瑕疵，天资过人；然而，她若当真如表面所见，那便可憎到了极处，可憎得……”

他黯然摇了摇头。

“她是在其中扮演要角呢，还是不过一个傀儡？”曼弗雷德问。

莱昂没有立刻回答。

“我也颇为纳闷。”他缓缓地说。他讲了在伯恩先生办公室里的经历，讲了他瞥见红发人的一眼，以及伯恩先生对他那场大发雷霆。

===Original===

“I do not doubt that the ‘she’ to whom reference was made was Margaret Vane. But that alone would not have shaken my faith in her guilt. After I left the Bayswater house I decided that I would discover where she was living. She had so skilfully evaded any question on this matter which I had put to her that I grew suspicious. I hired a taxicab and waited, sitting inside. Presently her car came out and I followed her. Mr. Birn has a house in Fitzroy Square and it was to there she drove. A man was waiting outside to take her car, and she went straight into the house, letting herself in. It was at this point that I began to think that Birn and she were much better friends than I had thought.

“I decided to wait, and stopped the car on the other side of the Square. In about a quarter of an hour the girl came out, and to my surprise, she had changed her clothes. I dismissed the cab and followed on foot. She lives at 803 Gower Street.”

“That certainly is puzzling,” agreed Manfred. “The thing does not seem to dovetail, Leon.”

===Chinese===

“我毫不怀疑，话里提到的那个‘她’就是玛格丽特·维恩。可单凭这一条，还动摇不了我相信她有罪。离开贝斯沃特那处之后，我决定查明她的住处。凡我向她问到这一层，她都机巧地闪避开去，我便起了疑心。我雇了一辆出租汽车，坐在车里守候。不多时她的车出来了，我便尾随。伯恩先生在菲茨罗伊广场（Fitzroy Square）有一所宅子，她驶去的就是那里。有个男人候在外面替她收车，她自己开了门，径直进屋。正是在这一节上，我开始觉得，伯恩同她的交情，比我想的要深得多。

“我决意等候，把车停在广场另一侧。约莫一刻钟后，姑娘出来了，叫我诧异的是，她换了衣裳。我打发走出租车，徒步跟上。她住在高尔街（Gower Street）803 号。”

“这确乎叫人纳闷，”曼弗雷德附和道，“这桩事前后接不上榫头啊，莱昂。”

===Original===

“That is what I think,” nodded Leon. “I am going to 803 Gower Street tomorrow morning.”

Gonsalez required very little sleep and at ten o’clock in the morning was afoot.

The report he brought back to Manfred was interesting.

“Her name is Elsie Chaucer, and she lives with her father, who is paralysed in both legs. They have a flat, one servant and a nurse, whose business it is to attend to the father. Nothing is known of them except that they have seen better times. The father spends the day with a pack of cards, working out a gambling system, and probably that explains their poverty. He is never seen by visitors, and the girl is supposed to be an actress—that is, supposed by the landlady. It is rather queer,” said Gonsalez thoughtfully. “The solution is, of course, in Birn’s house and in Birn’s mind.”

“I think we will get at that, Leon.”

Leon nodded.

===Chinese===

“我也这么想。”莱昂点头道，“明天上午我就去高尔街 803 号。”

冈萨雷斯素来少眠，早晨十点钟便已起身出门。

他带回给曼弗雷德的报告很有趣味。

“她名叫埃尔西·乔叟（Elsie Chaucer），同父亲住在一起——父亲双腿瘫痪。他们住一套公寓，雇一个女仆、一名看护，看护的职分就是侍候父亲。这家人来历无人知晓，只知道从前家道殷实过。父亲整日价摆弄一副纸牌，推敲一套赌博必胜的体系，他们的穷困多半正由于此。他从不露面见客；姑娘呢，据说是个演员——所谓据说，是房东太太的意思。这确有些蹊跷，”冈萨雷斯若有所思地说，“谜底么，自然在伯恩的宅子里，在伯恩的脑子里。”

“这个谜底，我想我们去揭一揭，莱昂。”

莱昂点了点头。

===Original===

“So I thought,” said he. “Mr. Birn’s establishment does not present any insuperable difficulties.”

Mr. Birn was at home that night. He was at home most nights. Curled up in a deep armchair, he puffed at a long and expensive cigar, and read the *London Gazette* which was to him the most interesting piece of literature which the genius of Caxton had made possible.

At midnight his housekeeper came in. She was a middle-aged Frenchwoman and discreet.

“All right?” queried Mr. Birn lazily.

“No, monsieur, I desire that you should speak to Charles.”

===Chinese===

“我也这么想，”他说，“伯恩先生那处基业，倒不存在什么攻不破的难关。”

那天夜里伯恩先生在家。他大多数夜里都在家。他蜷在一张深深的扶手椅里，抽着一支又长又贵的雪茄，读着《伦敦公报》（*London Gazette*）——在他看来，那便是卡克斯顿（Caxton）的天才赖以成真的文学品类中最有趣的一种。

午夜时分，他的管家进来了。那是个中年法国妇人，行事有分寸。

“没什么事吧？”伯恩先生懒洋洋地问。

“有事，先生，我想请您同查尔斯（Charles）说一说。”

===Original===

Charles was Mr. Birn’s chauffeur, and between Charles and Madame was a continuous feud.

“What has Charles been doing?” asked Mr. Birn with a frown.

“He is admitted every evening to the kitchen for supper,” explained madame, “and it is an order that he should close the door after he goes out. But, m’sieur, when I went this evening at eleven o’clock to bolt the door, it was not closed. If I had not put on the lights and with my own eyes have seen it, the door would have been open, and we might have been murdered in our beds.”

“I’ll talk to him in the morning,” growled Mr. Birn. “You’ve left the door of mademoiselle’s room unfastened?”

“Yes, m’sieur, the key is in the lock.”

===Chinese===

查尔斯是伯恩先生的汽车司机，查尔斯同太太之间则是一场经年不息的暗战。

“查尔斯又干了什么？”伯恩先生皱着眉问。

“每晚准他进厨房来吃晚饭，”太太解释道，“规矩是他出去之后要随手把门关上。可是，先生，今晚十一点我去上门闩，那门没有关上。要不是我开了灯、亲眼瞧见，门就那么敞着，咱们说不定都要在床上教人谋害了。”

“明早我跟他说。”伯恩先生嘟哝道。“小姐房里的门，你留着没上闩吧？”

“是的，先生，钥匙就插在锁眼里。”

===Original===

“Good night,” said Mr. Birn resuming his study. At half past two he heard the street door close gently and a light footstep passed through the hall. He looked up at the clock, threw away the end of his cigar and lit another before he rose and went heavily to a wall safe. This he unlocked and took out an empty steel box, which he opened and placed on the table. Then he resumed his chair.

Presently came a light tap at the door.

“Come in,” said Mr. Birn.

The girl who was variously called Vane and Chaucer came into the room. She was neatly but not richly dressed. In many ways the plainness of her street costume enhanced her singular beauty and Mr. Birn gazed approvingly upon her refreshing figure.

“Sit down, Miss Chaucer,” he said, putting out his hand for the little linen bag she carried.

===Chinese===

“晚安。”伯恩先生说罢接着读他的公报。两点半，他听见临街的大门轻轻合上，一串轻快的脚步穿过门厅。他抬眼看了看钟，丢掉雪茄烟尾，另点上一支，这才起身，拖着沉重的步子走向墙上的一具保险柜。他开了锁，取出一只空钢盒，打开来搁在桌上，然后重新落座。

少顷，门上响起轻轻的叩击。

“进来。”伯恩先生说。

那位时而姓维恩、时而姓乔叟的姑娘走进房来。她衣着齐整，却不华贵。好些时候，这身出门便装的素淡反倒更映衬出她出众的美貌，伯恩先生赞许地打量着她那令人爽心悦目的身影。

“请坐，乔叟小姐。”他说着，伸手去接她携带的那只小麻布袋。

===Original===

He opened it and took out a rope of pearls and examined every gem separately.

“I haven’t stolen any,” she said contemptuously.

“Perhaps you haven’t,” said Mr. Birn, “but I’ve known some funny things to happen.”

He took the diamond pin, the rings, the two diamond and emerald bracelets, and each of these he scrutinised before he returned them to the bag and put the bag into the steel box.

He did not speak until he had placed them in the safe.

“Well, how are things going tonight?” he asked.

She shrugged her shoulders.

===Chinese===

他打开袋子，取出一挂珍珠，一颗一颗分别验看。

“我一粒也没偷。”她鄙夷地说。

“也许没有，”伯恩先生说，“可我见识过些古怪事。”

他又取过钻石别针、几枚戒指、两只镶钻石和祖母绿的手镯，每一件都细细验过，才放回袋中，把袋子搁进钢盒。

直到把它们放进保险柜，他才开腔。

“怎么样，今晚光景如何？”他问。

她耸了耸肩。

===Original===

“I take no interest in gambling,” she said shortly and Mr. Birn chuckled.

“You’re a fool,” he said frankly.

“I wish I were no worse than that,” said Elsie Chaucer bitterly. “You don’t want me any more, Mr. Birn?”

“Sit down,” he ordered. “Who did you find tonight?”

For a moment she did not reply.

“The man whom Welby introduced last night,” she said.

===Chinese===

“我对赌博没有兴趣。”她淡淡地说。伯恩先生嘿嘿一笑。

“你是个傻瓜。”他坦率地说。

“我巴不得我顶坏也不过是个傻瓜呢。”埃尔西·乔叟苦涩地说，“您没有别的要我了，伯恩先生？”

“坐下。”他吩咐道，“今晚你物色到了谁？”

她半晌没有作声。

“韦尔比昨晚引见的那个人。”她说。

===Original===

“The South American?” Mr. Birn pulled a long face. “He wasn’t very profitable. I suppose you know that? We lost about four thousand pounds.”

“Less the ring,” said the girl.

“The ring he gave you? Well, that’s worth about a hundred, and I’ll be lucky to get sixty for it,” said Mr. Birn with a shrug. “You can keep that ring if you like.”

“No thank you, Mr. Birn,” said the girl quietly. “I don’t want those kinds of presents.”

“Come here,” said Birn suddenly, and reluctantly she came round the table and stood before him.

===Chinese===

“那个南美人？”伯恩先生拉长了脸，“他可不太上算。我想你是知道的吧？我们折了大约四千英镑。”

“还得刨去那枚戒指。”姑娘说。

“他送你的那枚戒指？哼，那玩意儿值一百镑上下，我出手时能拿到六十镑就算走运。”伯恩先生耸耸肩说，“你若喜欢，那戒指只管留着。”

“不必了，谢谢您，伯恩先生，”姑娘安静地说，“这样的礼物我不要。”

“过来。”伯恩突然说。她不情愿地绕过桌子，立在他面前。

===Original===

He rose and took her hand.

“Elsie,” he said, “I’ve got very fond of you and I’ve been a good friend of yours, you know. If it hadn’t been for me what would have happened to your father? He’d have been hung! That would have been nice for you, wouldn’t it?”

She did not reply but gently disengaged her hand.

“You needn’t put away those jewels and fine clothes every night, if you’re sensible,” he went on, “and—”

“Happily I am sensible, if by sensible you mean sane,” said the girl, “and now I think I’ll go if you don’t mind, Mr. Birn. I’m rather tired.”

===Chinese===

他起身握住她的手。

“埃尔西，”他说，“我已经很喜欢你了，而我一向是你的好朋友，这你是知道的。若不是有我，你父亲会落得什么下场？早上绞架了！那样你才体面呢，是不是？”

她没有回答，只轻轻把手抽了回来。

“你若识相，本不必夜夜把那些珠宝和漂亮衣裳收起来，”他接着说，“而且——”

“幸而我向来识相——倘若您所谓识相，是指神志健全的话，”姑娘说，“现在我想我该告辞了，您不见怪吧，伯恩先生。我相当累了。”

===Original===

“Wait,” he said.

He walked to the safe, unlocked it again and took out an oblong parcel wrapped in brown paper, fastened with tapes and sealed.

“There’s a diamond necklace inside there,” he said. “It’s worth eight thousand pounds if it’s worth a penny. I’m going to put it in my strong box at the bank tomorrow, unless—”

“Unless—” repeated the girl steadily.

“Unless you want it,” said Mr. Birn. “I’m a fool with the ladies.”

===Chinese===

“且慢。”他说。

他走到保险柜前，重新开了锁，取出一个牛皮纸裹的长方形纸包，系着带子，封着封缄。

“那里面是一挂钻石项链，”他说，“少说也值八千英镑。明天我就要把它存进银行的保险箱去，除非——”

“除非——”姑娘不动声色地接口。

“除非你想要它，”伯恩先生说，“在女人跟前，我是个傻子。”

===Original===

She shook her head.

“Does it occur to you, Mr. Birn,” she said quietly, “that I could have had many necklaces if I wanted them? No, thank you. I am looking forward to the end of my servitude.”

“And suppose I don’t release you?” growled Mr. Birn as he put back the package in the safe and locked the door. “Suppose I want you for another three years? How about that? Your father’s still liable to arrest. No man can kill another, even if he’s only a croupier, without hanging for it.”

“I’ve paid for my father’s folly, over and over again,” said the girl in a low voice. “You don’t know how I hate this life, Mr. Birn. I feel worse than the worst woman in the world! I spend my life luring men to ruin—I wish to God I had never made the bargain. Sometimes I think I will tell my father just what I am paying for his safety, and let him decide whether my sacrifice is worth it!”

===Chinese===

她摇了摇头。

“您可曾想到，伯恩先生，”她平静地说，“这样的项链，我若想要，本可以有过许多条？不必了，谢谢。我一心盼着的，是这场苦役的尽头。”

“倘若我偏不放你呢？”伯恩先生把纸包放回保险柜、锁上柜门，咬牙道，“倘若我还要你再干三年呢？你说如何？你父亲仍旧随时可能被捕。人杀了人——哪怕他不过是个荷官——没有不偿命的。”

“父亲的荒唐，我已经一遍又一遍地偿付了，”姑娘低声说，“您不知道我多恨这种生活，伯恩先生。我觉得自己比世上最坏的女人还不如！我的生涯就是引人走向毁灭——但愿上帝，我从未做过那笔交易。有时我想，索性把我在为他的平安付出什么，原原本本告诉父亲，让他自己评判，我的牺牲值不值得！”

===Original===

A momentary look of alarm spread on the man’s face.

“You’ll do nothing of the kind,” he said sharply. “Just as you’ve got into our ways! I was only joking about asking you to stay on. Now, my dear,” he said with an air of banter, “you’d better go home and get your beauty sleep.”

He walked with her to the door, saw her down the steps and watched her disappear in the darkness of the street, then he came back to lock up for the night. He drank up the half glass of whisky he had left and made a wry face.

“That’s a queer taste,” he said, took two steps towards the passage and fell in a heap.

===Chinese===

那人脸上掠过一瞬的惊惶。

“这类事你想都别想，”他厉声道，“倒像你已把我们这一套过惯了似的！留你接着干的话，不过说笑罢了。好了，亲爱的，”他带着打趣的口气说，“你还是回家睡你的美容觉去吧。”

他送她到门口，看她下了台阶，望着她的身影隐入街上的黑暗，这才回来锁门就寝。他喝干剩下的半杯威士忌，皱起个苦脸。

“这味道怪得很。”他说着朝过道迈出两步，便扑地一团倒了下去。

===Original===

The man who had slipped into the room when he had escorted Elsie Chaucer to the door came from behind the curtain and stooping, loosened his collar. He stepped softly into the dimly lit passage and beckoned somebody, and Manfred came from the shadows, noiselessly, for he was wearing rubber over-boots.

Manfred glanced down at the unconscious man and then to the dregs in the whisky glass.

“Butyl chloride, I presume?”

“No more and no less,” said the practical Leon, “in fact the ‘knockout-drop’ which is so popular in criminal circles.”

He searched the man, took out his keys, opened the safe and removing the sealed packet, he carried it to the table. Then he looked thoughtfully at the prostrate man.

===Chinese===

他送埃尔西·乔叟出门时潜进屋来的那个人，从帷幔后面走出，俯身替他解开了衣领。他轻步走进昏暗的过道，向某人招了招手，曼弗雷德便从暗影里出来，悄没声息——因为他脚上穿着橡胶套靴。

曼弗雷德低头看了看那不省人事的人，又看了看威士忌杯底的残滓。

“我猜是氯丁烷？”

“不多也不少，”务实的莱昂说，“其实便是犯罪圈子里大为流行的‘蒙汗药’。”

他搜了那人的身，取出钥匙，打开保险柜，把封着的纸包拿出来，端到桌上。然后他若有所思地看着那伏在地上的人。

===Original===
