“He will only be completely under the ‘drop’ for five minutes, Manfred, but I think that will be enough.”

“Have you stopped to consider what will be the pathological results of twilight sleep on top of butyl?” asked Manfred. “I saw you blending the hyocine with the morphia before we left Jermyn Street and I suppose that is what you are using?”

“I did not look it up,” replied Gonsalez carelessly, “and if he dies, shall I weep? Give him another dose in half an hour, George. I will return by then.”

He took from his pocket a small black case, and opened it; the hypodermic syringe it contained was already charged, and rolling back the man’s sleeve, he inserted the needle and pressed home the piston.

===Chinese===

“这‘药’叫他全然昏迷，只有五分钟，曼弗雷德，不过我想那尽够了。”

“你可曾想过，在氯丁烷之上再叠加‘半眠麻醉’（twilight sleep），病理上会有什么后果？”曼弗雷德问，“离开杰明街之前，我看见你把莨菪碱同吗啡调配在一起，我猜你现在用的便是那个？”

“我没有查证过，”冈萨雷斯漫不经心地答道，“他若是死了，难道要我哭吗？过半小时再给他一剂，乔治。那时我就回来了。”

他从口袋里取出一只小黑匣，打开来；里头的皮下注射器已然装好了药液。他捋起那人的袖子，将针头刺入，把活塞推到了底。

---

===Original===

Mr. Birn woke the next morning with a throbbing headache.

He had no recollection of how he had got to bed, yet evidently he had undressed himself, for he was clad in his violet pyjamas. He rang the bell and got on to the floor, and though the room spun round him, he was able to hold himself erect.

The bell brought his housekeeper.

“What happened to me last night?” he asked, and she looked astounded.

“Nothing, sir. I left you in the library.”

“It is that beastly whisky,” grumbled Mr. Birn.

===Chinese===

第二天早晨伯恩先生醒来，头疼得突突乱跳。

他全然不记得自己是怎么上的床，可分明是他自己脱的衣裳，因为他身上穿着那套紫色睡衣。他按了铃，挣扎下地，屋子虽在他眼前打转，他总算把身子站直了。

铃声唤来了管家。

“我昨晚出了什么事？”他问。她一脸愕然。

“没什么事呀，先生。我离开时您还在书房里。”

“准是那威士忌作怪。”伯恩先生抱怨道。

===Original===

A cold bath and a cup of tea helped to dissipate the headache, but he was still shaky when he went into the room in which he had been sitting the night before.

A thought had occurred to him. A terrifying thought. Suppose the whisky had been drugged (though what opportunity there had been for drugging his drink he could not imagine) and somebody had broken in …!

He opened the safe and breathed a sigh of relief. The package was still there. It must have been the whisky, he grumbled, and declining breakfast, he ordered his car and was driven straight to the bank.

When he reached his office, he found the hatchet-faced young man in a state of agitation.

“I think we must have had burglars here last night, Mr. Birn.”

“Burglars?” said Mr. Birn alarmed. And then with a laugh, “well, they wouldn’t get much here. But what makes you think they have been?”

===Chinese===

一场冷水浴、一杯茶，帮着把头疼散去了些，可当他走进前一夜坐过的那间屋子时，身子还在发虚。

他忽然想到一件事。一件骇人的事。倘若那威士忌叫人下了药（尽管人家能有什么机会往他的酒里下药，他实在想不出），又有谁闯了进来……！

他打开保险柜，长出了一口气。纸包还在。想必是威士忌的缘故，他咕哝着，早饭也不用，吩咐备车，径直驶去了银行。

到了办公室，他发觉那个刀削脸的年轻人正焦躁不安。

“我看昨夜咱们这里进了贼，伯恩先生。”

“进贼？”伯恩先生一惊，随即又笑道，“嗐，他们在这儿也捞不着什么。可你凭什么认定有人来过？”

===Original===

“Somebody has been in the room, that I’ll swear,” said the young man. “The safe was open when I came and one of the books had been taken out and left on your table.”

A slow smile dawned on Mr. Birn’s face.

“I wish them luck,” he said.

Nevertheless he was perturbed, and made a careful search of all his papers to see if any important documents had been abstracted. His promissory notes were at the bank, in that same large box wherein was deposited the necklace which had come to him for the settlement of a debt.

Just before noon his clerk came in quickly.

“That fellow is here,” he whispered.

===Chinese===

“有人进过这间屋，这个我敢起誓，”年轻人说，“我来的时候保险柜开着，有一本账簿被抽出来，撂在您桌上了。”

伯恩先生脸上慢慢漾开一丝笑。

“祝他们好运。”他说。

话虽如此，他心里到底忐忑，把所有文书细细搜检了一遍，看有无重要单据被人抽走。他的本票存在银行里，就在那只大箱子里，同那挂抵账而来、存进去的项链放在一起。

快到正午，他的文书急匆匆进来。

“那家伙来了。”他低声说。

===Original===

“Which fellow?” growled Mr. Birn.

“The man from Jermyn Street who stopped the payment of Eden’s cheques.”

“Ask him in,” said Mr. Birn. “Well, sir,” he said jovially, “have you thought better about settling those debts?”

“Better and better,” said Gonsalez. “I can speak to you alone, I suppose?”

Birn signalled his assistant to leave them.

“I’ve come to settle all sorts of debts. For example, I’ve come to settle the debt of a gentleman named Chaucer.”

The gambling-house keeper started.

===Chinese===

“哪个家伙？”伯恩先生沉声问。

“杰明街来的那个，止付伊登支票的人。”

“请他进来。”伯恩先生说。“好啊，先生，”他乐呵呵地说，“那几笔债，您回心转意打算清偿了？”

“转得一日好过一日。”冈萨雷斯说，“我想，可以同您单独谈谈吧？”

伯恩示意助手退下。

“我是来清偿各式各样的债的。比如说，我来清偿一位姓乔叟（Chaucer）的先生的债。”

赌窟老板猛然一怔。

===Original===

“A very charming fellow, Chaucer. I’ve been interviewing him this morning. Some time ago he had a shock which brought on a stroke of paralysis. He’s not been able to leave his room in consequence for some time.”

“You’re telling me a lot I don’t want to hear about,” said Mr. Birn briskly.

“The poor fellow is under the impression that he killed a red-haired croupier of yours. Apparently he was gambling and lost his head, when he saw your croupier taking a bill.”

“My croupier,” said the other with virtuous indignation. “What do you mean? I don’t know what a croupier is.”

“He hit him over the head with a money-rake. You came to Chaucer the next day and told him your croupier was dead, seeking to extract money from him. You soon found he was ruined. You found also he had a very beautiful daughter, and it occurred to you that she might be of use to you in your nefarious schemes, so you had a little talk with her and she agreed to enter your service in order to save her father from ruin and possibly imprisonment.”

===Chinese===

“乔叟是位非常可亲的先生。今天上午我刚会过他。前些时他受了一场惊吓，落下瘫痪发作，因此有一阵子出不得房门。”

“你净讲些我不爱听的。”伯恩先生干脆地说。

“那位可怜的先生一心以为，他打死了你的一个红头发荷官。事情明摆着：他当时在赌，输昏了头，撞见你的荷官私吞钞票。”

“我的荷官，”对方一脸正气的愤慨，“你这是什么话？我都不知道荷官是什么东西。”

“他用收钱的耙子击了他的头。第二天你去找乔叟，告诉他你的荷官死了，想从他身上榨出钱来。你很快发现他已然破产。你又发现他有个非常美丽的女儿，转念想到她在你的邪恶勾当里或许有用，于是你同她小小谈了一席话，她为救父亲免于倾家荡产、或许还有牢狱之灾，便同意入你门下听用。”

===Original===

“This is a fairy story you’re telling me, is it?” said Birn, but his face had gone a pasty white and the hand that took the cigar from his lips trembled.

“To bolster up your scheme,” Gonsalez went on, “you inserted an advertisement in the death column of *The Times* and also you sent to the local newspaper a very flowery account of Mr. Jinkins’ funeral, which was also intended for Chaucer and his daughter.”

“It’s Greek to me,” murmured Mr. Birn with a pathetic attempt at a smile.

===Chinese===

“你这是给我讲童话，是不是？”伯恩说，可他的脸已是一片死白，从唇边取下雪茄的那只手直发抖。

“为把你的把戏撑圆，”冈萨雷斯接着说，“你在《泰晤士报》（*The Times*）的讣闻栏登了一则启事，还给本地报纸寄去一篇花团锦簇的报道，写金金斯先生（Mr. Jinkins）的葬礼——那也是做给乔叟父女看的。”

“你说的这些，对我如同天书。”伯恩先生喃喃道，可怜巴巴地想赔个笑。

===Original===

“I interviewed Mr. Chaucer this morning and was able to assure him that Jinkins is very much alive and is living at Brighton, and is running a little gambling-house—a branch of your many activities, and by the way, Mr. Birn, I don’t think you will see Elsie Chaucer again.”

Birn was breathing heavily.

“You know a hell of a lot,” he began, but something in Leon’s eyes stopped him.

“Birn,” said Gonsalez softly. “I am going to ruin you—to take away every penny of the money you have stolen from the foolish men who patronise your establishments.”

“Try it on,” said Birn shakily. “There’s a law in this country! Go and rob the bank, and you’d have little to rob,” he added with a grin. “There’s two hundred thousand pounds’ worth of securities in my bank—gilt-edged ones, Mr. Clever! Go and ask the bank manager to hand them over to you. They’re in Box 65,” he jeered. “That’s the only way you can ruin me, my son.”

===Chinese===

“今天上午我会见了乔叟先生，并且能向他担保，金金斯活得好好的，就住在布莱顿（Brighton），开着一家小赌窟——你那众多营生的一家分号。顺便说一句，伯恩先生，我看你再见不着埃尔西·乔叟了。”

伯恩呼吸粗重。

“你知道的可真他妈不少。”他开口道，可莱昂眼色里的什么把他止住了。

“伯恩，”冈萨雷斯轻声说，“我要毁了你——把你从光顾你那些场子的蠢人身上诈来的钱，一个便士一个便士都拿走。”

“有种就试试。”伯恩发着抖说，“这个国家是有王法的！去抢银行呀——抢也抢不着几个钱，”他咧嘴一笑，又补了一句，“我存在银行里的证券值二十万英镑——金边的，聪明先生！去叫银行经理把它们交给你呀。都在 65 号箱里，”他讥笑道，“你能毁掉我的，就这一条路了，我的小老弟。”

===Original===

Leon rose with a shrug.

“Perhaps I’m wrong,” he said. “Perhaps after all you will enjoy your ill-gotten gains.”

“You bet your life I will.” Mr. Birn relit his cigar.

He remembered the conversation that afternoon when he received an urgent telephone message from the bank. What the manager said took him there as fast as a taxi could carry him.

“I don’t know what’s the matter with your strong box,” said the bank manager, “but one of my clerks who had to go into the vault said there was an extraordinary smell, and when we looked at the box, we found a stream of smoke coming through the keyhole.”

“Why didn’t you open it?” screamed Birn, fumbling for his key.

“Partly because I haven’t a key, Mr. Birn,” said the bank manager intelligently.

With shaking hands the financier inserted the key and threw back the lid. A dense cloud of acrid yellow smoke came up and nearly stifled him … all that remained of his perfectly good securities was a black, sticky mess; a glass bottle, a few dull gems and nothing …

===Chinese===

莱昂耸耸肩，起身。

“许是我错了，”他说，“说不定到头来，你那不义之财还享得成。”

“这个你拿命打赌好了。”伯恩先生重新点上雪茄。

那天下午的事他记得清楚：他接到银行一个紧急电话，经理的一番话，催得他恨不得出租车生出翅膀，立时赶了过去。

“我不知道您的保险箱出了什么毛病，”银行经理说，“我有位职员不得不进保险库去，说那里头有一股异样的气味。我们查看那只箱子时，只见一缕烟正从锁眼里往外冒。”

“你们怎么不打开它？”伯恩尖声叫道，手忙脚乱地摸钥匙。

“一来嘛，因为我没有钥匙呀，伯恩先生。”银行经理心照不宣地说。

这位金融家双手发抖，把钥匙插进去，掀开了箱盖。一股呛人的黄烟腾起，险些把他熏倒……他那批好端端的证券，只剩下黑黢黢黏糊糊的一摊；一只玻璃瓶，几颗黯淡的宝石，再没有别的……

---

===Original===

“It looks to me,” said the detective officer who investigated the circumstance, “as though you must have inadvertently put in a package containing a very strong acid. What the acid is our analysts are working on now. It must have either leaked out or burst.”

“The only package there,” wailed Mr. Birn, “was a package containing a diamond necklace.”

“The remnants of which are still there,” said the detective. “You are quite sure nobody could get at that package and substitute a destroying agent? It could easily be made. A bottle such as we found—a stopper made of some easily consumed material and there you are! Could anybody have opened the package and slipped the bottle inside?”

“Impossible, impossible,” moaned the financier.

He was sitting with his face in his hands, weeping for his lost affluence, for though a few of the contents of that box could be replaced, there were certain American bonds which had gone forever and promissory notes by the thousand which would never be signed again.

===Chinese===

“依我看，”查勘这桩事故的侦探警官说，“您准是无意之中放进去了一个装着强酸的包裹。那是什么酸，我们的化验师正在检验。它不是渗漏了出来，就是胀破了。”

“那里头唯一的一个包裹，”伯恩先生哀声道，“装的是一挂钻石项链啊。”

“它的残余这不还在里头么，”侦探说，“您能拿得准，没有人能碰到那个包裹、把它调换成毁坏剂么？那是很容易办到的。一只像我们找到的那样的瓶子——一只用易于烧掉的材料做的瓶塞——齐活！有没有人可能打开包裹，把瓶子塞进去呢？”

“不可能，不可能。”这位金融家呻吟道。

他双手掩面坐着，为失去的豪富哀哭，因为箱中之物虽有几样还补置得来，有些美国债券却是一去不返了，还有成千上万的本票，再不会有人签署了。

## The Man Who Would Not Speak / 不肯开口的人

===Original===

But for the fact that he was already the possessor of innumerable coats-of-arms, quarterings, family mottoes direct and affiliated, Leon Gonsalez might have taken for his chief motto the tag *homo sum, nihil humani a me alienum puto*. For there was no sphere of human activity which did not fascinate him. Wherever crowds gathered, wherever man in the aggregate was to be seen at his best or worst, there was Gonsalez to be found, oblivious to the attractions which had drawn the throng together, intensely absorbed in the individual members of the throng themselves.

Many years ago four young men, wealthy and intensely sincere, had come together with a common purpose inspired by one common ideal. There had been, and always will be, such combinations of enthusiasts. Great religious revivals, the creation of missions and movements of sociological reform, these and other developments have resulted from the joining together of fiery young zealots.

But the Four Just Men had as their objective the correction of the law’s inequalities. They sought and found the men whom the wide teeth of the legal rake had left behind, and they dealt out their justice with terrible swiftness.

None of the living three (for one had died at Bordeaux) had departed from their ideals, but it was Leon who retained the appearance of that youthful enthusiasm which had brought them together.

===Chinese===

若不是名下已经坐拥数不清的纹章、盾面分格、嫡系姻族的种种家训，莱昂·冈萨雷斯（Leon Gonsalez）本可以拿那句格言*homo sum, nihil humani a me alienum puto*（我是人，凡人之事无不与我相关）当作自己的头号座右铭。因为人类活动的任何领域，没有一处不叫他着迷。哪里有人群聚集，哪里能看到人类作为群体的至善或至恶，哪里便找得到冈萨雷斯——招揽众人聚拢的那些热闹，他浑不在意，一心沉潜于人群中一个一个的成员本身。

许多年前，四位家道殷实、心地至诚的年轻人，为同一个理想所点燃的共同目标聚到一处。这样的热心人组合，过去有过，将来也永远会有。宏大的宗教复兴、传教事业的开创、社会改良的种种运动——这些以及其他气象，都是炽热青年赤诚联手的结果。

但四义士（The Four Just Men）的目标，却是矫正法律的不公。他们寻访、查出那些从法律巨耙的宽齿间漏过的人，以可怖的迅捷向他们执行正义。

健在的三人（因为有一人已死在波尔多（Bordeaux））谁也不曾背离当年的理想，只是唯有莱昂，还留着那股把他们聚到一起的青春热忱的神采。

===Original===

He sought for interests in all manner of places, and it is at the back of the grandstand on Hurst Park racecourse that he first saw “Spaghetti” Jones. It is one of the clear laws of coincidence, that if, in reading a book, you come across a word which you have not seen before, and which necessitates a reference to the dictionary, that same word will occur within three days on some other printed page. This law of the Inexplicable Recurrences applies equally to people, and Leon, viewing the bulk of the big man, had a queer feeling that they were destined to meet again﻿—Leon’s instincts were seldom at fault.

Mr. Spaghetti Jones was a tall, strong and stoutly built man, heavy eyed and heavy jawed. He had a long dark moustache which curled at the ends, and he wore a green and white bow tie that the startling pink of his shirt might not be hidden from the world. There were diamond rings on his fat fingers, and a cable chain across his figured waistcoat. He was attired in a very bright blue suit, perfectly tailored, and violently yellow boots encased his feet, which were small for a man of his size. In fact, Mr. Spaghetti Jones was a model of what Mr. Spaghetti Jones thought a gentleman should be.

It was not his rich attire, nor his greatness of bulk, which ensured for him Leon’s fascinated interest. Gonsalez had strolled to the back of the stand whilst the race was in progress, and the paddock was empty. Empty save for Mr. Jones and two men, both smaller and both more poorly dressed than he.

Leon had taken a seat near the ring where the horses paraded, and it happened that the party strolled towards him. Spaghetti Jones made no attempt to lower his voice. It was rich and full of volume, and Leon heard every word. One of the men appeared to be quarrelling: the other, after a vain attempt to act as arbitrator, had subsided into silence.

===Chinese===

他到形形色色的场所寻访兴致，而正是在赫斯特公园（Hurst Park）赛马场大看台的背面，他头一回见到了“面条”琼斯（“Spaghetti” Jones）。巧合有一条明明白白的定律：读书时碰到一个从未见过的词，不得不去翻词典，那么不出三天，这个词必定又出现在另一张印刷的纸页上。这条“难解重现律”（the Inexplicable Recurrences）对人同样适用。莱昂打量着那人的庞大身量，隐隐觉得他们注定还要再打交道——莱昂的直觉很少出错。

“面条”琼斯先生生得高大、结实、粗壮，眼皮厚重，下颚厚重。他蓄着两撇末梢上卷的黑色长髭，系一条绿白相间的领结，好让他那件扎眼的粉红衬衫不至于被世人埋没。肥硕的手指上戴着钻戒，花纹马甲上横着一条粗实的表链。他穿一身鲜亮的蓝西装，裁剪无可挑剔，脚上那双黄得凶悍的皮靴裹着一双小脚——就他的块头而言，那双脚小得出奇。总而言之，“面条”琼斯先生正是“面条”琼斯先生心目中绅士典范的化身。

令莱昂生出这份着迷兴致的，既不是他华贵的衣装，也不是他的伟岸身量。比赛进行当中，冈萨雷斯信步走到看台背后，围场里空空如也——只余下琼斯先生和两个男人，那两位都比他矮小，衣着也比他寒酸。

莱昂在赛马列队的围栏近旁落了座，恰巧这一行人朝他这边踱来。“面条”琼斯没有半点压低嗓门的意思——那嗓音醇厚洪亮，每个字莱昂都听了个真切。其中一人像是在吵架；另一个当和事佬的尝试归于徒劳之后，已缄默不语。

===Original===

“I told you to be at Lingfield, and you weren’t there,” Mr. Jones was saying gently.

He was cleaning his nails with a small penknife Leon saw, and apparently his attention was concentrated on the work of beautification.

“I’m not going to Lingfield, or to anywhere else, for you, Jones,” said the man angrily.

He was a sharp, pale-faced man, and Leon knew from the note in his voice that he was frightened, and was employing this blustering manner to hide his fear.

“Oh, you’re not going to Lingfield or anywhere else, aren’t you?” repeated Spaghetti Jones.

He pushed his hat to the back of his head, and raised his eyes momentarily, and then resumed his manicuring.

===Chinese===

“我吩咐你到林菲尔德（Lingfield）去，你却没去。”琼斯先生语调温和地说。

莱昂瞧见，他正用一把小铅笔刀修指甲，心思显然全在那桩美化工程上。

“我不去林菲尔德，也不去任何别的地方，不为你琼斯卖命了。”那人怒冲冲地说。

他是个精瘦的、脸色苍白的男人，莱昂从他的话音里听出，他心里害怕，正拿这副咋咋呼呼的姿态遮掩恐惧。

“哦，你不去林菲尔德、也不去别处，是吧？”“面条”琼斯重复了一遍。

他把帽子推到后脑勺上，抬眼扫了一扫，随即接着修他的指甲。

===Original===

“I’ve had enough of you and your crowd,” the man went on. “We’re blooming slaves, that’s what we are! I can make more money running alone, now do you see?”

“I see,” said Jones. “But Tom, I want you to be at Sandown next Thursday. Meet me in the ring﻿—”

“I won’t, I won’t,” roared the other, red of face. “I’ve finished with you, and all your crowd!”

“You’re a naughty boy,” said Spaghetti Jones almost kindly.

===Chinese===

“你还有你那帮人，我都受够了，”那人接着说，“咱们全是十足的奴隶，就是这么回事！单干我能多挣钱，这下你明白了吧？”

“我明白，”琼斯说，“可是汤姆（Tom），我要你下星期四到桑当（Sandown）去。在围栏里同我碰头——”

“我不去，我不去！”对方涨红了脸吼道，“我跟你，还有你那一帮子，都一刀两断了！”

“你是个淘气的孩子。”“面条”琼斯几乎是和颜悦色地说。

===Original===

He slashed twice at the other’s face with his little penknife, and the man jumped back with a cry.

“You’re a naughty boy,” said Jones, returning to the contemplation of his nails, “and you’ll be at Sandown when I tell you.”

With that he turned and walked away.

The man called Tom pulled out a handkerchief and dabbed his bleeding face. There were two long shallow gashes﻿—Mr. Jones knew to an *n*th of an inch how deeply he could go in safety﻿—but they were ugly and painful.

The wounded man glared after the retreating figure, and showed his discoloured teeth in an ugly grin, but Leon knew that he would report for duty at Sandown as he was ordered.

===Chinese===

他挥起那把小铅笔刀，朝对方的脸上刷刷划了两下，那人喊叫着跳开了。

“你是个淘气的孩子，”琼斯说罢又回去端详他的指甲，“我让你几时到桑当，你就得几时到。”

说完，他转身扬长而去。

那个叫汤姆的掏出手帕，按住淌血的脸。脸上两道又长又浅的口子——多深之内下刀可以安然无事，琼斯先生掌握得精确到 n 分之一英寸——可是伤口难看，而且疼。

受伤的人瞪着那渐行渐远的背影，龇出一口污黄的牙，狞笑了一声；但莱昂知道，到了桑当，他还是会奉命前去当差。

===Original===

The sight was immensely interesting to Leon Gonsalez.

He came back to the flat in Jermyn Street full of it.

Manfred was out visiting his dentist, but the moment he came into the doorway Leon babbled forth his discovery.

“Absolutely the most amazing fellow I’ve seen in my life, George!” he cried enthusiastically. “A gorgeous atavism﻿—a survival of the age of cruelty such as one seldom meets. You remember that shepherd we found at Escorial? He was the nearest, I think. This man’s name is Spaghetti Jones,” he went on, “he is the leader of a racecourse gang which blackmails bookmakers. His nickname is derived from the fact that he has Italian blood and lives in the Italian quarter, and I should imagine from the general asymmetry of the face, and the fullness of his chin, that there is a history of insanity, and certainly epilepsy, on the maternal side of his family.”

Manfred did not ask how Leon had made these discoveries. Put Leon on the track of an interesting “subject” and he would never leave it until it was dissected fibre by fibre and laid bare for his examination.

===Chinese===

这幅景象，在莱昂·冈萨雷斯眼里有趣极了。

他满心装着这件事，回到杰明街（Jermyn Street）的公寓。

曼弗雷德出门看牙医去了，待他一跨进门道，莱昂便把自己的发现滔滔不绝倒了出来。

“绝对是我这辈子见过的最叫人惊叹的家伙，乔治！”他兴冲冲地喊道，“一个精彩的返祖现象——残忍时代的孑遗，轻易碰不上一遭。还记得咱们在埃斯科里亚尔（Escorial）遇见的那个牧羊人么？我看要数他最接近了。这人名叫‘面条’琼斯，”他接着说，“是赛马场上一帮勒索赌档的匪帮的头领。他这诨名，一半因为他有意大利血统，又住在意大利侨民区；再从他那脸盘总体的不对称、下颏的丰隆来看，我揣想他家母系一面有精神病史，而且十有八九有癫痫。”

曼弗雷德没有问这些发现是打哪儿来的。只要把莱昂放上一个有趣“对象”的踪迹，他就再不撒手，非把它一根纤维一根纤维解剖开来、袒露在眼前供他查验不可。

===Original===

“He has a criminal record﻿—I suppose?”

Gonsalez laughed, delighted.

“That is where you’re wrong, my dear Manfred. He has never been convicted, and probably never will be. I found a poor little bookmaker in the silver ring﻿—the silver ring is the enclosure where smaller bets are made than in Tattersall’s reservation﻿—who has been paying tribute to Caesar for years. He was a little doleful and maudlin, otherwise he would not have told me what he did. I drove him to a public-house in Cobham, far from the madding crowd, and he drank gin (which is the most wholesome drink obtainable in this country, if people only knew it) until he wept, and weeping unbuttoned his soul.”

Manfred smiled and rang the bell for dinner.

“The law will lay him low sooner or later: I have a great faith in English law,” he said. “It misses far fewer times than any other law that is administered in the world.”

“But will it?” said Leon doubtfully. “I’d like to talk with the courteous Mr. Fare about this gentleman.”

===Chinese===

“他是有前科的——我猜？”

冈萨雷斯开心地笑了。

“这你可就错了，我亲爱的曼弗雷德。他从没被定过罪，多半永远也不会。我在银圈围场里寻见一个可怜的小赌档——所谓银圈围场，就是比塔特索尔（Tattersall’s）专区赌注下得小的场子——此人给凯撒进贡已有多年。他有点儿愁苦、伤感易泣，不然也不会对我说出他说的那些话。我开车送他到科巴姆（Cobham）一家酒馆，远离尘嚣，他喝金酒（只要人们知道，金酒原是这个国家买得到的最养人的酒）喝到落泪，一哭，灵魂的纽扣便解开了。”

曼弗雷德微微一笑，摇铃吩咐开晚饭。

“法律早晚要放倒他：我对英国法律抱着莫大的信心，”他说，“比起世上任何别的在执行的法律来，它脱靶的次数少得多。”

“可它会吗？”莱昂将信将疑地说，“我倒想同那位彬彬有礼的费尔先生（Mr. Fare）谈谈这位君子。”

===Original===

“You’ll have an opportunity,” said Manfred, “for we are dining with him tomorrow night at the Metropolitan Restaurant.”

Their credentials as Spanish criminologists had served them well with Mr. Fare and they in turn had assisted him﻿—and Fare was thankful.

It was after the Sunday night dinner, when they were smoking their cigars, and most of the diners at the Metropolitan had strayed out into the dancing-room, that Leon told his experience.

Fare nodded.

“Oh yes. Spaghetti Jones is a hard case,” he said. “We have never been able to get him, although he has been associated with some pretty unpleasant crimes. The man is colossal. He is brilliantly clever, in spite of his vulgarity and lack of education: he is remorseless, and he rules his little kingdom with a rod of iron. We have never been able to get one man to turn informer against him, and certainly he has never yet been caught with the goods.”

===Chinese===

“你会等来机会的，”曼弗雷德说，“明晚我们就要同他一起在大都会餐厅（Metropolitan Restaurant）用饭。”

他们那套西班牙犯罪学家的证件，在费尔先生那里很是管用；他们反过来也帮过他的忙——费尔对此心怀感激。

到了星期日晚宴之后，两人正抽着雪茄，大都会餐厅的宾客大半已踱进舞厅，莱昂这才讲起他的见闻。

费尔点了点头。

“哦，是啊。‘面条’琼斯是块难啃的料，”他说，“我们从来没能逮住他，尽管他同几桩相当不像样的罪案有牵连。此人了不得。尽管粗俗、没受过教育，他聪明得耀眼：他毫无怜悯之心，用铁腕统治着他的小王国。我们从来没能让一个人出面告发他，而且可以肯定，他也从没有过人赃并获。”

===Original===

He flicked the ash of his cigar into his saucer, and looked a long time thoughtfully at the grey heap.

“In America the Italians have a Black Hand organisation. I suppose you know that? It is a system of blackmail, the operations of which, happily, we have not seen in this country. At least, we hadn’t seen it until quite recently. I have every reason to believe that Spaghetti Jones is the guiding spirit in the one authentic case which has been brought to our notice.”

“Here in London?” said Manfred in surprise. “I hadn’t the slightest idea they tried that sort of thing in England.”

The Commissioner nodded.

===Chinese===

他把雪茄烟灰弹进茶碟，对着那撮灰堆出神了好一阵。

“在美国，意大利人有个黑手党组织（the Black Hand），这个你们想必知道？那是一套敲诈勒索的体系，所幸它那一套行径我们在这个国家还没领教过——至少，直到最近还没领教过。我有一切理由相信，在递到我们面前的唯一一桩真案子里，运筹帷幄的正是‘面条’琼斯。”

“就在这儿，伦敦？”曼弗雷德讶然道，“我万万没想到，他们在英国也试这一手。”

总监点了点头。

===Original===

“It may, of course, be a fake, but I’ve had some of my best men on the track of the letter-writers for a month, without getting any nearer to them. I was only wondering this morning, as I was dressing, whether I could not interest you gentlemen in a case where I confess we are a little at sea. Do you know the Countess Vinci?”

To Leon’s surprise Manfred nodded.

“I met her in Rome, about three years ago,” he said. “She is the widow of Count Antonio Vinci, is she not?”

“She is a widow with a son aged nine,” said the Commissioner, “and she lives in Berkeley Square. A very wealthy lady and extremely charming. About two months ago she began to receive letters, which had no signature, but in its stead, a black cross. They were written in beautiful script writing, and that induced a suspicion of Spaghetti Jones who, in his youth, was a sign-writer.”

Leon nodded his head vigorously.

“Of course, it is impossible to identify that kind of writing,” he said admiringly. “By ‘script’ I suppose you mean writing which is actually printed? That is a new method, and a particularly ingenious one, but I interrupted you, sir. Did these letters ask for money?”

===Chinese===

“当然，也可能是假局。可我派了几员最得力的干将，追查那些写信人追了一个月，也没能挨近半分。就在今天早晨穿衣裳的时候，我还在盘算，能不能说动两位先生对一桩案子发生点兴趣——我承认，在这案子里我们有点儿茫无头绪。你们认识温奇伯爵夫人（the Countess Vinci）么？”

令莱昂意外的是，曼弗雷德竟点了头。

“大约三年前我在罗马（Rome）见过她，”他说，“她是安东尼奥·温奇伯爵（Count Antonio Vinci）的遗孀，对吧？”

“她带着一个九岁的儿子守寡，”总监说，“住在伯克利广场（Berkeley Square）。极有钱的夫人，人也极有风致。大约两个月前，她开始收到一些信，信上没有署名，代替署名的是一个黑十字。信是一手漂亮的印刷体，这让人疑到‘面条’琼斯头上——他年轻时是写招牌的。”

莱昂用力点头。

“当然，那种字迹没法鉴定归属，”他叹赏道，“您说的‘印刷体’，指的是写得跟排印出来一模一样的字吧？这是种新法子，而且格外巧妙。不过我打断了您，先生。那些信是要钱么？”

===Original===

“They asked for money and threatened the lady as to what would happen if she failed to send to an address which was given. And here the immense nerve of Jones and his complicity was shown. Ostensibly Jones carries on the business of a newsagent. He has a small shop in Notting Hill, where he sells the morning and evening papers, and is a sort of local agent for racing tipsters whose placards you sometimes see displayed outside newspaper shops. In addition, the shop is used as an accommodation address﻿—”

“Which means,” said Manfred, “that people who do not want their letters addressed to their houses can have them sent there?”

The Commissioner nodded.

“They charge twopence a letter. These accommodation addresses should, of course, be made illegal, because they open the way to all sorts of frauds. The cleverness of the move is apparent: Jones receives the letter, ostensibly on behalf of some client, the letter is in his hands, he can open it or leave it unopened so that if the police call﻿—as we did on one occasion﻿—there is the epistle intact! Unless we prevent it reaching his shop we are powerless to keep the letter under observation. As a matter of fact, the name of the man to whom the money was to be sent, according to the letter which the Countess received, was ‘H. Frascati, care of John Jones.’ Jones, of course, received the answer to the Countess’s letter, put the envelope with dozens of other letters which were waiting to be claimed, and when our man went in in the evening, after having kept observation of the shop all day, he was told that the letter had been called for, and as, obviously, he could not search everybody who went in and out of the shop in the course of the day, it was impossible to prove the man’s guilt.”

“A wonderful scheme!” said the admiring Gonsalez. “Did the Countess send money?”

“She sent £200 very foolishly,” said Fare with a shake of his head, “and then when the next demand came she informed the police. A trap letter was made up and sent to Jones’s address, with the result as I have told you. She received a further note, demanding immediate payment, and threatening her and her boy, and a further trap letter was sent; this was last Thursday: and from a house on the opposite side of the road two of our officers kept observation, using field-glasses, which gave them a view of the interior of the shop. No letter was handed over during the day by Jones, so in the evening we raided the premises, and there was that letter on the shelf with the others, unopened, and we looked extremely foolish,” said the Commissioner with a smile. He thought awhile. “Would you like to meet the Countess Vinci?” he asked.

===Chinese===

“信里要钱，还威胁夫人：若不把钱寄到给出的一个地址，会有什么后果。而琼斯的斗胆和他的同伙关系，正是在这儿显露出来的。明面上，琼斯开着报贩的营生，在诺丁山（Notting Hill）有家小店，卖晨报晚报，还替赛马贴士行当充当本地代理——他们张挂在报店外头的那些告示，你们有时会见着。除此之外，这家店还被用作代收信件的‘通信地址’——”

“这就是说，”曼弗雷德道，“不愿意把信寄到家里的人，可以把信寄到那边去？”

总监点了点头。

“每封信收两便士。这种代收地址，按理早该定为非法，因为它给五花八门的诈骗开了方便之门。这一手的巧妙一望可知：琼斯名义上代某位‘客户’收信，信到了他手里，他可以拆，也可以不拆——这样警察一旦登门——我们就登过一回——那封信原封未动！除非拦住信件不让它到他店里，否则我们根本无力对它保持监控。事实上，照伯爵夫人收到的那封信的说法，收款人的姓名是‘H. 弗拉斯卡蒂（H. Frascati），转交约翰·琼斯’。琼斯自然收到了伯爵夫人回信的那只信封，把它同几十封待人认领的信放在一处；我们的人监视那家店一整天，傍晚走进去，却被告知信已经有人来取。显然，他不可能搜遍一天之内进进出出那家店的每一个人，于是便无从证明此人的罪。”

“妙极了的方案！”叹赏不已的冈萨雷斯说，“伯爵夫人寄钱了么？”

“她糊里糊涂寄出了 200 英镑，”费尔摇着头说，“待下一封索款信到了，她才报了警。我们做了一封诱饵信，寄到琼斯的地址，结果就是我方才说的那样。她又收到一张便条，勒令立刻付钱，还威胁她和她的孩子；于是又寄出一封诱饵信——那是上星期四：我们两名警官从马路对面的房子里实施监视，用双筒望远镜正望得见店堂内部。一整天琼斯没有交出任何信，于是傍晚我们查抄了那家店：那封信好端端同别的信一起摆在架上，未拆。我们显得蠢极了。”总监微笑着说。他想了一会儿。“你们愿不愿见一见温奇伯爵夫人？”他问。
===Original===

“Very much indeed,” said Gonsalez quickly, and looked at his watch.

“Not tonight,” smiled the Commissioner. “I will fix an interview for you tomorrow afternoon. Possibly you two ingenious gentlemen may think of something which has escaped our dull British wits.”

On their way back to Jermyn Street that night, Leon Gonsalez broke the silence with a startling question.

“I wonder where one could get an empty house with a large bathroom and a very large bath?” he asked thoughtfully.

“Why ever﻿—?” began Manfred, and then laughed. “I’m getting old, I think, Leon,” he said as they turned into the flat. “There was a time when the amazing workings of your mind did not in any way surprise me. What other characteristics must this ideal home of yours possess?”

===Chinese===

“非常愿意。”冈萨雷斯应声便答，看了看表。

“今晚不成，”总监含笑道，“明天下午我替你们安排一次会面。说不定你们两位足智多谋的先生，能想出什么逃过我们英国人迟钝脑筋的法子。”

当夜回杰明街的路上，莱昂·冈萨雷斯用一个惊人的问题打破了沉默。

“不知上哪儿能寻着一幢空屋，要有一间大浴室、一只很大的浴缸？”他若有所思地问。

“这又是为哪般——？”曼弗雷德刚起了个头，随即笑了。“我想我是老了，莱昂，”他们拐进公寓时他说，“从前有那么一阵子，你脑子里那些惊人的运作一点儿也不叫我惊奇。你这座理想宅邸，还须具备哪些特质？”

===Original===

Leon scientifically twirled his hat across the room that it fell neatly upon a peg of the hat-rack.

“How is that for dexterity, George?” he asked in self-admiration. “The house﻿—oh well, it ought to be a little isolated, standing by itself in its own grounds, if possible. Well away from the road, and the road not often frequented. I should prefer that it was concealed from observation by bushes or trees.”

“It sounds as if you’re contemplating a hideous crime,” said Manfred good-humouredly.

“Not I,” corrected Leon quickly, “but I think our friend Jones is a real nasty fellow.” He heaved a big sigh. “I’d give anything for his head measurements,” he said inconsequently.

Their interview with the Countess Vinci was a pleasant one. She was a tall, pretty woman, of thirty-four, the “grande dame” to her fingertips.

===Chinese===

莱昂用科学的手法把帽子旋过房间，端端正正落在帽架的一枚挂钩上。

“这一手灵敏劲儿如何，乔治？”他自我欣赏地问，“那房子嘛——哦，总该稍许偏僻些，可能的话，独门独院，自成格局。离大路要远，那条路还得少有人走。最好再有灌木或树木遮蔽，叫人望不见它。”

“听起来你像是在盘算一桩骇人的罪行，”曼弗雷德好脾气地说。

“不是我，”莱昂连忙更正，“不过我想，咱们这位朋友琼斯是个真正歹毒的家伙。”他长叹一声。“拿什么都换他的头骨测量值，我都肯。”他没头没尾地补了一句。

他们与温奇伯爵夫人的会面甚是愉快。她三十四岁，高挑貌美，从头到脚是一位十足的“贵妇人”（grande dame）。

===Original===

Manfred, who was human, was charmed by her, for Leon Gonsalez she was too normal to be really interesting.

“Naturally I’m rather worried,” she said. “Philip is not very strong, though he is not delicate.”

Later the boy came in, a straight, little fellow with an olive skin and brown eyes, self-possessed and more intelligent than Manfred had expected from his years. With him was his governess, a pretty Italian girl.

“I trust Beatrice more than I trust your police,” said the Countess when the girl had taken her charge back to his lessons. “Her father is an officer in the Sicilian police, and she has lived practically all her life under threat of assassination.”

“Does the boy go out?” asked Manfred.

“Once a day, in the car,” said the Countess. “Either I take him or Beatrice and I, or Beatrice alone.”

===Chinese===

曼弗雷德到底是个凡人，被她迷住了；至于莱昂·冈萨雷斯——她未免太正常，正常得难以真正有趣。

“我自然相当忧心，”她说，“菲利普（Philip）不算很强壮，虽说也说不上孱弱。”

过了一会儿孩子进来了，一个小巧挺拔的男孩，橄榄色皮肤，棕色眼睛，沉静自持，聪明程度超出曼弗雷德对他这个年纪的预期。跟着他的是他的家庭女教师，一个漂亮的意大利姑娘。

“我信得过贝阿特丽丝（Beatrice），胜过信得过你们的警察，”姑娘带孩子回去上课后，伯爵夫人说，“她父亲是西西里警察里的一名警官，她自己几乎一辈子都活在暗杀的威胁之下。”

“孩子出门么？”曼弗雷德问。

“一天一次，坐汽车出去，”伯爵夫人说，“有时我带他，有时我和贝阿特丽丝一起，有时贝阿特丽丝独自带。”

===Original===

“Exactly what do they threaten?” asked Gonsalez.

“I will show you one of their letters,” said the Countess.

She went to a bureau, unlocked it, and came back with a stout sheet of paper. It was of excellent quality and the writing was in copperplate characters:

> “You will send us a thousand pounds on the first of March, June, September and December. The money should be in banknotes and should be sent to H. Frascati, care of J. Jones, 194 Notting Hill Crescent. It will cost you more to get your boy back than it will cost you to keep him with you.”

===Chinese===

“他们究竟威胁些什么？”冈萨雷斯问。

“我给你们看一封他们的信，”伯爵夫人说。

她走到写字台前开了锁，取回一张厚实的信纸。纸质极佳，字迹是一笔铜版花体：

> “你须于三月一日、六月一日、九月一日、十二月一日，各寄给我们一千英镑。钱须用钞票，寄交H. 弗拉斯卡蒂，由J. 琼斯代收，诺丁山新月街 194 号（194 Notting Hill Crescent）。要想赎回你的男孩，代价要比把他留在身边大得多。”

===Original===

Gonsalez held the paper to the light, then carried it to the window for a better examination.

“Yes,” he said as he handed it back. “It would be difficult to trace the writer of that. The best expert in the world would fail.”

“I suppose you can suggest nothing,” said the Countess, shaking her head in anticipation, as they rose to go.

She spoke to Manfred, but it was Gonsalez who answered.

“I can only suggest, madame,” he said, “that if your little boy does disappear you communicate with us immediately.”

“And my dear Manfred,” he said when they were in the street, “that Master Philip *will* disappear is absolutely certain. I’m going to take a cab and drive round London looking for that house of mine.”

===Chinese===

冈萨雷斯把信纸举到光前，又拿到窗前细验。

“是啊，”他把纸递还时说，“要追查出这信的执笔人很难。世上最好的笔迹专家也要徒劳。”

“想必你们提不出什么高见，”两人起身告辞时，伯爵夫人预先摇着头说。

她这话是对曼弗雷德说的，答话的却是冈萨雷斯。

“我只能建议一件事，夫人，”他说，“倘若您的小儿子当真失踪了，请立刻与我们联络。”

“还有，我亲爱的曼弗雷德，”到了街上他说，“菲利普少爷*必定*会失踪，这一点板上钉钉。我要去叫一辆车，绕着伦敦转，找我那座房子。”

===Original===

“Are you serious, Leon?” asked Manfred, and the other nodded.

“Never more serious in my life,” he said soberly. “I will be at the flat in time for dinner.”

It was nearly eight o’clock, an hour after dinnertime, when he came running up the stairs of the Jermyn Street establishment, and burst into the room.

“I have got﻿—” he began, and then saw Manfred’s face. “Have they taken him?”

Manfred nodded.

“I had a telephone message an hour ago,” he said.

===Chinese===

“你是当真的，莱昂？”曼弗雷德问。对方点了点头。

“这辈子没有比这更当真的了，”他正色道，“我会赶回公寓吃晚饭的。”

将近八点钟，晚饭时间已过了一个钟头，他才跑上杰明街这处公寓的楼梯，一头冲进房间。

“我找到——”他刚开口，一眼看见曼弗雷德的脸色。“他们把那孩子掳走了？”

曼弗雷德点了点头。

“一小时前我接到一个电话。”他说。

===Original===

Leon whistled.

“So soon,” he was speaking to himself. And then: “How did it happen?”

“Fare has been here. He left just before you came,” said Manfred. “The abduction was carried out with ridiculous ease. Soon after we left, the governess took the boy out in the car, and they followed their usual route, which is across Hampstead Heath to the country beyond. It is their practice to go a few miles beyond the Heath in the direction of Beacon’s Hill and then to turn back.”

“Following the same route every day was, of course, sheer lunacy,” said Leon. “Pardon me.”

“The car always turns at the same point,” said Manfred, “and that is the fact which the abductors had learnt. The road is not especially wide, and to turn the big Rolls requires a little manoeuvring. The chauffeur was engaged in bringing the car round, when a man rode up on a bicycle, a pistol was put under the chauffeur’s nose, and at the same time two men, appearing from nowhere, pulled open the door of the car, snatched away the revolver which the governess carried, and carried the screaming boy down the road to another car, which the driver of the Vinci car had seen standing by the side of the road, but which apparently had not aroused his suspicion.”

===Chinese===

莱昂吹了声口哨。

“这么快。”他是自言自语。随即又问：“怎么发生的？”

“费尔来过，你回来前一刻刚走，”曼弗雷德说，“这场绑架干得容易得近乎荒唐。我们离开后不久，家庭教师便带孩子坐车出门，走的还是老路线：穿过汉普斯特德荒原（Hampstead Heath），到那头的乡间去。他们的惯例，是出了荒原再朝比肯山（Beacon’s Hill）的方向走上几英里，然后折返。”

“天天走同一条路线，自然是彻头彻尾的失心疯，”莱昂说，“恕我直言。”

“汽车总在同一处调头，”曼弗雷德说，“绑匪摸清的正是这一条。那条路不算宽，大劳斯莱斯（Rolls）调个头总要稍费些周折。司机正忙着把车倒转过来，一个人骑自行车赶到，一支手枪抵到了司机的鼻子底下；与此同时，两个不知从哪儿冒出来的人拉开车门，夺走家庭教师带着的左轮枪，把尖声哭喊的孩子抱下车，顺着路带到另一辆汽车上——温奇家的司机先前见过那辆车停在路旁，只是显然没起疑心。”

===Original===

“The men’s faces, were they seen?”

Manfred shook his head.

“The gentleman who held up the chauffeur wore one of those cheap theatrical beards which you can buy for a shilling at any toyshop, and in addition a pair of motor goggles. Both the other men seemed to be similarly disguised. I was just going to the Countess when you came. If you’ll have your dinner, Leon﻿—”

“I want no dinner,” said Leon promptly.

Commissioner Fare was at the house in Berkeley Square when they called, and he was endeavouring vainly to calm the distracted mother.

He hailed the arrival of the two men with relief.

===Chinese===

“那些人的脸，看清了么？”

曼弗雷德摇了摇头。

“拦住司机的那位先生，戴着一副一先令就能在任何玩具店买到的廉价舞台假胡子，外加一副汽车风镜。另外两个人似乎也是同样的装束。你回来的时候，我正要去伯爵夫人那儿。你要是用晚饭的话，莱昂——”

“我不用晚饭。”莱昂当即道。

他们到时，费尔总监正在伯克利广场的宅子里，徒劳地劝慰那位心乱如焚的母亲。

见这两位到来，他如释重负。

===Original===

“Where is the letter?” said Leon immediately he entered the room.

“What letter?”

“The letter they have sent stating their terms.”

“It hasn’t arrived yet,” said the other in a low voice. “Do you think that you can calm the Countess? She is on the verge of hysteria.”

She was lying on a sofa deathly white, her eyes closed, and two maidservants were endeavouring to rouse her. She opened her eyes at Manfred’s voice, and looked up.

“Oh my boy, my boy?” she sobbed, and clasped his hands in both of hers. “You will get him back, please. I will give anything, anything. You cannot name a sum that I will not pay!”

===Chinese===

“信在哪儿？”莱昂一进屋就问。

“什么信？”

“他们开条件的那封信。”

“还没到，”对方低声说，“你看你能不能让伯爵夫人镇静下来？她眼看要犯歇斯底里了。”

她直挺挺躺在沙发上，脸白得像死了一样，双目紧闭，两个女仆正设法唤醒她。听见曼弗雷德的声音，她睁开眼睛，抬眼望去。

“哦，我的孩子，我的孩子？”她抽噎着，双手把他的手紧紧攥住。“求求你们，一定要把他找回来。我什么都肯给，什么都肯。你们只管开个数，没有我付不出的！”

===Original===

It was then that the butler came into the room bearing a letter on a salver.

She sprang up, but would have fallen had not Manfred’s arm steadied her.

“It is from﻿—them,” she cried wildly and tore open the envelope with trembling fingers.

The message was a longer one:

> “Your son is in a place which is known only to the writer. The room is barred and locked and contains food and water sufficient to last for four days. None but the writer knows where he is or can find him. For the sum of twenty-five thousand pounds his hiding place will be sent to the Countess, and if that sum is not forthcoming, he will be left to starve.”

===Chinese===

正当此时，管家托着一只银盘走进屋来，盘上端着一封信。

她霍地站起来，若不是曼弗雷德一把扶住，几乎栽倒。

“是他们——寄来的，”她狂乱地喊道，颤抖的手指撕开信封。

这一封信的要旨长些：

> “你儿子在一个只有写信人知晓的所在。房间装了栅栏、上了锁，备有够维持四天的食水。除写信人之外，无人知道他在何处，也无人找得到他。缴付两万五千英镑，藏匿地点便会奉告伯爵夫人；倘若这笔钱不到位，他就将被丢在那里饿死。”

===Original===

“I must send the money immediately,” cried the distraught lady. “Immediately! Do you understand? My boy﻿—my boy﻿ ﻿… !”

“Four days,” murmured Leon, and his eyes were bright. “Why it couldn’t be better!”

Only Manfred heard him.

“Madam,” said Mr. Fare gravely, “if you send twenty-five thousand pounds what assurance have you that the boy will be restored? You are a very rich woman. Is it not likely that this man, when he gets your money, will make a further demand upon you?”

“Besides which,” interrupted Leon, “it would be a waste of money. I will undertake to restore your boy in two days. Perhaps in one, it depends very much upon whether Spaghetti Jones sat up late last night.”

===Chinese===

“我必须立刻把钱寄去，”这位心神散乱的夫人大声道，“立刻！你们明不明白？我的孩子——我的孩子……！”

“四天，”莱昂喃喃道，双眼发亮，“嘿，这再好不过了！”

只有曼弗雷德听见了。

“夫人，”费尔先生正色道，“您寄出两万五千英镑，拿什么担保孩子会归还？您是极有钱的人。此人拿到您的钱之后，难道不会向您再索上一回？”

“何况，”莱昂插话道，“这钱纯属白花。我担保两天之内把孩子交还给您。兴许一天就成——这很大程度要看‘面条’琼斯昨儿夜里睡得晚不晚了。”
===Original===

---

Mr. Spaghetti Jones was nicknamed partly because of his association with the sons and daughters of Italy, and partly because, though a hearty feeder, he invariably finished his dinner, however many courses he might have consumed, with the Italian national dish.

He had dined well at his favourite restaurant in Soho, sitting aloof from the commonplace diners, and receiving the obsequious services of the restaurant proprietor with a complacency which suggested that it was no more than his right.

He employed a toothpick openly, and then paying his bill, he sauntered majestically forth and hailed a taxicab. He was on the point of entering it when two men closed in, one on each side of him.

===Chinese===

---

“面条”琼斯先生这诨名，一半得自他同意大利儿女们的过从，一半得自这一桩：他虽然饭量豪雄，可无论吃过多少道菜，最后总要拿意大利国菜来收尾。

他在索霍区（Soho）自己钟爱的那家餐馆里吃了一顿好饭，同庸碌的食客们隔席而坐，受着店主殷勤备至的伺候，那副自得的神气，仿佛这一切不过是他的分内应得。

他公然用起牙签，随后会了账，雍容威严地踱出店门，唤了一辆出租汽车。他正要跨进去，两个男人一左一右地围拢上来。

===Original===

“Jones,” said one sharply.

“That’s my name,” said Mr. Jones.

“I am Inspector Jetheroe from Scotland Yard, and I shall take you into custody on a charge of abducting Count Philip Vinci.”

Mr. Jones stared at him.

Many attempts had been made to bring him to the inhospitable shelter which His Majesty’s Prisons afford, and they had all failed.

===Chinese===

“琼斯。”其中一个厉声道。

“那是我的名字。”琼斯先生说。

“我是苏格兰场（Scotland Yard）的杰瑟罗探长（Inspector Jetheroe）。你因涉嫌绑架菲利普·温奇伯爵，现在由我拘捕你。”

琼斯先生瞪着他。

把他送进国王陛下监狱那不甚好客的屋檐底下的尝试，已经有过许多回，全都失败了。

===Original===

“You have made a bloomer, haven’t you?” he chuckled, confident in the efficiency of his plans.

“Get into that cab,” said the man shortly, and Mr. Jones was too clever and experienced a juggler with the law to offer any resistance.

Nobody would betray him﻿—nobody could discover the boy, he had not exaggerated in that respect. The arrest meant no more than a visit to the station, a few words with the inspector and at the worst a night’s detention.

One of his captors had not entered the cab until he had a long colloquy with the driver, and Mr. Jones, seeing through the window the passing of a five-pound note, wondered what mad fit of generosity had overtaken the police force.

They drove rapidly through the West End, down Whitehall, and to Mr. Jones’s surprise, did not turn into Scotland Yard, but continued over Westminster Bridge.

===Chinese===

“你们这回栽了吧，嗯？”他咯咯笑道，对自己的计划何等周全信心十足。

“上车。”那人简短地说。琼斯先生是个同法律耍把戏的太聪明、太老到的艺人，不会作任何抗拒。

没有人会出卖他——也没有人找得到那孩子，这一层他没有夸口。这场拘捕无非是到警局走一遭，同探长说几句话，至多拘留一夜。

押他的人里有一个，先同司机嘀咕了好一阵才上车。琼斯先生隔着车窗，眼见一张五镑钞票递了出去，心中纳闷警队是犯了什么疯，慷慨到这步田地。

车子风驰电掣穿过西区（West End），下了白厅（Whitehall）。令琼斯先生诧异的是，车没有拐进苏格兰场，而是径直驶过威斯敏斯特桥（Westminster Bridge）。

===Original===

“Where are you taking me?” he asked.

The man who sat opposite him, the smaller man who had spoken to the cabman, leant forward and pushed something into Mr. Jones’s ample waistcoat, and glancing down he saw the long black barrel of an automatic pistol, and he felt a momentary sickness.

“Don’t talk﻿—yet,” said the man.

Try as he did, Jones could not see the face of either detective. Passing, however, under the direct rays of an electric lamp, he had a shock. The face of the man opposite to him was covered by a thin white veil which revealed only the vaguest outlines of a face. And then he began to think rapidly. But the solutions to his difficulties came back to that black and shining pistol in the other’s hands.

On through New Cross, Lewisham, and at last the cab began the slow descent of Blackheath Hill. Mr. Jones recognised the locality as one in which he had operated from time to time with fair success.

===Chinese===

“你们要带我去哪儿？”他问。

坐在他对面的那个人——就是同司机说过话的矮个子——俯身向前，把一件东西顶进琼斯先生肥阔的马甲里。他低头一瞥，望见自动手枪那长长的黑色枪管，霎时一阵恶心。

“先别说话。”那人说。

琼斯想尽法子，也瞧不见两个侦探中任何一人的脸。然而车子驶过一盏电灯的正下方时，他吃了一惊：对面那人的脸上罩着一层薄薄的白纱，只显出一张脸最模糊的轮廓。于是他开始飞快地动起脑筋来。可他种种难题的解法，兜来转去，还是绕回对方手中那把乌黑锃亮的手枪上。

一路穿过新克罗斯（New Cross）、刘易舍姆（Lewisham），出租车终于开始缓缓下布莱克希思山（Blackheath Hill）的坡。琼斯先生认得这一带——他从前不时在这儿做活，成绩尚可。

===Original===

The cab reached the Heath Road, and the man who was sitting by his side opened the window and leant out, talking to the driver. Suddenly the car turned through the gateway of a garden and stopped before the uninviting door of a gaunt, deserted house.

“Before you get out,” said the man with the pistol, “I want you to understand that if you talk or shout or make any statement to the driver of this cab, I shall shoot you through the stomach. It will take you about three days to die, and you will suffer pains which I do not think your gross mind can imagine.”

Mr. Jones mounted the steps to the front door and passed meekly and in silence into the house. The night was chilly and he shivered as he entered the comfortless dwelling. One of the men switched on an electric lamp, by the light of which he locked the door. Then he put the light out, and they found their way up the dusty stairs with the assistance of a pocket lamp which Leon Gonsalez flashed before him.

“Here’s your little home,” said Leon pleasantly, and opening the door, turned a switch.

It was a big bathroom. Evidently Leon had found his ideal, thought Manfred, for the room was unusually large, so large that a bed could be placed in one corner, and had been so placed by Mr. Gonsalez. George Manfred saw that his friend had had a very busy day. The bed was a comfortable one, and with its white sheets and soft pillows looked particularly inviting.

===Chinese===

车到荒原路，坐在他身旁那人摇下车窗，探出身去同司机说话。冷不防，车子拐进一座园子的大门，停在一幢瘦骨嶙峋、无人居住的宅子那扇绝不诱人的大门前。

“下车之前，”持枪的人说，“我要你明白：你若是说话、喊叫，或对这司机吐露半个字，我就一枪打穿你的肚子。你大约要三天才死得成，那种种痛楚，我看你那粗鄙的脑子是想象不出来的。”

琼斯先生登上台阶走到正门前，乖乖地、一声不吭地进了屋。夜气寒凉，他跨进这栋无处可栖身的宅子时打了个寒噤。其中一人拧亮一盏电灯，就着灯光锁了门，随即又把灯熄了；三人借着莱昂·冈萨雷斯在身前晃亮的一支袖珍手电，摸上积尘的楼梯。

“这就是你的小家。”莱昂和颜悦色地说着，推开门，拧亮了开关。

这是间大浴室。曼弗雷德心想，莱昂显然找到了他的理想之宅：这间屋子大得出奇，大得可以在墙角安放一张床——冈萨雷斯先生也确实放了一张。乔治·曼弗雷德看得出，他的朋友这一天着实忙碌。那床很是舒适，白床单、软枕头，瞧着格外招人。

===Original===

In the bath, which was broad and deep, a heavy Windsor chair had been placed, and from one of the taps hung a length of rubber hosing.

These things Mr. Jones noticed, and also marked the fact that the window had been covered with blankets to exclude the light.

“Put out your hands,” said Leon sharply, and before Spaghetti Jones realised what was happening, a pair of handcuffs had been snapped on his wrists, a belt had been deftly buckled through the connecting links, and drawn between his legs.

“Sit down on that bed. I want you to see how comfortable it is,” said Leon humorously.

“I don’t know what you think you’re doing,” said Mr. Jones in a sudden outburst of rage, “but by God you’ll know all about it! Take that veil off your face and let me see you.”

===Chinese===

那只又宽又深的浴缸里，摆着一把厚重的温莎椅（Windsor chair），一只水龙头上垂下一截橡皮软管。

这些物件琼斯先生都注意到了，也留意到窗户已用毯子蒙严，不透光亮。

“伸出手来。”莱昂厉声说。“面条”琼斯还没弄清发生了什么，一副手铐已经咔哒扣上他的双腕，一条皮带灵巧地穿过链环系好，又从他两腿之间拉了过去。

“到那张床上坐坐。我要你瞧瞧它有多舒服。”莱昂打趣道。

“我不知道你们自以为在干什么，”琼斯先生勃然大怒，“可是上帝作证，有你们好看的！把你脸上那块纱摘了，让我瞧瞧你的脸！”

===Original===

“I’d rather you didn’t,” said Leon gently. “If you saw my face I should be obliged to kill you, and that I have no desire to do. Sit down.”

Mr. Jones obeyed wonderingly, and his wonder increased when Leon began to strip his patent shoes and silk socks, and to roll up the legs of his trousers.

“What is the game?” asked the man fearfully.

“Get on to that chair.” Gonsalez pointed to the chair in the bath. “It is an easy Windsor chair﻿—”

“Look here,” began Jones fearfully.

===Chinese===

“还是不摘的好。”莱昂温声道，“你若是看见了我的脸，我便不得不杀你，而我不愿意那么做。坐下。”

琼斯先生满腹狐疑地照办了；待到莱昂动手脱下他的漆皮鞋和真丝袜、又卷起他的裤腿时，他的狐疑更重了。

“这演的是哪一出？”这人怯生生地问。

“到那把椅子上去。”冈萨雷斯指着浴缸里的椅子，“那是把舒坦的温莎椅——”

“我说，”琼斯胆战心惊地开口。

===Original===

“Get in,” snapped Leon, and the big fellow obeyed.

“Are you comfortable?” asked Leon politely.

The man glowered at him.

“You’ll be uncomfortable before I’m through with you,” he said.

“How do you like the look of that bed?” asked Leon. “It looks rather cosy, eh?”

===Chinese===

“进去。”莱昂厉声道。大块头照办了。

“还舒服么？”莱昂客气地问。

那人恶狠狠地瞪着他。

“等我收拾完了你，有你的好受的。”他说。

“你看那张床如何？”莱昂问，“瞧着挺安逸的，是吧？”

===Original===

Spaghetti Jones did not answer, and Gonsalez tapped him lightly on the shoulder.

“Now, my gross friend, will you tell me where you have hidden Philip Vinci?”

“Oh, that is it, is it?” grinned Mr. Jones. “Well, you can go on asking!”

He glared down at his bare feet, and then from one to the other of the two men.

“I don’t know anything about Philip Vinci,” he said. “Who is he?”

===Chinese===

“面条”琼斯没有答话，冈萨雷斯在他肩上轻轻一拍。

“好了，我粗壮的朋友，肯不肯告诉我，你把菲利普·温奇藏在哪儿了？”

“哦，敢情是为这个啊？”琼斯先生咧嘴一笑，“那你就接着问吧！”

他低头瞪着自己光裸的双脚，又把两个男人挨个瞪了一遍。

“菲利普·温奇，我压根儿不知道有这么个人，”他说，“他是谁？”

===Original===

“Where have you hidden Philip Vinci?”

“You don’t suppose if I knew where he was I’d tell you, do you?” sneered Jones.

“If you know, you certainly will tell me,” answered Leon quietly, “but I fancy it is going to be a long job. Perhaps in thirty-six hours’ time? George, will you take the first watch? I’m going to sleep on that very comfortable bed, but first,” he groped at the back of the bath and found a strap and this he passed round the body of his prisoner, buckling it behind the chair, “to prevent you falling off,” he said pleasantly.

He lay down on the bed and in a few minutes was fast asleep. Leon had that gift of sleeping at will, which has ever been the property of great commanders.

Jones looked from the sleeper to the veiled man who lounged in an easy chair facing him. Two eyes were cut in the veil, and the watcher had a book on his knee and was reading.

===Chinese===

“你把菲利普·温奇藏在哪儿了？”

“你总不至于以为，我即便知道他在哪儿，也会告诉你吧？”琼斯冷笑道。

“你要是知道，就一定会告诉我。”莱昂不动声色地答道，“不过我瞧这是桩旷日持久的活计。也许要三十六个钟头？乔治，你替我守头一班岗？我要到那张舒服极了的床上去睡觉。不过先——”他往浴缸后壁摸索，寻出一条皮带，绕过囚徒的身子，在椅背后扣紧，“省得你摔下去。”他悦声说。

他往床上一躺，几分钟便睡熟了。莱昂有那种想睡就睡的本事——历来都是伟大统帅的专利。

琼斯的眼光从熟睡的人移到对面安乐椅里懒洋洋坐着的蒙面人身上。面纱上剪出两个眼洞，看守者膝上摊着一本书，正读着。

===Original===

“How long is this going on?” he demanded.

“For a day or two,” said Manfred calmly. “Are you very much bored? Would you like to read?”

Mr. Jones growled something unpleasant, and did not accept the offer. He could only think and speculate upon what their intentions were. He had expected violence, but apparently no violence was intended. They were merely keeping him prisoner till he spoke. But he would show them! He began to feel tired. Suddenly his head drooped forward, till his chin touched his breast.

“Wake up,” said Manfred shortly.

He awoke with a start.

===Chinese===

“这要搞到什么时候？”他质问。

“一两天吧。”曼弗雷德平静地说，“很无聊么？想不想读点什么？”

琼斯先生咕哝了句难听的话，没有领这份情。他只能思来想去，揣度他们的意图。他原以为免不了动粗，可看样子人家根本无意动粗。他们不过是囚着他，等他开口。可他自有颜色给他们瞧！他渐渐觉得困了。忽然，他的脑袋向前一垂，垂到下巴抵住前胸。

“醒醒。”曼弗雷德简短地说。

他猛地惊醒。

===Original===

“You’re not supposed to sleep,” explained Manfred.

“Ain’t I?” growled the prisoner. “Well, I’m going to sleep!” and he settled himself more easily in the chair.

He was beginning to doze when he experienced an acute discomfort and drew up his feet with a yell. The veiled man was directing a stream of ice-cold water upon his unprotected feet, and Mr. Jones was now thoroughly awake. An hour later he was nodding again, and again the tiny hosepipe was directed to his feet, and again Manfred produced a towel and dried them as carefully as though Mr. Jones were an invalid.

At six o’clock in the morning, red-eyed and glaring, he watched Manfred rouse the sleeping Leon and take his place on the bed.

Again and again nature drew the big man’s chin down to his chest, and again and again the icy stream played maddeningly upon him and he woke with a scream.

===Chinese===

“你可不许睡觉。”曼弗雷德解释道。

“不许么？”囚徒咕哝道，“哼，我偏要睡！”他在椅子里挪了挪，坐得更舒坦些。

他刚要打盹，便尝到一阵尖锐的苦楚，喊叫着把双脚缩了回去——蒙面人正把一股冰凉的水柱，对准他毫无遮护的双脚浇下去，琼斯先生这下彻底醒了。一个钟头之后，他又点头打起了瞌睡；那根细橡皮管又对准他的双脚，曼弗雷德又掏出毛巾，把那双脚仔细擦干，好像琼斯先生是一位抱恙的病人。

早晨六点钟，他红着眼睛、怒目而视，看着曼弗雷德唤醒熟睡的莱昂，自己上了床。

一次又一次，困倦把大块头的下巴拽到胸口；一次又一次，冰冷的水流疯狂地浇在他身上，他尖叫着醒来。

===Original===

“Let me sleep, let me sleep!” he cried in helpless rage, tugging at the strap. He was half-mad with weariness, his eyes were like lead.

“Where is Philip Vinci?” demanded the inexorable Leon.

“This is torture, damn you﻿—” screamed the man.

“No worse for you than for the boy locked up in a room with four days’ supply of food. No worse than slitting a man’s face with a penknife, my primitive friend. But perhaps you do not think it is a serious matter to terrify a little child.”

“I don’t know where he is, I tell you,” said Spaghetti hoarsely.

===Chinese===

“让我睡，让我睡！”他无可奈何地狂怒嘶喊，拽扯着身上的皮带。他困得半疯，双眼沉得像灌了铅。

“菲利普·温奇在哪儿？”毫不通融的莱昂追问。

“这是酷刑，该死的——”那人尖叫。

“比起那个锁在屋里、食水只够四天的孩子，这对你不算更狠。比起拿铅笔刀划开一个人的脸，也不算更狠，我这位原始人朋友。或许你不觉得，吓唬一个小孩子算什么了不得的正经事。”

“我告诉你们，我不知道他在哪儿。”“面条”嗓音嘶哑地说。

===Original===

“Then we shall have to keep you awake till you remember,” replied Leon, and lit a cigarette.

Soon after he went downstairs and returned with coffee and biscuits for the man, and found him sleeping soundly.

His dreams ended in a wail of agony.

“Let me sleep, please let me sleep,” he begged with tears in his eyes. “I’ll give you anything if you’ll let me sleep!”

“You can sleep on that bed﻿—and it’s a very comfortable bed too﻿—” said Leon, “but first we shall learn where Philip Vinci is.”

===Chinese===

“那我们只好让你醒着，直到你想起来。”莱昂答道，点起一支烟。

不多时他下楼去，端了咖啡和饼干回来给这人，却发现他已睡得酣沉。

他的梦，在一声痛楚的哀号里收了场。

“让我睡吧，求你们让我睡，”他眼里含着泪哀求，“只要让我睡，我什么都给！”

“你可以到那张床上去睡——那可真是张舒服的床——”莱昂说，“但先得让我们知道菲利普·温奇在哪儿。”

===Original===

“I’ll see you in hell before I’ll tell you,” screamed Spaghetti Jones.

“You will wear your eyes out looking for me,” answered Leon politely. “Wake up!”

At seven o’clock that evening a weeping, whimpering, broken man, moaned an address, and Manfred went off to verify this information.

“Now let me sleep!”

“You can keep awake till my friend comes back,” said Leon.

===Chinese===

“想叫我开口，除非你到地狱里来见我。”“面条”琼斯尖声喊道。

“那你满地狱找我，这双眼睛可要找坏了。”莱昂彬彬有礼地答道，“醒醒！”

晚上七点钟，一个哭哭啼啼、呜呜咽咽、彻底垮了的人，呻吟着吐出一个地址，曼弗雷德便去核实。

“现在总可以让我睡了吧！”

“等我朋友回来之前，你还醒着吧。”莱昂说。

===Original===

At nine o’clock George Manfred returned from Berkeley Square, having released a frightened little boy from a very unpleasant cellar in Notting Hill, and together they lifted the half-dead man from the bath and unlocked the handcuffs.

“Before you sleep, sit here,” said Manfred, “and sign this.”

“This” was a document which Mr. Jones could not have read even if he had been willing. He scrawled his signature, and crawling on to the bed was asleep before Manfred pulled the clothes over him. And he was still asleep when a man from Scotland Yard came into the room and shook him violently.

Spaghetti Jones knew nothing of what the detective said; had no recollection of being charged or of hearing his signed confession read over to him by the station sergeant. He remembered nothing until they woke him in his cell to appear before a magistrate for a preliminary hearing.

“It’s an extraordinary thing, sir,” said the gaoler to the divisional surgeon. “I can’t get this man to keep awake.”

“Perhaps he would like a cold bath,” said the surgeon helpfully.

===Chinese===

九点钟，乔治·曼弗雷德从伯克利广场回来了——他把一个吓坏了的小男孩，从诺丁山一个很不舒服的地窖里放了出来。两人合力把那半死不活的人从浴缸里抬出来，卸下手铐。

“睡觉之前，坐到这儿来，”曼弗雷德说，“把这个签了。”

“这个”，是一份琼斯先生即便愿意也读不成的文书。他信手涂下名字，爬上床去，曼弗雷德还没来得及替他掖好被子，人已睡着。苏格兰场来的人走进房间、使劲摇撼他的时候，他还在睡。

侦探说了些什么，“面条”琼斯一概不知；自己如何被指控、警局警长如何向他宣读那份他签了字的供词，他一概没有印象。直到他们在牢房里唤醒他、押他去见治安法官接受预审之前，他什么也不记得。

“真是桩咄咄怪事，长官，”狱卒对分区警医说，“我怎么也没法叫这人醒着。”

“没准儿他想要个冷水澡呢。”警医热心地献计道。

## The Man Who Was Acquitted / 被无罪开释的人

===Original===

“Have you ever noticed,” said Leon Gonsalez, looking up from his book and taking off the horn-rimmed glasses he wore when he was reading, “that poisoners and baby-farmers are invariably mystics?”

“I haven’t noticed many baby-farmers—or poisoners for the matter of that,” said Manfred with a little yawn. “Do you mean by ‘mystic’ an ecstatic individual who believes he can communicate directly with the Divine Power?”

Leon nodded.

“I’ve never quite understood the association of a superficial but vivid form of religion with crime,” said Leon, knitting his forehead. “Religion, of course, does not develop the dormant criminality in a man’s ethical system; but it is a fact that certain criminals develop a queer form of religious exaltation. Ferri, who questioned 200 Italian murderers, found that they were all devout: Naples, which is the most religious city in Europe, is also the most criminal. Ten percent of those inmates of British prisons who are tattooed are marked with religious symbols.”

“Which only means that when a man of low intelligence submits to the tattoo artist, he demands pictures of the things with which he is familiar,” said Manfred, and took up the paper he was reading. Suddenly he dropped the journal on his knees.

===Chinese===

“你可曾留意过，”莱昂·冈萨雷斯（Leon Gonsalez）说道，一面从书上抬起眼，摘下他读书时戴的那副角质框眼镜，“投毒犯和育婴农场主（baby-farmer）一概都是神秘主义者？”

“育婴农场主我可没见过几个——投毒犯也一样。”曼弗雷德（Manfred）轻轻打了个呵欠说，“你说的‘神秘主义者’，是指那种自以为能同神力直接沟通、如醉如痴的人物么？”

莱昂点了点头。

“我一直不甚明了，一种浮浅却狂热的宗教形式何以同犯罪搭上关系，”莱昂皱着眉头说，“宗教当然不会把一个人伦理体系中潜伏的恶性诱发出来；可事实是，某些罪犯会生出一种古怪的宗教狂热。费里（Ferri）讯问过 200 名意大利杀人犯，发现他们个个虔诚：那不勒斯（Naples）是欧洲最虔诚的城市，也是犯罪最盛的城市。英国监狱里纹身的犯人当中，一成身上刺的是宗教符号。”

“这只能说明，一个智力低下的人让刺青师傅下针时，点名要的图案必是他熟悉的东西。”曼弗雷德说着，重新拿起正读着的报纸。忽然，他把报纸往膝上一撂。

===Original===

“You’re thinking of Dr. Twenden,” he said and Leon inclined his head slowly.

“I was,” he confessed.

Manfred smiled.

“Twenden was acquitted with acclamation and cheered as he left the Exeter Assize Court,” he said, “and yet he was guilty!”

“As guilty as a man can be—I wondered if your mind was on the case, George. I haven’t discussed it with you.”

===Chinese===

“你在想特温登博士（Dr. Twenden）。”他说。莱昂缓缓地垂了垂头。

“正是。”他坦承道。

曼弗雷德莞尔一笑。

“特温登被判无罪时博得满堂喝彩，离开埃克塞特（Exeter）巡回法院时更受人欢呼，”他说，“然而他是有罪的！”

“罪大恶极，罪得无以复加——我还嘀咕你是不是也在琢磨这案子呢，乔治。我一直没同你谈过它。”

===Original===

“By the way, was he religious?” asked Manfred.

“I wouldn’t say that,” said the other, shaking his head. “I was thinking of the pious letter of thanks he wrote, and which was published in the Baxeter and Plymouth newspapers—it was rather like a sermon. What he is in private life I know no more than the account of the trial told me. You think he poisoned his wife?”

“I am sure,” said Manfred quietly. “I intended discussing the matter with you this evening.”

The trial of Dr. Twenden had provided the newspaper sensation of the week. The doctor was a man of thirty: his wife was seventeen years his senior and the suggestion was that he had married her for her money—she had a legacy of £2,000 a year which ceased at her death. Three months before this event she had inherited £63,000 from her brother, who had died in Johannesburg.

===Chinese===

“对了，他信教么？”曼弗雷德问。

“那倒说不上。”对方摇了摇头，“我想的是他写的那封虔诚谢函——登在巴克斯特和普利茅斯的报纸上——倒像一篇布道文。私下里他是个什么样的人，我知道的不比庭审报道告诉我的更多。你认为是他毒死了太太？”

“我是确信不疑。”曼弗雷德平静地说，“我原就打算今晚同你谈谈这事。”

特温登博士一案是本周报纸的轰动新闻。博士三十岁，太太比他大十七岁；风言风语都说他图钱财娶了她——她有一笔每年 2,000 镑的遗产进项，她一死便告终止。就在她死前三个月，她又从死在约翰内斯堡（Johannesburg）的兄弟手里继承了 63,000 镑。

===Original===

Twenden and his wife had not been on the best of terms, one of the subjects of disagreement being her unwillingness to continue paying his debts. After her inheritance had been transferred to her, she sent to Torquay, to her lawyer, the draft of a will in which she left the income from £12,000 to her husband, providing he did not marry again. The remainder of her fortune she proposed leaving to her nephew, one Jacley, a young civil engineer in the employ of the Plymouth Corporation.

The lawyer drafted her bequests and forwarded a rough copy for her approval before the will was engrossed. That draft arrived at Newton Abbot where the doctor and his wife were living (the doctor had a practice there) and was never seen again. A postman testified that it had been delivered at the 8 o’clock “round” on a Saturday. That day the doctor was called into consultation over a case of viper bite. He returned in the evening and dined with his wife. Nothing happened that was unusual. The doctor went to his laboratory to make an examination of the poison sac which had been extracted from the reptile.

In the morning Mrs. Twenden was very ill, showed symptoms which could be likened to blood-poisoning and died the same night.

===Chinese===

特温登同妻子素来不甚和睦，争执的内容之一，便是她不愿再替他还债。遗产过到她名下之后，她把一份遗嘱草稿寄给托基（Torquay）她的律师，稿中立言：把 12,000 镑的收益留给丈夫，条件是他不得再娶。其余的家财，她打算留给她的侄子贾克利（Jacley）——一个受雇于普利茅斯市政会的年轻土木工程师。

律师照她的遗愿拟好条文，在遗嘱正式誊清之前，先寄了一份草稿请她核准。草稿寄抵医生夫妇当时居住的牛顿阿伯特（医生就在那里开业行医），从此再无人见过。一名邮差作证说，那是星期六早上 8 点钟那一班投递的。当天，医生被请去会诊一例蝰蛇咬伤，傍晚才回来同妻子共进晚餐，并无异状。医生到实验室去，检验从蛇身上取出的毒囊。

次日清晨，特温登太太病势危重，症状近似血液中毒，当夜便殁了。

===Original===

It was found that in her arm was a small puncture such as might be made by a hypodermic needle, such a needle as, of course, the doctor possessed—he had in fact ten.

Suspicion fell upon him immediately. He had not summoned any further assistance besides that which he could give, until all hope of saving the unfortunate woman had gone. It was afterwards proved that the poison from which the woman had died was snake venom.

In his favour was the fact that no trace of the poison was found in either of the three syringes or the ten needles in his possession. It was his practice, and this the servants and another doctor who had ordered the treatment testified, to give his wife a subcutaneous injection of a new serum for her rheumatism.

This he performed twice a week and on the Saturday the treatment was due.

===Chinese===

人们发现她胳膊上有一个小小的针孔，像是皮下注射的针头扎出来的——这种针头，医生自然不缺，实际上他有十枚。

疑点顿时全落在他身上。直到救活这位不幸妇人的指望断绝已久，他才去另请援手。事后查明，夺去那妇人性命的毒物正是蛇毒。

对他有利的是：他手边三支注射管、十枚针头，都没有验出那毒物的丝毫痕迹。按惯常的做法——仆人们和另一位嘱行此疗法的医生都如此作证——他每周给妻子皮下注射一种治疗风湿症的新血清。

这治疗每周施行两次，而案发那个星期六，正该轮到注射。

===Original===

He was tried and acquitted. Between the hour of his arrest and his release he had acquired the popularity which accumulates about the personality of successful politicians and good-looking murderers, and he had been carried from the Sessions House shoulder high through a mob of cheering admirers, who had discovered nothing admirable in his character, and were not even aware of his existence, till the iron hand of the law closed upon his arm.

Possibly the enthusiasm of the crowd was fanned to its high temperature by the announcement the accused man had made in the dock—he had defended himself.

“Whether I am convicted or acquitted, not one penny of my dear wife’s money will I touch. I intend disposing of this accursed fortune to the poor of the country. I, for my part, shall leave this land for a distant shore and in a strange land, amidst strangers, I will cherish the memory of my dear wife, my partner and my friend.”

Here the doctor broke down.

===Chinese===

他受了审，被判无罪。从被捕到获释，他积攒起一种通常只聚拢在成功政客和漂亮杀人犯周身的民望；人们把他从刑事法庭大楼（Sessions House）里高抬过肩，穿过一群欢呼的仰慕者——这些人在他身上找不出半点可慕之处，直到法律铁手攥上他的胳膊之前，甚至连世上有他这号人都不知道。

人群的热情之所以烧到这般高温，或许是被被告在被告席上的那番告白扇起来的——他是自我辩护的。

“无论我被定罪还是开释，我亲爱的妻子的钱，我一个便士也不碰。我打算把这笔可诅咒的家财散给这个国家的穷人。至于我，将离开这片国土，远赴重洋；在陌生的土地上，在陌生人中间，我将珍藏对我亲爱的妻子——我的伴侣、我的朋友——的忆念。”

说到此处，博士泣不成声。

===Original===

“A distant shore,” said Manfred, recalling the prisoner’s passionate words. “You can do a lot with sixty-three thousand pounds on a distant shore.”

The eyes of Leon danced with suppressed merriment.

“It grieves me, George, to hear such cynicism. Have you forgotten that the poor of Devonshire are even now planning how the money will be spent?”

Manfred made a noise of contempt, and resumed his reading, but his companion had not finished with the subject.

===Chinese===

“远赴重洋，”曼弗雷德复述着囚犯那慷慨陈词，“63,000 镑拿到了重洋彼岸，能办的事可多着呢。”

莱昂眼里闪着强忍的笑意。

“听你说出这种愤世嫉俗的话来，真叫我痛心，乔治。你忘了不成？德文郡（Devonshire）的穷人们此刻正盘算着这笔钱该怎么花呢。”

曼弗雷德嗤了一声，重新读起报来；可他的同伴还意犹未尽。

===Original===

“I should like to meet Twenden,” he said reflectively. “Would you care to go to Newton Abbot, George? The town itself is not particularly beautiful, but we are within half an hour’s run of our old home at Babbacombe.”

This time George Manfred put away his paper definitely.

“It was a particularly wicked crime,” he said gravely. “I think I agree with you, Leon. I have been thinking of the matter all the morning, and it seems to call for some redress. But,” he hesitated, “it also calls for some proof. Unless we can secure evidence which did not come before the Court, we cannot act on suspicion.”

Leon nodded.

“But if we prove it,” he said softly, “I promise you, Manfred, a most wonderful scheme.”

===Chinese===

“我想会一会特温登。”他若有所思地说，“你愿意去一趟牛顿阿伯特么，乔治？那城本身算不得如何秀美，可我们离巴巴科姆（Babbacombe）的旧居不过半小时车程。”

这一回，乔治·曼弗雷德干脆把报纸搁下了。

“这桩罪行恶毒得很，”他正色道，“我想我是同意你的，莱昂。这事我想了一上午，看来非得讨个昭雪不可。不过，”他迟疑了一下，“也非得有些凭证不可。若拿不到未曾呈上法庭的证据，咱们便不能凭疑心行事。”

莱昂点了点头。

“可咱们一旦证实了它，”他柔声说，“我担保，曼弗雷德，有一场妙不可言的好戏。”

===Original===

That afternoon he called upon his friend, Mr. Fare of Scotland Yard, and when the Commissioner heard his request, he was less surprised than amused.

“I was wondering how long it would be before you wanted to see our prisons, Señor,” he said. “I can arrange that with the Commissioners. What prison would you like to see?”

“I wish to see a typical county prison,” said Leon. “What about Baxeter?”

“Baxeter,” said the other in surprise. “That’s rather a long way from London. It doesn’t differ very materially from Wandsworth, which is a few miles from this building, or Pentonville, which is our headquarters prison.”

“I prefer Baxeter,” said Leon. “The fact is I am going to the Devonshire coast, and I could fill in my time profitably with this inspection.”

===Chinese===

当天下午，他去拜访他在苏格兰场的朋友费尔先生（Mr. Fare）。这位助理警务总监听罢他的请求，与其说是惊讶，不如说是觉得有趣。

“我还正纳闷，您过多久才会提出要参观我们的监狱呢，塞尼奥尔（Señor）。”他说，“这事我可以同总监们打个招呼。您想看哪一座？”

“我想看一座典型的郡立监狱，”莱昂说，“巴克斯特如何？”

“巴克斯特？”对方诧异道，“那可离伦敦远得很。它同旺兹沃思（Wandsworth）监狱没有多大分别——那座离我们这里不过几英里；同彭顿维尔监狱也差不多，那是我们的总部监狱。”

“我偏要巴克斯特，”莱昂说，“实情是我要到德文郡海边去，顺道看看监狱，这点时间也不算白花。”

===Original===

The order was forthcoming on the next day. It was a printed note authorising the Governor of H.M. Prison, Baxeter, to allow the bearer to visit the prison between the hours of ten and twelve in the morning, and two and four in the afternoon.

They broke their journey at Baxeter, and Leon drove up to the prison, a prettier building than most of its kind. He was received by the Deputy Governor and a tall, good-looking chief warder, an ex-Guardsman, who showed him round the three wings, and through the restricted grounds of the gaol.

Leon rejoined his companion on the railway station just in time to catch the Plymouth express which would carry them to Newton Abbot.

===Chinese===

第二天批文便下来了，是一纸印就的凭单，授权巴克斯特皇家监狱（H.M. Prison）狱长准许持单人在上午 10 点至 12 点、下午 2 点至 4 点之间入监参观。

他们在巴克斯特中途下车，莱昂驱车到监狱去——那是一座比同类建筑秀气些的屋子。副狱长和一位高大英俊的主任看守接待了他；那看守是近卫团出身，领他逛了三座监区翼楼，又看了监狱有限的场地。

莱昂赶回火车站与同伴会合，正好搭上载他们去牛顿阿伯特的普利茅斯快车。

===Original===

“A thoroughly satisfactory visit,” said Leon. “In fact, it is the most amazingly convenient prison I have ever been in.”

“Convenient to get into or convenient to get away from?” asked Manfred.

“Both,” said Leon.

They had not engaged rooms at either of the hotels. Leon had decided, if it was possible, to get lodgings near to the scene of the tragedy, and in this he was successful. Three houses removed from the corner house where Doctor Twenden was in residence he discovered furnished lodgings were to let.

A kindly rosy-faced Devonshire woman was the landlady, and they were the only tenants, her husband being a gunner on one of His Majesty’s ships, and he was at sea. She showed them a bright sitting-room and two bedrooms on the same floor. Manfred ordered tea, and when the door closed on the woman, he turned to behold Leon standing by the window gazing intently at the palm of his left hand, which was enclosed, as was the other, in a grey silk glove.

===Chinese===

“一次彻头彻尾令人满意的参观，”莱昂说，“说实在的，这是我所进过的监狱里方便得出奇的一座。”

“进去方便，还是出来方便？”曼弗雷德问。

“两样都方便。”莱昂说。

两家旅馆他们都没有订房。莱昂打定主意，若有可能，就借宿在出事地点近旁，而这一点他办到了。离特温登博士住的那座街角宅子隔三家门面，他寻着一处带家具的寓所出租。

房东太太是位和气的红脸膛德文郡妇人；房客只有他们两位——她丈夫在一艘皇家军舰上当炮手，此刻正在海上。她领他们看了一间敞亮的起居室和同一层的两间卧室。曼弗雷德吩咐上茶；房门在妇人身后合拢后，他一转身，只见莱昂立在窗前，正凝神打量自己的左手掌心——那只手同另一只一样，笼在灰丝手套里。

===Original===

Manfred laughed.

“I don’t usually make comments on our attire, my dear Leon,” he said, “and remembering your Continental origin, it is remarkable that you commit so few errors in dress—from an Englishman’s point of view,” he added.

“It’s queer, isn’t it,” said Leon, still looking at his palm.

“But I’ve never seen you wearing silk gloves before,” Manfred went on curiously. “In Spain it is not unusual to wear cotton gloves, or even silk—”

“The finest silk,” murmured Leon, “and I cannot bend my hand in it.”

===Chinese===

曼弗雷德笑了。

“我一向不对咱们的衣着发表意见，亲爱的莱昂，”他说，“况且想到你出身欧陆，你在穿戴礼数上出的差错竟这样少，实在难得——以英国人的眼光看。”他补了一句。

“有点怪，是不是？”莱昂说，眼睛仍盯着掌心。

“可我从没见你戴过丝手套，”曼弗雷德好奇地接下去，“在西班牙，戴棉线手套不算稀奇，戴丝手套也——”

“顶细的丝，”莱昂喃喃道，“可我的手在里头弯不得。”

===Original===

“Is that why you’ve been carrying it in your pocket,” said Manfred in surprise, and Gonsalez nodded.

“I cannot bend my hand in it,” he said, “because in the palm of my hand is a stiff copperplate and on that plate is half an inch thickness of plastic clay of a peculiarly fine texture.”

“I see,” said Manfred slowly.

“I love Baxeter prison,” said Leon, “and the Deputy Governor is a dear young man: his joy in my surprise and interest when he showed me the cells was delightful to see. He even let me examine the master key of the prison, which naturally he carried, and if, catching and holding his eye, I pressed the business end of the key against the palm of my gloved hand, why it was done in a second, my dear George, and there was nothing left on the key to show him the unfair advantage I had taken.”

He had taken a pair of folding scissors from his pocket and dexterously had opened them and was soon cutting away the silk palm of the glove.

===Chinese===

“所以你才一直把手揣在口袋里？”曼弗雷德讶然道，冈萨雷斯点了点头。

“我的手在里头弯不得，”他说，“因为我掌心里衬着一块硬铜板，铜板上又敷着半英寸厚、质地格外细腻的可塑黏土。”

“原来如此。”曼弗雷德缓缓地说。

“我爱巴克斯特监狱，”莱昂说，“副狱长是位招人疼的年轻人：他领我看牢房时，我脸上露出的惊讶与兴致叫他欢喜得不得了。他甚至让我察看监狱的万能钥匙——那自然须臾不离他身——而我呢，一边拿眼睛定住他，一边把钥匙使唤的那一头往我戴手套的掌心一按——这就成了，我亲爱的乔治，前后不过一秒钟，钥匙上不剩一点痕迹，他瞧不出我占了这个不地道的便宜。”

他已从口袋里摸出一把折剪，灵巧地展开，不多时便把那只手套的丝掌剪了开来。

===Original===

“‘How wonderful,’ said I, ‘and that is the master key!’ and so we went on to see the punishment cell and the garden and the little unkempt graves where the dead men lie who have broken the law, and all the time I had to keep my hand in my pocket, for fear I’d knock against something and spoil the impression. Here it is.”

The underside of the palm had evidently been specially prepared for the silk came off easily, leaving a thin grey slab of slate-coloured clay in the centre of which was clearly the impression of a key.

“The little hole at the side is where you dug the point of the key to get the diameter?” said Manfred, and Leon nodded.

“This is the master key of Baxeter Gaol, my dear Manfred,” he said with a smile, as he laid it upon the table. “With this I could walk in—no, I couldn’t.” He stopped suddenly and bit his lip.

“You are colossal,” said Manfred admiringly.

===Chinese===

“‘妙极了，’我说，‘这就是万能钥匙！’于是我们接着去看惩戒室、菜园子，还有那些没人打理的小坟——躺的都是犯了王法的死人。这一路我只好把手老插在口袋里，就怕磕着碰着，毁了印模。喏，在这儿。”

手套掌面的里侧显然是特意敷治过的，丝料轻轻一揭便了下来，露出一方薄薄的石板灰色黏土，正中清清楚楚印着一把钥匙的模子。

“边上这个小孔，是你拿钥匙尖扎出来量口径用的？”曼弗雷德说，莱昂点了点头。

“我亲爱的曼弗雷德，这就是巴克斯特监狱的万能钥匙。”他微笑着把它搁到桌上。“凭它我能大摇大摆走进去——不，走不进去。”他猛地打住，咬了咬嘴唇。

“你真是个巨人。”曼弗雷德叹服地说。

===Original===

“Aren’t I,” said Leon with a wry face. “Do you know there is one door we can’t open?”

“What is that?”

“The big gates outside. They can only be opened from the inside. H’m.”

He laid his hat carefully over the clay mould when the landlady came in with the tray.

Leon sipped his tea, staring vacantly at the lurid wallpaper, and Manfred did nor interrupt his thoughts.

===Chinese===

“可不是么。”莱昂苦着脸说，“你可知道，有一扇门咱们打不开？”

“哪一扇？”

“外头的大门。那门只能从里面开。嗯。”

房东太太端着茶盘进来时，他不动声色，把帽子端端正正罩在黏土模子上。

莱昂啜着茶，怔怔地望着那艳俗扎眼的墙纸出神；曼弗雷德也不打搅他的思绪。

===Original===

Leon Gonsalez had ever been the schemer of the Four Just Men, and he had developed each particular of his plan as though it were a story he was telling himself. His extraordinary imagination enabled him to foresee every contingency. Manfred had often said that the making of the plan gave Leon as much pleasure as its successful consummation.

“What a stupid idiot I am,” he said at last. “I didn’t realise that there was no keyhole in the main gate of a prison—except of course Dartmoor.”

Again he relapsed into silent contemplation of the wall, a silence broken by cryptic mutterings.

“I send the wire … It must come, of course, from London … They would send down if the wire was strong enough. It must be five men—no five could go into a taxi—six … If the door of the van is locked, but it won’t be … If it fails then I could try the next night.”

===Chinese===

莱昂·冈萨雷斯从来就是四义士（The Four Just Men）里运筹帷幄的那一个；他把计划的每一处细节铺展开来，就像在给自己讲一个故事。他那超凡的想象力，足以把种种不虞之患一一料到。曼弗雷德常说，出谋划策这件事本身给莱昂的乐趣，不亚于计划的大功告成。

“我真是蠢货一个，”他终于开口，“我竟没想到，监狱大门上根本没有锁眼——当然，达特穆尔（Dartmoor）除外。”

他又沉入对墙壁的默默凝望，那沉默时不时被几句没头没尾的咕哝打破。

“我去发电报……电报自然得从伦敦发……只要电文够分量，他们会把人派下来。得是五个人——不，五个人坐不进一辆出租汽车——六个……囚车的门若上了锁——不会上的……万一不成，第二天夜里我还能再试。”

===Original===

“What on earth are you talking about?” asked Manfred good-humouredly.

Leon woke with a start from his reverie.

“We must first prove that this fellow is guilty,” he said, “and we’ll have to start on that job tonight. I wonder if our good landlady has a garden.”

The good landlady had one. It stretched out for two hundred yards at the back of the house, and Leon made a survey and was satisfied.

===Chinese===

“你到底在念叨什么呀？”曼弗雷德好脾气地问。

莱昂从沉思中悚然惊觉。

“咱们得先证明这家伙有罪，”他说，“这桩差事今晚就得着手。不知咱们那位好房东太太有没有个园子。”

好房东太太有一个。园子在屋后铺展开去，足有两百码，莱昂踏勘了一遍，心下满意。

===Original===

“The doctor’s place?” he asked innocently, as the landlady pointed out this object of interest. “Not the man who was tried at Baxeter?”

“The very man,” said the woman triumphantly. “I tell you, it caused a bit of a sensation round here.”

“Do you think he was innocent?”

The landlady was not prepared to take a definite standpoint.

===Chinese===

“医生的房子？”趁房东太太指点这处景致，他装出不经意的口气问，“不会就是在巴克斯特受审的那位吧？”

“正是他本人。”妇人得意地说，“跟您说吧，这一带着实轰动过一阵子。”

“您觉得他是清白的么？”

房东太太不肯把话说死。

===Original===

“Some think one thing and some think the other,” she replied, in the true spirit of diplomacy. “He’s always been a nice man, and he attended my husband when he was home last.”

“Is the doctor staying in his house?”

“Yes, sir,” said the woman. “He’s going abroad soon.”

“Oh yes, he is distributing that money, isn’t he? I read something about it in the newspapers—the poor are to benefit, are they not?”

===Chinese===

“有人这么想，有人那么想。”她以一种十足的外交气度答道，“他一向是个好人；我丈夫上次回家，还是他给瞧的病呢。”

“医生如今还住在他自己宅子里么？”

“是的，先生，”妇人说，“他不久就要出国了。”

“哦对了，他不是要把那笔钱散出去么？我在报上读到过一点——穷人们要沾光了，是不是？”

===Original===

The landlady sniffed.

“I hope they get it,” she said significantly.

“Which means that you don’t think they will,” smiled Manfred, strolling back from an inspection of her early chrysanthemums.

“They may,” said the cautious landlady, “but nothing has happened yet. The vicar went to the doctor yesterday morning, and asked him whether a little of it couldn’t be spared for the poor of Newton Abbot. We’ve had a lot of unemployment here lately, and the doctor said ‘Yes he would think about it,’ and sent him a cheque for fifty pounds from what I heard.”

===Chinese===

房东太太嗤了嗤鼻子。

“我盼着他们拿到手呢。”她话里有话地说。

“这就是说，您觉得他们拿不到。”曼弗雷德微笑道，他刚把她的早菊赏看了一遍，正踱回来。

“也许拿得到，”谨慎的房东太太说，“可至今还不见动静。牧师昨天上午去见医生，问这笔钱能不能匀出一点来给牛顿阿伯特的穷人。我们这儿近来失业的人多，医生说‘行，他会考虑的’——据我听说，还给他送去了一张 50 镑的支票。”

===Original===

“That’s not a great deal,” said Manfred. “What makes you think he is going abroad?”

“All his trunks are packed and his servants are under notice, that’s how I came to know,” said the landlady. “I don’t think it’s a bad thing. Poor soul, she didn’t have a very happy life.”

The “poor soul” referred to was apparently the doctor’s wife, and when asked to explain, the landlady knew no more than that people had talked, that there was probably nothing in it, and why shouldn’t the doctor go motoring on the moor with pretty girls if he felt that way inclined.

“He had his fancies,” said the landlady.

===Chinese===

“这可算不上大手笔，”曼弗雷德说，“您怎么知道他要出国？”

“他的箱笼全捆扎停当，仆人也都被辞退了，我就是这么知道的。”房东太太说，“我看这不是坏事。可怜的人，她这辈子过得可不痛快。”

那位“可怜的人”指的显然是医生的太太。再问究竟，房东太太也只知道人们嚼过舌根，多半是捕风捉影；又说，医生若真有那个心思，带漂亮姑娘坐汽车去荒原兜兜风，又有什么使不得。

“他有些风流癖好。”房东太太说。

===Original===

Apparently those “fancies” came and went through the years of his married life.

“I should like to meet the doctor,” said Leon, but she shook her head.

“He won’t see anyone, not even his patients, sir,” she said.

Nevertheless Leon succeeded in obtaining an interview. He had judged the man’s character correctly thus far, and he knew he would not refuse an interview with a journalist.

===Chinese===

看来这些“癖好”在他成亲的岁月里来来去去，就没有断过。

“我倒想见见这位医生。”莱昂说，可她摇了摇头。

“他谁也不见，连病人都不见，先生。”她说。

莱昂终究还是见着了。到目前为止，他对这人脾性的揣摩没有落空，他知道此人不会拒绝记者的访问。

===Original===

The servant took Leon’s name, closing the front door in his face while she went to see the doctor, and when she came back it was to invite him in.

He found the medical gentleman in his study, and the dismantled condition of the room supported Mrs. Martin’s statement that he was leaving the town at an early date. He was in fact engaged in destroying old business letters and bills when Leon arrived.

“Come in,” grumbled the doctor. “I suppose if I didn’t see you, you’d invent something about me. Now what do you want?”

He was a good-looking young man with regular features, a carefully trimmed black moustache and tiny black side-whiskers.

“Light-blue eyes I do not like,” said Leon to himself, “and I should like to see you without a moustache.”

===Chinese===

仆人记下莱昂的名姓，当着他的面掩上前门进去通传；再出来时，便是请他进去了。

他在书房里见到这位医学绅士；屋里拆卸一空的景象，印证了马丁太太（Mrs. Martin）说他日内便要离城的话。莱昂到时，他正忙着销毁旧日的业务信函和账单。

“进来吧。”医生咕哝道，“我要是不见你，你们准会编排我的新闻。说吧，你要什么？”

他是个相貌堂堂的年轻人，五官端正，黑髭修剪得一丝不苟，颊边蓄着两小绺黑连鬓胡。

“浅蓝色的眼睛，我不喜欢，”莱昂心里说，“我倒想看看你剃了髭须是什么模样。”

===Original===

“I’ve been sent down from London to ask to what charities you are distributing your wife’s money, Dr. Twenden,” said Leon, with the brisk and even rude directness of a London reporter.

The doctor’s lips curled.

“The least they can do is to give me a chance to make up my mind,” he said. “The fact is, I’ve got to go abroad on business, and whilst I’m away I shall carefully consider the merits of the various charity organisations of Devon to discover which are the most worthy and how the money is to be distributed.”

“Suppose you don’t come back again?” asked Leon cruelly. “I mean, anything might happen; the ship may sink or the train smash—what happens to the money then?”

“That is entirely my affair,” said the doctor stiffly, and closing his eyes, arched his eyebrows for a second as he spoke. “I really don’t wish to reopen this matter. I’ve had some very charming letters from the public, but I’ve had abusive ones too. I had one this morning saying that it was a pity that the Four Just Men were not in existence! The Four just Men!” he smiled contemptuously, “as though I should have cared a snap of my fingers for that kind of cattle!”

===Chinese===

“我是从伦敦下来的，特温登博士，想请问您打算把太太的钱散给哪些慈善团体。”莱昂说，拿出伦敦记者那股麻利乃至无礼的直劲。

医生的嘴角撇了撇。

“他们至少也该容我把主意拿定。”他说，“实情是，我有事必须出国；趁我不在，我要细细考察德文各家慈善团体的优劣，看哪些最配受助，这笔钱又该怎么个散法。”

“倘若您一去不回呢？”莱昂刻薄地问，“我是说，什么事都保不齐；船会沉，火车会撞——那时候钱又归谁？”

“那纯属我个人的事。”医生板起脸说；说话间他阖上眼，眉毛扬起了一瞬。“我真不愿再翻这桩旧账了。公众来信有讨人喜欢的，也有骂街的。今早我就收到一封，说可惜四义士不存在了！四义士！”他轻蔑地一笑，“好像那种货色值得我弹一下指头似的！”

===Original===

Leon smiled too.

“Perhaps it would be more convenient if I saw you tonight,” he suggested.

The doctor shook his head.

“I’m to be the guest of honour of a few friends of mine,” he said, with a queer air of importance, “and I shan’t be back until half past eleven at the earliest.”

===Chinese===

莱昂也笑了笑。

“或许我今晚再来见您，更方便些。”他提议道。

医生摇了摇头。

“几位朋友今晚请我去做主宾，”他带着一种说不出的自矜神气说，“我最早也得十一点半才能回来。”

===Original===

“Where is the dinner to be held? That might make an interesting item of news,” said Leon.

“It’s to be held at the Lion Hotel. You can say that Sir John Murden is in the chair, and that Lord Tussborough has promised to attend. I can give you the list of the people who’ll be there.”

“The dinner engagement is a genuine one,” thought Leon with satisfaction.

The list was forthcoming, and pocketing the paper with due reverence, Gonsalez bowed himself out. From his bedroom window that evening he watched the doctor, splendidly arrayed, enter a taxi and drive away. A quarter of an hour later the servant, whom Leon had seen, came out pulling on her gloves. Gonsalez watched her for a good quarter of an hour, during which time she stood at the corner of the street. She was obviously waiting for something or somebody. What it was he saw. The Torquay bus passed by, stopped, and she mounted it.

===Chinese===

“宴席设在哪儿？这倒能凑一条有趣的新闻。”莱昂说。

“设在狮子酒店（Lion Hotel）。你可以写上约翰·默登爵士（Sir John Murden）做主席，塔斯伯勒勋爵（Lord Tussborough）应允出席。到场人物的名单，我也能给你一份。”

“这顿饭局是真的。”莱昂满意地想。

名单拿来了，冈萨雷斯恭而敬之地把那张纸收进衣袋，躬身告退。当晚，他从卧室窗口望见医生衣冠楚楚，钻进一辆出租汽车驶走了。一刻钟后，莱昂白天见过的那个女仆出来，一边走一边往上戴手套。冈萨雷斯盯了她足有一刻钟，这段时间里她就站在街角，分明在等什么人或什么东西。等的是什么，他看见了：开往托基的公共汽车驶过来，停住，她上了车。

===Original===

After dinner he had a talk with the landlady and brought the conversation back to the doctor’s house.

“I suppose it requires a lot of servants to keep a big house like that going.”

“He’s only got one now, sir: Milly Brown, who lives in Torquay. She is leaving on Saturday. The cook left last week. The doctor has all his meals at the hotel.”

He left Manfred to talk to the landlady—and Manfred could be very entertaining.

===Chinese===

晚饭后他同房东太太闲话，把话头又引回医生宅子上。

“那么大一座宅子，运转起来总得不少仆人吧。”

“他如今只留一个了，先生：米莉·布朗（Milly Brown），住在托基。她星期六就要走了。厨子上礼拜辞的。医生一日三餐都在酒店用。”

他把曼弗雷德留下陪房东太太说话——曼弗雷德逗起趣来是很有一手的。

===Original===

Slipping through the garden he reached a little alleyway at the back of the houses. The back gate giving admission to the doctor’s garden was locked, but the wall was not high. He expected that the door of the house would be fastened, and he was not surprised when he found it was locked.
